# Changelog

All notable changes to this project will be documented in this file.

The format is based on [Keep a Changelog](https://keepachangelog.com/en/1.1.0/),
and this project adheres to [Semantic Versioning](https://semver.org/spec/v2.0.0.html).

Older release notes are archived under [`docs/`](docs/) when the leading
version digit moves up. The active file holds the current `[Unreleased]`
section plus the most recent leading-digit cycle (currently `0.1.x`); past
cycles live in dedicated archives:

- [`docs/CHANGELOG-0.0.x.md`](docs/CHANGELOG-0.0.x.md) — `0.0.1` through `0.0.9`.

## [Unreleased]

### Added

- **[cli]** `rigor check --format` gains six CI-native output formats so diagnostics surface inline in a pull / merge request instead of only in the job log ([ADR-51](docs/adr/51-ci-diagnostic-output-formats.md)):
  - `--format sarif` writes a SARIF 2.1.0 report. GitHub's `upload-sarif` renders it on the PR diff and in the Security tab, and any other SARIF-aware tool consumes the same file — the cross-platform option.
  - `--format github` emits GitHub Actions workflow commands (`::error file=…,line=…::`) that the runner turns into inline PR annotations, with no upload step.
  - `--format gitlab` emits a GitLab Code Quality (CodeClimate-subset) report that GitLab reads from a `codequality` artifact to populate the merge-request widget.
  - `--format checkstyle` emits Checkstyle XML — the format [reviewdog](https://github.com/reviewdog/reviewdog) reads (`-f=checkstyle`) to post findings to any of its reporters (GitHub PR review, GitLab MR discussion, Gerrit, Bitbucket, Gitea), and that Jenkins and other tools consume too.
  - `--format junit` emits JUnit XML — the test-report format GitHub test reporting, GitLab, CircleCI, and Jenkins render.
  - `--format teamcity` emits TeamCity inspection service messages for the TeamCity Inspections view.
  - All six are a presentation layer over the same diagnostics as `--format json` — no new analysis. Severities map per format (e.g. error/warning/info → SARIF `error`/`warning`/`note`, GitHub `::error`/`::warning`/`::notice`, GitLab `major`/`minor`/`info`), and the exit code is unchanged (`0` clean, `1` on errors) so the job still gates the pipeline.
- **[cli]** `rigor check` now detects the CI environment at run time and surfaces diagnostics the platform-native way on the default output, with no `--format` needed — modelled on PHPStan's CI auto-detection ([ADR-51](docs/adr/51-ci-diagnostic-output-formats.md) WD7).
  - Under **GitHub Actions** (or **TeamCity**) it appends that platform's inline annotations on top of the human text output, so the readable log and the inline PR surface both appear.
  - Under **GitLab CI** it prints a one-line hint to use `--format gitlab` with a `codequality` artifact; under other CIs (CircleCI, Jenkins, Travis, Azure Pipelines, Bitbucket, Buildkite, Drone, …) it hints the reviewdog or `--format junit` path.
  - It augments only the default `text` output — an explicit `--format` is left untouched — and is fully suppressible with `--no-ci-detect` or `RIGOR_CI_DETECT=0`.
- **[docs]** Copy-paste CI setup templates under [`docs/manual/ci-templates/`](docs/manual/ci-templates/) — SARIF / annotations / reviewdog `.github/workflows/rigor.yml` variants, a `.gitlab-ci.yml`, and a generic recipe — wiring the new formats into a Ruby-4.0 isolated job (the templates [ADR-27](docs/adr/27-tool-distribution-model.md) § WD3 left queued). The CI manual chapter ([docs/manual/11-ci.md](docs/manual/11-ci.md)) documents each.
- **[skill]** New bundled `rigor-ci-setup` Agent Skill (discoverable via `rigor skill`) that walks a project through wiring Rigor into its CI — choosing the platform, the output format, and the optional reviewdog path via [`reviewdog/action-setup`](https://github.com/reviewdog/action-setup).
- **[plugin-api]** `Rigor::Scope#discovery` / `#with_discovery` expose the new immutable `Scope::DiscoveryIndex` — the single object now carrying every seed-time discovery table (`discovered_*`, `declared_types`, the class-level accumulators, `data_member_layouts`) that control flow never varies ([ADR-53](docs/adr/53-scope-discovery-index-separation.md) Track A). All existing per-table readers and `with_discovered_*` writers keep working unchanged; the writers are now shims over `with_discovery`.

### Changed

- **[cache]** `.rigor/cache` no longer stores the two RBS definitions blobs (`rbs.instance_definitions` / `rbs.singleton_definitions`, ~23 MB per project) — method definitions are now built on demand from the already-cached RBS environment, which measured *faster* than loading the blobs ([ADR-54](docs/adr/54-cache-slimming.md) WD1). Warm runs get quicker and allocate less; diagnostics are unchanged. Stale blob entries left by older versions are ignored.
- **[cache]** Cache entries are now zlib-compressed on disk ([ADR-54](docs/adr/54-cache-slimming.md) WD2) — the RBS environment entry shrinks from ~11 MB to ~1.8 MB (16 %), at a read cost an order of magnitude below the deserialisation it sits next to. Together with the blob retirement, a typical project's cache drops from ~33 MB to ~2 MB. The on-disk format version is bumped: entries written by older Rigor versions are transparently treated as misses and rebuilt on the next run — no action needed.
- **[cache]** Cache eviction is now on by default: `cache.max_bytes` defaults to 256 MB instead of unlimited ([ADR-54](docs/adr/54-cache-slimming.md) WD3). Content-keyed entries orphaned by a gem upgrade or signature change were previously never deleted (one long-lived checkout had accumulated ~180 MB of them); the cap reaps least-recently-used entries at the end of a run while staying far above any active set (~2 MB). Set `cache.max_bytes: null` to restore the old unbounded behaviour. The cap is documented in the [caching](docs/manual/12-caching.md) and [configuration](docs/manual/03-configuration.md) manual chapters.
- **[cache]** Upgrading across the cache-format change now also *reclaims* the old cache's disk space, not just ignores it: the cache root's version marker covers the on-disk format, so the first run after the upgrade clears the unreadable old entries (up to ~32 MB per project) instead of leaving them stranded below the eviction cap. The `--incremental` snapshot is now zlib-compressed too, matching every other cache artefact on disk.
- **[engine]** Plugin consultation on the analysis hot paths now goes through a contribution table compiled once per run ([ADR-52](docs/adr/52-compiled-plugin-contribution-dispatch.md) WD1, slice 1). Per-call `dynamic_return` / `type_specifier` consultation is gated by compiled method-name sets, `block_as_methods` DSL matching is a hash lookup keyed by verb, `owns_receivers` / `open_receivers` / `additional_initializers` / protocol-contract queries answer from frozen precomputed aggregates instead of re-deriving them per call site, and the per-file plugin diagnostic loop skips plugins that contribute neither a `diagnostics_for_file` override nor a `node_rule`. Pure overhead reduction on plugin-heavy projects — diagnostics are unchanged.

### Fixed

- **[engine]** An `untyped` argument no longer selects a value-pinning RBS overload purely by declaration order. `Kernel#Array` declares `(nil) -> []` first, so `Array(arg)` over an untyped `arg` typed as the empty tuple `[]` — folding downstream guards like `keys.uniq.size == keys.size` to constants and firing a false `flow.always-truthy-condition` (surfaced by the self-check on `Type::HashShape`). Overloads whose parameters admit only specific values (`nil` / literal types) now require the argument to prove the value; calls with a literal argument (`Array(nil)` → `[]`) keep their precise fold, and methods whose only overload is value-pinning are unaffected.

- **[engine]** `Data.define` member folding now works inside method bodies. The method-entry scope dropped the member-layout table recorded at index time, so a member read like `Point.new(3, 4).x` inside a `def` silently degraded to `Dynamic[top]` instead of folding to `3` ([ADR-48](docs/adr/48-data-struct-value-folding.md)). The restored precision also taught acceptance that a folded member instance is a value of its tagging class, so a declared return such as `AssertEffect?` accepts it without a spurious `def.return-type-mismatch`. The [ADR-53](docs/adr/53-scope-discovery-index-separation.md) Track A extraction then closed the same gap structurally at the second hand-copy site (re-typed user-method bodies, which had also been dropping the method-visibility table): nested body scopes now inherit the whole discovery index by reference, so a table can no longer be lost to a missed per-field copy.

## [0.1.17] - 2026-06-06

v0.1.17 focuses on making analysis of real projects markedly faster — most visibly an incremental analysis cache that re-checks only the files an edit affects, an unchanged-project fast path, and a large allocation reduction on big Rails apps. Inspired by the [Elixir v1.20 type system](docs/notes/20260604-elixir-v1.20-type-system-rigor-review.md), control-flow narrowing is strengthened for several methods, so non-empty `Array` guards and `Hash` key-presence checks now refine reads precisely. It also folds `Data.define` value objects to precise member types and adds new clause-reachability and conformance diagnostics. Fixes include a block-parameter binding crash on the `|a, *b, c|` shape and a pathological route-helper re-read.

### Added

- **[performance]** `rigor check --incremental` re-analyzes only the files that changed since the last run plus the files that depend on them, serving every other file's diagnostics from a cross-process disk snapshot ([ADR-46](docs/adr/46-incremental-dependency-graph.md)).
  - Where the unchanged-project cache re-runs the whole project on any change, this re-runs only the affected closure: editing a leaf controller re-checks one file, editing a model re-checks the model and its callers. On Rigor's own `lib` (262 files) a warm no-change run is 0.75s vs 7.2s full (~9.6×) and a one-file leaf edit is 1.15s (~6.3×).
  - File additions and removals are reconciled incrementally instead of forcing a full rebuild — an added file re-checks whatever depended on its now-defined names, a removed file's dependents are re-checked.
  - Dependencies are tracked at `(file, "Class#method")` granularity, so editing one method re-checks only that method's callers, not every caller of the file.
  - Diagnostics are guaranteed identical to a full run by `rigor check --verify-incremental` (run in CI as `make check-incremental`); the snapshot is keyed on the configuration, gem set, project RBS, and engine version, drops on any change, and a cache error never breaks a run — so a stale result is never served.
- **[performance]** `rigor check` serves an unchanged project's whole result from a record-and-validate cache instead of re-running the per-file inference ([ADR-45](docs/adr/45-unchanged-project-fast-path.md)).
  - An unchanged Mastodon `app/models` (248 files) drops from ~11.6s to ~1.8s (~6×). The cache records every file the run actually read — including files a plugin reads mid-analysis — and re-digests them next run, so the result always matches a fresh run.
- **[engine]** `Data.define` value objects now fold member reads to precise types ([ADR-48](docs/adr/48-data-struct-value-folding.md)): `Point = Data.define(:x, :y); Point.new(1, 2).x` reads `1` (a `Constant`) instead of `Dynamic[top]`.
  - It covers all three definition forms (constant-assigned, the `class X < Data.define(...)` subclass idiom, and a bare local), both positional and keyword construction, and the `.x` / `[:x]` / `to_h` / `deconstruct` / `members` / `with` projections.
  - It is precision-additive only: every uncertain shape (non-literal members, a `.new` arity / keyword mismatch, a body-redefined reader) degrades to the class nominal, so it can only make a read more precise, never make a working program fail a check. `Struct` folding is deferred because its instances are mutable.
- **[diagnostics]** New `flow.unreachable-clause` rule flags a `case`/`when` or `case`/`in` clause the engine's own narrowing proves can never match — a `when String` over a subject narrowed to `Integer`, or a clause an earlier one already covers ([ADR-47](docs/adr/47-narrowing-driven-clause-reachability.md)).
  - It handles bare class patterns (`when C` / `in C` / `in C => x`) and stays inside `flow.always-truthy-condition`'s false-positive envelope: never on a `Dynamic` subject, never inside loops or blocks. It ships `:info` in the lenient and balanced profiles and `:warning` under strict; suppress a site with `# rigor:disable unreachable-clause`.
- **[diagnostics]** New `%a{rigor:v1:conforms-to _Interface}` directive and `rbs_extended.unsatisfied-conformance` rule let an RBS class or module assert it satisfies a named structural interface, checked as a design assertion whether or not any call site exercises it ([rbs-extended.md](docs/type-specification/rbs-extended.md)).
  - It checks method presence and signature compatibility (covariant return, contravariant parameters); the check is false-positive-safe because both sides are authored RBS, the same construction as the ADR-35 `def.override-*` rules. It is opt-in and authored `:warning` (`:error` under strict).
- **[diagnostics]** New `call.self-undefined-method` rule flags an implicit-self call (no explicit receiver) that resolves to no method — the self-call companion to `call.undefined-method` ([ADR-24](docs/adr/24-self-method-call-resolution.md)).
  - It fires only on a confidently-closed class (no superclass or `include`, no `method_missing`, not a plugin-declared open receiver) and never inside a module or on a `Dynamic` self. It ships `:off` in every profile pending a regression-corpus gate; opt in with `severity_overrides: { call.self-undefined-method: warning }`.
- **[engine]** Inside an `arr.empty?` (false edge), `arr.any?` (true edge), or `arr.none?` (false edge) guard, an `Array[T]` receiver narrows to non-empty, so `arr.size` / `length` / `count` read `positive-int` instead of `non-negative-int`. It narrows only a bare call on an `Array` local or ivar; `Dynamic` and non-`Array` receivers are untouched.
- **[engine]** Inside an `h.key?(:foo)` / `h.has_key?(:foo)` guard (literal key), an optional hash-shape key narrows to present on the true branch — so a later `h[:foo]` reads the declared value type instead of `T | nil` — and to absent on the false branch. It removes only the optionality `nil` (never the value's own intrinsic `nil`) and narrows only literal-key calls on a known optional-key shape.
- **[cache]** `cache.max_bytes:` in `.rigor.yml` bounds the analysis cache: the least-recently-used entries are evicted at the end of a run when the total exceeds the limit ([ADR-6](docs/adr/6-cache-persistence-backend.md)). The default (`null`) keeps the prior unbounded behaviour.

### Changed

- **[performance]** Core analysis on a large Rails app allocates ~42% fewer objects with byte-identical diagnostics — Mastodon `app`+`lib` (1,303 files): 87.8M → 51.3M objects, wall ~30s → ~26s — via memoised ancestor and RBS-type-name lookups and a single-pass control-flow join ([profiling note](docs/notes/20260604-mastodon-allocation-profile.md)).
- **[performance]** Plugin-heavy analysis allocates far less with byte-identical diagnostics — a GitLab subset (2,630 files, 11 plugins) dropped from ~522M to ~213M objects (wall ~163s → ~120s) by memoising plugin-contribution snapshots, visiting only the plugins that implement a per-call path, and collapsing body-scope construction into a single allocation ([ADR-44](docs/adr/44-dispatch-allocation-churn.md)).

### Fixed

- **[config]** `dependencies: { source_inference: false }` in `.rigor.yml` no longer crashes the run — `false` (and an omitted key) now mean "source-inference disabled" instead of being fed into the per-entry parser. A genuinely malformed value still reports a clear configuration error.
- **[engine]** Block parameter binding no longer raises `NoMethodError` on a trailing required positional after a rest (the `|a, *b, c|` shape).
- **[plugins/rigor-rails-routes]** A missing or unparseable `config/routes.rb` no longer triggers a re-read and re-parse of the routes file on every route-helper call site — the unbuilt helper table is now memoised (a cold GitLab run dropped from 105,724 route-file reads to 2). Diagnostics are unchanged.

## [0.1.16] - 2026-06-03

### Removed

- **[plugin contract]** The inert `protocols:` manifest field is retired ([ADR-2](docs/adr/2-extension-api.md) vestige); it was declared but read by no rule, dispatcher, loader, or the `rigor plugins --capabilities` catalogue.
  - This sharpens the vocabulary: **interface** is the structural type (RBS `interface`), while **protocol contract** is the path-scoped behavioural contract of [ADR-28](docs/adr/28-path-scoped-protocol-contracts.md). A pre-1.0 breaking change to `Manifest` with near-zero blast radius — no bundled plugin declared it.

### Added

- **[plugin contract]** Node-scoped diagnostic rules: a plugin declares `node_rule(Prism::CallNode) { |node, scope, path| … }` and the engine owns a single AST walk per file, so an author writes the check without hand-rolling traversal ([ADR-37](docs/adr/37-plugin-interface-segregation.md) Slice 1).
  - `node_file_context { |root, scope| … }` runs once per file before the walk and is threaded to each rule, supporting same-file two-pass (collect-then-validate) plugins.
  - Every bundled diagnostic-emitting plugin is migrated onto `node_rule`; `diagnostics_for_file` remains as the whole-file escape valve.
- **[plugin contract]** Node rules now receive a `Rigor::Plugin::NodeContext` exposing a node's lexical ancestors (`enclosing_def` / `enclosing_module` / `enclosing_block(name)` / `ancestors`), so a per-node rule can see its enclosing class, method, or block DSL ([ADR-37](docs/adr/37-plugin-interface-segregation.md) Slice 1d).
- **[plugin contract]** Two declaratively-gated class DSLs split return-type and narrowing contributions out of the now-deprecated `flow_contribution_for`: `dynamic_return(receivers:)` for a per-call-site return type and `type_specifier(methods:)` for predicate/assertion narrowing ([ADR-37](docs/adr/37-plugin-interface-segregation.md) Slice 2).
- **[rigor plugins]** New `--capabilities` flag emits a machine-readable catalogue of what each loaded plugin contributes — the node types its rules match, the receiver classes its `dynamic_return`s gate on, the methods its `type_specifier`s narrow, and the facts it produces / consumes.
  - With `--format=json` an AI agent or human can learn a plugin's surface without reading its source; the same surfaces also appear in the default `rigor plugins` report.
- **[plugin contract]** A plugin's `config_schema` may now declare a default value alongside the kind — `"dsl_method" => { kind: :string, default: "state_machine" }` — and `Plugin::Base#config` merges declared defaults under the user config, retiring the `DEFAULT_*`-constant idiom ([ADR-40](docs/adr/40-config-schema-defaults.md)).
  - A wrongly-typed default fails loudly at load. Thirteen bundled plugins are migrated; the bare-kind form is unchanged.
- **[plugin contract]** New author-facing helpers cut diagnostic boilerplate: `Plugin::Base#diagnostic(node, path:, message:, …)` internalises the 1-based line / column convention, and `Rigor::Source::Literals` extracts a literal `:sym` / `"str"` argument.
  - `Source::Literals` now covers both axes of its extraction grid — accepted kinds (`SymbolNode` only / `SymbolNode` | `StringNode`) × returned type (`Symbol` / `String`). Ten bundled plugins and one example are migrated onto it.
  - `diagnostic(…, location:)` points a diagnostic at a sub-location such as the method name rather than the receiver-spanning whole call.
- **[plugin contract]** New `Plugin::Base.suggest(name, candidates)` "did you mean …?" helper backed by `DidYouMean::SpellChecker`, retiring the hand-rolled Levenshtein copies in several plugins. It only changes the suggestion text on an already-emitted diagnostic, never whether one fires.
- **[plugin contract]** Rails-family inflection (`posts` ↔ `post`, `BlogPost` → `blog_posts`) now uses the real `ActiveSupport::Inflector` via a shared `Rigor::Plugin::Inflector`, so inflected route-helper and model names match Rails exactly instead of three hand-rolled approximations ([ADR-39](docs/adr/39-plugin-target-library-invocation.md)).
  - A plugin MAY call the pure, allow-listed methods of its declared target library directly; the analyzed application's own code is still never executed. When `ActiveSupport::Inflector` cannot load, the inflector declines rather than guessing.
  - `rigor-rspec-rails`'s `have_http_status(:symbol)` validation likewise now reads the real `Rack::Utils::SYMBOL_TO_STATUS_CODE` instead of a vendored snapshot that could lag a newly-added status symbol.
- **[plugin contract]** Selectable isolation strategy for target-library invocation, `process` by default: a forked persistent worker loads and calls the library so even a `SIGSEGV` is contained ([ADR-39](docs/adr/39-plugin-target-library-invocation.md) slice 5).
  - Chosen via `.rigor.yml` `plugins_isolation:` (`process` / `none` / experimental `ruby_box`); falls back to `none` where `fork` is unavailable.
- **[plugin contract]** Executable evidence that the contract supports out-of-tree third-party `rigor-*` gems: a fixture plugin outside `plugins/` / `examples/` depends only on the public surface and is loaded through the real `require` and run end-to-end ([ADR-31](docs/adr/31-contribution-and-supply-chain-policy.md) WD4).
- **[plugin contract, ci]** The bundled plugin and example `lib` trees are now contract-checked by a new `make check-plugins` target, chained into `make verify` and gated in CI, so a plugin that misuses the `Plugin::Base` contract surface fails the build with `call.undefined-method` ([ADR-43](docs/adr/43-rbs-complete-ancestor-resolution.md)).
- **[engine]** RBS-complete ancestor resolution: `rigor check` now resolves a Ruby subclass's inherited method calls against an allow-listed RBS-only ancestor (seeded with `Rigor::Plugin::Base`), so a typo'd or renamed contract call such as `manifest.bogus` on a plugin fires `call.undefined-method` ([ADR-43](docs/adr/43-rbs-complete-ancestor-resolution.md)).
  - The allow-list is the false-positive boundary: a non-allow-listed ancestor (e.g. `ActionController::Base`) keeps the existing `Dynamic[Top]` fallback, so a controller calling a method an incomplete gem RBS omits is never flagged. Measured zero net new diagnostics on the bundled plugin tree.
- **[engine]** New plugin manifest field `additional_initializers:` lets a plugin tell the engine that named `def`-form lifecycle methods (e.g. `setup`) also establish instance-variable state, removing false nil-receiver diagnostics where an ivar is assigned in a framework callback and read in a sibling method ([ADR-38](docs/adr/38-additional-initializers.md)).
  - `rigor-minitest` ships the first declarations (`Minitest::Test` / `ActiveSupport::TestCase` / `Test::Unit::TestCase` → `setup`). The contribution can only suppress a nil widening, so it is false-positive-safe.
- **[engine]** New macro-substrate nested-class emission tier: a plugin declares an enum-shaped block DSL (`nested_class_templates:`) whose `variant <Const>, <Type>` rows mint nested subclasses with a typed payload reader ([ADR-36](docs/adr/36-mangrove-enum-nested-class-emission.md)).
- **[engine]** Project `signature_paths:` RBS that declares a qualified name (`class Foo::Bar`) without declaring its enclosing namespace (`module Foo`) is now usable instead of silently inert — Rigor synthesizes the missing namespace so the signatures resolve.
  - Previously such a sig set made every method on every affected class degrade to `Dynamic[Top]`. A new `:info` diagnostic `rbs.coverage.synthesized-namespace` names each synthesized namespace so the user can declare it at the source. (On shugo/textbringer, `rigor coverage` rose 40.3% → 49.9% precise.)
- **[engine]** Project RBS that references a type no loaded signature declares (e.g. `-> DRb::DRbServer` when the `drb` RBS is absent) no longer takes down every method on the class — Rigor synthesizes an empty stub for the undeclared type so the rest of the class builds, and a call on the stub stays an open surface rather than a false positive.
- **[rigor check]** New `RIGOR_BUDGET_TRACE` env var prints, at end of run, how often each of the engine's three previously-silent inference guards fired (recursion re-entry, the ancestor-walk cap, the HKT reducer fuel budget), for diagnosing where inference actually stopped.
- **[plugins/rigor-mangrove]** New precision plugin for the [Mangrove](https://github.com/kazzix14/mangrove) functional toolkit (Result / Option carriers): unwrap call sites (`result.unwrap!`, `option.unwrap_or(x)`, …) now resolve to the carried value type instead of `untyped`, so a typo'd chained call against the unwrapped value is caught.
  - It also synthesises `Enum` variant subclasses from the `variants do variant <Const>, <Type> end` DSL, typing each variant's payload reader ([ADR-36](docs/adr/36-mangrove-enum-nested-class-emission.md)). It is a precision layer, not a type source — Mangrove's signatures flow in through `rigor-sorbet` — and emits no diagnostics of its own.
- **[docs]** New handbook appendix [Protocols, interfaces, and structural typing](docs/handbook/appendix-protocols-and-structural-typing.md) answers the question Python and Swift readers ask — "where is Rigor's `Protocol`?" — by separating the **interface** (RBS structural type) from the **protocol contract** ([ADR-28](docs/adr/28-path-scoped-protocol-contracts.md)'s path-scoped behavioural contract). It also establishes a wording convention (handbook README): qualify "interface" on first use as "structural interface" / "RBS interface," since Ruby has no `interface` keyword and the bare word otherwise reads as the Java / PHP *nominal* sense rather than the structural one RBS implements.
- **[docs]** New handbook appendix [Coming from TypeProf](docs/handbook/appendix-typeprof.md) maps Rigor's vocabulary onto Ruby's official type-inference tool, joining the Steep / TypeScript / PHPStan / mypy appendices.

### Fixed

- **[plugins/rigor-activerecord]** A project whose configured `schema_file` (e.g. `db/schema.rb`) is missing no longer balloons memory and runtime on a large app; the failed schema read is now memoized instead of retried once per ActiveRecord call site.
  - On Redmine (migrations-only, no `schema.rb`) `rigor check app lib` drops from 1.5 GB / 173 s to 217 MB / 84 s, with no change to diagnostics. Apps that ship `schema.rb` were never affected.
- **[rigor check]** A method introduced by `attr_reader` / `attr_writer` / `attr_accessor` no longer fires a false `call.undefined-method` when the class also ships RBS that omits the accessor; the in-source scanner now records `attr_*` accessors and propagates them project-wide. (On shugo/textbringer this removed ~137 false errors.)
- **[engine]** A call that passes a block to a method whose RBS declares no block-bearing overload no longer degrades to `Dynamic[Top]`; the selector falls back to the block-less overloads, while still preferring a block overload when one exists.
- **[plugins/rigor-sorbet]** A `sig { returns(T) }` placed above an `attr_reader` / `attr_writer` / `attr_accessor` no longer raises a spurious `parse-error`, and the accessor's type is contributed at call sites (`attr_accessor` types both the reader and the writer). (On dependabot-core this cleared 85 false warnings across 10 files.)
- **[plugins/rigor-sorbet]** A `sig { … }` above a visibility-wrapped def — `private def foo`, `private_class_method def self.bar`, `module_function def baz`, and the `public` / `protected` / `public_class_method` variants — likewise no longer raises a spurious dangling-sig `parse-error`.
- **[plugins/rigor-factorybot, rigor-pundit, rigor-sidekiq]** A `rigor check` run reusing a warm persistent cache across processes no longer serves a stale discovery index after a factory / policy / worker file changes; each index producer now keys its cache on a glob digest of the discovered files.
- **[plugins/rigor-shoulda-matchers]** Diagnostics no longer render with a doubled `plugin.shoulda-matchers.shoulda-matchers.…` provenance prefix; the rule strings are now bare like every other plugin's.
- **[plugins/rigor-dry-validation]** The bundled RBS overlay (typing `Contract#call → Result` and the `Result` API) now loads automatically via the manifest's `signature_paths: ["sig"]` ([ADR-25](docs/adr/25-plugin-contributed-rbs.md)); previously the docs pointed at a `vendor/bundle/…` path that does not exist under the single-bundled-gem model.
- **[docs]** A user-friendliness overhaul of the handbook, manual, and `docs/types.md` corrected several documented forms that did not match shipped behaviour, so an example copied from the docs now does what it claims (the engine is unchanged).
  - `assert_type` requires the type string first (`assert_type("Integer", expr)`); all 41 handbook examples were written value-first and silently no-op'd, and are now flipped.
  - The suppression config key is `disable`, not the `disabled_rules:` shown in the docs; corrected across the handbook and manual.
  - `--workers=N` / `parallel.workers` are fork-based ([ADR-15](docs/adr/15-ractor-concurrency.md)), not "Ractor workers"; corrected in the caching / troubleshooting / configuration / CLI pages. The diagnostics catalogue gained four shipped rules it was missing (`call.unresolved-toplevel`, the `def.override-*` family).

### Changed

- **[rigor coverage]** The precision metric now measures value-producing expressions only; non-expression syntax (argument / parameter lists, `key => value` pairs, clause headers) is excluded from both numerator and denominator instead of counting as opaque.
  - This is a measurement correction, not an inference change: shugo/textbringer now reports 62.9% precise (≈ +13 points). A `rigor coverage --threshold` tuned to the old number may need raising.
- **[plugins/rigor-sorbet]** The Sorbet type-vocabulary translator now maps a user-defined generic application in `sig` position (`Mangrove::Result::Ok[String, StandardError]`, `Box[Integer]`, any non-`T::` `Const[A, B]`) to `Nominal[name, type_args]` instead of degrading it to `untyped`, so a generic receiver keeps its instantiation — what lets `rigor-mangrove` read `type_args`.
- **[engine]** Union display now adopts two concise RBS spellings so diagnostics read like the RBS the user wrote: a `true | false` pair renders as `bool`, and a single member with `nil` renders with the optional sugar `T?` (`String | nil` → `String?`), the two composing so `false | true | nil` reads as `bool?`.
  - Both collapses are display-only per [normalization.md](docs/type-specification/normalization.md) § "Interaction with display" and [rbs-compatible-types.md](docs/type-specification/rbs-compatible-types.md) § "Optionals"; `Union#members`, type identity, and RBS erasure are unchanged.

## [0.1.15] - 2026-05-29

Adds the Liskov override-compatibility diagnostic family (ADR-35) and the `rigor plugin` source-browsing command, and sharpens how Rigor reports — and how `rigor triage` explains and the onboarding skills route — undefined-method diagnostics that are really uninstalled project monkey-patches or project-specific generated DSLs.

### Added

- **[rigor check]** New `def.override-*` diagnostic family enforces the Liskov Substitution Principle on a method override against the signature it inherits from a project-defined ancestor ([ADR-35](docs/adr/35-override-signature-compatibility.md)).
  - `def.override-visibility-reduced` flags an override that reduces inherited visibility (public → protected/private, protected → private), which would break a caller holding the supertype.
  - `def.override-return-widened` flags an override whose declared return widens the inherited return (covariance); fires only on a proven violation when both sides carry an authored RBS signature.
  - `def.override-param-narrowed` flags an override that narrows an inherited parameter type (contravariance), comparing matching positional parameters and requiring an authored single-overload RBS signature on both sides.
  - All three fire only when both signatures are statically observable, resolve the ancestor chain cross-file (superclass + included/prepended modules), map severity through `severity_profile:` (`strict` → error, `balanced` → warning, `lenient` → suppressed), and are suppressible with `# rigor:disable <rule>`.
- **[rigor plugin]** New `rigor plugin` subcommand exposes the on-disk source of the plugins bundled in the toolchain so you can read a real, working plugin as a worked example when authoring your own.
  - `rigor plugin list` / `path <name>` / `print <name>` / `root` cover the production `plugins/` set, the tutorial `examples/`, and the engine source root with the public `Rigor::Plugin::Base` entry point.
  - This is distinct from `rigor plugins` (plural), which reports the activation status of the plugins configured in your `.rigor.yml`.
  - Paths resolve at runtime from the gem location (a documented caveat for container setups), and `print` inlines the source body so it works with no file-reading tool.
- **[rigor check]** A `call.undefined-method` on a method the project itself defines elsewhere on the receiver class (a reopened core/stdlib/gem class Rigor does not apply cross-file) now names that definition site and points at `pre_eval:`, instead of reading like a typo.
  - The definition site is also exposed as a structured `project_definition_site` field on the diagnostic, including in `--format json`.
  - The diagnostic still fires — auto-application of project monkey-patches remains deferred ([ADR-17](docs/adr/17-monkey-patch-pre-evaluation.md)) — but it is now actionable.
- **[rigor triage]** Two new heuristic recognisers route project monkey-patch and toplevel-call diagnostics to their fix.
  - `project-monkey-patch-known` (high confidence) names the proven definition file and recommends copying it straight into `pre_eval:`, instead of the spread-based guess.
  - `unresolved-toplevel` groups `call.unresolved-toplevel` diagnostics ([ADR-34](docs/adr/34-toplevel-unresolved-self-call-default.md)) and routes them to `pre_eval:` or the genuine-typo review path.
- **[docs]** The README and Rails quickstart now provide AI bootstrap prompts in many languages (BCP 47 tagged), so a non-English user can paste a one-line onboarding prompt in their own language.
- **[docs]** New handbook appendix explains the Liskov Substitution Principle and how Rigor's robustness principle reaches its signature rule, as the conceptual companion to the `def.override-*` diagnostics.

### Changed

- **[skill: rigor-project-init]** Onboarding now distinguishes statically-defined project monkey-patches (fixable with `pre_eval:`) from dynamically-generated DSLs that need a project-owned plugin, and routes the latter to the `rigor-plugin-author` handoff instead of leaving it in the baseline.
  - A generated DSL (`define_method` factory, `method_missing` accessor, or `class_eval` heredoc such as Redmine's `Setting.define_setting`) survives `pre_eval:` because the walker finds no literal method to register; that surviving cluster is the signal to author a plugin, which Rigor does not bundle per application ([ADR-16](docs/adr/16-macro-expansion.md) § Audience).
  - Adds a Phase 6a pre-baseline cleanup loop, sig-quality false-positive guidance, and a final step that names every generated file and its commit recommendation to the user.
- **[skill: rigor-plugin-author]** Corrected the guidance a from-scratch external authoring run tripped over: shipping RBS — not `flow_contribution_for` — is what suppresses `call.undefined-method` for a generated method.
  - Method existence and call type are independent checks, so the "Shipping RBS for the DSL" section is now the canonical undefined-method fix and documents both wiring routes (the plugin-manifest `signature_paths:` per [ADR-25](docs/adr/25-plugin-contributed-rbs.md), and a project-side `.rigor.yml signature_paths:`).
  - Project-private activation for a standalone (mise / gem install) `rigor` now leads with an absolute `RUBYLIB`, demotes the `path:`-gem + `bundle install` route to the CI / isolated-bundle case, and documents the `bundle exec` pitfall (it rebuilds `$LOAD_PATH` and drops `RUBYLIB` — use `RUBYOPT="-I…"`).
  - Both skills now point authors at `rigor plugin` to read bundled plugins as worked examples.

## [0.1.14] - 2026-05-29

Improves the AI-assisted onboarding experience and fixes a silent environment failure when `rbs collection install` has been run.

### Added

- **[docs]** New machine-readable install guide at `docs/install.md` lets an AI coding agent detect the available tool manager (mise / asdf / Ruby 4.0 on PATH), install `rigortype`, and hand off to `rigor skill print rigor-project-init` from a single prompt.
  - The Rails quickstart (`docs/manual/14-rails-quickstart.md`) gains a callout at Step 1 pointing AI-assisted setups at the guide URL.
  - `README.md`'s Installation section now opens with the one-line agent prompt so users arrive at a working setup without reading the full manual.

### Fixed

- **[rigor check]** Running `rbs collection install` in a project no longer breaks Rigor's RBS environment with `RBS::DuplicatedDeclarationError`. Gems whose RBS Rigor already loads from another source — stdlib-extracted default gems (`cgi`, `logger`, `base64`, …, shipped by `ruby/gem_rbs_collection` under a `git` source) and the `data/vendored_gem_sigs/` bundle (`redis`, `nokogiri`, `pg`, …) — are now dropped from the discovered `.gem_rbs_collection/` signature paths so the collection copy cannot double-declare against the bundled RBS. Previously the collision left the whole environment unbuilt, silently degrading most type-of queries to `Dynamic[top]` and suppressing rule diagnostics.

## [0.1.13] - 2026-05-29

Targets two user-experience surfaces: AI-assisted onboarding and single-file script analysis. The new `rigor skill` subcommand lets any AI coding agent installed alongside `rigortype` (via `mise use gem:rigortype` and friends) discover and follow Rigor's bundled Agent Skills without a project-side source checkout, and the new `call.unresolved-toplevel` rule means a typo at the top of a standalone script — silent until now — surfaces as a diagnostic the first time `rigor check` runs.

### Added

- **[rigor skill]** New subcommand exposes the bundled Agent Skills so AI coding agents installed via `mise use gem:rigortype` can find and follow them without a project-side source checkout.
  - `rigor skill list` prints name + absolute path for every bundled skill (`rigor-project-init`, `rigor-baseline-reduce`, `rigor-plugin-author`).
  - `rigor skill print <name>` writes the SKILL body to stdout with a header pointing at the `references/` directory, so a single stdout stream is enough for the agent to act on.
  - `rigor skill path <name>` writes the single-line absolute SKILL.md path, suitable as input to a file-reading tool.
  - The `skills/` tree now ships inside the `rigortype` gem itself; the Rails quickstart (Path A) was updated to invoke the project-init skill via `rigor skill print rigor-project-init` instead of pointing at the in-repo `skills/` path.
- **[rigor check]** New diagnostic `call.unresolved-toplevel` warns when an implicit-self call at the top of a file (outside any `def` / `class` / `module`) does not resolve against a same-file `def`, an [ADR-17](docs/adr/17-monkey-patch-pre-evaluation.md) `pre_eval:` monkey-patch, or a `Kernel` / `Object` method — surfacing typos in standalone scripts and the Playground that previously typed silently as `Dynamic[top]`.
  - Severity is mapped through `severity_profile:`: `strict` → `:error`, `balanced` (default) → `:warning`, `lenient` → suppressed.
  - Calls inside class / module / `def` bodies stay lenient per [ADR-24 WD3](docs/adr/24-self-method-call-resolution.md); this rule is intentionally toplevel-only.
  - The escape hatch for projects with toplevel monkey-patches is `.rigor.yml`'s `pre_eval:` array — list the file that declares the method on `Object` / `Kernel` and the diagnostic is suppressed at the same precedence the dispatcher resolves the call.
  - See [ADR-34](docs/adr/34-toplevel-unresolved-self-call-default.md) for the full design.

### Fixed

- **[rigor check]** A method patched in a `pre_eval:` file is no longer reported as `call.undefined-method` when called on a concretely-typed receiver (`s = "hello"; s.patched_method`). The diagnostic check now consults the same project-side monkey-patch registry (`Environment#project_patched_methods`) the dispatcher uses, so `pre_eval:` suppression is consistent across type inference and rule emission.

## [0.1.12] - 2026-05-28

This release is dominated by realism work against three OSS Rails codebases — Mastodon, Redmine, and GitLab FOSS — driving down the analyzer's false-positive rate. Cumulative effect: Mastodon `app + lib` errors **789 → 6 (−99.2%)**, Redmine errors **163 → 79 (−51%)**, GitLab FOSS `app/{controllers,mailers,workers,services}` errors **~670 → ~140**. The flow-folding "G2" follow-ups (retry edge, intervening method call, read-before-write nil) are all closed.

### Added

- **[rigor plugins]** New subcommand reports the activation status of every plugin in `.rigor.yml`, so a misconfigured plugin no longer silently fails to contribute.
  - Surfaces load status (loaded / load-error with reason), manifest id and version, and every manifest-declared extension surface (`signature_paths:`, `open_receivers:`, `owns_receivers:`, `produces:`, `consumes:`, macro substrate counts, `protocol_contracts:`, `source_rbs_synthesizer:`, `type_node_resolvers:`, HKT registrations).
  - `--format json` for tooling; `--strict` exits 1 on any load error (CI gate).

### Changed

- **[rigor init]** After writing the config template, prints a "Next steps" hint pointing at `rigor plugins` for activation verification (with `--strict` as the canonical CI gate).

### Fixed

- **[inference]** A class of `flow.always-truthy-condition` / `always-falsey-condition` false positives no longer fires against idiomatic ivar-state patterns. Specifically, six patterns previously folded predicates to a single Constant where they shouldn't have:
  - **Read-before-write nil** — `Rails.logger.warn '…' unless @warning_issued; @warning_issued = true`. An ivar read before its first write in the same method body now contributes `nil` to the class-wide seed (gated by: `initialize` writes the ivar → exempt; a class-body-level `@x = …` write → exempt; the contribution only widens existing accumulator entries).
  - **Intervening method-call invalidation** — `@performed = false; perform_request; if @performed`. Any implicit-self or `self.foo` call now widens each ivar back to its class-wide seed, so the predicate observes the full type union rather than the pre-call narrowing.
  - **`retry` flow edge** — `tries = 0; begin; ...; rescue; tries += 1; retry; end`. A rescue body containing `retry` now widens rebound locals / ivars to their Nominal envelope (`Constant[v] → Nominal[v.class]`, Tuple → Array, HashShape → Hash) and re-evaluates the begin body under the widened entry.
  - **Defensive falsey-rvalue init** — `@x = nil unless @x` / `@y = false unless @y`. The guarded falsey-Constant write is now skipped during class-ivar seeding, matching the existing `@x ||= v` no-seed behaviour.
  - **Polarity-aware defensive init** — `@x = v unless @x` / `@x = v if @x.nil?` / `unless defined?(@x); @x = v; end`. The class-ivar pre-pass now unions `nil` into the seed when a write sits in the THEN body of an ivar-truthiness / nil-ness / definedness guard.
  - **Mutator widening for ivar literal shapes** — `arr = []; items.each { |x| arr << x }`. A literal Tuple / HashShape bound to a local or ivar now widens to its `Nominal[Array, …]` / `Nominal[Hash, …]` parent when an in-place mutator runs against it (including from a non-escaping block body). Across method boundaries, a Tuple/HashShape seed widens to `Nominal[Array, [untyped]]` / `Nominal[Hash, [untyped, untyped]]` once any sibling method mutates the ivar.
  - All six are always-safe (lose precision, never invent facts). Empirical effect on tdiary-core: `flow.always-truthy-condition` **24 → 0**. `rigor check lib` is fully clean for the first time since the warning class shipped.

- **[inference]** New narrowing primitive: `receiver[key] ||= default` records that subsequent `receiver[key]` reads are non-nil, so `params[:f] ||= []; params[:f] << field` now dispatches the `<<` on the Array carrier instead of mistyping the indexed read as `Constant[nil]`.
  - Receiver must be a local or instance variable read; key must be a literal Symbol / String / Integer. Invalidated by receiver rebind, intervening `[]=` to the same key, or any mutator against the receiver. Worked site: Redmine 6.1.2 `app/models/query.rb#as_params` (6-error cluster).

- **[inference]** New narrowing primitive: `if x.last.is_a?(Array); x.last << y` now narrows the second `x.last` read to the post-`is_a?` type. Single-hop only (Law of Demeter); chains with arguments, a block, or multiple hops are not recorded.
  - Recognised predicates: `is_a?` / `kind_of?` / `instance_of?`. Invalidated by root rebind or any intervening call against the root receiver.

- **[inference]** `Class.new(Parent) { |c| ... }` now properly types the block parameter and the result as `Singleton[Parent]` so `klass.<class-method>` calls (the canonical Rails-migration / Sequel-test / Ractor-fixture idiom) type-check.
  - Falls back to `Nominal[Class]` when the parent argument isn't a static class reference.

- **[inference]** `Hash#each { |k, v| ... }` and any other "yields a single Tuple" iterator now auto-splats the Tuple across multi-positional block parameters, matching Ruby's runtime semantics. Multi-arg yields (e.g. `each_with_index`'s `(element, index)`) are deliberately not splatted.

- **[analyzer]** A `.rb` file containing the ERB closing marker `%>` (typically a Rails generator template) is now silently skipped instead of emitting one parse-error diagnostic per scrap Prism trips on. Redmine 6.1.2's `lib/generators/redmine_plugin_model/templates/migration.rb` alone produced 22 such parse errors.

- **[rigor-rails-routes]** Massive parser expansion driven by the Mastodon / Redmine / GitLab-FOSS surveys. With the full plugin set: Mastodon `unknown-helper` **567 → 0**, `wrong-arity` **30 → 0**; Redmine `unknown-helper` **92 → 11**; GitLab FOSS `app/controllers` `unknown-helper` **486 → 71**.
  - Recognised route shapes added: `devise_for :resource [, skip: [...]]` and the Devise URL-helper family; `use_doorkeeper do … end`; `mount Foo::Engine, at: '/x', as: :name`; `concern :name do … end` bodies captured at definition and replayed under `resources :foo, concerns: :name`; `with_options X do … end` propagating defaults; `resource :foo do … end` singular-block prefix; member/collection action shorthand (`member { post :memorialize }`); `:on => :member` / `:on => :collection` inline form; `match 'path', :via => [...]`; optional path-segment arity (`'settings(/:tab)'`); `*wildcard` segments counted toward arity; `draw :name` / `draw_all :name` (action_dispatch-draw_all); keyword-style `scope(path:, as:)`; hashrocket-key path form (`get 'help/*path' => 'help#show'`); iterative `direct(name.sub(...)) do … end` alias generation; general `direct(:name) do … end` Rails DSL.
  - Helper-name fixes: `only: [:create]` registers the collection helper; `only: [:update]` / `[:destroy]` / `except: [:show]` registers the member helper; singular `resource :foo, as: :bar` uses `bar` as the root; `_index_` suffix on the index helper for same-singular-plural resources (INCLUDING UNCOUNTABLE nouns — `resources :news` → `news_index_path`); irregular singulars (`media → medium`, `data → datum`, `criteria → criterion`, `phenomena → phenomenon`); extended singularize for `*shes`/`*ches`/`*xes`/`*zes`/`(alias|status|bus)es` and `ss`-preserving (`resources :custom_css` → `custom_css_path`).
  - False-positive scrubs: walks the whole `app/` tree by default (`helper_paths: ["app"]`) for project-defined `*_path` / `*_url` methods, visibility-agnostic — so `private def page_url(page)` registers and the paired view's `link_to "Next", page_url(2)` is silent; per-file shadowing-locals detection (`let(:foo_url)` / `subject(:foo_url)` / `def foo_url`); kwargs-only arity tolerance (`short_account_status_url(account_username: u, id: i)` against an arity-2 route); path-based exclusion of `unknown-helper` and `wrong-arity` for `app/models/` / `lib/` / `db/` / `config/` / `app/services/` / `app/workers/` / `app/finders/` / `app/policies/` / `app/validators/` / `app/uploaders/`; underflow arity tolerance inside controllers / mailers / views / helpers / components (Rails fills `:namespace_id` / `:project_id` from `request.params` at runtime); twelve Rails asset helpers (`asset_path`/`url`, `image_*`, `javascript_*`, `stylesheet_*`, `font_*`, `video_*`, `audio_*`) added to `BUILTIN_PASSTHROUGH`; Devise scoped bare helpers + OmniAuth helpers; `vite_asset_path` / `vite_asset_url`.

- **[rigor-actionpack]** Filter and render checks aligned with Rails' constant-resolution semantics; gem-shipped parent silenced; controller discoverer corrected for nested-module declarations. Mastodon: `unknown-filter-method` **42 → 0**; `missing-template` **27 → 0**. GitLab FOSS: `unknown-filter-method` 3 → 0; `missing-template` 5 → 0.
  - Controller discoverer qualifies nested-module class declarations (`module Admin; class AccountsController` registers as `Admin::AccountsController`, not bare `AccountsController`), walks the full ancestor chain, and resolves parent / `include` constants lexically (`< BaseController` inside `module Admin` first tries `Admin::BaseController`).
  - Analyzer side mirrors the qualification so `diagnose_filters` and `diagnose_renders` see the same qualified class name the index carries.
  - Filter checks silence on gem-shipped parents (`< Devise::ConfirmationsController` / `< Doorkeeper::AuthorizedApplicationsController`), and render checks silence on gem-shipped ancestor chains (Doorkeeper's own views). Render checks also tolerate abstract base controllers (`BaseController`-named or view-dir-of-subdirectories-only) and file-path-derived controller paths for nested `module Admin; class Users::RolesController` declarations.

- **[rigor-activerecord]** Schema-aware checks now correctly handle migrations, virtual-table models, Postgres array columns, and scope-body call resolution. Mastodon: `unknown-column` **12 → 0**; AR-shape `undefined-method` from `Array[Integer]` mistyping resolved.
  - `db/migrate/*.rb` and `db/post_migrate/*.rb` are excluded from column validation (they reference the schema as it was at migration time, not the current one).
  - Models whose schema-side column set is empty (DB-view-backed like Mastodon's `Instance`, or external-store models) skip column validation entirely — `Instance.where(domain:)` no longer false-fires.
  - Postgres array columns declared via `t.<type> "name", array: true` propagate as `Array[<inner>]` (was just `<inner>`), so `@report.status_ids + ids).uniq` type-checks.
  - Implicit-self class-side calls (inside class bodies, `def self.<method>`, scope lambdas) resolve through the AR finder / scope / relation contribution path the same way `Model.where(...)` does explicitly. `scope :duplicate_uris, -> { select(:uri).group(:uri) }` no longer mistypes `select(:uri)` against `Kernel#select`.

- **[rigor-actionmailer]** Mailer-action discovery now follows `include` of concern modules; reflection methods are exempted. GitLab FOSS: `unknown-action` **100 → 0**; Mastodon: `unknown-action` 2 → 0.
  - Two-pass discovery: build module-action tables first, then per-mailer-class merge actions from each `include`d module. GitLab's `Notify < ApplicationMailer` deriving 100+ actions from ~20 `Emails::*` concerns now type-checks. Unresolved includes (gem-shipped concerns) silence `unknown-action` the same way the actionpack filter check does.
  - Trailing-keyword-hash arity tolerance: `Notify.foo(uid, gid, success_count: 5)` against `def foo(uid, gid, success_count:)` no longer over-counts the kwargs hash as a positional.
  - `respond_to?`, `public_send`, `send`, `__send__`, `method`, `instance_method`, `methods` are exempt from `unknown-action` (dynamic dispatch on the mailer class isn't an action call).

- **[rigor-rails-i18n]** Rails / `rails-i18n` shipped key prefixes (`date.`, `time.`, `datetime.`, `support.array.`, `errors.format`, `errors.messages.`, `number.`, `helpers.{select,submit,label}.`, `i18n.transliterate.`, `activerecord.errors.{messages,models}.`) are skipped from `unknown-key`. Closes Mastodon's `I18n.t('date.order')` (used by the date-of-birth select).

- **[rigor-activesupport-core-ext]** Added `Date#midnight` / `at_midnight` / `beginning_of_day` / `end_of_day` (and `at_*` aliases) returning `Time`, plus `Hash#without` (aliases `Hash#except`). Closes Mastodon's `Date.current.at_midnight` and `options.without(...).merge(...)` chains.

## [0.1.11] - 2026-05-27

### Added

- **[plugins]** All bundled plugins now ship inside the `rigortype` gem, so `require "rigor-foo"` works without any `RUBYLIB` or `Gemfile` workaround.
  - Activate any plugin with one line in `.rigor.yml`: `plugins: [rigor-foo]`.
- **[playground]** New `rigor playground` command starts the browser playground server ([ADR-29](docs/adr/29-browser-playground.md)).
- **[docs]** A new [Rails quickstart guide](docs/manual/14-rails-quickstart.md) covers end-to-end setup from `mise install` to a first `rigor check` on a Rails project.

### Changed

- **[baseline]** Generated `.rigor-baseline.yml` files now use project-relative paths, making them portable across machines and CI.

### Fixed

- **[rigor-rails-i18n]** Lazy translation keys (`t(".key")`) in controller files no longer produce false-positive `unknown-key` diagnostics. The key is now expanded to `<controller_scope>.<action>.<key>` using the file path and the innermost enclosing `def` — matching Rails' lazy lookup convention. Lazy keys in non-controller `.rb` files (models, helpers, mailers, …) are silently skipped since the controller/action scope cannot be determined statically.
- **[rigor baseline generate]** Fixed a crash when `plugins:` entries in `.rigor.yml` were plain strings (e.g. `plugins: [rigor-rails]`).
- **[rigor-rails-routes]** Fixed incorrect helper names for `new` and `edit` actions inside namespaced resources — `new_admin_widget_path` is now generated correctly instead of the wrong `admin_new_widget_path` form.
- **[rigor-rails-routes]** `get "/login", to: "sessions#new"` (no `as:` key) now registers `login_path`; previously anonymous static routes were silently skipped, producing false-positive `unknown-helper` diagnostics.
- **[rigor-rails-routes]** `scope "/:event_slug", as: "event" do ... end` now generates correctly prefixed helpers (`event_talks_path`, `event_talk_path`, etc.) and counts the scope's dynamic path segments in helper arity. Previously the `as:` prefix was silently ignored, producing false-positive `unknown-helper` diagnostics for every helper defined inside such a block.
- **[rigor-rails-routes]** `draw(:admin)` routing partials (`config/routes/admin.rb`, etc.) are now followed; previously all helpers defined in split routes files were missing, producing false-positive `unknown-helper` diagnostics for every call to those helpers.
- **[rigor-rails-routes]** `concern :name do ... end` blocks are now skipped during parsing; previously the concern body was evaluated at definition time with no parent-resource arity, producing false-positive `wrong-arity` diagnostics for every helper call that included a URL segment argument.
- **[rigor-rails-routes]** Route helpers now accept one trailing options/query-params hash without triggering `wrong-arity`; calls such as `users_path(page: 2)` or `user_path(@user, pagination_params(...))` are no longer flagged.

## [0.1.10] - 2026-05-27

### Added

- **[rigor mcp]** New `rigor mcp --transport stdio` subcommand: a long-running MCP server exposing Rigor's analysis as JSON-RPC 2.0 tool calls over stdio ([ADR-33](docs/adr/33-mcp-server.md)). No new runtime dependency.
  - Seven read-only tools: `rigor_check`, `rigor_type_of`, `rigor_triage`, `rigor_annotate`, `rigor_sig_gen`, `rigor_explain`, `rigor_coverage`. Each returns JSON.
  - Wire any MCP client (Claude Desktop, Cursor, Cline, …): `"command": "rigor", "args": ["mcp"]`. See [manual ch. 10](docs/manual/10-mcp-server.md).

- **[sig-gen]** `rigor sig-gen --params=observed` now infers return types for `attr_reader` / `attr_writer` / `attr_accessor` by propagating argument types observed at `initialize` call sites through ivar assignments ([ADR-14](docs/adr/14-rbs-sig-generation.md)).
  - Previously every ivar-backed accessor fell back to `untyped`; the observation pass fills the gap without requiring annotations.

- **[rigor coverage]** New `rigor coverage PATH...` command reports the fraction of expressions Rigor assigned a useful static type, broken down per file. `--threshold RATIO` exits 1 for CI gates; `--format json` for tooling.

- **[rigor check]** New `--treat-all-as-inline-rbs` flag: treats every analysed file as inline-RBS-annotated without editing `.rigor.yml` ([ADR-32](docs/adr/32-rbs-inline-comment-ingestion.md)).
  - Intended for single-file or ad-hoc runs; projects that always want this configure `require_magic_comment: false` in `.rigor.yml` instead.

- **[rigor-rbs-inline]** Bundled plugin that ingests `# @rbs` inline-RBS comments as type sources, contributing the synthesised RBS to the analysis environment ([ADR-32](docs/adr/32-rbs-inline-comment-ingestion.md)). Activate with `plugins: [rigor-rbs-inline]`.
  - Files must contain `# rbs_inline: enabled` by default. Set `require_magic_comment: false` in the plugin config to disable the gate for contexts that own the whole analysis scope.
  - A failed parse surfaces as a `source-rbs-synthesis-failed` info diagnostic naming the file and the error message.

- **[playground]** Browser playground end-to-end ([ADR-29](docs/adr/29-browser-playground.md) slices 1–4): Rack/Puma backend + CodeMirror 6 frontend in `plugins/rigor-playground/`, deployable to Fly.io + Cloudflare Pages.
  - Inline-RBS annotations (`# @rbs …`) are recognised on load without `# rbs_inline: enabled`. A 300 ms hover tooltip calls `/type-of` and displays the inferred type inline.

- **[rigor annotate]** `rigor annotate` now annotates each `def` header line with the method's inferred return type instead of the parameter list.
  - When no return type can be derived, the annotation is omitted rather than showing a placeholder.

- **[type system]** `non-empty-string` and `non-zero-int` / `positive-int` refinements now propagate through String and Integer operations: concatenation, casing transforms, `abs`, integer multiplication, and more.

### Changed

- **[type system]** `case` / `when` now folds to the matching branch type when the subject is statically known, instead of always unioning every branch. `case 1 when Integer then :int else :other end` → `:int`.

### Fixed

- **[type system]** `return if @ivar.nil?` (and `unless @ivar`) now correctly narrows the ivar to non-`nil` after the guard when the ivar's only visible assignment was `nil`. Previously the guard was a no-op and subsequent calls on the ivar raised `call.undefined-method`.

- **[rigor annotate]** Fixed a crash on files containing non-ASCII characters (em-dash, Japanese comments) in environments where `Encoding.default_external` is `US-ASCII`.

- **[rigor annotate]** Fixed single-line ternary and `if`/`else` expressions annotating to the last branch's type instead of the union of all branches.

## [0.1.9] - 2026-05-23

### Added

- **ADR-25 Slice 1 — plugin-contributed RBS signatures.** A plugin gem can now contribute RBS signature directories to Rigor's analysis environment through its manifest, closing the gap that forced an RBS-only bundle gem to be hand-wired via a non-portable `signature_paths:` path (`.rigor.yml` has no ERB, so a gem reference cannot resolve). `Plugin::Manifest` gains an optional `signature_paths:` field — an array of directory paths relative to the plugin gem root. `Plugin::Base#signature_paths` resolves them to absolute dirs against that root (derived from the plugin class's defining file: the directory above `lib/`, falling back to the file's own directory; `[]` for an anonymous class). `Plugin::Loader` validates each resolved directory exists at load time — a missing one is a `LoadError` that drops the plugin, loud rather than silent because a missing `sig/` means the bundle gem is broken. `Plugin::Registry#signature_paths` aggregates the resolved set across loaded plugins in registration order, and `Environment.for_project` merges it into the signature-path set fed to `RbsLoader` — ranked below the user's explicit `signature_paths:` and above the opportunistic `bundler:` / `rbs_collection:` discovery; duplicate-declaration conflicts degrade through the existing O7 failure-memo. The change is additive to the pre-1.0 plugin contract (a new optional manifest field; no existing plugin breaks). Spec coverage: +11 examples across `manifest_spec` (field accept / default / validation / `to_h` round-trip), `base_spec` (gem-root resolution + anonymous-class / no-declaration `[]`), `loader_spec` (existing-dir load + missing-dir `LoadError` drop), `registry_spec` (aggregation); `public_api_drift_spec` updated for the three new public methods. See [ADR-25](docs/adr/25-plugin-contributed-rbs.md).

- **ADR-25 Slice 2 — `rigor-activesupport-core-ext` becomes a plugin.** The ActiveSupport `core_ext` RBS bundle, previously a gem with no analyzer code that users had to hand-wire through `signature_paths:` (a non-portable absolute path or a vendored copy — `.rigor.yml` has no ERB), is now a **pure RBS-bundle plugin**: a trivial `Rigor::Plugin::ActivesupportCoreExt < Plugin::Base` whose manifest declares `signature_paths: ["sig"]` and which ships no diagnostics hook. Activation collapses to one portable line — `plugins: [rigor-activesupport-core-ext]` — with no path and no vendoring; the plugin loader resolves the bundled `sig/` against the gem root automatically. The gem entry point now requires the plugin class; the demo's `.rigor.yml` switches from `signature_paths:` to `plugins:`; the README's two-option manual-wiring section is replaced by the one-line `plugins:` setup. New integration spec `spec/integration/plugins/activesupport_core_ext_plugin_spec.rb` (2 examples) proves the end-to-end path — with the plugin active, `3.days` / `"x".camelize` / `Time.current` / `.squish` / `Array.wrap` / `.symbolize_keys` / `.blank?` all type-check with zero `call.undefined-method`. See [ADR-25](docs/adr/25-plugin-contributed-rbs.md) slice 2.

- **ADR-25 Slice 3 — `rigor-project-init` SKILL follow-through.** With `rigor-activesupport-core-ext` now a plugin (slice 2), the onboarding SKILL no longer routes it through a `signature_paths:` vendoring step: `references/01-detect.md` describes it as an RBS-bundle plugin listed under `plugins:`, the `references/02-configure.md` `.rigor.dist.yml` template moves it into the `plugins:` list (the `signature_paths:` block — previously the bundle's vendored-copy wiring — drops out, leaving `signature_paths:` for a project's own local `sig/` only), and the `references/03-baseline-and-bugs.md` triage-hint action for `activesupport-core-ext` points at `plugins:` instead of `signature_paths:`. With this the ADR-25 implementation (slices 1+2+3) is complete.

- **ADR-22 Slice 5 — `rigor baseline regenerate` + the `--baseline-strict` CI gate.** Two pieces close the baseline CLI surface. (1) `rigor baseline regenerate` — a fifth `baseline` subcommand that rewrites the baseline file unconditionally from a fresh `rigor check` run; it is `generate --force` without the existence guard or the `--force` flag, so `rigor baseline regenerate` reads cleanly as the end-of-quality-improvement-session refresh after landing baseline-reducing fixes. Shares the new `BaselineCommand#write_baseline` helper with `generate`; honours `--config` / `--output` / `--match-mode`. (2) `rigor check --baseline-strict` — a CI gate that fails the run (`exit 1`) on **any** baseline drift. Excess drift (a bucket over threshold) already fails via the surfaced diagnostics; the gate additionally catches **deficit drift** (`actual < count` — the baseline has grown looser than the code and should be regenerated), which a plain `rigor check` exits 0 on. The gate audits the pre-filter diagnostic stream, lists every drifted bucket with its `count → actual` delta and status on stderr, and points the user at `rigor baseline regenerate`. It is a no-op with an explanatory note when no baseline is active — the flag never implicitly loads a baseline the config did not name (WD2). Spec coverage: +5 examples in `spec/rigor/cli/baseline_command_spec.rb` (`regenerate` overwrite / fresh-write; `--baseline-strict` exact-match pass / deficit-drift fail / no-baseline no-op). With this the ADR-22 `rigor baseline {generate, regenerate, dump, drift, prune}` subcommand family is complete. See [ADR-22](docs/adr/22-baseline-and-project-onboarding.md) § "Implementation slicing" slice 5.

- **ADR-22 Slice 3 — the `rigor-project-init` onboarding SKILL.** First member of the v0.1.9 external-user SKILL trio (ADR-22 WD8), under the top-level `skills/` tree: `skills/rigor-project-init/SKILL.md` (router) + three `references/` modules (`01-detect.md`, `02-configure.md`, `03-baseline-and-bugs.md`). End-to-end onboarding for a project that has never run Rigor — detect the stack from `Gemfile` / `Gemfile.lock`, select a plugin set, write `.rigor.dist.yml`, and either snapshot a baseline or commit to a zero-diagnostic gate. The SKILL frames the onboarding around an explicit **adoption-mode choice** the user makes up front (a realised shape of ADR-22 phase 3 + 6, recorded in ADR-22 § "SKILL: rigor-project-init"): **acknowledge mode** (`severity_profile: lenient`, generate a baseline, parenthesise today's diagnostics and lean on the test / spec suite for runtime correctness of those sites) vs. **strict mode** (`severity_profile: strict`, no baseline, fix or consciously `# rigor:disable` every site). Both modes preserve the regression guarantee — a *new* diagnostic surfaces either way. It also surfaces two escalation paths for clusters that are neither a quick fix nor honest baseline material: application-specific metaprogramming → write a project-private plugin (hand off to `rigor-plugin-author`); an unsupported external gem → `rbs collection install` / `dependencies.source_inference:` / open a Rigor issue. The SKILL is external-user-facing — published `rigor` binary, public CLI / config surface only, agentskills.io-portable conventions (validated `Meets agentskills.io specification` via `waza check`). **ADR-23 Slice 3 (the project-init half)** rides along: the SKILL's Phase 5 consumes `rigor triage --format json` as the deterministic diagnosis layer rather than counting the raw `rigor check` stream. See [ADR-22](docs/adr/22-baseline-and-project-onboarding.md), [ADR-23](docs/adr/23-diagnostic-triage-command.md).

- **ADR-22 Slice 4 — the `rigor-baseline-reduce` SKILL.** Second member of the v0.1.9 external-user SKILL trio (ADR-22 WD8): `skills/rigor-baseline-reduce/SKILL.md` (router) + two `references/` modules (`01-classify.md`, `02-fix-or-suppress.md`). The ongoing-quality companion to `rigor-project-init` acknowledge mode — opportunistic, session-bounded reduction of a project's `.rigor-baseline.yml`. Phase 1 prioritises with the `rigor triage` hint catalogue (`genuine-bugs` first, then ascending bucket count; config-gap hints handed back to the user) plus `rigor baseline dump --format json` for the authoritative bucket list — **ADR-23 Slice 3's `rigor-baseline-reduce` half**, completing slice 3. Phase 2 samples 3–5 sites per rule and classifies each real bug / stylistic-safe / false positive; Phase 3 acts (fix the code / intentful per-line `# rigor:disable` / open a Rigor GitHub issue for FPs); Phase 4 refreshes the baseline with `rigor baseline drift` + `rigor baseline regenerate` (the slice-5 command). Session stop conditions (site budget, wall-time) and systemic-rule escalation are built in. Spec-compliant via `waza check`. See [ADR-22](docs/adr/22-baseline-and-project-onboarding.md) § "SKILL: rigor-baseline-reduce", [ADR-23 WD5](docs/adr/23-diagnostic-triage-command.md).

- **ADR-22 — the external-author `rigor-plugin-author` SKILL.** Third member of the v0.1.9 SKILL trio: `skills/rigor-plugin-author/SKILL.md` (router) + three `references/` modules (`01-plan-and-scaffold.md`, `02-walker-and-types.md`, `03-test-and-ship.md`). The plugin-authoring workflow re-targeted for users writing a plugin in **their own** repository — a standalone `rigor-`-prefixed gem or a project-private plugin — distinct from the contributor `rigor-plugin-author` SKILL bundled under the monorepo's `.claude/skills/` (which assumes the in-repo `plugins/` / `examples/` layout, `plugin_helpers.rb`, and `make verify`). Covers Phase 0 gem-vs-project-private packaging; the `rigor-<id>` gemspec and project-private path-gem / `RUBYLIB` activation; the `Rigor::Plugin::Base` skeleton; the `diagnostics_for_file` AST walker, `Diagnostic` construction, `scope.type_of`, and the optional `flow_contribution_for` return-type hook; shipping `sig/` RBS; and fixture-based testing via `rigor check --format json` under RSpec **or** Minitest (no rigor internals). Threads a prominent caveat throughout: the ADR-2 plugin contract is **pre-1.0** and stabilises at `rigortype` v0.2.0, so plugins pin `rigortype` to `< 0.2.0` and expect to revisit on each minor bump. Spec-compliant via `waza check`. With this the v0.1.9 external-user SKILL trio (onboarding → ongoing-quality → extension) is complete. See [ADR-22](docs/adr/22-baseline-and-project-onboarding.md) WD8, [`docs/ROADMAP.md`](docs/ROADMAP.md) § "Agent workflows / SKILLs".

- **`rigor annotate` — per-line last-expression type listing.** A new CLI subcommand that prints a source file with every line annotated by the type of the expression the line evaluates to: `rigor annotate FILE` appends a `#=> dump_type: <type>` comment to each line. The annotated expression is the last statement that closes on the line (so `1; 2; 3` reports `3`), or — for a line no statement closes, such as an `if` / block header — the widest expression ending there (so `if nil` reports its condition `nil`). The whole-`if` type lands on the `end` line. The output is re-parsed with Prism as a sanity gate and printed with IRB-style syntax highlighting; colouring is done by a self-contained `PrismColorizer` that re-lexes with Prism and maps token categories to ANSI escapes (no `irb` gem dependency, per ADR-0). Colour is on by default for a tty and suppressed when the [`NO_COLOR`](https://no-color.org) environment variable is present and non-empty; `--no-color` / `--color` override both the tty auto-detection and `NO_COLOR`. An existing `#=> dump_type:` suffix is stripped before re-annotating, so the command is idempotent. New `lib/rigor/cli/annotate_command.rb` (+ `LineTypeCollector`) and `lib/rigor/cli/prism_colorizer.rb`. Spec coverage: 7 examples in `cli_spec.rb`, 8 in `spec/rigor/cli/prism_colorizer_spec.rb`.

- **Per-element `select` / `filter` / `reject` fold over a Tuple receiver.** A predicate filter on a statically-known array — `[1, 2, nil].reject(&:nil?)`, `[1, 2, 3, 4].select { |n| n.even? }` — now evaluates the predicate once per Tuple position and assembles the surviving elements into a `Tuple`, strictly tighter than the RBS-projected `Array[Elem]`. Both block shapes are supported: the full `do … end` / `{ … }` block (typed per position with the element bound to the parameter) and the `&:symbol` predicate shorthand (the symbol is dispatched as a zero-arg method on each element type). `select` / `filter` keep the Ruby-truthy positions, `reject` keeps the Ruby-falsey positions; an all-dropped result folds to the empty-tuple carrier. The fold is purely additive — it folds tightly only when *every* position's predicate result is a decisive `Constant`; a single non-`Constant` position declines the whole fold and the RBS tier widens to `Array[Elem]` as before. `select` / `filter` / `reject` join `PER_ELEMENT_TUPLE_METHODS` in `ExpressionTyper`, and the `&:symbol` shorthand is now recognised for every per-element method (so `[1, 2, 3].map(&:to_s)` folds to `["1", "2", "3"]`). Spec coverage: new `spec/integration/fixtures/per_element_filter.rb` fixture (10 `assert_type` self-assertions) wired into `type_construction_spec.rb`, +8 examples in `fold_runtime_parity_spec.rb`.

- **`rigor-hanami` — Hanami::Action protocol plugin.** Production plugin under `plugins/rigor-hanami/` enforcing the Hanami 3.x action contract (`#handle(Hanami::Action::Request, Hanami::Action::Response) → void`) across `app/actions/**/*.rb`. Uses the [ADR-28](docs/adr/28-path-scoped-protocol-contracts.md) provide-and-check mechanism: **provide** — the engine binds `Hanami::Action::Request` / `Hanami::Action::Response` into every matching `#handle` body so misuse (e.g. `request.no_such_method`) surfaces as a core diagnostic; **check** — the plugin confirms `#handle` is defined on each class; no return-type check (void). The plugin ships `Hanami::Action::Request` / `Hanami::Action::Response` / `Hanami::Action::Params` RBS stubs via `signature_paths: ["sig"]`. Config override: `action_path:` (retargets the contract glob for custom slice layouts). `ProtocolContract#return_type_name` is now optional (`nil` = void / skip return-type conformance check); `ProtocolChecker` (rigor-web) guards nil before attempting nominal lookup. **Extended in this cycle:** (1) RBS stubs expanded — `Hanami::Action::Request` gains `cookies`, `host`, `port`, `url`, `base_url`, `query_string`, `multipart?`, `form_data?`; `Hanami::Action::Response` gains `set_cookie`, `delete_cookie`, `content_type`, `content_type=`; `Hanami::Action::Params` gains `fetch`, `slice`. (2) `handle-arity-mismatch` diagnostic (`:error`) — emitted when a class defines `#handle` with a parameter count other than 2; the message names the class, the expected count (2), and the actual count. Spec coverage: 12 integration examples (`spec/integration/plugins/hanami_plugin_spec.rb`).

- **ADR-28 — path-scoped method-protocol contracts.** A new plugin extension point lets a plugin make a *behavioural* protocol — "every class defined under this directory must define a method of this shape" — statically enforceable, with no opt-in on the conforming class. A new `protocol_contracts:` `Manifest` field carries frozen `Rigor::Plugin::ProtocolContract` value objects (`path_glob` + `method_name` + `singleton` + positional `param_types` + `return_type_name` + `severity`). The contract is consumed at two sites — **provide-and-check**: **provide** is engine-side, a new top-precedence tier in `Inference::MethodParameterBinder` (`apply_protocol_contract`) that substitutes the contract's parameter types for the usual `Dynamic[Top]` fallback regardless of RBS presence — so a method body whose parameter has no RBS is analysed as if the parameter carried its protocol type, surfacing misuse as ordinary core diagnostics and making the inferred return type precise; **check** is plugin-side, the contributing plugin's `#diagnostics_for_file` confirms method presence and return-type conformance. New surfaces: `Rigor::Plugin::ProtocolContract` (+ nested `ParamType`); `Plugin::Base#protocol_contracts` (manifest-backed, override-friendly so a plugin can fold per-project config into the contract — the `#signature_paths` indirection pattern); `Plugin::Registry#protocol_contracts` / `#contracts_for_path`. `MethodParameterBinder.new` gains an optional `source_path:` keyword; `StatementEvaluator#build_fresh_body_scope` now carries `source_path` through body-scope derivation (it was previously dropped). See [ADR-28](docs/adr/28-path-scoped-protocol-contracts.md). Spec coverage: `protocol_contract_spec` (17 examples), `+4` in `manifest_spec`, `+2` in `registry_spec`, and the `examples/rigor-web` integration spec (8 examples).

- **`examples/rigor-web/` plugin walkthrough.** The sixth tutorial walkthrough, the reference example for the ADR-28 path-scoped protocol-contract surface. It pairs a deliberately tiny virtual web framework (`RigWeb` — a `[ControllerClass, action]` router over `Rack::Request` / `Rack::Response`) with the `rigor-web` plugin, which enforces — for every class under the project's `lib/controller/` — that `#get` takes a `Rack::Request` and returns a `Rack::Response`, with no base class or `include` on the controller. The plugin ships minimal RBS for `Rack::Request` / `Rack::Response` via `signature_paths:` (ADR-25). The convention path is overridable per project through a `controller_path:` config key.

- **`Date` / `DateTime` / `Time` constant carrier and folding.** `Type::Constant` now carries `Date`, `DateTime`, and `Time` values (added to `SCALAR_CLASSES`; `Date`/`Time` are stored frozen via `freezable_carrier?`, joining `String`/`Set`). The deterministic constructors fold to the new carrier: `Date.new(y, m, d)` / `DateTime.new(...)` → `Constant[Date]` / `Constant[DateTime]` (via `date_new_lift` in `MethodDispatcher#meta_new`), and `Time.utc(...)` / `Time.gm(...)` → `Constant[Time]` (via the new `TimeFolding` Tier D module). Once a date/time value is a `Constant`, its whole catalog-classified reader surface folds precisely — `Date.new(2026, 1, 1).year` → `Constant[2026]`, `.leap?` → `Constant[false]`, `.strftime("%Y/%m")` → `Constant["2026/01"]`, `.next_day` and `>> 1` → fresh `Constant[Date]`, `Time.utc(2026, 1, 1).utc?` → `Constant[true]`, `Time.utc(...) + 3600` → `Constant[Time]`. `Constant[Date]` renders in ISO-8601 in diagnostics (`Date#inspect`'s astronomical-Julian-day form is unreadable); `Constant[Time]` keeps the compact `Time#inspect`. **Machine-independence is enforced**: only the zone-pinned `Time.utc` / `Time.gm` constructors fold — `Time.now` / `Time.at` / `Time.local` / `Time.new` (local-zone or non-deterministic) and `Date.today` / `Date.parse` keep their `Nominal` answer — and `Time#getlocal` is added to the `TIME_CATALOG` blocklist so a UTC `Constant[Time]` can never fold into a host-timezone-dependent value (`getutc` / `getgm` stay foldable). Driven by the audit at [`docs/notes/20260523-date-time-method-coverage.md`](docs/notes/20260523-date-time-method-coverage.md). Spec coverage: 9 unit examples in `time_folding_spec.rb`, integration fixture `date_time_folding/demo.rb` (24 `assert_type` self-assertions).

- **`Kernel#Integer` / `Kernel#Float` constant folding.** The numeric-conversion functions `Integer(...)` and `Float(...)` now fold to a precise `Constant` when called on constant arguments — joining the already-folded `Rational(...)` / `Complex(...)` constructors. `Integer("42")` → `Constant[42]`, `Integer("ff", 16)` → `Constant[255]`, `Integer(3.7)` → `Constant[3]`, `Float("3.14")` → `Constant[3.14]`, `Float(42)` → `Constant[42.0]`.
