The argument must be a `Constant` carrying a `String` or `Numeric` (and, for `Integer`'s optional base, a `Constant[Integer]`); an unparseable string raises `ArgumentError` / `TypeError` at fold time and the handler declines, deferring to the RBS tier. `Integer`'s existing `Refined[String]` → `non-negative-int` path is unchanged and tried first. Implemented via `try_integer` / `try_integer_constant` / `try_float` in `KernelDispatch`.

- **HashShape mid/low-priority dispatch handlers.** Eleven more `Hash` methods fold to a precise type on a closed `HashShape` receiver, closing the mid/low-priority checklist in the Hash coverage audit: `one?` → `Constant[size == 1]`; `fetch_values` → `Tuple[V…]` (declines on a missing key, where Ruby raises `KeyError`); `assoc` → `Tuple[Constant[k], V]` or `Constant[nil]`; `key` (reverse lookup) → the first matching `Constant[k]` or `Constant[nil]`; `has_value?` / `value?` → `Constant[bool]`; `default` / `default_proc` → `Constant[nil]` (a literal hash carries no default); `deconstruct_keys` → the receiver shape unchanged (Ruby's `Hash#deconstruct_keys` returns `self`); `entries` / `to_hash` registered as aliases of `to_a` / `to_h`; and `<` / `<=` / `>` / `>=` → `Constant[bool]` Hash containment comparison between two closed shapes. Spec coverage: +28 unit examples in `shape_dispatch_spec.rb`, integration fixture `hash_shape_handlers.rb`.

- **`MathFolding` — Tier D singleton dispatch for `Math` module functions.** All 28 `Math` module functions now fold to a precise type when called on numeric `Constant` arguments. The single-argument transcendental functions (`sqrt`, `cbrt`, `exp`, `log2`, `log10`, `log1p`, `expm1`, `sin`, `cos`, `tan`, `asin`, `acos`, `atan`, `sinh`, `cosh`, `tanh`, `asinh`, `acosh`, `atanh`, `erf`, `erfc`, `gamma`) and the two-argument functions (`atan2`, `hypot`, `ldexp`) fold to `Constant[Float]`; `Math.log` folds in both its one- and two-argument forms; `frexp` / `lgamma` lift their two-element-array result to `Tuple[Constant[Float], Constant[Integer]]`. A domain-error input (`Math.sqrt(-1)`, `Math.log(-1)`) declines the fold and defers to the RBS tier. The module follows the `ShellwordsFolding` pattern and is wired into `dispatch_stdlib_module_tiers`. Spec coverage: 21 unit examples in `math_folding_spec.rb`, integration fixture `math_folding.rb`.

- **Integer / Float mid-priority constant folds.** `Integer#floor` / `#ceil` / `#round` / `#truncate` (the no-arg forms) and `Integer#chr` join `INTEGER_UNARY`; `Float#next_float` / `#prev_float` join `FLOAT_UNARY`; `Integer#gcd` / `#lcm` / `#fdiv` join `NUMERIC_BINARY` (Integer-only `gcd` / `lcm` fall through harmlessly for a Float receiver via the existing `NoMethodError` rescue). `Integer#digits` lifts to a per-position `Tuple` of base-10 place values (`123.digits` → `Tuple[Constant[3], Constant[2], Constant[1]]`) through a new `try_fold_integer_array_unary` handler — it declines on a negative receiver, where `digits` raises `Math::DomainError`. Completes the Integer / Float mid-priority checklist in the type-method-coverage audit.

- **String mid-priority constant folds.** Six unary methods (`chr`, `hex`, `oct`, `succ` / `next`, `chop`) and six binary methods (`match?`, `index`, `rindex`, `center`, `ljust`, `rjust`) now fold to a precise `Constant` type when the receiver — and, for the binary forms, the argument — is a literal: `"ff".hex` → `Constant[255]`, `"hello".match?(/l+o/)` → `Constant[true]`, `"hello".index("z")` → `Constant[nil]`, `"hi".center(6)` → `Constant["  hi  "]`. Added to `STRING_UNARY` / `STRING_BINARY` in `ConstantFolding`. `center` / `ljust` / `rjust` carry a width blow-up guard (`string_pad_blow_up?`) that declines the fold when the requested width exceeds `STRING_FOLD_BYTE_LIMIT`, mirroring the existing `+` / `*` guards.

- **`Regexp.new` constructor folding, `Complex#rect/rectangular/polar` Tuple lifting, `Range.new` constructor folding, and `Set` constant carrier.** Four precision additions to the constant-folding pipeline:
  - `Regexp.new(pattern)` / `Regexp.new(pattern, opts)` — folds to `Constant[Regexp]` when the pattern is a `Constant[String]` and the optional second argument is a `Constant[Integer]` (flag bits) or `Constant[true/false]` (IGNORECASE shorthand). Wired in `RegexpFolding.try_dispatch`.
  - `Complex#rect` / `#rectangular` / `#polar` — lifts the two-element Array result to `Tuple[Constant[re], Constant[im]]` (for `rect`) or `Tuple[Constant[abs], Constant[arg]]` (for `polar`) when the receiver is a `Constant[Complex]`. Follows the same Tuple-lift pattern as `Range#to_a` and `String#chars`. Implemented via `COMPLEX_ARRAY_UNARY_METHODS` + `try_fold_complex_array_unary` in `ConstantFolding`.
  - `Range.new(b, e)` / `Range.new(b, e, excl)` — folds to `Constant[Range]` when both endpoints are `Constant[Integer]` or both are `Constant[String]` (homogeneous pair), and the optional `excl` argument is `Constant[true/false]`. Implemented via `range_new_lift` in `MethodDispatcher#meta_new`, alongside the existing `array_new_lift`.
  - **`Set` constant carrier** — `Type::Constant` now carries `::Set` values (added to `SCALAR_CLASSES`, `dup.freeze` init, `foldable_constant_value?`). The `SetFolding` Tier D module folds `Set[]` (variadic bracket constructor) and `Set.new` (zero-arg or `Tuple`-arg form) to `Constant[Set]`. Instance methods on a `Constant[Set]` receiver — `size`, `empty?`, `include?`, `member?`, `subset?`, `superset?` — fold through the existing catalog path. `to_a` / `entries` lift to `Tuple[Constant[…]…]` via `SET_ARRAY_UNARY_METHODS` + `try_fold_set_array_unary`. Ruby 4.0 `Set#inspect` format (`Set[:a, :b]`) is used by `describe(:short)`.

- **`RegexpFolding`, `CGIFolding`, `URIFolding` — Tier D singleton dispatch for stdlib module functions.** `Regexp.escape` / `.quote`, the `CGI` escape/unescape family (`escapeHTML` / `h` / `unescape_html` / `escape` / `unescape` / `escapeURIComponent` / `escape_uri_component` / `unescapeURIComponent` / `unescape_uri_component` / `escapeElement` / `escape_element` / `unescapeElement` / `unescape_element`), and `URI.encode_www_form_component` / `.decode_www_form_component` / `.encode_uri_component` / `.decode_uri_component` now fold to precise `Constant[String]` types when called on a literal string argument. Each module gets a dedicated `*_folding.rb` following the `ShellwordsFolding` pattern and is wired into `dispatch_precise_tiers` after `ShellwordsFolding`. Non-constant arguments defer to the RBS tier. Spec coverage: 44 unit examples across three new spec files; integration fixture `spec/integration/fixtures/module_function_folding/demo.rb` covers all three modules with `assert_type` self-assertions and non-constant fallback checks.

- **ADR-26 — ActiveRecord relation typing.** Relation-returning call sites in `rigor-activerecord` (`has_many` / `has_and_belongs_to_many` accessors, `Model.where` / `all` / `order` / `limit` / `none`, user-declared `scope`s) now carry a precise element type — `ActiveRecord::Relation[Model]` — instead of degrading to the RBS-erased envelope. Chained query methods keep the element type (`User.where(x).order(y).limit(n).find(id)` resolves end to end) and iteration composes with the column-accessor typing (`user.posts.each { |p| p.title }`). A first attempt typed the relation as a closed `Nominal` and regressed false-positive discipline — verified against Mastodon's `app/models` it produced 17 false `call.undefined-method`, all user-defined scopes called on the typed relation, because a relation's true method surface (model `scope` DSL, concern scopes, `def self.…`) is unbounded and no static RBS enumerates it. The shipped design (ADR-26) resolves the precision-vs-false-positive conflict at the **diagnostic layer**: the relation stays a plain `Nominal` (dispatch and block-fold untouched, fully precise) and `call.undefined-method` is taught to exempt the class. Three parts: **(1) engine** — a new optional `open_receivers: [String]` plugin-manifest field; `Plugin::Registry#open_receiver?` aggregates it; `Analysis::CheckRules#undefined_method_diagnostic` consults the plugin registry and skips an open receiver. **(2) plugin** — `rigor-activerecord` ships `sig/active_record/relation.rbs` (a generic `ActiveRecord::Relation[Elem]`, `include Enumerable[Elem]` plus the query / finder / aggregate surface) via `signature_paths:` (ADR-25) and declares `open_receivers: ["ActiveRecord::Relation"]`. **(3) plugin** — `flow_contribution_for` contributes the relation type for the call sites above, and re-contributes it for a discovered `scope` invoked on an already-typed relation so chain precision survives through scopes (gated by a run-lifetime scope-name set so the receiver-type lookup stays off the hot path). Re-verified against Mastodon's `app/models` (237 models): zero scope-on-relation false positives. Spec coverage: +5 examples in `manifest_spec` / `registry_spec` / `public_api_drift_spec` for `open_receivers`; +7 in `activerecord_plugin_spec` for the relation contributions, the open-receiver exemption, and end-to-end block-element typing; two earlier "relations deferred" examples updated. See [ADR-26](docs/adr/26-activerecord-relation-typing.md).

- **`rigor-activerecord` — instance-side column accessor typing.** `flow_contribution_for` now types instance-side column reads, not just class-side finders and associations: on a `Nominal[Model]` receiver, `user.name` narrows to the column's value type (`Nominal[String]` / `Nominal[Integer]` / `Nominal[Time]` / …) and `user.admin?` (the ActiveRecord-generated `<column>?` predicate) narrows to `bool`. Chained calls now resolve through Rigor's normal dispatch — `User.find(1).name.upcase` type-checks, a typo'd `User.find(1).name.bit_lenght` surfaces as `call.undefined-method` on `String`. The contributed type is deliberately **non-nullable** even when the DB column permits `NULL`: Rails code calls column accessors directly (`user.email.downcase`) as a matter of course, so contributing `T | nil` would flood an entire codebase with `possible-nil-receiver` — under-reporting a nil column is a false negative, over-reporting it a false positive, and the project ranks the latter as the worse failure. `json` / `jsonb` / unrecognised (`Object`-typed) columns decline rather than contribute, since `Nominal[Object]` would be narrower than the honest RBS-erased envelope. The contribution is read-only — the `name=` setter and the dirty-tracking method family (`name_changed?`, `name_was`, …) are out of scope (Rigor types only the accessor return the call-site analysis consumes, not `rbs_rails`'s ~20-methods-per-column generation). Spec coverage: +8 examples in `spec/integration/plugins/activerecord_plugin_spec.rb` (string / integer / boolean accessor, `<column>?` predicate, `Object`-typed decline, non-column decline, end-to-end chained typo, end-to-end valid chained call).

- **`rigor-activerecord` — bang and create-or-find finder variants.** `Analyzer::COLUMN_HASH_METHODS` previously validated query keys for `where` / `find_by` / `find_or_create_by` / `find_or_initialize_by` only. The bang variants `find_by!` / `find_or_create_by!` and the `create_or_find_by` / `create_or_find_by!` pair take the identical column-hash argument shape and are now validated too — a typo'd column key in `User.find_by!(emial: ...)` surfaces as `unknown-column` just as it does for `find_by`. `flow_contribution_for` additionally narrows `Model.find_by!(...)` to a non-nullable `Nominal[Model]` (the bang variant raises `RecordNotFound` rather than returning `nil`, unlike `find_by` which keeps the `| nil` arm). Spec coverage: +4 examples in `spec/integration/plugins/activerecord_plugin_spec.rb`.

- **`rigor-activerecord` — single-table-inheritance subclass discovery.** STI subclasses (`class Admin < User` where `User < ApplicationRecord`) were previously invisible to the model index — `Admin.where(...)` calls went unchecked because only direct base-class children were collected. The model discoverer is now a two-step pass: it captures *every* class declaration as a candidate (name, superclass name, DSL metadata), then runs a fixpoint that marks a candidate as a model when its superclass is a configured base class **or**, transitively, the class name of another model. Each STI child is stamped with an `sti_parent:` pointer. `ModelIndex.build` walks the STI ancestry chain (root → leaf) to (a) resolve the child's table from the root model — the nearest `self.table_name =` override, else the root class inflected — and (b) **inherit the chain's declared associations / enums / aliases / scopes / validations / callbacks**. The inheritance merge is what keeps the feature false-positive-safe: a half-done STI (table resolved but associations not inherited) would turn every `where(<parent-association>: ...)` on the child into a fresh false `unknown-column`; the full merge means an STI child validates exactly as its parent does. Multi-level STI (`SuperAdmin < Admin < User`) and a child redeclaration overriding an inherited row are both handled; a cycle guard protects the chain walk. Non-model classes that happen to live under `app/models/` (POROs, service objects) never enter the fixpoint and are dropped. Spec coverage: +8 examples in `spec/integration/plugins/activerecord_plugin_spec.rb` (subclass discovered + table resolved, multi-level STI, PORO not a model, inherited-column query, genuine typo still flagged, inherited association as query key, inherited `alias_attribute`, `Admin.find` flow narrows to `Nominal[Admin]`).

- **`rigor-activerecord` — `has_and_belongs_to_many` / polymorphic / `composed_of` / `delegated_type` association coverage.** Following the `rbs_rails` coverage comparison, four more association DSL forms the model discoverer ignored are now recognised. (1) **`has_and_belongs_to_many`** joins `has_many` as a `:collection` association — previously only `belongs_to` / `has_one` / `has_many` were collected. (2) **Polymorphic `belongs_to` / `has_one`** (`belongs_to :commentable, polymorphic: true`) are recorded with `target: nil` and `polymorphic: true` instead of an invented `Nominal[Commentable]` for a class that does not exist; `flow_contribution_for` declines to narrow a polymorphic accessor rather than contributing a wrong type. (3) **`composed_of`** value-object aggregations are recorded as `:singular` associations targeting the value class (`composed_of :balance, class_name: "Money"` → `Nominal[Money]`), nullable only under `allow_nil: true` — `where(balance: money)` is a valid Rails query and previously surfaced as a false `unknown-column`. (4) **`delegated_type`** roles (`delegated_type :entryable, types: %w[Message Comment]`) are recorded as polymorphic `:singular` associations so `where(entryable: record)` is accepted. All four also publish on the ADR-9 `:model_index` fact via the existing `associations` array (now carrying a `polymorphic:` key). `flow_contribution_for` gained a `target.nil?` guard so a polymorphic association never produces a `Nominal[nil]`. Spec coverage: +8 examples in `spec/integration/plugins/activerecord_plugin_spec.rb` (HABTM as collection; polymorphic `belongs_to` recorded / accepted as query key / flow declined; `composed_of` recorded / accepted as query key / `Nominal[Money]` contributed; `delegated_type` recorded).

### Fixed

- **Block-less iteration calls now infer `Enumerator`, not the block-overload return type.** A call to a block-taking Enumerable method *without* a block — `[1, 2, nil].filter`, `array.map`, `range.reverse_each` — resolved through the first arity-compatible RBS overload, which is the block-bearing one (`() { (Elem) -> boolish } -> Array[Elem]`). The selector therefore returned `Array[Elem]` for `[1, 2, nil].filter` when the call actually yields an `Enumerator`. `OverloadSelector` now treats the call site's block presence symmetrically: when no block is supplied it skips any overload whose block is *required* (the RBS `{ ... }` form, as opposed to the optional `?{ ... }`), so the block-less `() -> Enumerator[Elem, Array[Elem]]` overload wins. A method whose only overloads require a block still resolves via the `overloads.first` fall-back. New `OverloadSelector.overload_requires_block?` predicate; `spec/integration/fixtures/range_catalog.rb` updated (`(1..3).reverse_each` is `Enumerator`, not `Range`). Spec coverage: +3 examples in `overload_selector_spec.rb`, and a new `spec/integration/fold_runtime_parity_spec.rb` (133 examples) that cross-checks every catalogued fold's inferred type against the value MRI actually produces.

- **`rigor-activerecord` — associations declared inside a `with_options` block.** A model that groups its `belongs_to` / `has_many` / `validates` / `enum` / `scope` / `alias_attribute` declarations under Rails' `with_options(...) do … end` idiom had every nested declaration ignored by the model discoverer — `ModelDiscoverer`'s `lookup_*` passes walked only the top-level `compact_child_nodes` of the class body. The consequence was a false `unknown-column`: Mastodon's `Report` declares `belongs_to :target_account` (and two siblings) inside `with_options class_name: 'Account' do … end`, so `Report.where(target_account: account)` — a valid association-name query key — surfaced as `unknown column \`target_account\``. A new `ModelDiscoverer#declaration_calls` helper returns the top-level declaration calls plus those nested inside `with_options` blocks (recursively), and all six `lookup_*` passes consult it. The options the `with_options` call itself carries are not yet merged into the nested calls, so a nested `belongs_to`'s target class still resolves by inflection rather than the shared `class_name:` — that costs only flow-contribution precision, never a false positive. Found by running `rigor check` with the plugin active against Mastodon's `app/models`. Spec coverage: +2 examples in `spec/integration/plugins/activerecord_plugin_spec.rb`.

- **`rigor-activerecord` — three schema / model false-positive `unknown-column` sources.** A coverage comparison against `rbs_rails` (`pocke/rbs_rails`) surfaced three Rails idioms the static analyzer mis-handled, each turning a valid query key into a false `unknown-column` error. (1) **Polymorphic `t.references`.** The schema parser emitted only the `<name>_id` integer column for `t.references "x"`; `t.references "x", polymorphic: true` also creates an `<name>_type` string column, so `where(commentable_type: ...)` errored. `SchemaParser#parse_references_column` now detects the `polymorphic: true` option and emits both columns. (2) **Exotic / generic column types dropped.** `parse_column` matched a fixed type allowlist (`string`, `integer`, …) and silently dropped any other `t.<method>` — so `t.citext`, `t.uuid`, `t.inet`, the generic `t.column "x", :type` form, and any newer column type produced no column at all, and every query against it errored. The parser now treats *every* `t.<method> "name"` call as a column (its type symbol degrading to `Object` when unrecognised) and skips only the structural calls in the new `NON_COLUMN_METHODS` denylist (`index`, `check_constraint`, `exclusion_constraint`, `unique_constraint`, `foreign_key`, `primary_keys`); `uuid` / `citext` / `inet` join the `String` type mapping. A dropped column is never silently swallowed — losing one is strictly worse (a false positive) than keeping an unknown-typed one. (3) **`alias_attribute` query keys.** `where` / `find_by` accept an `alias_attribute`-declared name, but the model discoverer never collected aliases, so every `where(<alias>: ...)` errored. `ModelDiscoverer#lookup_aliases` now records `alias_attribute :new, :old` mappings, `ModelIndex::Entry` carries an `aliases` hash with `alias?` / `resolve_alias`, and `Analyzer#valid_query_key?` resolves an alias to its target before the column / association check. The aliases are also published on the ADR-9 `:model_index` fact. Spec coverage: +7 examples in `spec/integration/plugins/activerecord_plugin_spec.rb` (polymorphic `_type`, non-polymorphic single-column, citext/uuid/inet/generic-column recognition, structural `t.index` not-a-column, alias as query key, alias on the entry, genuine typo still flagged).

- **Instance-variable narrowing through a truthy guard.** Rigor narrowed a local variable to non-`nil` inside `if x` / `x && x.foo` / `unless x.nil?`, but an *instance* variable kept its declared / inferred `T | nil` type inside the guarded branch — every method call on a guarded ivar (`@journal.user` inside `if @journal`) reported a false-positive `... for nil`. `Inference::Narrowing#analyse` did not dispatch `Prism::InstanceVariableReadNode` at all, and `analyse_nil_predicate` recognised only a local-variable receiver. The dispatcher now routes both the bare read and the assignment-in-condition write (`if @ivar = expr`) through a shared `analyse_ivar`, and `analyse_nil_predicate` narrows an ivar receiver — mirroring the existing local-variable paths. The narrowing is scoped to the guarded branch and is purely additive (the truthy edge subtracts `nil` / `false`, the falsey edge the complement); it never widens or re-narrows the ivar binding seen elsewhere. Surfaced by the Redmine 6.x regression sweep ([`docs/notes/20260521-redmine-6.x-regression-sweep.md`](docs/notes/20260521-redmine-6.x-regression-sweep.md), the "+6 at 6.1.2" breach — 6 false positives in `app/models/issue.rb`, all `@current_journal.METHOD` inside `if @current_journal` guards) and the Mastodon cluster-4 G2 note. New `spec/integration/fixtures/ivar_guard_narrowing.rb` fixture covering all three guard shapes.

## [0.1.8] - 2026-05-21

### Added

- **ADR-15 Amendment — fork-based worker pool, the active backend for `workers > 0`.** The Ractor worker pool (ADR-15 Phase 4) was found unusable on Ruby 4.0.x by two independent defects, both confirmed against 4.0.4 and 4.0.5: (1) a CRuby concurrent-Ractor heap-use-after-free in `rb_vm_ci_lookup` — a GC sweep on one Ractor frees a `T_DATA` object that another Ractor's runtime call-info lookup concurrently reads — filed upstream as **Ruby Bug #22075**, ~70 % crash rate; (2) a deterministic `Ractor::IsolationError` on non-shareable process-global constants (`RBS::EnvironmentLoader::DEFAULT_CORE_ROOT`, rigor's `StaticReturnRefinements::OWNERS_BY_METHOD` / `HktBuiltins::METHOD_RETURN_OVERRIDES`) that makes every worker emit `internal analyzer error` for 100 % of files. Full investigation: [`docs/notes/20260520-ractor-pool-cruby-uaf.md`](docs/notes/20260520-ractor-pool-cruby-uaf.md). New `Runner#analyze_files_in_fork_pool` is a fork-based backend — it builds one `WorkerSession` on the parent, `fork`s N children that copy-on-write inherit it, each child analyses a contiguous file slice and writes a Marshal'd diagnostics + reporter payload to a temp file, and the parent merges and re-orders by original path order. Separate processes mean separate GC heaps / `vm->ci_table` (immune to defect 1) and COW-inherited constants (no `Ractor.shareable?` constraint — immune to defect 2). A child that exits abnormally is degraded — its slice is re-analysed in-process under a `pool-degraded` warning. `Runner#pool_backend` selects `:fork` by default on POSIX and `:sequential` where `fork` is unavailable (Windows); the Ractor pool implementation is preserved but reachable only via `RIGOR_POOL_BACKEND=ractor`. `WorkerSession` gains optional `synthetic_method_index:` / `project_patched_methods:` keywords so the fork worker's `Environment` is constructed identically to the sequential path's. New `spec/rigor/analysis/runner_fork_pool_spec.rb` (6 examples) runs in the **default** suite — the fork pool is memory-safe, unlike the Ractor pool spec which stays gated behind `RIGOR_INCLUDE_RACTOR_POOL=1`. Measured on the Mastodon codebase (1303 files, 12-core): real analysis with zero isolation errors and zero crashes, ~2× faster (sequential ~5.4 s → fork `--workers=8` ~2.5 s) at +5–10 % memory. Known limitation: on large codebases the fork pool diverges from the sequential path by a small, deterministic number of diagnostics (Mastodon: 12 / 488, all `undefined method … for Array[String]` on ActiveRecord call sites) — a cross-file-analysis-order effect inherent to the worker-pool model, tracked as a follow-up. See the ADR-15 Amendment.

- **ADR-23 Slices 1+2 — `rigor triage` diagnostic-triage subcommand.** A `check`-derived subcommand that summarises a project's diagnostic stream instead of dumping the raw per-line list. Three sections: (1) **rule-ID distribution** — histogram by `qualified_rule`, split by severity; (2) **per-file hotspots** with their per-rule breakdown; (3) a **six-recogniser heuristic-hint catalogue** — H1 likely ActiveSupport `core_ext`, H2 likely a project monkey-patch / refinement, H3 a gem shipping no RBS, H4 a possible ActiveRecord relation misinference on `Array[...]`, H5 a systemic single-file cluster, H6 low-count scattered rules = likely genuine bugs — each naming a likely cause and a suggested action. `--format json` for tooling / the ADR-22 onboarding SKILLs; `--hints-only` / `--no-hints` / `--top N`. Read-only and advisory: never edits config, never writes a baseline, always exits 0 (`rigor check` remains the gate). New `Rigor::Triage` (pure over the diagnostic stream) + `Triage::Catalogue` + `CLI::TriageCommand` + `TriageRenderer`. On Mastodon (1303 files, default config) the report condenses 489 diagnostics into five hints headed by "365 likely ActiveSupport core_ext — wire the RBS bundle". Spec coverage: 19 examples (`spec/rigor/triage_spec.rb` unit + `spec/rigor/cli/triage_command_spec.rb` end-to-end). ADR-23 slice 3 (SKILL integration) + slice 4's plugin-contributed recognisers remain. See [ADR-23](docs/adr/23-diagnostic-triage-command.md).

- **ADR-23 Slice 4 (structured `Diagnostic` fields) — removes the WD3 message-parsing coupling.** `Analysis::Diagnostic` gains two optional structured fields, `receiver_type` and `method_name`, populated by the `call.undefined-method` rule (`CheckRules#build_undefined_method_diagnostic`) with the rendered receiver type and the called method name. The `rigor triage` heuristic catalogue (H1 / H2 / H4) now reads these fields directly instead of regex-parsing the diagnostic message — ADR-23 WD3 acknowledged the message parse as fragile and recorded the structured fields as the robust fix. `Triage::Catalogue#parse_undefined_method` prefers the structured pair and falls back to message parsing only when it is absent (rule-less diagnostics, plugin-emitted rules), so behaviour is unchanged where the fields are not populated. The catalogue is no longer coupled to the `undefined method '%s' for %s` message wording. Spec coverage: +5 examples (`diagnostic_spec` field plumbing, `triage_spec` structured-field recognition + message fallback, `runner_spec` end-to-end engine population). Slice 4's other half — a `Plugin` hook letting plugins contribute recognisers — stays deferred. See [ADR-23](docs/adr/23-diagnostic-triage-command.md) WD3 / § "Implementation slicing".

- **ADR-24 Slice 1 — implicit-self method-call resolution inside a method body.** A call written with no explicit receiver, inside a method body, typed `Dynamic[top]` — the body never saw the callee's inferred return type (confirmed via `type-of`: a top-level `w = nr` resolved a divergent `def nr` to `bot`, but `def g; v = nr; end` typed `v` as `Dynamic[top]`). Root cause: `StatementEvaluator#build_fresh_body_scope` — the fresh scope for every class / method body — did not carry `discovered_def_nodes`, so the engine's existing inter-procedural resolution (`Scope#top_level_def_for` / `#user_def_for`, used for top-level call sites since v0.0.3 A) was dead code inside method bodies. The scope now carries the table, so an implicit-self call resolves against the enclosing class's own instance-method definitions and the file's top-level defs. Adoption of the resolved return type is **deliberately conservative**: at top-level / inside a DSL block (`scope.self_type` is nil — the pre-slice-1 surface) it is adopted unchanged; inside a class body it is adopted ONLY when the resolved type is `Bot` (an always-diverging guard helper), which can only ever enable correct terminating-branch narrowing, never a new false positive. Unconditional adoption of precise non-`Bot` returns was measured to regress `rigor check lib` by 16 diagnostics (pre-existing callee-return-inference imprecisions surfacing downstream) and awaits a later slice. The user-method inference recursion guard is re-keyed from `(receiver, method, arg_types)` to `(receiver, method)` per ADR-24 WD5 — keying on argument types let mutual recursion through a `module_function` module (`Acceptance#accepts` → `accepts_one` → `accepts_dynamic` → `accepts`) recurse unboundedly once self-calls began resolving, observed as a `SystemStackError`. New `spec/integration/fixtures/self_method_call.rb` integration fixture. See [ADR-24](docs/adr/24-self-method-call-resolution.md); slices 2 (ancestors + cross-file) + 3 (`Bot`-branch flow narrowing) + 4 (gated closed-class diagnostics) remain.

- **ADR-24 Slice 3 — a `Bot`-typed branch is a terminating branch.** `StatementEvaluator`'s `eval_if` / `eval_unless` detected a terminating guard branch (one that lets the fall-through observe the predicate's complement — `raise ... if x.nil?` narrows `x` to non-`nil` afterwards) **syntactically**: `branch_unconditionally_exits?` matched `return` / `next` / `break` and a hardcoded `EXIT_CALL_NAMES` list (`raise` / `throw` / `exit` / `abort` / `fail`). A guard written as a helper call — `fail_with_message(...) if x.nil?` — was invisible to it. Per ADR-24 WD6 the detection is now generalised: a branch whose **inferred type is `Bot`** also terminates (a `Bot` expression cannot produce a value, so control never falls through it), regardless of how it is spelled. The new `branch_terminates?(branch_node, branch_type)` ORs the syntactic test with `branch_type.is_a?(Type::Bot)`; the branch type is already computed by `eval_if` / `eval_unless`, so this is a zero-cost addition. Composes with slice 1: an implicit-self call to an always-diverging guard helper now types `bot` (slice 1) AND narrows the fall-through (slice 3), closing same-class / top-level guard-clause false positives. The ancestor-helper case (the Mastodon `fail_with_message` cluster, ≈42 `possible-nil-receiver`) closes with slice 2. New `spec/integration/fixtures/bot_branch_guard.rb` integration fixture. See [ADR-24](docs/adr/24-self-method-call-resolution.md).

- **ADR-24 Slice 2 — implicit-self resolution through the user-class superclass chain, cross-file.** Slice 1 resolved an implicit-self call against the enclosing class's *own* defs only; the Mastodon `possible-nil-receiver` cluster (≈42 in `lib/mastodon/cli/accounts.rb`) is an *ancestor* case — the `fail_with_message` guard helper lives in `Mastodon::CLI::Base`, a superclass declared in a different file. Resolution now walks the superclass chain: `ExpressionTyper#try_user_method_inference`, on a same-class miss, follows `Scope#discovered_superclasses` (a new per-class `class → superclass-name` map) and retries `user_def_for` at each ancestor. The superclass name recorded by `ScopeIndexer` is the name AS WRITTEN at `class Foo < Bar`; it is resolved to a qualified class at the call site by `resolve_ancestor_class_name`, which follows Ruby's `Module.nesting` constant lookup (try the raw name under each enclosing namespace of the subclass, innermost first). Cross-file resolution is driven by a new project pre-pass — `ScopeIndexer.discovered_def_index_for_paths` — that walks every project file once and returns the merged `discovered_def_nodes` table plus the merged superclass map; `Runner` seeds both onto each file's scope exactly as it already seeds cross-file `discovered_classes`. The walk is depth-capped (20) and cycle-guarded. RBS-known ancestors are deliberately NOT walked here — the `MethodDispatcher` RBS tier runs first and already covers them; a superclass that resolves to no project-discovered class ends the walk. Adoption stays gated by slice 1's `adoptable_self_call_result?` (inside a class body only a `Bot` return is adopted), so an ancestor guard helper resolves to `bot` and — with slice 3 — narrows the guard, while non-`Bot` ancestor returns stay `Dynamic[top]`. Included-module (`include`) resolution is a separate follow-up: no `include` tracking exists yet, and the surface that drove this slice is the superclass chain. New `spec/integration/fixtures/inherited_guard.rb` fixture + a cross-file `Runner` spec. See [ADR-24](docs/adr/24-self-method-call-resolution.md); slice 4 (gated closed-class diagnostics) + included-module resolution remain.

- **ADR-24 Slice 2 (continued) — implicit-self resolution through included / prepended modules.** Completes slice 2's ancestor chain: slice 2 walked only the superclass chain, so an implicit-self call to a method mixed in via `include` / `prepend` did not resolve. The engine now tracks mixins — a new `ScopeIndexer.build_discovered_includes` walker records, per class / module, the module names it `include`s / `prepend`s (constant arguments only; `extend` is not tracked — it adds singleton methods, out of scope for the instance-side chain). The cross-file pre-pass `discovered_def_index_for_paths` returns the merged include map alongside the def-node and superclass maps; `Runner` seeds it onto each scope via the new `Scope#discovered_includes` / `#includes_of`. `ExpressionTyper#resolve_user_def_through_ancestors` is rewritten as a breadth-first walk over the full user-class ancestor set — included / prepended modules (transitive) first, then the superclass — cycle-guarded and node-count-capped (100). Each as-written module name is resolved to a qualified class by the same `Module.nesting` lookup slice 2 introduced for superclasses. Adoption stays gated by `adoptable_self_call_result?` (inside a class body only a `Bot` return is adopted), so a guard helper mixed in from a module resolves to `bot` and narrows the guard via slice 3. New `spec/integration/fixtures/included_module_guard.rb` fixture + a cross-file `Runner` spec. With this the ADR-24 ancestor chain (WD1) is complete; slice 4 (gated closed-class diagnostics) remains.

- **ADR-24 WD6 — `Bot`-branch terminating-branch narrowing extended to `&&` / `||`.** Slice 3 generalised terminating-branch detection from the syntactic `EXIT_CALL_NAMES` list to "branch inferred type is `Bot`" for `eval_if` / `eval_unless`; `eval_and_or` carried the *same* syntactic-only check and so missed the same case. `x = src or fail_now` — where `fail_now` is a divergent guard helper rather than a bare `raise` — left `x` typed `String | nil` instead of `String`. `StatementEvaluator#eval_and_or` now evaluates the RHS first and tests `branch_terminates?(node.right, right_type)` (the slice-3 OR-test) instead of `branch_unconditionally_exits?`, so a `Bot`-typed RHS — a `raise`, OR a resolved guard helper (ADR-24 slices 1 / 2) — narrows the LHS to its surviving edge (`a or guard` → `a` truthy afterwards; `a and guard` → `a` falsey). The RHS is now always walked once before the decision, matching the prior behaviour (the old exit path already walked it for side-effects). New `spec/integration/fixtures/or_guard_narrowing.rb` integration fixture.

### Fixed

- **Engine — explicit-receiver calls no longer resolve to private `Object` / `Kernel` / `Class` methods through the user-class fallback.** A call with an explicit, non-`self` receiver on a class rigor has no RBS for (e.g. `Favourite.select(:status_id)` on an ActiveRecord model) was routed through the user-class ancestor fallback, which resolved it against `singleton(Class)` and adopted the **private** `Kernel#select` signature — the `IO.select`-style global, declared `(...) -> ::Array[::String]`. The call therefore typed `Array[String]` instead of `Dynamic[top]`, and every downstream `.where` / association call on that wrong `Array[String]` cascaded into `call.undefined-method` / `call.argument-type-mismatch` false positives (the largest non-`possible-nil-receiver` cluster in the Mastodon survey). Ruby raises `NoMethodError` for a private method called with an explicit receiver, so a private signature must never be the answer of an explicit-receiver fallback. `MethodDispatcher#try_user_class_fallback` now passes `public_only: true` to `RbsDispatch.try_dispatch` when the call node carries an explicit, non-`self` receiver; `RbsDispatch` skips a method whose RBS accessibility is `:private` under that flag and the call falls through to `Dynamic[top]`. Implicit-self calls (`puts`, `raise`, `require`) and `self.`-receiver calls — the fallback's intended private-`Kernel` targets — keep resolving, since both may legally reach a private method in modern Ruby. Confirmed via `type-of`: `Favourite.select(:status_id)` now types `Dynamic[top]` (was `Array[String]`); the Mastodon `argument-type-mismatch` cluster (5 diagnostics) drops to 0.

- **`rigor-activesupport-core-ext` — `Hash#deep_transform_keys!` / `deep_transform_values` family.** The bundle declared `deep_transform_keys` but not its `!` sibling, nor `deep_transform_values` / `deep_transform_values!`, so those ActiveSupport `core_ext` selectors surfaced as `call.undefined-method` false positives (observed on the Mastodon survey). Added the four signatures to the bundle's `Hash` section.

- **`rigor-activerecord` v0.2.0 — `belongs_to` associations are non-nullable by default.** The plugin contributed `Nominal[Target] | nil` for every `:singular` association accessor. Since Rails 5 `belongs_to` is *required* (non-`nil`) by default (`belongs_to_required_by_default`), so `post.user` is `Nominal[User]`, not `User?`; modelling it nullable produced `call.possible-nil-receiver` false positives on `belongs_to` chains. The model discoverer now computes a per-association `nullable` flag — `has_one` stays nullable (it genuinely returns `nil` when there is no associated record), `belongs_to` is non-nullable unless the declaration passes `optional: true` / `required: false` — and `flow_contribution_for` honours it. Plugin version 0.1.0 → 0.2.0.

## [0.1.7] - 2026-05-20

### Changed

- **SKILL re-home — `rigor-plugin-author` reverted from `skills/` to `.claude/skills/`** as a contributor-only SKILL. Earlier in the same `[Unreleased]` cycle the SKILL was promoted to `skills/rigor-plugin-author/` under the framing "distributable to external plugin authors". On review the content is **rigor-monorepo-internal** end-to-end — it assumes the repo's `Makefile` (`make verify`), `spec/integration/{plugins,examples}/` layout, `spec/integration/support/plugin_helpers.rb`, `.rubocop.yml`'s `plugins/**/*` + `examples/**/*` excludes, the Flake (`nix develop --command ...`), and the `plugins/<id>/demo/` / `examples/<id>/demo/` placement convention. The mismatch surfaced when a Troubleshooting bullet referenced `make verify` (rigor-internal Makefile target) — usable for contributors but not for an external `rigor-foo` gem author. Cleaner to call this what it is. Path adjustments: all outbound docs/spec links reverted from absolute `https://github.com/rigortype/rigor/{blob,tree}/master/...` URLs back to relative `../../../X` (SKILL.md, 3 ups from `.claude/skills/rigor-plugin-author/`) or `../../../../X` (references/*.md, 4 ups). The top-level `skills/` directory is removed entirely; the slot is reserved for the **external-author SKILL committed to v0.2.0** per `docs/ROADMAP.md` § "Agent workflows / SKILLs". `CLAUDE.md` skills table re-collapsed to a single-tree shape (four contributor SKILLs under `.claude/skills/`) with a forward-pointer to the v0.2.0 commitment.

- **Repository layout — split SKILL tree into `.claude/skills/` (contributor-only) + `skills/` (distributable).** Pre-release re-home of the four agent-facing SKILLs that previously lived under `.codex/skills/` (a misleading path — none of the skills are Codex-specific, and the directory broke when read by Codex). Three contributor-only workflows (`rigor-release-prep`, `rigor-builtin-import`, `rigor-add-reference`) moved to `.claude/skills/` so they are auto-discovered by Claude Code when contributors work in this repository. The one user-facing skill (`rigor-plugin-author`) moved to `skills/rigor-plugin-author/` at the repository root — a self-contained, portable, [agentskills.io](https://agentskills.io/)-compatible layout intended for installation by third parties writing plugins on top of `rigortype`. All four `SKILL.md` files already follow the Anthropic Agent Skills shape (YAML `name:` + `description:` frontmatter, kebab-case directory name). Internal relative-link depth inside `skills/rigor-plugin-author/SKILL.md` re-anchored from `../../../` to `../../` to match the new two-deep position; cross-repo references in `CLAUDE.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CHANGELOG.md`, `docs/ROADMAP.md`, `docs/CURRENT_WORK.md`, `docs/handbook/09-plugins.md`, multiple ADRs (`12-dry-rb-packaging`, `13-typenode-resolver-plugin`, `16-macro-expansion`, `19-language-server-packaging`, `21-rubydex-evaluation`), `docs/design/20260508-rails-plugins-roadmap.md`, and the `plugins/rigor-{activerecord,rails}/README.md` updated to the new paths. The `.codex/` directory is removed entirely.

- **Repository layout — split `examples/` into `plugins/` (production) + `examples/` (walkthroughs).** Pre-release refactor that separates the two conceptually distinct categories accumulated under `examples/`: (1) **production plugins** targeting real gems / frameworks (Rails ecosystem, RSpec / Minitest / rspec-rails / shoulda-matchers, dry-rb foundation, Sorbet, Devise, Sidekiq, Pundit, GraphQL, Statesman, TypeScript utility types, the `rigor-rails` meta-gem) — moved to `plugins/`; (2) **plugin-contract walkthroughs** (`rigor-deprecations` / `rigor-lisp-eval` / `rigor-pattern` / `rigor-routes` / `rigor-units`) — stay in `examples/` as tutorial references over deliberately simplified virtual use cases. Twenty-seven plugins moved from `examples/<id>/` to `plugins/<id>/`; twenty-six integration specs moved from `spec/integration/examples/<id>_plugin_spec.rb` to `spec/integration/plugins/<id>_plugin_spec.rb` (five walkthrough specs stay under `spec/integration/examples/`). New `plugins/README.md` carries the production catalogue (Tier 1 / Tier 2 / Tier 3 / testing-and-matchers / dry-rb foundation / ADR-16 substrate consumers / cross-plugin fact channels); rewritten `examples/README.md` carries only the five walkthroughs and the architectural-surface map for plugin authoring. Top-level `README.md`, `AGENTS.md`, `CLAUDE.md`, `.rubocop.yml`, ROADMAP, CURRENT_WORK, handbook chapters, ADRs, design notes, and the `rigor-plugin-author` SKILL all updated to reference the new dual layout. `git mv` preserved file history across the move. The shared `PluginHelpers` module moved from `spec/integration/examples/support/` to `spec/integration/support/` so both directories' plugin specs auto-include it via the `define_derived_metadata` filter (broadened to match both `spec/integration/plugins/` and `spec/integration/examples/`). All 484 integration specs pass after the move; rubocop's `Exclude:` lists for `Metrics` / `Naming/FileName` / `Style/TopLevelMethodDefinition` / `Style/OneClassPerFile` / `Lint/StructNewOverride` / `Layout/LineLength` / `Lint/DuplicateBranch` / `Style/EmptyElse` extended from `examples/**/*` to both `examples/**/*` and `plugins/**/*` so the production plugins keep the relaxed demo-friendly cops. The plugin-publication trajectory — subtree-split + RubyGems publish per `rigor-rails` meta-gem's `add_dependency` template — now has a cleaner home (`plugins/` is the publication queue; `examples/` is the documentation tree).

### Added

- **ADR-22 Slices 1 + 2 — per-project baseline mechanism + `rigor baseline {generate,dump,drift,prune}` CLI.** Initial v0.1.7 deliverable on the ADR-22 track (baseline + project-onboarding SKILLs). PHPStan-shaped per-project diagnostic suppression with rigor-side adjustments: dedicated schema (`version: 1` + `ignored:` — WD9), `(file, qualified_rule, count)` granularity by default with opt-in `--match-mode message` per-call-site precision (WD1), ALL-or-NOTHING per-bucket threshold semantics (WD4 — `actual <= count` silences every diagnostic in the bucket; `actual > count` surfaces every diagnostic), explicit-loading only via `baseline: <path>` in `.rigor.yml` / `.rigor.dist.yml` or `--baseline=PATH` CLI override (WD2 (b) — file presence on disk alone is dormant), filter applied as the LAST suppression layer after `# rigor:disable` / severity_profile (WD6), stderr "N diagnostic(s) silenced by baseline" summary line so silent CI passes are surfaced visibly (WD7). New `Rigor::Analysis::Baseline` value object (frozen; load / from_diagnostics / filter / audit / without / to_yaml); new `Rigor::CLI::BaselineCommand` subcommand router (`generate` writes a fresh baseline from a `rigor check` run; `dump` inspects with `--rule` / `--file` glob filters + `--format json|text`; `drift` audits per-bucket and groups by `:within` / `:over` / `:cleared` / `:reducible` status; `prune` drops cleared buckets, supports `--dry-run`); `rigor check --baseline=PATH` / `--no-baseline` CLI flags plus matching `.rigor.yml`'s `baseline:` config key + JSON Schema property. Spec coverage: 45 examples (24 unit + 21 CLI integration). Slice 5 (`regenerate` + `--baseline-strict` CI gate) and the v0.1.9 external-user SKILL trio (`rigor-project-init` / `rigor-baseline-reduce` / external `rigor-plugin-author`) stay queued per the ADR. Driven by the five-project survey ([`docs/notes/20260519-oss-library-survey.md`](docs/notes/20260519-oss-library-survey.md)) where mature codebases carry hundreds-thousands of diagnostics on first contact.

- **Pillar 2 Slice 2 — `rigor-rspec` v0.3.0 binds `let` / `subject` locals in `it` bodies to their inferred return type.** Completes the Pillar 2 implementation track (Slices 1+2+3 all landed). New `LetScopeIndex` walker builds a per-file describe-scope hierarchy (`outer_range`, `describe_const`, `lets`) keyed on the line range of each block body; nested describes inherit the outer constant anchor. New `LetTypeResolver` infers the runtime type of a `let(:name) { body }` block from its body's tail expression, recognising four shapes: (1) `ConstantClass.new(...)` / `Foo::Bar.new(...)` → `Nominal[ConstantClass]`; (2) `described_class.new(...)` → `Nominal[<describe const>]` when the enclosing describe has a constant anchor; (3) `create(:factory)` / `build(:factory)` / `build_stubbed(:factory)` / `attributes_for(:factory)` (and the `FactoryBot.` / `FactoryGirl.` explicit-receiver forms) → `Nominal[<factory.model_class>]` via the `:factory_index` ADR-9 fact published by `rigor-factorybot` v0.2.0 (Pillar 2 Slice 3); (4) multi-statement block bodies use the LAST statement. The plugin's `flow_contribution_for(call_node:, scope:)` consults `scope.source_path` to look up the cached `LetScopeIndex` for the file, finds the innermost describe scope containing the call's line, matches `call_node.name` against the scope's `lets`, and returns `FlowContribution(return_type: <inferred>)` so the engine binds the local-shape call to the let block's type. Composes with Slice 1 — a `let(:user) { User.new }` followed by `expect(user).to be_a(User)` first binds `user` via Slice 2 then narrows further (if needed) via Slice 1. Plugin version bumped 0.2.0 → 0.3.0; declares `consumes: [{ plugin_id: "factorybot", name: :factory_index, optional: true }]`. Spec coverage: 15 new cases (LetScopeIndex shape + LetTypeResolver per body shape + end-to-end through the engine). With this slice the README's "two design commitments" lead can expand to three; the ROADMAP § Pillar 2 status moves from "Slice 1 + Slice 3 landed" to "Slices 1+2+3 all landed".

- **Pillar 2 Slice 3 — `rigor-factorybot` v0.2.0 publishes per-factory `model_class` in the `:factory_index` fact.** Per the ROADMAP § "Pillar 2 implementation track" Slice 3 commitment. `FactoryIndex::Entry` gains a `:model_class` field carrying the inferred or explicit Ruby class the factory builds. Resolved from (in priority order): (1) an explicit `factory :name, class: <Const> do` ConstantReadNode / ConstantPathNode value; (2) an explicit `factory :name, class: "<class_name>"` String value (supports `"Admin::User"`); (3) inflected from the factory name via a pure-Ruby camelize (`user` → `User`, `blog_post` → `BlogPost`, `admin/user` → `Admin::User`). Downstream consumers — including the future `let(:user) { create(:user) }` Slice 2 SUT binding and `rigor-rspec-rails` / `rigor-shoulda-matchers`-style cross-checks — can now map a factory name to a Ruby class without re-implementing the inflection. Plugin version bumped 0.1.0 → 0.2.0. Spec coverage: 6 new cases under `spec/integration/plugins/factorybot_plugin_spec.rb` (simple-name inflection, snake_case camelize, explicit ConstantReadNode / ConstantPathNode / String forms, attribute_names preservation alongside the new field). All 13 prior factorybot specs continue to pass.

- **`plugins/rigor-shoulda-matchers/` — shoulda-matchers ↔ `:model_index` cross-check.** Thirty-second `examples/` entry. New plugin walks `RSpec.describe <ModelConst> do ... end` (or bare `describe <ModelConst> do`) blocks and validates the shoulda-matchers calls inside against the model's known columns / associations. Consumes `:model_index` from `rigor-activerecord` (optional cross-plugin dependency via ADR-9); the plugin falls silent when `rigor-activerecord` is NOT loaded — the cross-check is opt-in. Recognised matchers: **column-side** (`validate_presence_of` / `validate_uniqueness_of` / `validate_length_of` / `validate_numericality_of` / `validate_acceptance_of` / `validate_inclusion_of` / `validate_exclusion_of` / `validate_absence_of` / `validate_format_of` / `validate_confirmation_of` / `have_db_column` / `have_db_index`) — fires `shoulda-matchers.unknown-column` when the symbol arg doesn't name a column on the model. **Association-side** (`belong_to` / `have_one` ↔ `:singular`; `have_many` / `have_and_belong_to_many` ↔ `:collection`) — fires `shoulda-matchers.unknown-association` for missing associations and `shoulda-matchers.association-kind-mismatch` when the matcher's expected kind disagrees with the model's recorded kind. Recognises matcher invocation through `should MATCHER`, `expect(...).to MATCHER`, `is_expected.to MATCHER`, and `subject.should MATCHER` chains uniformly. Spec coverage: 19 cases (per-matcher unit + describe-anchor resolution + no-index fall-silent + chain shape coverage).

- **`plugins/rigor-rspec-rails/` — rspec-rails behavioral matcher validation (`have_http_status` floor).** Thirty-first `examples/` entry. New plugin recognises `have_http_status(int_or_symbol)` at every matcher call site (positive `.to`, negative `.not_to`, bare `should have_http_status(...)`) and emits domain-specific diagnostics for the two static-checkable failure modes: `have_http_status.out-of-range` (IntegerNode arg outside `100..599`) and `have_http_status.unknown-symbol` (SymbolNode arg not in Rack's `SYMBOL_TO_STATUS_CODE` keys nor a Rails status-group alias `:success` / `:successful` / `:missing` / `:redirect` / `:error` / `:client_error` / `:server_error` / `:informational`). Vendored Rack symbol catalogue (`http_status_codes.rb`) snapshot avoids the runtime dependency on `rack` / `actionpack`. Variable / method-call / String args are intentionally skipped — the plugin only fires when the arg is statically checkable. Composes with `rigor-rspec` (which handles the type-narrowing matchers); the two activate independently in `.rigor.yml`. Heavyweight matchers (`render_template`, `route_to`, `redirect_to`, `have_enqueued_job`, `have_received`) deferred per the README — each needs cross-plugin coordination (routes table from `rigor-rails-routes`; overlap with `rigor-actionpack`'s render-target check or the engine's `call.undefined-method`). Spec coverage: 20 cases (valid + invalid numerics + recognised + typo'd symbols + non-checkable args + matcher-invocation contexts).

- **`plugins/rigor-minitest/` — Minitest + Test::Unit assertion narrowing.** Sibling to Pillar 2 Slice 1 covering the second framework family. Thirtieth `examples/` entry. New plugin recognises the `assert_*` / `refute_*` / `assert_not_*` API (Minitest core + Test::Unit aliases) plus the Minitest/spec `_(x).must_*` / `.wont_*` matchers (`value(x)` / `expect(x)` wrappers covered too; matchers_vaccine layered RSpec-style matchers fall through the same recogniser). Matcher table: `assert_kind_of(T, x)` / `assert_instance_of(T, x)` / `assert_nil(x)` / `assert_equal(literal, x)` / `assert_match(regex, x)` narrow `x` toward the matcher's type; `refute_*` / `assert_not_*` mirrors emit the same fact with `negative: true` so the engine narrows AWAY from the matcher's type. Spec-style `_(x).must_be_kind_of(T)` / `_(x).must_be_nil` / `_(x).must_equal(literal)` / `_(x).must_match(regex)` + `wont_*` mirrors share the same `:local`-kind `post_return_fact` channel. Single plugin covers BOTH Minitest and Test::Unit (their `assert_*` API is compatible); a single project that mixes the two activates it once. Spec coverage: 29 cases under `spec/integration/plugins/minitest_plugin_spec.rb` (per-assertion unit + end-to-end through the engine + non-matching call shape guards). New demo file `plugins/rigor-minitest/demo/test/narrowing_test.rb`.

- **`rigor-rspec` MatcherAnalyzer extension — `match(/regex/)` matcher + `not_to` / `to_not` negation verbs.** Builds on the Pillar 2 Slice 1 floor. `expect(x).to match(/.../)` narrows `x` to `Nominal[String]` (the matcher requires the receiver to support `=~`, which String is the only carrier that does cleanly). `not_to` and `to_not` (older spelling) are recognised as negation verbs: every supported matcher now has a positive and negative form, so `expect(x).not_to be_nil` emits a negative-polarity `:local`-kind fact narrowing `x` AWAY from `Constant<nil>`, and `expect(x).not_to be_a(String)` removes the String subtree from `x`. `be_an_instance_of(T)` added as an `be_instance_of` alias for compatibility with older RSpec docs. Spec coverage: +7 cases under `spec/integration/plugins/rspec_plugin_spec.rb` (match-regex + not_to + to_not + be_an_instance_of).

- **Pillar 2 Slice 1 — `rigor-rspec` narrows `expect(x).to MATCHER` downstream in `it` bodies.** The first slice of the Pillar 2 ("Your specs are types") track (ROADMAP § v0.1.7/v0.1.8). `rigor-rspec` v0.2.0 implements a new `MatcherAnalyzer` consulted at every `Prism::CallNode` via `Plugin::Base#flow_contribution_for`. When the call shape matches `expect(<local>).to <matcher>` (six-matcher floor: `be_a(T)`, `be_kind_of(T)`, `be_instance_of(T)`, `be_nil`, `eq(literal)`, `eql(literal)`), the plugin emits a `FlowContribution(post_return_facts: [...])` carrying a single `:local`-target Fact that narrows the named local to the matcher's type. Closes today's gap where `rigor check` reads specs as opaque Ruby and misses both spec-internal bugs (assertions against a wrong variable's type) and spec-derived contracts (downstream calls in the same `it` body that benefit from the narrowed receiver). Engine plumbing extension: `Rigor::FlowContribution::Fact::FACT_VALID_TARGET_KINDS` gains `:local` so plugins recognising bespoke call shapes can name a scope-bound local directly without routing through the parameter-name lookup that requires an authoritative RBS sig on the called method; `StatementEvaluator#apply_post_return_fact` routes `:local` facts through a new `apply_local_post_return_fact` that consults `current_scope.local(name)`. Composes with the existing core matcher path (`apply_rspec_matcher_narrowing`) which still handles `be_a` / `be_kind_of` / `be_an_instance_of` / `not_to be_nil` — the plugin path adds the `be_nil` positive and `eq(literal)` / `eql(literal)` matchers and is poised to absorb the rest as the core path retires. Spec coverage: 16 new cases under `spec/integration/plugins/rspec_plugin_spec.rb` (per-matcher unit + end-to-end through the engine + non-matching call shape guards) + new demo file `plugins/rigor-rspec/demo/spec/matcher_narrowing_spec.rb`. Promotes the README's "two design commitments" lead to three once Slice 2 + Slice 3 also land.

- **`data/vendored_gem_sigs/bundler/` — full Bundler module + class RBS stub.** New `data/vendored_gem_sigs/bundler/bundler.rbs` (MPL-2.0, hand-written) covers the top-75 surface of the `bundler` gem: every singleton method on `module Bundler` (`Bundler.ui` / `.settings` / `.rubygems` / `.local_platform` / `.root` / `.frozen_bundle?` / `.default_gemfile` / `.app_cache` / ...) plus `Bundler::Definition` / `LockfileParser` / `LazySpecification` / `Dependency` / `ConfigFile` / `UI::Shell` / `Source::{Gemspec,Path,Git,Rubygems}` / four error classes. Modelled on `references/ruby/lib/bundler.rb`'s `class << self` block and the `references/ruby/lib` survey's top selectors. Returns are mostly `untyped` (Bundler's surface is dynamic) — the goal is to silence `call.undefined-method`, not infer precise returns. The vendored stub is loaded by default through `VENDORED_GEM_NAMES` so users analysing bundler-using code no longer need to author the missing RBS themselves.

- **`data/vendored_gem_sigs/rubygems/rubygems_extras.rbs` — supplement to the rbs gem's `core/rubygems/*.rbs`.** Re-opens `module Gem` and the rubygems classes (`Gem::Platform` / `Gem::Dependency` / `Gem::Specification` / `Gem::ConfigFile` / `Gem::SourceList` / `Gem::RequestSet` / `Gem::Uninstaller` / `Gem::DependencyInstaller` / `Gem::PathSupport` / `Gem::Installer` / `Gem::MissingSpecError` / `Gem::BasicSpecification` / `Gem::Version` / `Gem::Requirement` / `Gem::LoadError`) to add the singleton methods (`Gem.target_rbconfig` / `Gem.verbose` / `Gem.really_verbose` / `Gem.load_safe_marshal` / `Gem.extension_api_version` / `Gem.vendor_dir` / `Gem.activate_bin_path` / ...) and the missing `initialize` declarations on `Gem::Platform.new(arg)` / `Gem::Dependency.new(name, *reqs, type:)` etc. that the upstream RBS omits — the upstream rows declare class shells with no constructor, so `Gem::Platform.new(arg)` was firing wrong-arity (33 sites, plus 36 on `Gem::Dependency`). Adds new declarations only; never redeclares anything in the upstream RBS, so the loader does not raise `RBS::DuplicatedDeclarationError`.

- **`data/vendored_gem_sigs/did_you_mean/did_you_mean_extras.rbs` — supplement to the rbs gem's `stdlib/did_you_mean/0/did_you_mean.rbs`.** Re-opens `module DidYouMean` to add the four top-level singleton methods (`.formatter` / `.formatter=` / `.spell_checkers` / `.correct_error`) the upstream RBS omits. Adds new declarations only; never redeclares anything in the upstream RBS. Survey delta: **−7 errors / −2 files** (`did_you_mean/core_ext/name_error.rb`, `optparse.rb`, `bundler/vendor/thor/lib/thor/error.rb`).

- **`VENDORED_GEM_NAMES` bumped 7 → 10** to include `bundler`, `rubygems`, and `did_you_mean`.

- **Vendored stubs expansion — `Gem::Specification` / `Gem::ConfigFile` / `Gem::RequestSet` / `Gem::DependencyInstaller` / `Bundler::Source::{Gemspec,Path,Git,Rubygems}` member additions.** Driven by the second-pass survey after the v1 bundler+rubygems stubs landed: `Gem::Specification` gained `.activate` / `.specification_version` / `.files` / `.default_gem?` / `.spec_file` / `.runtime_dependencies` / `.required_rubygems_version` / `.required_ruby_version` / `.require_paths` + `attr_accessor` for `name` / `version` / `full_gem_path` / `loaded_from` / `post_install_message`. `Gem::ConfigFile` gained `.ssl_client_cert` / `.ssl_verify_mode` / `.cert_expiration_length_days` / `.sources` / `.unset_api_key!` / `.set_api_key` / etc. `Gem::RequestSet` gained `.installed_gems` / `.install` / `.resolve_*` / `.errors` + 8 `attr_accessor` flags. `Gem::DependencyInstaller` gained `.install` / `.installed_gems` / `.errors`. `Bundler::Source::{Gemspec,Path,Git,Rubygems}` each gained `initialize: (*untyped) -> void` and the most-touched `attr_accessor`s. Survey delta: **−136 errors / −34 files** (526 → 390 / 145 → 111). The new stubs are loaded by default by `Rigor::Environment::RbsLoader.vendored_gem_sig_paths`. Survey delta on `references/ruby/lib`: **1668 → 533 errors / 248 → 147 files (−1135 / −101)**. `make verify` clean (3824 examples / 0 failures); rigor self-check unchanged (3 known loop-mutation warnings).

- **Engine — module-mixin receivers fall back to `Nominal[Object]` for dispatch + rule lookup.** Driven by the `references/ruby/lib` survey item (f) (`pp.rb:369-371,404`: `module PP::ObjectMixin; def pretty_print(q); ... self.inspect; self.respond_to?; self.class; ...; end; end`). An instance method on a mixin-only module observes Kernel / Object instance methods through every concrete includer's ancestor chain, but pre-fix the dispatcher resolved `self.inspect` against `Nominal[PP::ObjectMixin]` literally and reported `undefined-method` because the module itself does not declare `inspect`. New `Environment#rbs_module?(name)` API (backed by `RbsLoader#rbs_module?` checking `RBS::Environment::ModuleEntry`) lets two surfaces detect the module-mixin shape: (a) `MethodDispatcher#user_class_fallback_receiver` retries the dispatch against `Nominal[Object]` when the receiver is an RBS-known module; (b) `CheckRules#undefined_method_diagnostic` mirror — when the method isn't on the module itself, check Object before emitting. Survey delta: **references/ruby/lib 1691 → 1687 errors / 250 → 249 files (−4 / −1)**, all in `pp.rb`. `make verify` clean (3813 examples / 0 failures); rigor self-check unchanged.

- **`StaticReturnRefinements` — `File.expand_path` and `File.dirname` tighten to `non-empty-string`.** Both class methods are pure functions over their path-string arguments and always return a non-empty result (`File.expand_path("")` expands to the cwd absolute path; `File.dirname("foo")` returns `"."`). The upstream `ruby/rbs` rows return `String`; the refinement closes the gap [`lib/rigor/inference/method_dispatcher/file_folding.rb`](lib/rigor/inference/method_dispatcher/file_folding.rb) already named as the "future direction" for the platform-agnostic default mode. `File.basename` is intentionally NOT refined — `File.basename("")` returns `""`, so `non-empty-string` would be unsound. The opt-in `fold_platform_specific_paths: true` path still wins for constant args (FileFolding runs in the `dispatch_precise_tiers` chain ABOVE static refinement and produces the more precise `Constant<String>` envelope); only variable args / default-mode constant args now route through the new `non-empty-string` row. Carries over the queued single-table rows recorded under [`docs/CURRENT_WORK.md`](docs/CURRENT_WORK.md) § "Queued StaticReturnRefinements extensions" minus the three entries that fact-check as no-ops against the actual upstream RBS (`Kernel#caller()` / `caller_locations()` are already precise as `Array[String]`; `__method__` / `__callee__` are already `Symbol?` which is structurally identical to `Symbol | nil`). `spec/integration/fixtures/file_path_folding.rb` updated to assert the tightened `non-empty-string` shape for `File.dirname`.

### Changed

- **Rule — `def.ivar-write-mismatch` skips leading nil-placeholder writes when establishing the canonical type.** Driven by the post-Bundler `references/ruby/lib` survey: the `@parse_method = nil` placeholder in `initialize` followed by per-state `@parse_method = :parse_source` / `:parse_dependency` / ... assignments fired the divergence rule on every concrete write because `first_class = NilClass` and each subsequent `Symbol` write counted as drift. The canonical type is now the first non-NilClass write (the existing `other_class == "NilClass"` skip already tolerates downstream nil resets — the nullable-slot idiom is two-sided). Genuine drift between two distinct concrete classes still fires even with a leading nil placeholder. Survey delta: **−16 nil-placeholder warnings** removed (Bundler::CompactIndexClient::Parser, Bundler::Definition, Bundler::Dsl, Bundler::EndpointSpecification, Bundler::LockfileParser, Bundler::Settings::Mirror, Bundler::Source::Git::GitProxy, Bundler::FileUtils::Entry_, Bundler::Worker, FileUtils::Entry_, OpenURI::Buffer + the rubygems vendored copies). Spec coverage: +2 cases (nullable-slot idiom does not fire; genuine `Symbol → String` drift after a nil placeholder still fires).

- **Engine — user-class fallback preserves the original receiver type for `self` substitution.** Driven by the second-pass `references/ruby/lib` survey: `base = self.dup` inside a `Bundler::URI::Generic` instance method typed `base` as `Object` (the fallback receiver) instead of `Bundler::URI::Generic` (the actual receiver), so subsequent `base.fragment=` / `base.set_path(...)` / `base.query=` calls fired `undefined-method for Object`. New `self_type_override:` kwarg on `MethodDispatcher::RbsDispatch.try_dispatch`; `MethodDispatcher#try_user_class_fallback` now passes the ORIGINAL receiver as the override so `Kernel#dup: () -> self` and other `self`-returning methods route through Object's RBS while still returning the caller's type. Survey delta: **−36 errors / −7 files** (390 → 354 / 111 → 104), distributed across `bundler/vendor/uri/lib/uri/generic.rb`, `rubygems/vendor/uri/lib/uri/generic.rb`, and similar `URI`-vendored copies.

- **Engine — `ScopeIndexer` walks `Module.new do ... end` and `Class.new do ... end` block bodies as the named-constant's body.** Driven by the `references/ruby/lib` survey item (e) (`resolv.rb` ~8 sites of `ClassHash = Module.new do; def []=(k, v); ...; end; end` followed by `ClassHash[k] = v`; vendored rubygems copy adds 8 more). Pre-fix the block body went unwalked: `[]=` was never registered, `ClassHash` typed as `Singleton[Module]` per RBS, and `ClassHash[k] = v` fired `undefined method '[]='`. The existing `meta_new_block_body` helper (which already handled `Data.define(*sym) do ... end` and `Struct.new(*sym) do ... end`) now also recognises `Module.new` and `Class.new` block forms via two new `module_new_call?` / `class_new_call?` predicates. The same `walk_methods` / `walk_def_nodes` / `walk_method_visibilities` / `walk_class_ivars` / `walk_class_cvars` paths automatically pick up the new shape. `record_constant_write` also types the named constant as `Singleton[<full>]` (instead of the rvalue's default `Nominal[Module]` / `Nominal[Class]`) so dispatch routes through the freshly registered `discovered_methods` table. Survey delta: **references/ruby/lib 1684 → 1668 errors / 248 → 248 files (−16)**, all 16 removed errors at `ClassHash[]=` sites in `resolv.rb` (8) and the rubygems vendored copy (8). `make verify` clean (3824 examples / 0 failures); rigor self-check unchanged. Floor connects to ADR-16 Tier A "block-as-method" — this slice covers the constant-write shape; broader `module_eval` / `module_function`-promoted singleton-side dispatch remains queued.

- **Engine — `=~` against a regex literal narrows the match-data globals (`$~` / `$&` / `` $` `` / `$'` / `$+` / `$1..$N`) on each predicate edge.** Driven by the `references/ruby/lib` survey item (b) (`net/http/header.rb:528` and the rubygems vendored copy at `:524`: `unless /\Abytes=(\d+)\z/ =~ value; raise ...; end; byte_range_set = $1; byte_range_set.split(...)`) and the `time.rb` `argument-type-mismatch` at `:594`. Pre-fix `$1` resolved to its default `String | nil` regardless of any `=~`-success guard, so `$1.split(...)` after `unless ... raise` fired `possible-nil-receiver`. New `Narrowing#analyse_regex_match_predicate` recognises `/regex/ =~ x` (or the reverse `x =~ /regex/`) and binds the back-reference globals: truthy edge — `$~` to `Nominal[MatchData]`, `$&` / `` $` `` / `$'` / `$+` and `$1..$N` to `Nominal[String]` (N from a lightweight regex-source scan that skips backslash escapes, `(?:...)`, `(?=...)` / `(?!...)` lookarounds, and `(?<=...)` / `(?<!...)` lookbehinds, while counting `(?<name>...)` named groups); falsey edge — all bound to `Constant<nil>`. `ExpressionTyper#type_of_numbered_reference` / `#type_of_back_reference` now consult `scope.global(...)` before falling back to the default `String | nil`. Survey delta: **references/ruby/lib 1687 → 1684 errors / 249 → 248 files (−3 / −1)** (`net/http/header.rb`, `rubygems/vendor/net-http/.../header.rb`, `time.rb`). `make verify` clean (3820 examples / 0 failures); rigor self-check unchanged.

- **Engine — `eval_begin` drops rescue arms whose body unconditionally exits from the post-begin join.** Driven by the `references/ruby/lib` survey item (c) (`resolv.rb:430`'s `Socket.ip_address_list` pattern: `begin; list = expr; rescue X; return ...; end; list.any? ...`). Pre-fix the rescue arm's scope joined into the post-`begin` scope with nil-injection for `list` (the rescue arm never bound `list`), forcing `list` to widen to `Array[...] | nil` so `list.any?` fired `possible-nil-receiver` even though the rescue path was unreachable. `StatementEvaluator#eval_begin` now consults `branch_unconditionally_exits?` (the same helper the `eval_if` / `eval_unless` / `eval_and_or` early-return narrowing uses) on each rescue arm's statements; arms that exit unconditionally (`return` / `next` / `break` / `raise` / `throw` / `exit` / `abort` / `fail`) contribute neither a value-fragment to the union nor a scope-binding to the join. Survey delta: **references/ruby/lib 1724 → 1691 errors / 252 → 250 files (−33 / −2)**, across `bundler/cli/check.rb`, `fileutils.rb`, `find.rb`, `net/http.rb`, `optparse.rb`, `resolv.rb`, `rubygems/local_remote_options.rb`, `rubygems/package/tar_reader.rb`, `uri/generic.rb`, and the rubygems vendored copies of `optparse` / `resolv` / `uri`. `make verify` clean (3802 examples / 0 failures); rigor self-check unchanged.

- **Engine — `eval_and_or` narrows the post-OR / post-AND scope when the RHS unconditionally exits.** Driven by the `references/ruby/lib` survey (`net/http/header.rb` 8+ sites; `optparse.rb` `private def parse_arg` body; vendored rubygems copies of both files) and the Steep 2.0.0 self-analysis (~7 occurrences) recorded under [`docs/CURRENT_WORK.md`](docs/CURRENT_WORK.md) § "Queued engine items (a)". The `a or raise` / `a or return` / `a or throw` / `a or exit` / `a or abort` / `a or fail` / `a or next` / `a or break` idiom is widely used to assert non-nil; pre-fix `m = expr or raise` left `m` typed as the unnarrowed rvalue (e.g. `MatchData | nil`) so subsequent uses like `m[1]` fired `possible-nil-receiver`. `StatementEvaluator#eval_and_or` now consults the existing `branch_unconditionally_exits?` helper (same surface `eval_if` / `eval_unless` already use for early-return narrowing) and, when the RHS terminates, returns the LHS-surviving edge alone instead of joining with the (non-returning) RHS scope. Symmetric for `a and raise` (post-scope is the falsey edge — LHS truthy is the exiting branch). Survey delta: **references/ruby/lib 1746 → 1724 errors / 254 → 252 files (−22 / −2)**, all 22 removed errors traced to the documented `or raise` sites. `make verify` clean (3790 examples / 0 failures); rigor self-check unchanged (3 known loop-mutation `flow.always-truthy-condition` warnings; queued separately).

## [0.1.6] - 2026-05-19

### Added

- **Engine — `OverloadSelector` pre-sort demotes disjoint-sibling overloads when the receiver carries a usable class name + `Nominal.accepts(Constant)` / `Nominal.accepts(Nominal)` fall back to `actual.class.ancestors` when the target class is unloadable (BigDecimal-coerce regression).** Driven by a 22-library external-codebase survey (`rigor-survey/REPORT.md`): seven libraries (`algorithms`, `parser`, `kramdown`, `protobuf`, `numo-narray`, `hamlit`, `haml`) reported a total of 25 `undefined method 'upto' / '<<' / 'times' / 'to_i' / 'to_f' for BigDecimal | Numeric` false-positives on plain `Integer` arithmetic such as `(i + 1).upto(n)`. Root cause: rigor's own process never `require`s `bigdecimal` (the `bigdecimal` gem was demoted from default in Ruby 3.4), so `Acceptance#accepts_nominal_from_constant` / `class_subtype_result` could not `Object.const_get("BigDecimal")` and conservatively answered `:maybe`. The `bigdecimal` stdlib RBS reopens `Integer#+` / `-` / `*` / `/` / `%` etc. with `def +: (BigDecimal) -> BigDecimal | ...` at the FRONT of the overload list, so `OverloadSelector` (which accepts `yes` OR `maybe` matches) picked the BigDecimal arm first and propagated a `Nominal[BigDecimal]` return wherever an Integer arithmetic expression flowed downstream. Two-part fix: **(a)** `Acceptance` now uses the actual side's `class.ancestors.map(&:name)` as an authoritative subtype answer when the target class can't be resolved but the actual side can — `Integer.ancestors` does not contain `"BigDecimal"`, so the relation is definitively `:no`, not `:maybe`. The fully-unresolved (both-user-class) case stays `:maybe`. **(b)** New `Rigor::Inference::MethodDispatcher::ReceiverAffinity.reorder` runs once at the head of `OverloadSelector.select`: it stable-partitions overloads so arms whose every positional param's class is either `self_type.class_name` itself OR one of its proper RBS ancestors come first, demoting disjoint-sibling arms below. The pre-sort fires whenever the receiver carries a usable class name and the env can answer `class_ordering` (not only when args are untyped) — a misordered `overloads.first` fallback is just as wrong as a misordered Pass 1 / 2 winner. Survey verification: BigDecimal/Numeric-shape false positives across the 22-library corpus reduced from 25 to 0; total error count dropped by 24 (protobuf 16 → 1, parser 11 → 8, hamlit 18 → 16, haml 15 → 13, algorithms 53 → 52, kramdown 42 → 41, concurrent-ruby 12 → 11). Spec coverage: +2 `acceptance_spec.rb` cases (`Nominal[Unresolvable].accepts(Constant<Integer>)` → `:no` / both-unresolved still `:maybe`) + 1 amended (Union maybe case rewritten to use both-unresolved nominals) + +4 `overload_selector_spec.rb` cases (`Integer#+(Integer)` / `Integer#+(untyped)` / `Integer#-(untyped)` prefer Integer arm over BigDecimal arm / `Integer#*(Float)` still picks Float arm — pre-sort is stable, not "always prefer receiver class"). Test count: 3783 → 3789 (5 added, 1 amended).
- **Engine — new `Rigor::Builtins::StaticReturnRefinements` dispatcher tier + `Kernel#__dir__` entry returning `non-empty-string | nil`.** Driven by a user request after `__FILE__` and `__LINE__` were tightened — `Kernel#__dir__` carries the canonical source-directory path when defined, or `nil` for `-e` / eval contexts; the upstream `references/rbs/core/kernel.rbs` declares it as `() -> String?` because RBS has no annotation slot in that submodule that Rigor can write to. Adding a Rigor-bundled `signature_paths` overlay would conflict with the vendored declaration; instead, a new dispatcher tier consults a `(owner_class_name, method_name, kind) => handler` table between `try_hkt_builtin_return` and `RbsDispatch.try_dispatch`. Today's only entry maps `("Kernel", :__dir__, :both) → non-empty-string | nil`. Match policy treats every non-`BasicObject` Nominal / Singleton receiver as Kernel-mixed-in, so the three call shapes (`__dir__`, `Kernel.__dir__`, `self.__dir__`) all resolve to the refined union. Future stdlib methods whose upstream RBS is broader than their documented behaviour (`Kernel#caller`, `Kernel#caller_locations`, `File.expand_path`, …) extend the same table; no engine work beyond adding rows. Spec coverage: +8 cases in `static_return_refinements_spec.rb` (lookup happy paths / unknown method / unknown owner / nil owner / `owners_for` index / String method-name accepted) + +4 dispatcher-tier integration cases in `method_dispatcher_spec.rb` (singleton receiver / Nominal-Object receiver / arbitrary user-class receiver fires / BasicObject receiver does NOT fire). Test count: 3458 → 3458 (8 module + 4 dispatcher = 12 new examples; +2 from the existing slice's spec count baseline).
- **`__FILE__` → `non-empty-string`, `__LINE__` → `positive-int`.** The two leaf-node handlers added in the earlier `references/ruby/lib` survey slice (`Prism::SourceFileNode` → `Nominal[String]`, `Prism::SourceLineNode` → `Nominal[Integer]`) are tightened to their canonical imported refinements. `__FILE__` always carries a path the parser saw (`"(stdin)"`, `"-e"`, or a real path — never the empty string), so the `String - ""` refinement (`non-empty-string`) fits exactly. `__LINE__` is 1-indexed in Ruby, so `positive-int` (`Integer ∈ [1, +Inf)`) is the canonical refinement. Both erase to their RBS base (`String` / `Integer`), so RBS-erased downstream observers see no change. CLI verification: `rigor type-of` on `x = __FILE__` shows `non-empty-string`; on `x = __LINE__` shows `positive-int`.
- **`data/vendored_gem_sigs/prism/` — RBS supplement for the `prism` gem's C-extension-bound surfaces and Ruby-source methods omitted from the gem's bundled `sig/`.** The gem ships its own RBS (loaded via `Rigor::Environment::DEFAULT_LIBRARIES`), but two categories of declarations are missing: (a) `Prism::ParseResult#attach_comments!` and `Prism::ParseResult#mark_newlines!` are defined in `lib/prism/parse_result.rb` but the gem's RBS template omits them, and (b) `Prism::StringQuery.{local?, constant?, method_name?}` are bound by the C extension as class methods but only the instance methods are declared in the gem's RBS. The Rigor-side overlay re-opens both classes to add only the missing surfaces (no redeclarations — so the loader does not raise `RBS::DuplicatedDeclarationError` even though the gem's RBS is also loaded). `VENDORED_GEM_NAMES` (the `RbsCoverageReport` table) is bumped from 6 to 7 to match the on-disk listing. End-to-end check on `references/ruby/lib/prism/{relocation,string_query}.rb` is now clean. Total references-survey error count: 1747 → 1743 (-4 errors, -2 files cleared).
- **Engine — `StatementEvaluator#eval_call` recurses into argument-position `DefNode`s.** Driven by `references/ruby/lib/delegate.rb:85` (`self.__getobj__` inside `ruby2_keywords def method_missing(...)` reported `undefined method '__getobj__' for singleton(Delegator)`). The wrapper-call idioms — `ruby2_keywords def m(...)`, `private def m(...)`, `public def m(...)`, `module_function def m(...)` — parse as a CallNode whose argument is the DefNode. `ExpressionTyper#type_of_def` types it as `Constant[:m]` without walking the body, so `ScopeIndexer.propagate` later filled the body's scope-index entries from the surrounding class-body scope (`self_type = singleton(C)`) rather than from a proper method-entry scope (`self_type = nominal(C)`). `eval_call` now calls `eval_def` on each `Prism::DefNode` argument so the body's scope-index entries get the correct instance / singleton `self_type`. The def's effects do not bind into the surrounding scope (matching how the wrapper call observes only the `Constant[:method_name]` return value). Spec coverage: +2 `statement_evaluator_spec.rb` cases (ruby2_keywords-wrapped def body sees `Nominal[Foo]`; private-def-wrapped body sees `Nominal[Foo]`). Side benefit: surfaces additional real errors in `references/ruby/lib/optparse.rb`'s `private def parse_arg(...)` body (8 newly-visible flow-narrowing issues that were previously hidden by the wrong self_type — these are genuine signal, not regressions).
- **Engine — `OverloadSelector` pass 1.5 prefers the canonical-alias overload whose strict arm matches the arg.** Driven by `references/ruby/lib/ipaddr.rb:51` (`(["%.4x"] * 8).join(':')` reporting `undefined method 'join' for String`). `Array#*` ships two overloads: `(string str) -> ::String` and `(int int) -> ::Array[Elem]`.
