Both `string` and `int` are core aliases (`String | _ToStr` / `Integer | _ToInt`) that the translator collapses to `Dynamic[Top]`, so pass 1 (strict) skips both and pass 2 (gradual) picks the first arity-compatible overload regardless of arg type — `(string) -> String` wins for an Integer literal arg. New `ALIAS_STRICT_NOMINALS` table maps each canonical core alias to its strict-arm class name(s) (`::int → Integer`, `::string → String`, `::interned → Symbol | String`, `::io → IO`, `::encoding → Encoding | String`, `::path → String`, `::boolean → TrueClass | FalseClass`). New pass 1.5 between strict and gradual filters overloads via alias-resolved strict matching: `Array#*(8)` now selects `(int) -> Array[Elem]`, and `Array#*("x")` keeps selecting `(string) -> String`. Optional / Union / nested aliases are walked; unrecognised aliases (and Interfaces) make the pass abstain so gradual matching still owns the fallback. Spec coverage: +2 `overload_selector_spec.rb` cases (`Array#*(Integer)` resolves to the `int` overload; `Array#*(String)` stays on the `string` overload).
- **Engine — `class << Foo` (explicit-constant singleton-class) opens Foo's singleton scope.** Driven by the `references/ruby/lib` survey — Ruby's bundled `time.rb` defines `Time.zone_offset` / `Time.utc` / `Time.local` etc. inside a `class Time; class << Time; ...; end; end` block. The engine's previous `SingletonClassNode` handlers (one in `StatementEvaluator#singleton_context_for`, three in `ScopeIndexer`'s `walk_methods` / `walk_def_nodes` / `walk_method_visibilities`) only recognised `class << self`, so calls to `self.now` / `self.utc(2020, 1, 1, ...)` inside the body resolved against `Time`'s instance methods rather than its singleton — producing 12 false-positive errors in time.rb alone (`undefined method 'now' for Time`, `wrong number of arguments to 'utc' on Time (given 6, expected 0)`, etc.). New `singleton_class_prefix` helper resolves the receiver constant to its FQN and either collapses to the enclosing class (when `class << Foo` is nested inside `class Foo`) or replaces the prefix with `[target]` (when the receiver is unrelated to the lexical enclosing class). Mirrored in `StatementEvaluator#singleton_context_for` so the def's `self_type` lands on `singleton(Foo)`. time.rb error count dropped 13 → 1 (the residual is a real `String | nil → Integer | String` param-type mismatch). Whole-references error count: 1747 → 1735 (-12). Spec coverage: +2 `scope_indexer_spec.rb` cases (`class << Time` inside `class Time` registers on Time / top-level `class << Foo` registers on Foo).
- **Engine — `def Foo.method` explicit-receiver form recognised as singleton on the enclosing class.** Driven by the `references/ruby/lib` survey — open-uri.rb (Ruby's bundled stdlib) defines its private helpers as `def OpenURI.check_options(...)` / `def OpenURI.scan_open_optional_arguments(...)` / etc. inside `module OpenURI`, and similarly `def Meta.init(...)` inside `module OpenURI; module Meta`. The form is semantically equivalent to `def self.method` (the receiver constant resolves to `self` at the def-site), but the engine's `ScopeIndexer#record_def_method` / `record_def_node` / `collect_def_ivar_writes` + `StatementEvaluator#singleton_def?` previously only recognised `Prism::SelfNode` receivers, so call sites within the same module reported `undefined method 'check_options' for singleton(OpenURI)`. New `def_receiver_targets_lexical_self?` helper (mirrored in both files) checks whether the def's receiver is a `Prism::ConstantReadNode` whose name matches the last segment of the lexical class path; if so, the def promotes to singleton on the enclosing class. Cross-class explicit-receiver defs (`def Bar.baz` inside `module Foo` where the receiver names a different constant) are deliberately not promoted at this slice — they would need a target-class redirect on the discovery side, which has no observed real-world consumer. open-uri.rb error count dropped 24 → 15 (the residual nine are `possible nil receiver` flow-narrowing items). Spec coverage: +3 cases in `scope_indexer_spec.rb` (canonical `def Foo.bar` inside `module Foo` / nested `def Meta.init` inside `module Outer; module Meta` / cross-class case stays unpromoted).
- **Engine — `ExpressionTyper` handlers for built-in Prism leaf nodes (`__FILE__` / `__LINE__` / backtick xstring / `%x{...}` / `END { … }` / shareable-constant magic comment / shorthand-hash `ImplicitNode` / block `it` parameter).** Driven by running `rigor check --explain references/ruby/lib` (Ruby's own bundled core / stdlib) and finding ~60 `:info` fail-soft fallback events whose carrier is trivially known. The `PRISM_DISPATCH` table gains entries for `Prism::XStringNode` / `Prism::InterpolatedXStringNode` → `Nominal[String]`, `Prism::SourceFileNode` → `Nominal[String]`, `Prism::SourceLineNode` → `Nominal[Integer]`, `Prism::PostExecutionNode` → `Constant[nil]`, `Prism::ShareableConstantNode` (passes through to `node.write`), `Prism::ImplicitNode` (passes through to `node.value`), and `Prism::ItParametersNode` (non-value declaration). `Prism::PreExecutionNode` (`BEGIN { ... }`) is deliberately left out of the dispatch — `spec/rigor/analysis/runner_spec.rb`'s explain-mode regression test uses it as a stable "still-unrecognised" canary, and `BEGIN { }` appears in zero project files in the references survey vs. the single `END { }` site in `mkmf.rb` that motivates `PostExecutionNode`. Spec coverage: +8 literal-node cases in `spec/rigor/inference/expression_typer_spec.rb`. Verified end-to-end via `rigor check --no-cache --explain /tmp/builtin_consts.rb` — all four fallback events removed.
- **Engine — cross-file class discovery pre-pass.** Driven by external-codebase analysis of `google-cloud-storage`: 5 false-positive `undefined method ... for singleton(File)` errors stemmed from rigor's per-file `discovered_classes` table not seeing user-defined classes (`Google::Cloud::Storage::File`, etc.) declared in sibling files. Lexical constant lookup then fell back to top-level `::File` (stdlib). New `ScopeIndexer.discovered_classes_for_paths(paths, buffer:)` walks every project file once, extracts `class Foo` declarations, and unions them into a frozen project-wide table. `Runner#run_project_pre_passes` invokes it after the existing pre-passes; `Runner#analyze_file` seeds each per-file `default_scope` with the table so `ExpressionTyper#resolve_constant_name`'s existing lexical walk now finds cross-file user classes alongside RBS-known ones. **Modules are intentionally excluded** from the seed — a `module M; module_function; def x; end; end` body, when surfaced as `singleton(M)`, falls through to inherited Module / Kernel methods when the project's per-file `discovered_methods` doesn't know `M.x` (rigor's own `OverloadSelector.select` regression surfaced this trade-off; queued follow-up: project-wide `discovered_methods` to support module_function idioms). Limitations: `rigor type-of` does NOT yet thread the table; `Analysis::ProjectScan` does NOT yet carry it. Spec coverage: +5 `discovered_classes_for_paths` cases (multi-file union / modules NOT registered / classes nested in modules / fail-soft on unreadable paths / frozen result). Total examples 3743 → 3748.
- **Engine fix — parameter default value expressions execute under the method's body scope (instance `self_type`).** Driven by running `rigor check` on `prism-1.9.0/lib` (the rigor project's own bundled dep) and surfacing 938 errors, of which 915 (97.5%) were the same shape: `def copy(node_id: self.node_id, ...)` (auto-generated on every Prism Node subclass) reporting `undefined method 'node_id' for singleton(Prism::AliasGlobalVariableNode)`. Same idiom in `rbs-4.0.2/lib` accounted for 63 of 255 errors. Root cause: `StatementEvaluator#eval_def` only `sub_eval`ed `node.body`; the parameter subtree was never visited at the body's instance scope, so `ScopeIndexer#propagate` filled parameter-default nodes with the outer class-body scope where `self_type = singleton(C)`. Fix: `eval_def` now also `sub_eval`s `node.parameters` under the same `body_scope`. Singleton methods (`def self.factory(seed: self)`) keep `self_type = singleton(C)` because `build_method_entry_scope` already routes through `self_type_for_method_body(singleton: true)`. External-codebase verification: prism 938 → 26 errors (-97.2%); rbs 255 → 197 errors (-22.7%). Spec coverage: +2 `scope_indexer_spec` cases (instance-side / singleton-side kwarg-default `self_type`). Total examples: 3741 → 3743.
- **Self-analysis cleanup — defensive ivar nil-guards in `sig_gen/Generator` + `Analysis::Runner` dropped.** Driven by running Steep over `lib/` (lenient profile, 0 actual type errors — but Steep's internal `RuntimeError` on `lib/rigor/sig_gen/generator.rb` exposed a "scratch ivar conditionally initialised" pattern in two files). The three per-file scratch ivars in `Generator` (`@namespace_kinds`, `@module_function_methods`, `@class_shells`) were only assigned at the top of `analyse_file`, leaving call sites carrying `|| {}` / `|| Set.new` / `&.include?` guards because the ivars were technically nil from `Generator.new` until the first `analyse_file` call. Same anti-pattern in `Runner` for `@class_decl_paths_snapshot`, `@signature_paths_snapshot`, `@cached_plugin_prepare_diagnostics`. In practice every reader is private and called only after the writer fires, so the nil branches were dead defensive code (AGENTS.md guidance: "trust internal code and framework guarantees; only validate at system boundaries"). Pre-seed all six ivars to empty frozen containers in their `#initialize` methods and drop the seven defensive guards. Runtime behaviour unchanged; downstream type checkers (including Rigor itself) can now narrow each ivar to its concrete type without splitting on an unreachable nil branch. `make verify` clean (3741 examples / 0 failures, 598 lint files clean, rigor self-check still at the three queued loop-mutation warnings).
- **Self-analysis cleanup — `rigor check lib` self-check warnings 6 → 3.** Driven by running Rigor over its own `lib/` and triaging every diagnostic surfaced. Two underlying issues fixed (the third — a loop-mutation tracking gap for `arr = [seed]; while ...; arr << x; end` size/empty narrowing — stays queued because it needs an engine-level `Inference::Narrowing` mutation-effect-model change). **(a)** `def.ivar-write-mismatch` rule now normalises `TrueClass` / `FalseClass` to a single `"bool"` token in `CheckRules#ivar_class_for`. The previous shape fired on the canonical Ruby idiom of a boolean memoization flag (`@loaded = false` in `initialize`, `@loaded = true` on first work) because the two writes resolved to distinct class names; the new behaviour treats them as the same conceptual type. A real `bool → String` drift still fires (`"bool" ≠ "String"`). Spec coverage: +2 cases (bool flag idiom does not fire / bool→String drift still fires with the "bool" label in the message); 6 → 8 ivar-write-mismatch cases. **(b)** `Rigor::Environment::RbsLoader`'s `@state` memo bucket now initialises via `Hash.new` instead of a `{ env: nil, builder: nil }` literal. The literal previously gave Rigor a fixed-key `HashShape` to narrow against; subsequent slot reads against later-added keys (`:env_loaded`, `:env_build_warned`) folded to a constant `nil`, producing `flow.always-truthy-condition` warnings on the loader's own self-check. The loader instance is `.freeze`d on `RbsLoader.default` per ADR-15's reflection-facade contract, so the inner Hash must stay mutable (individual ivars don't work — they hit `FrozenError` on first write); `Hash.new` keeps the mutable-inner / frozen-outer contract AND denies Rigor a HashShape to narrow against. Closes the 2 `rbs_loader.rb:536,546` warnings; the loader's runtime behaviour is bit-for-bit unchanged.
- **[ADR-20](docs/adr/20-lightweight-hkt.md) `METHOD_RETURN_OVERRIDES` expansion — `JSON.load_file` / `JSON.load_file!` now also return the `json::value[K]` recursive union.** Stdlib's `JSON.load_file(path, opts={})` and `JSON.load_file!(path, opts={})` (added in Ruby 3.1 / `json` 2.6) read a file and `JSON.parse` its contents, so they share `JSON.parse`'s result shape. Upstream rbs declares both as `(string, ?options) -> untyped`; the HKT-builtin tier now tightens to the same `json::value[String]` envelope that backs `JSON.parse` / `JSON.load`. The `?options` slot carries the same `symbolize_names:` discriminator behaviour — `JSON.load_file("data.json", symbolize_names: true)` resolves to `Hash[Symbol, json::value[Symbol]]` instead of the default `Hash[String, json::value[String]]`. Spec coverage: +4 cases (`load_file` / `load_file!` fire / shape includes JSON leaf atoms / `symbolize_names: true` discriminator path). Total HKT spec count: 185.
- **[ADR-20](docs/adr/20-lightweight-hkt.md) `CSV.parse` / `CSV.read` overrides + JSON body author through the parser + parser arg-list accepts unions.** Two related improvements: **(a)** `CSV.parse(str)` / `CSV.read(path)` now return `Array[Array[String | nil]]` instead of the upstream `Array[Array[untyped]]?` shape. New `csv::parsed[K]` HKT (body: `Array[Array[K | nil]]`) covers the no-headers shape; `K = String` matches the default call. `headers: true` calls fall through to the upstream RBS — `CSV::Table` / `CSV::Row` is a separate URI that a future slice may add when concrete demand surfaces. CLI verification: `rigor type-of /tmp/csv_demo.rb:2:1` on `rows = CSV.parse("a,b,c\n1,2,3\n")` shows the precise `Array[Array[String | nil]]` shape. **(b)** `HktBuiltins.json_value_body_tree` is now built via `HktBodyParser.parse(...)` instead of programmatic node construction — the bundled overlay exercises the same authoring surface third-party plugins use. **(c)** Parser bug fix: `parse_arg_list` now uses `parse_union` so per-arg `A | B` forms work (`Array[K | nil]` reads as one Array arg whose value is a `K | nil` union, not as a syntax error). `Hash[K, V]` still parses as two args because the COMMA at the top level separates args — each arg is a single-arm union that collapses to the arm. **(d)** `HktBuiltins.registry` no longer memoises through a module-level `@registry` ivar — `Ractor::IsolationError` was surfacing in pool workers reading the ivar from a non-main Ractor. Fresh-allocation per Environment is cheap enough; an eager-frozen constant is a future optimisation once ADR-15 phase 4b.x covers the full dependency graph. Spec coverage: +2 dispatcher cases (CSV.parse shape / CSV.read parity); existing JSON / YAML CLI regressions still clean. Total HKT spec count: 181. Full make verify (3736 examples / 598 lint files / rigor check) pass cleanly.
- **[ADR-20](docs/adr/20-lightweight-hkt.md) § D3 `in [...]` membership in body parser — grammar complete.** Symmetrically extends the parser with the membership form `(left in [opt1, opt2, ...] ? then : else)`, parsing to the `HktBody::TestMembership` node the reducer already understood. `in` is disambiguated from a lowercase atom by position — only valid as a test operator inside a conditional. Spec coverage: +3 cases (multi-option / single-option / bare lowercase identifier other than `in` rejected). Total HKT spec count: 179. ADR-20 § D3 grammar (subtype + equality + membership conditional + reducer trinary evaluation + parser surface) is now complete end-to-end at the body level.
- **[ADR-20](docs/adr/20-lightweight-hkt.md) § D3 conditional syntax in the body-string parser.** `HktBodyParser` now recognises the `(test ? then_branch : else_branch)` form so `%a{rigor:v1:hkt_define: ...body=(K <: String ? Integer : Float)}` directives author conditional types directly in `.rbs` — closing the loop on the conditional body forms ADR-20 § D3 specifies. Tokenizer gains six new tokens: `:lparen` / `:rparen` / `:question` / `:colon` (`:` standalone, distinguished from `:sep` aka `::` via lookahead) / `:sub` (`<:`) / `:eq` (`==`). Grammar: `conditional := "(" test "?" union ":" union ")"`; `test := type_expr ("<:" | "==") type_expr`. Branches accept unions; test sides are single arms (wrap in `App[my_union, ...]` if you need a union there). Nested conditionals work end-to-end. `in [opt1, opt2]` membership tests are programmatically supported via `HktBody::TestMembership` but the parser does not yet recognise the `in` keyword (no concrete demand yet — follow-up if a user files for it). Spec coverage: +10 parser integration cases (basic subtype / equality / nested conditional / unions in branches / NominalApp on test side / AppRef in branch / missing operator / missing `?` / missing `:` / unclosed paren) + end-to-end reduction verifying the parser-built tree reduces correctly. Total HKT spec count: 176.
- **[ADR-20](docs/adr/20-lightweight-hkt.md) § D3 conditional body forms (`Conditional` + `TestSubtype` / `TestEquality` / `TestMembership` body nodes + reducer evaluation).** Promotes ADR-20 § D3 from "deferred to a follow-up slice" to programmatically usable: body trees can now branch on the bound type. Four new `HktBody` node types: `Conditional(test:, then_branch:, else_branch:)` plus three `Test*` value objects for the supported predicates. The reducer evaluates each test against the current bindings and picks a branch via trinary verdict — `:yes` (`then_branch`), `:no` (`else_branch`), `:maybe` (widen to the union of both, per ADR-20 WD7 / robustness principle). Verdict policy at this slice: structural equality → `:yes`; disjoint nominals / disjoint constants → `:no`; everything else (Dynamic[T] on either side, unrelated carrier shapes) → `:maybe`. **Body-string parser extension is a separate follow-up slice** — at this slice authors construct conditional bodies programmatically through `Builtins::HktBuiltins`-style code, but the `%a{rigor:v1:hkt_define: ...body=...}` directive cannot yet write `E <: T ? A : B` directly (the body parser ignores the `?:` syntax). Spec coverage: +7 reducer integration cases (TestSubtype yes/no / TestEquality yes/no / TestMembership yes/no / undecided → maybe → union widening) + +9 HktBody value-object validation cases for the four new node types. Total HKT spec count: 166.
- **[ADR-20](docs/adr/20-lightweight-hkt.md) slice 6 — plugin-manifest-declared HKT registrations + definitions.** Plugin authors can now ship Lightweight HKT URIs directly through their manifest, instead of (or alongside) a separate `.rbs` overlay. Two new `Rigor::Plugin::Manifest` fields: `hkt_registrations:` (Array of `Rigor::Inference::HktRegistry::Registration` value objects) and `hkt_definitions:` (Array of `Definition` value objects — typically built via `HktRegistry.definition_with_body_tree(...)` so plugin authors can construct body trees programmatically via the `HktBody` node API without parsing a string). `Plugin::Registry#hkt_overlay_registry` aggregates every loaded plugin's manifest entries into one `HktRegistry` overlay. `Environment#hkt_registry` (the lazy getter from slice 2c) merges in this order: bundled `Builtins::HktBuiltins.registry` → plugin overlay → RBS env scan. Last-write-wins on URI collisions, so user `.rbs` overlays beat plugin entries, which beat the bundled JSON_VALUE. Integration spec: a fake plugin declaring `hkt_registrations: [...]` + `hkt_definitions: [...]` shows up in `env.hkt_registry`, reduces end-to-end alongside the bundled JSON_VALUE without collision. Public-API drift snapshots updated for the two new Manifest attr_readers + `Plugin::Registry#hkt_overlay_registry`. Total HKT spec count: 150.
- **[ADR-20](docs/adr/20-lightweight-hkt.md) `:yaml_permitted_classes` post-reduce hook + lazy `Environment#hkt_registry`.** Two related improvements: **(a)** `YAML.safe_load(yaml, permitted_classes: [Date, Symbol])` now unions each literal-Array permitted class into the result. The new `:post_reduce` slot on `METHOD_RETURN_OVERRIDES` entries runs after reduction and can augment the result based on `arg_types`; today's `:yaml_permitted_classes` discriminator parses the call's literal `permitted_classes: [...]` Array / Tuple for Singleton-class elements, maps each to a `Nominal[class_name]`, and unions them with the reduced `json::value` Union. Non-literal `permitted_classes:` values (a variable, a Dynamic, a non-Singleton element) silently no-op. CLI verification: `rigor type-of /tmp/yaml_date_demo.rb:3:1` on `parsed = YAML.safe_load(yaml, permitted_classes: [Date])` shows `Array[json::value[String]] | Date | Float | … | Hash[String, json::value[String]] | …`; `[Date, Symbol]` adds `Symbol` too. **(b)** `Environment#hkt_registry` is now a lazy memoized getter (via `HktRegistryHolder` mutable slot) instead of an eager attr_reader populated at `for_project` time. The slice-2e RBS scan runs on FIRST `hkt_registry` access, not at Environment construction. Fast paths that never consult HKT (e.g. `rigor check --cache-stats --no-stats`) no longer pay the eager RBS env build cost. Lazy-only behaviour also fixes the Ractor-pool worker IsolationError that surfaced when workers triggered eager env build through scan_rbs_loader in non-main Ractors. Defence in depth: `VENDORED_GEM_SIGS_ROOT` constant is now `.freeze`d so reading it from a non-main Ractor is safe regardless of when scan happens. Spec coverage: +7 `:yaml_permitted_classes` integration cases (single permitted class / multi-class / empty permitted / non-literal no-op / Psych.safe_load parity / JSON.parse explicitly NOT augmented). Total HKT spec count: 149.
- **[ADR-20](docs/adr/20-lightweight-hkt.md) slice 2e — `Environment.for_project` scans the loaded RBS env for HKT annotations.** User-authored `.rbs` overlays (e.g. `%a{rigor:v1:hkt_register: uri=user::box arity=1 variance=out bound=untyped}` + `%a{rigor:v1:hkt_define: uri=user::box params=K body=K | nil}` on a class/module declaration) now flow into `env.hkt_registry` and become visible to the reducer at call sites — previously only the bundled `Builtins::HktBuiltins.registry` (JSON_VALUE seed) was live. Three new pieces wired the loop: (1) `RbsLoader#each_class_decl_annotation` — a public, fail-soft iterator that walks every class/module declaration's `%a{...}` annotations in the loaded RBS env, yielding `(annotation_string, source_location)` pairs. (2) `HktRegistry.scan_rbs_loader(rbs_loader, base:, name_scope:, reporter:)` — class method that drives the iterator through `HktDirectives.parse_register` / `parse_define` and returns a new registry that merges parsed entries on top of `base` (last-write-wins on URI collisions per ADR-20 OQ3 tentative — user overlays beat the bundled JSON_VALUE if they re-register the same URI). (3) `Environment.for_project` now seeds `hkt_registry: scan_rbs_loader(loader, base: Builtins::HktBuiltins.registry)` so every analyzer instance carries both the builtins AND user-authored overlays. The scan triggers an eager RBS env build (otherwise lazy), bringing forward an unavoidable cost rather than adding net work. Spec coverage: +4 integration cases in `environment_spec.rb` (bundled JSON_VALUE present; user `:"user::box"` URI registered + defined from a fixture overlay; end-to-end reduction `App[user::box, [String]]` → `Union[String, Constant<nil>]`; bundled + user URIs coexist without collision). Total HKT spec count: 142.
- **[ADR-20](docs/adr/20-lightweight-hkt.md) `HktDirectives` payload format refactor (JSON-flow → kv-form) — bug fix.** Slice 1's directive parser accepted a JSON-flow payload (`{"uri": "json::value", "arity": 1, ...}`), but **RBS's `%a{...}` annotation grammar rejects payloads containing `"` quotes**, so the JSON form would never have reached the parser from a real `.rbs` file. The parser is refactored to accept the kv-form actually compatible with RBS: `%a{rigor:v1:hkt_register: uri=json::value arity=1 variance=out,out bound=untyped}` and `%a{rigor:v1:hkt_define: uri=json::value params=K body=nil | true | …}`. The `body=` key is special-cased to gobble everything from `body=` to the end of the payload, so the body String can contain spaces, `|`, `[]` etc. without quoting. Per-key parsers: `arity` is a positive Integer; `variance` is a comma-separated list (`out,in,inv`); `params` is a comma-separated UCName list; `bound` accepts `untyped` (→ `Dynamic[Top]`) or a bare class name (→ `Nominal`). Fail-soft on every error (missing key, malformed value, unrecognised bound) — emit an `:info` reporter entry and drop the directive. **Verified via raw `RBS::EnvironmentLoader`**: the new kv-form annotations parse cleanly when attached to a class/module declaration in an actual `.rbs` file; the JSON-flow form raised `RBS::ParsingError`. The remaining wiring (`Environment.for_project` walking the loaded RBS env's class/module decls and merging hkt annotations into the registry) ships in a follow-up slice — until then the `Builtins::HktBuiltins.registry` seed (JSON_VALUE) is the only registry source. Spec coverage: rewrote 22 `HktDirectives` spec cases + 2 directive-integration cases in `HktBodyParser` spec to the kv-form. Total HKT spec count unchanged at 138.
- **[ADR-20](docs/adr/20-lightweight-hkt.md) `METHOD_RETURN_OVERRIDES` expansion — `YAML.safe_load` / `YAML.safe_load_file` / `Psych.safe_load` / `Psych.safe_load_file` now also return the `json::value[String]` recursive union.** Default `permitted_classes: []` admits exactly the JSON vocabulary (nil / true / false / Integer / Float / String / Array / Hash), so the same Lightweight HKT registration that backs `JSON.parse` also describes these methods. Non-default `permitted_classes` broadens the runtime answer; the analyzer reports the json::value envelope and users opt into per-method RBS overrides when they configure custom permits. `YAML.load` / `YAML.unsafe_load` deliberately stay out of the override table (can return ANY Ruby object — no useful HKT envelope). CLI demonstration: `rigor type-of /tmp/yaml_demo.rb:2:1` on `parsed = YAML.safe_load("foo: 1")` shows the same `Array[json::value[String]] | … | Hash[String, json::value[String]] | …` Union as JSON.parse. Spec coverage: +4 cases (YAML.safe_load / YAML.safe_load_file / Psych.safe_load fire; YAML.load deliberately does NOT fire). Total HKT spec count: 138.
- **[ADR-20](docs/adr/20-lightweight-hkt.md) slice 2b — body-string grammar parser (`HktBodyParser`).** Plugin / Rigor-bundled overlay authors can now write HKT bodies as plain strings in their `.rbs` files via `%a{rigor:v1:hkt_define: ...body: "nil | bool | ..."}` instead of having to ship Ruby code that builds the body tree programmatically. The minimum-viable grammar covered (sufficient for `JSON.parse`'s `json::value` recursive sum and similar recursive-data-shape signatures) is **union + atoms + nominal_app + app_ref + param**: `body := union; union := type_expr ("|" type_expr)*; type_expr := atom | param | nominal_app | app_ref; atom := "nil" | "true" | "false" | "bool" | "untyped"; param := UCNAME (when ∈ params); nominal_app := class_name ("[" type_expr ("," type_expr)* "]")?; app_ref := "App" "[" uri "," type_expr ("," type_expr)* "]"`. The conditional / indexed-access forms (`E <: T ? A : B`, `E in [k1, k2]`) drafted in ADR-20 § D3 remain a follow-up slice. `HktBodyParser.parse(string, params:)` returns an `HktBody::*` tree; the same JSON_VALUE body that `Builtins::HktBuiltins.json_value_body_tree` builds programmatically can be expressed as `"nil | true | false | Integer | Float | String | Array[App[json::value, K]] | Hash[K, App[json::value, K]]"` and reduces to the equivalent Union (verified end-to-end via a reducer-equivalence spec). `RbsExtended::HktDirectives.parse_define` now calls the parser automatically, populating `Definition#body_tree` from the body String; parse failures fail-soft to `body_tree: nil` plus an `:info` reporter entry so the registration survives even when the body grammar isn't recognised. Disambiguation: a UCName matching one of the declared params (`K` when `params: [:K]`) becomes a `Param` node, but only when NOT followed by `::` (qualified class) or `[` (parameterised app) — those continuations force the nominal interpretation. Spec coverage: 33 new examples across `spec/rigor/inference/hkt_body_parser_spec.rb` (atoms / params / nominal_app / app_ref / union / JSON_VALUE end-to-end reducer-equivalence / error cases / directive integration). Total HKT spec count: **134 examples**.
- **[ADR-20](docs/adr/20-lightweight-hkt.md) slice 3 follow-up — `JSON.parse(s, symbolize_names: true)` discriminates `K = Symbol`.** Extends `Builtins::HktBuiltins.method_return_override` with a `:discriminator` keyword on each `METHOD_RETURN_OVERRIDES` entry that consults the call's `arg_types` to swap the default args for an alternate before constructing the App. Ships one built-in discriminator (`:json_symbolize_names`) that observes the optional 2nd argument's `Type::HashShape` for a literal `symbolize_names: true` entry; matching swaps `K = String` for `K = Symbol`. The dispatcher's `try_hkt_builtin_return` tier now threads `arg_types` through to the override resolver. CLI demonstration: `rigor type-of /tmp/json_sym_demo.rb:1:1` on `parsed = JSON.parse(str, symbolize_names: true)` shows `Hash[Symbol, json::value[Symbol]]` instead of the default `Hash[String, json::value[String]]`. Spec coverage: +3 cases (symbolize_names true → Symbol / false → String / absent → String).
- **[ADR-20](docs/adr/20-lightweight-hkt.md) slices 2c + 2d + 3 — `JSON.parse` now returns the recursive `json::value` union instead of `untyped`.** End-to-end landing of the ADR's headline goal: `JSON.parse("...")` typed through the inference engine now resolves to `Union[nil, true, false, Integer, Float, String, Array[App[json::value, String]], Hash[String, App[json::value, String]]]` rather than `Dynamic[Top]`. Four pieces ship together: (1) **Slice 2c — `Environment#hkt_registry`.** New attr_reader threading a frozen `Rigor::Inference::HktRegistry` through every analyzer call. `Environment.default` and `Environment.for_project` seed it from a new `Rigor::Builtins::HktBuiltins.registry` module that pre-builds the `json::value[K]` registration + recursive body tree (six leaf atoms + lazy `Array[App[json::value, K]]` / `Hash[K, App[json::value, K]]` arms via `HktBody::AppRef`). `Environment.new` without explicit `hkt_registry:` defaults to `HktRegistry::EMPTY` so existing test constructions stay green. (2) **Slice 2d — `App[<uri>, <ClassName>, ...]` syntax in `%a{rigor:v1:return:}` payloads.** Extends `RbsExtended.parse_return_type_override` with a new `APP_PAYLOAD_PATTERN` regex that matches the App syntax before falling through to the refinement-name parser; resolves bare class-name args through the env's `name_scope`, builds a `Type::App`, and eagerly reduces it via `environment.hkt_registry.reduce(app)` so call sites observe the unfolded form. (3) **Slice 3 — `Rigor::Builtins::HktBuiltins::METHOD_RETURN_OVERRIDES` table + `try_hkt_builtin_return` dispatcher tier.** Hardcoded `(class_name, method_name, kind) => {uri:, args:}` map covering `JSON.parse` / `JSON.parse!` / `JSON.load` (singleton); the new dispatcher tier sits ABOVE `RbsDispatch.try_dispatch` so these methods bypass the upstream rbs gem's `untyped` slot. RBS-overlay-annotation merging was investigated and rejected for this slice (RBS does not propagate `%a{...}` annotations from extension-form `def m: ...` declarations onto the resolved `RBS::Definition::Method`); the hardcoded table is the pragmatic shortcut while the annotation-based path stays the general extension surface for user-authored sigs. (4) **End-to-end integration spec** (9 cases): `JSON.parse(String)` returns a Union, the union contains the leaf JSON atoms, the Array / Hash arms carry the lazy self-recursive App carriers, `K = Symbol` substitution distinct from `K = String` works, `JSON.parse!` / `JSON.load` also fire, the override only fires on Singleton receivers (not on JSON instance methods), and unrelated singletons like `YAML.parse` are unaffected. Public-API drift snapshot updated for the new `Environment#hkt_registry` attr_reader; corresponding RBS sig (`sig/rigor/environment.rbs`) gains the matching declaration so `make verify` stays clean. Full test (3653 examples) + lint + check pass cleanly.
- **[ADR-20](docs/adr/20-lightweight-hkt.md) slice 2a — HKT reducer (body-tree representation + lazy self-recursion + fuel budget).** Slice 1 shipped the opaque carrier; slice 2a now reduces `Type::App[F, A]` against a registered definition body. Three new pieces: (1) **`Rigor::Inference::HktBody`** — five `Data.define` node types (`TypeLeaf` / `Param` / `AppRef` / `Union` / `NominalApp`) that plugin and Rigor-bundled overlay authors compose into a programmatic body tree. The string-grammar parser that reads `Definition#body` into a tree is deferred to slice 2b — until it ships, `body_tree` is the evaluable form. (2) **`Rigor::Inference::HktReducer`** — walks the body tree against an `App`'s concrete args per ADR-20 § D4: substitutes `Param` nodes from the application's positional args, builds normalised types through `Type::Combinator.{union, nominal_of}`, and recurses on `AppRef` nodes. Cycle detection uses a per-call in-progress stack keyed on `(uri, reduced_args)` — same-key recursion returns the in-progress `Type::App` carrier as-is (lazy "tying the knot" handling that lets recursive sums like `json::value[K] = nil | bool | … | Array[App[json::value, K]] | Hash[K, App[json::value, K]]` terminate). Fuel budget defaults to 64 reduction steps per WD3; exhaustion unwinds (via a private `FuelExhausted` rescue) to `app.bound`. (3) **`Rigor::Inference::HktRegistry::Definition#body_tree`** — new optional slot alongside the slice-1 `body` String; `HktRegistry.definition_with_body_tree(uri:, params:, body_tree:)` factory constructs a Definition with the tree populated and an empty `body` placeholder. **`HktRegistry#reduce(app, fuel:)`** + **`Type::App#reduce(registry, fuel:)`** are convenience wrappers that allocate a fresh reducer per call (concurrent reductions are safe). **No call-site wiring yet** — the reducer is a pure mechanism; an integration point that calls `app.reduce(env.hkt_registry)` at dispatch / acceptance time ships in a later slice. Spec coverage: 33 new examples across `spec/rigor/inference/hkt_body_spec.rb` (14 — value-object validation for all five node types) + `spec/rigor/inference/hkt_reducer_spec.rb` (19 — unknown URI / body_tree absent / Param substitution / Union normalisation / NominalApp parameterisation / JSON-shaped recursive body terminates / leaf atoms / Array arm lazy self-ref / Hash arm lazy self-ref / `K=Symbol` distinct from `K=String` / fuel exhaustion → bound / fuel headroom / arity mismatch / cross-URI inline reduction / `HktRegistry#reduce` shortcut / `Type::App#reduce` shortcut / explicit fuel kwarg). Total HKT spec count: **89 examples** (56 slice 1 + 33 slice 2a). Full test (3644 examples) + lint + check pass cleanly.
- **[ADR-20](docs/adr/20-lightweight-hkt.md) slice 1 — Lightweight HKT carrier + registry + parser (no reduction yet).** First implementation slice of the Lightweight HKT mechanism proposed in ADR-20 (defunctionalised tag + `App[F, A]` carrier per Yallop & White 2014 / fp-ts `URItoKind`). Ships three independently-testable pieces: (1) **`Rigor::Type::App`** — opaque carrier holding `(uri, args, bound)` where `uri` is a namespaced Symbol (`:author::name`, ADR-20 WD1), `args` is a non-empty frozen Array of `Rigor::Type` values, and `bound` is the type to erase to when reduction is blocked (defaults to `Dynamic[Top]` per WD2). Display form follows OQ5: bare RBS-style `uri[arg1, arg2]`. Lattice probes (`top`/`bot`/`dynamic`) and `accepts` delegate to `bound` since no evaluator exists yet — Slice 2 introduces the conditional / indexed-access evaluator that reduces `App` to its registered body. (2) **`Rigor::Inference::HktRegistry`** — frozen registry mapping URIs to `Registration` (arity / variance / bound) and `Definition` (params / body / source attribution) value objects. `#merge` produces a new registry with last-write-wins on URI collisions (OQ3 tentative). (3) **`Rigor::RbsExtended::HktDirectives`** — pure parser functions `parse_register` / `parse_define` that accept the canonical `%a{rigor:v1:hkt_register: <JSON-flow payload>}` and `%a{rigor:v1:hkt_define: <JSON-flow payload>}` annotation strings and emit `Registration` / `Definition` instances. JSON payload form keeps the single-line discipline RBS annotations work best with while avoiding YAML's aliasing surprises. Bound vocabulary is intentionally narrow at this slice — only `"untyped"` (→ `Dynamic[Top]`) and bare class names (→ `Nominal`); richer bounds (generics, unions, refinements) wait for Slice 2's expression parser. Fail-soft on payload errors: every validation failure surfaces an `:info`-shaped reporter entry and drops the registration rather than crashing the loader. **No call-site wiring yet** — the registry is not yet populated from an `Environment.for_project` walk; that integration ships at the Slice 2 boundary when the evaluator needs it. Spec coverage: 56 new examples across three spec files (15 carrier — construction validation / freezing / `describe` + `erase_to_rbs` / lattice delegation / structural equality / `inspect`; 19 registry — `Registration` + `Definition` validation / indexing / `merge` collision policy / `EMPTY` constant; 22 parser — happy-path single-arg / multi-arg / variance default / bound default / class-name bound / wrapped `%a{...}` form / unrelated-directive skip / JSON error / missing-uri / un-namespaced-uri / non-positive-arity / variance mismatch / unrecognised-bound fallback / `parse_define` happy path / empty params / unrelated-directive skip / missing-body / non-Array-params).
- **`plugins/rigor-dry-validation/` slice 1 — `Dry::Validation::Contract` recognition + RBS overlay.** Per [the dry-validation slicing plan](docs/design/20260517-dry-validation-slicing.md), `rigor-dry-validation` is the next dry-rb adapter beyond the foundation pair (`rigor-dry-types` + `rigor-dry-schema`). Slice 1 ships **two surfaces**: (1) walks the project for `class T < Dry::Validation::Contract` subclasses — accepts both the full-path `Dry::Validation::Contract` and the lexical-Dry-nested `Validation::Contract` (when the class body lives inside `module Dry`); the bare `< Contract` form is NOT recognised (too ambiguous); other same-tail roots (`< MyApp::Validation::Contract`) do NOT register. The contract FQN set publishes as the `:dry_validation_contracts` cross-plugin fact, sorted + frozen. (2) Ships an **RBS overlay** under `sig/dry_validation.rbs` typing `Dry::Validation::Contract#call` (returns `Result`) and `Result#{success?, failure?, to_h, errors, []}` so `contract.call(input).to_h` chains resolve to `Hash[Symbol, untyped]` (the generic shape — slice 2 refines per-Contract via `:dry_schema_table` integration). Users add `<gem-root>/sig` to their `.rigor.yml`'s `signature_paths:` to wire the overlay. **Examples count: twenty-eight → twenty-nine.** Spec coverage: 8 integration cases (manifest shape / full-path Contract recognition / lexical-Dry path / unrelated `Contract`-tail subclasses ignored / nested constant chain / sorted-frozen ordering guarantee / no-publish-when-absent / RBS overlay shipped).
- **`plugins/rigor-rails/` meta-gem scaffold — Tier 1+2 Rails ecosystem umbrella.** Per [ADR-12 WD1](docs/adr/12-dry-rb-packaging.md) ("Per-gem plugins + meta umbrella") and the Rails plugins roadmap, `rigor-rails` is the Gemfile-convenience meta-gem that pulls in the seven Tier 1+2 sub-plugins (`rigor-rails-routes` / `rigor-rails-i18n` / `rigor-actionmailer` / `rigor-activejob` / `rigor-activerecord` / `rigor-actionpack` / `rigor-factorybot`) through `add_dependency` declarations + a one-stop `require "rigor-rails"` entry point. **The umbrella is Gemfile-convenience ONLY**: per ADR-12 WD1 the Rigor plugin loader still walks `.rigor.yml`'s `plugins:` list and instantiates only the plugins enumerated there, so users mix-and-match the subset their project needs (a route-helper-light app can skip `rigor-rails-routes`, etc.). Tier 3 plugins (pundit / sidekiq / rspec / actioncable / activestorage / graphql) stay separate gems. Until the sub-plugins subtree-split + publish to RubyGems, the meta-gem's `add_dependency` declarations name gems that don't yet exist on RubyGems — installation in real projects requires `path:` overrides in the `Gemfile`; the example directory is the publication-ready template. **Examples count: twenty-seven → twenty-eight** (twenty-six worked plugins + one RBS-only bundle + one meta-gem). Spec coverage: 4 integration cases (gemspec name / seven dependency declarations / gemspec ↔ `lib/rigor-rails.rb` regression guard / requiring the meta-gem registers all seven sub-plugins).
- **`plugins/rigor-graphql/` slice 2c + 2d — `Schema::InputObject` + `Schema::Mutation` recognition.** Extends the AST walk to also collect `class T < GraphQL::Schema::InputObject` (publishes `:graphql_input_object_table` fact) and `class T < GraphQL::Schema::Mutation` (publishes `:graphql_mutation_table`). The `argument :name, Type, required: ...` DSL parses with the same shape as `field` but uses `required:` (default `false`) instead of `null:` (default `true`) — `required: true` is `{required: true, ...}`. Mutation rows carry both `arguments:` and `fields:` sub-tables; their value shapes are symmetric with the standalone InputObject argument table and Object field table so consumers can treat them uniformly. New helper `collect_arguments` reusable across InputObject + Mutation. `manifest(produces:)` now advertises all four facts (`:graphql_type_table`, `:graphql_enum_table`, `:graphql_input_object_table`, `:graphql_mutation_table`); each is published independently — the plugin's per-fact `publish_if_present` skips empty tables. Spec coverage: +12 cases (6 InputObject — arg table / list args / user-defined types / `required:` default / nested constant chain / no-publish-when-absent; 5 Mutation — arguments+fields / arguments-only / nested chain / four-kind mixed project / no-publish-when-absent; +1 manifest assertion expansion).
- **`plugins/rigor-dry-schema/` slice 2 — `each(<Type>)` list recognition + symmetric `list:` slot.** Per-row value shape in `:dry_schema_table` changes from a bare underlying-class string to a `{type: String, list: Boolean}` tuple. The `each` predicate verb (the only one that semantically denotes a collection in dry-schema's vocabulary) sets `list: true`; the other three type-bearing predicates (`filled` / `value` / `maybe`) yield `list: false`. The new slot is symmetric with `rigor-graphql`'s field-table shape so future cross-plugin consumers (e.g. `rigor-dry-validation`) can reason about list-vs-scalar fields uniformly. **Breaking** for any downstream consumer of `:dry_schema_table` (none exist today — the fact was published in slice 1 with no live consumers yet). Spec coverage: +1 case (`each(:string)` marks list, `value(:array)` does NOT — single Array scalar vs list-of-element disambiguation); 7 existing slice-1 cases updated for the new tuple shape.
- **`plugins/rigor-graphql/` slice 2b — `Schema::Enum` recognition + `:graphql_enum_table` fact.** Extends the AST walk to also collect `class T < GraphQL::Schema::Enum` subclasses (same lexical-nested + fully-qualified superclass match as Object). Inside each enum body, every `value "STRING"` declaration registers the literal name in a per-enum value list. The plugin now publishes BOTH `:graphql_type_table` (slice 1) AND `:graphql_enum_table` from one project walk — `manifest(produces:)` updated to advertise both. Recognition floor: only literal-String first arguments register; symbol-form (`value :ACTIVE`) and constant-form (`value SOME_CONSTANT`) drop silently. The `value:` Ruby-side override and `description:` kwargs ride along but stay out of the published table at this slice. Spec coverage: +6 cases (single enum / nested enum / non-string arg dropped / additional kwargs preserved / mixed project publishes both facts / no enum present → no enum-table publish).
- **`plugins/rigor-graphql/` slice 2a — list wrapper recognition.** Extends slice 1 with `field :tags, [String]` / `field :authors, [Types::Author]` shape recognition. Single-element `Prism::ArrayNode` forms unwrap and set `list: true` on the published field row (`{type: "String", nullable: false, list: true}`); the new `list:` slot is present on every row (`list: false` for non-list fields). Multi-element list literals (`[String, Integer]`) and empty lists (`[]`) silently drop — not valid GraphQL list type expressions. The `<Type>.array` / `<Type>!` chain forms graphql-ruby also accepts stay deferred. Spec coverage: +3 list-wrapper cases (scalar list / user-defined-type list / invalid list shapes ignored).
- **`plugins/rigor-graphql/` slice 1 — `GraphQL::Schema::Object` field recognition + `:graphql_type_table` fact publication.** Tier 3D per the Rails plugins roadmap; closes the last pending Tier 3 entry. The macro-expansion library survey at [`docs/notes/20260515-macro-expansion-library-survey.md`](docs/notes/20260515-macro-expansion-library-survey.md) § "GraphQL-Ruby" documents why graphql-ruby is NOT an ADR-16 substrate consumer (the `field` DSL is a pure metadata recorder — it doesn't emit Ruby methods, so there is no synthesisable method to expand). The plugin therefore ships as a STATIC TYPE TABLE producer rather than a method synthesiser. **What slice 1 does**: walks every `paths:` `.rb` file during `prepare(services)` for `class T < GraphQL::Schema::Object` subclasses (top-level and nested module chains: `module Types; class User < ...`). Inside each subclass, extracts `field :name, Type, null: ...` declarations where `Type` is a constant reference (`String`, `Integer`, `Boolean`, `Float`, `ID`, or a user-defined `Types::*`). Maps the canonical GraphQL scalar names to underlying Ruby classes via a `CANONICAL_TYPES` table; user-defined types preserved as qualified names so downstream consumers can resolve them. Nullability follows graphql-ruby's own default: `null: true` unless explicitly negated. Publishes the per-type field map as the `:graphql_type_table` ADR-9 cross-plugin fact: `{type_class_fqn => {field_name => {type:, nullable:}}}`. **Recognition tightness**: subclass match checks the last two segments of the superclass chain (`Schema::Object` tail), so `< GraphQL::Schema::Object` (fully qualified) AND `< Schema::Object` (lexically inside `module GraphQL`) both register; unrelated `< Container::Object` parents do NOT. **Examples count: twenty-six → twenty-seven.** Spec coverage: 10 integration cases (manifest shape / full scalar vocabulary / nested constant chain / user-defined types / nullability default / non-symbol field name ignored / string-form type ignored / lexical-nested superclass / no-publish when absent / unrelated `Object`-tail parent ignored).
- **`plugins/rigor-dry-schema/` slice 1 — dry-schema declaration recognition + `:dry_schema_table` fact publication.** Per [the dry-validation slicing plan](docs/design/20260517-dry-validation-slicing.md), `rigor-dry-schema` is the next dry-rb adapter in the foundation pair: `rigor-dry-types` publishes the alias table; `rigor-dry-schema` publishes the schema table; `rigor-dry-validation` (future) consumes both. Slice 1 ships **fact-publication only**: the plugin walks every `paths:` `.rb` file during `prepare(services)` for `Foo = Dry::Schema.{Params,JSON,define} { ... }` assignments (top-level OR class-level, with the enclosing constant chain prefixed: `"App::Schemas::UserCreate"`). Inside each block it extracts `required(:key).<predicate>(<arg>)` / `optional(:key).<predicate>(<arg>)` rows where the predicate verb is one of `filled` / `value` / `maybe` / `each`. Predicate arguments map to underlying classes via a CANONICAL_TYPES vocabulary (12 entries: `:string` → `String`, `:integer` → `Integer`, …); constant arguments (`value(Types::Email)`) resolve through the `:dry_type_aliases` fact when `rigor-dry-types` is loaded — unresolvable references silently drop. The published table shape is `{schema_const_fqn => {required: {key => underlying_class}, optional: {…}}}`. **Examples count: twenty-five → twenty-six.** Spec coverage: 9 integration cases (manifest shape / Params declaration / JSON + define entries / class-level constant chain / full canonical vocabulary / unknown-type drop / `:dry_type_aliases` cross-plugin resolution / unresolvable reference drop / absent-declaration no-publish).
- **`rigor-dry-types` slice 4 — transitive composition reference resolution.** Extends slice 3 with a two-pass walk inside `AliasScanner#collect_compositions`: pass 1 collects direct compositions (RHS rooted on a canonical name); pass 2 resolves remaining `ConstantWriteNode`s whose RHS targets another local composition (`ManagerEmail = Email`, `ChainedEmail = Email.constrained(...)`). Multi-level chains resolve through the table (`SeniorManagerEmail = ManagerEmail = Email → String`). Cycles silently drop (`A = B; B = A` publishes neither — `resolve_transitive_ref` carries a `visited:` set and bails on re-entry). Unknown references (`DanglingAlias = NotAComposition`) silently drop. The `Types::ManagerEmail`-shape pattern is now resolved end-to-end into the published `:dry_type_aliases` fact, completing the dry-types alias coverage at the WD13 floor. Spec coverage: +3 integration cases (transitive 3-level resolution / dangling reference dropped / cycle dropped).
- **Design note — `rigor-dry-validation` slicing decision.** [`docs/design/20260517-dry-validation-slicing.md`](docs/design/20260517-dry-validation-slicing.md) records the dependency tree (`rigor-dry-schema` must land before `rigor-dry-validation` because the `params { ... }` block's typed payload routes through dry-schema), proposes a five-slice plan (schema-1 + schema-2+ + validation-1 + validation-2 + validation-3), and confirms NO ADR-3 amendment is needed for the validation surface itself. `rigor-dry-monads` is separately blocked on the `Result[T, E]` / `Maybe[T]` carrier question — two routes documented (new `Rigor::Type::*` kinds vs `Union`-based workaround), decision deferred until concrete demand. No code change; design-only.
- **LSP / editor mode — `Environment` sharing across per-buffer publishes (`Runner.new(environment:)` + `Environment#attach_reporters!`).** The LSP `ProjectContext` now caches the FULL `Environment` (plugin registry, dependency-source index, synthetic-method / project-patched indexes, AND every Bundler / RBS-collection axis Runner consults) and passes it to every per-publish `Runner.new` through a new `environment:` kwarg. Sequential `analyze_files` uses the supplied env instead of building a fresh one via `Environment.for_project` — saving the per-publish bundler discovery / lockfile parse / collection scan + `RbsLoader` construction (≈10-50 ms on real Rails projects with vendored gems). Pool mode unchanged (each worker still builds its own env). Per-publish reporter isolation handled via new `Environment#attach_reporters!`: reporters live in a mutable `Reporters` slot held by the otherwise-frozen Environment so the runner can stamp its per-call reporter pair onto the shared env at the start of every `Runner.run`. Diagnostic events stay scoped to a single publish and do NOT accumulate across publishes (the Bus Error concern that originally kept env LOCAL-scoped applied only to Ractor pool workers, not the LSP single-threaded path). Spec coverage: +4 `attach_reporters!` cases (slot replace / frozen-env callable / other-attr preserved / event scope); +3 Runner `environment:`-override integration cases (override skips `Environment.for_project` / reporters attach correctly / two sequential runs do not accumulate reporter events).
- **Plugin cache descriptor correctness fix — `actioncable` / `actionmailer` / `rails-i18n` now invalidate on project file changes.** Three plugins were caching their discovery producer (`:channel_index`, `:mailer_index`, `:locale_index`) against a descriptor that depended on the `IoBoundary`'s in-process read history — empty on the first call of a fresh process, so warm cache hits served stale producer output when project files had changed between sessions. The bug is invisible under `rigor check`'s default cache because the first run primes the cache with correct data; subsequent runs in the SAME process re-read the relevant files (boundary populated) so the descriptor catches changes. But any new process (`rigor check` shelled-out per editor save, parallel-test workers, second invocation after `bundle install`) starts with an empty boundary and would happily serve the prior session's cache regardless of project state. New `Plugin::Base#glob_descriptor(roots, *patterns)` helper builds a `Cache::Descriptor` listing every glob-matched file with `:digest` comparator entries — pass it through `cache_for(..., descriptor: …)` so the cache key reflects the project files the producer reads BEFORE the producer runs. Each affected plugin now calls the helper at its `cache_for` site: actioncable covers `app/channels/**/*.rb`, actionmailer covers `app/mailers/**/*.rb` + `app/views/**/*` (mailer + view templates), rails-i18n covers `config/locales/**/*.{yml,yaml}`. The three plugin specs that previously had to use `cache_store: nil` to avoid stale-cache surfaces now opt in to the shared cache via `let(:default_run_plugin_cache_store) { :shared }` and pass all 31 examples. Spec coverage: +6 `glob_descriptor` unit cases (digest entries / content change detection / add-remove detection / non-existent root tolerance / multi-pattern union / directory skip).
- **LSP / editor mode — project-context pre-pass snapshot cache (`Analysis::ProjectScan`).** New `Rigor::Analysis::ProjectScan` value object bundling the project-wide pre-pass outputs Runner consumes on every call: the plugin registry (post `#prepare`), the dependency-source index, the synthetic-method index, the project-patched-methods registry, and the diagnostics emitted by the prepare / pre-eval scanners. `Runner#prepare_project_scan(paths:)` builds the snapshot in one pass; `Runner.new(prebuilt: scan)` adopts a snapshot and skips the per-call pre-passes that would have built those slots. The LSP's `ProjectContext` now exposes `#project_scan` (lazy, memoised, dropped by `#invalidate!`), and `DiagnosticPublisher#run_analysis` threads the cached scan through every `Runner.new` it creates. Per-buffer publishes no longer re-run `Plugin::Loader.load`, plugin `#prepare`, `DependencySourceInference::Builder.build`, or the synthetic-method / project-patched scanners — the cold pre-pass cost is paid once per `ProjectContext` generation (i.e. once per session, plus once per `workspace/didChangeWatchedFiles` / `workspace/didChangeConfiguration`). For a 1-plugin warm-cache trivial project the win is small (1.8 ms → 1.6 ms per publish); the gap widens with substrate-using plugins (`rigor-devise`, `rigor-dry-struct`, `rigor-sinatra`), opt-in `dependencies.source_inference:`, or sizeable `pre_eval:` lists, where the cached `SyntheticMethodScanner` walk (now project-wide instead of buffer-only) replaces what was a per-publish scan. **Correctness bonus**: the cached scanner observes every project file rather than only the buffer's path, so synthetic methods declared in other files become visible during buffer analysis — closing a known gap in the slice-7 LSP design. Trade-off documented in `ProjectContext` rdoc: edits to a file that itself declares substrate DSL consumers stay invisible until `invalidate!` fires (typical editor flow: save → file watch → invalidate → next publish refreshes). Runtime API: `Runner.new(prebuilt:)` kwarg + `Runner#prepare_project_scan` defaulting to `@configuration.paths`. Spec coverage: 3 `project_scan_spec` unit cases (frozen / value-comparable / six readers); 4 new `runner_spec` integration cases (snapshot shape / adopt / `Plugin::Loader.load` skip via stub / no-prebuilt regression guard); 3 extended `project_context_spec` cases (lazy build / empty-state shape / invalidate drops scan).
- **LSP polish + new capabilities — multi-overload signatureHelp + RBS documentation + per-parameter info + hover range + foldingRange + selectionRange.** Six small slices extending the v2 LSP surface beyond the first follow-up cluster. Design pointers stay in [`docs/design/20260517-lsp-hover-completion.md`](docs/design/20260517-lsp-hover-completion.md). **(C2)** signatureHelp now emits one `SignatureInformation` per RBS overload (previously first overload only); composite-receiver coverage extended to match completion's slice B3 (Tuple → Array, HashShape → Hash, Refined / Difference → underlying nominal). **(C3)** hover + signatureHelp surface the method's RBS comments (`RBS::Definition::Method#comments`) as markdown: hover appends below the code block separated by a `---` rule; signatureHelp attaches per-`SignatureInformation` `documentation: { kind: "markdown", value: ... }`. The doc field is omitted (not nil) when no comments exist. **(C4)** `SignatureInformation.parameters` is now populated — one `ParameterInformation` per RBS parameter in canonical order (required positionals, optional positionals, rest, trailing, required keywords, optional keywords, rest keywords). Labels are string form (`"::int width"`, `"?::string pad_string"`); offset-tuple labels for in-signature highlighting stay queued. Combined with the C1 base, signatureHelp now ships the full v1 capability set. **(E1)** hover responses include the LSP `range` field carrying the source range of the hovered expression so editors highlight the exact node instead of a single-character cursor. **(F1)** new `textDocument/foldingRange` via `FoldingRangeProvider`: walks the Prism AST emitting one fold per multi-line class / module / singleton-class / def / block. `startLine` = opener line; `endLine` = line BEFORE `end` keyword so both visible parts of the construct stay shown in the collapsed view. Single-line constructs filtered out. **(G1)** new `textDocument/selectionRange` via `SelectionRangeProvider`: for each requested position, returns an outward-chained linked list of `SelectionRange` entries (innermost first, `parent` chained outward) so editors can expand selection one AST level per keystroke. Server gains two more advertised capabilities: `foldingRangeProvider: true`, `selectionRangeProvider: true`. Spec coverage: +12 cases across hover_renderer / signature_help_provider / folding_range_provider / selection_range_provider.
- **LSP follow-ups — `textDocument/signatureHelp` + hash-key completion + editor-integration guide + end-to-end spec.** Four queued follow-ups to LSP v2 land in a single follow-up cluster: (1) **`docs/lsp-integration.md`** — user-facing setup guide for Neovim (nvim-lspconfig) / VSCode (generic LSP client) / Helix (`languages.toml`) / Emacs (Eglot + lsp-mode) with troubleshooting + performance expectations sections. First user-facing LSP doc (prior docs were internal design notes). (2) **`spec/rigor/language_server/end_to_end_spec.rb`** — spawns the real `exe/rigor lsp` via `Open3.popen3`, runs a full LSP session (initialize → didOpen → hover → completion → shutdown → exit), asserts the capability set, hover markdown, completion catalog, and clean exit. Closes the regression-defence gap between the per-collaborator unit specs and the manual shell smoke scripts. (3) **`textDocument/signatureHelp`** — new `Rigor::LanguageServer::SignatureHelpProvider`. When the user types `obj.foo(` editors fire signatureHelp; the provider parses the buffer (with `__rigor_lsp_arg_sentinel__)` sentinel patching for mid-edit shapes), AST-walks for the smallest CallNode enclosing the cursor offset, looks up the method via `Reflection.instance_method_definition` / `singleton_method_definition`, returns the first overload as a `SignatureInformation` with `activeParameter` = comma count up to cursor. Server advertises `signatureHelpProvider.triggerCharacters: ["(", ","]`. (4) **Hash-key completion (slice D1)** — when the cursor is at `hash[:|` and the receiver's inferred type is a `Type::HashShape`, completion now returns the shape's keys as `CompletionItem`s with `kind: 5` (Field) and labels in their literal form (`:foo` / `"bar"`). This is the most type-driven completion Rigor ships: HashShape carriers come directly from inference of literal hashes. Parse-recovery via new `SENTINEL_HASH_KEY` (`__rigor_lsp_key__]`) for `[:` triggers. Dispatch falls through to method completion when receiver isn't HashShape (preserves slice-B1 `array[0]` behaviour). Spec coverage: +10 cases across signature_help_provider_spec (7) and completion_provider_spec (3). End-to-end smoke: `"hi".center(` returns `center(::int width, ?::string pad_string) -> ::String` signature; `h = {alpha: 1, beta: 2}; h[:` returns `:alpha` / `:beta` field items.
- **Language Server v2 — type-aware hover + `textDocument/completion`.** Follow-up to LSP v1 ([`docs/design/20260517-language-server.md`](docs/design/20260517-language-server.md)) extending two user-visible surfaces with type-driven behaviour. Design at [`docs/design/20260517-lsp-hover-completion.md`](docs/design/20260517-lsp-hover-completion.md). **Hover (4 slices)**: new `Rigor::LanguageServer::HoverRenderer` collaborator with per-Prism-node-class dispatch. (A1) `CallNode` shows receiver type + RBS-erased method signature (`String#upcase: () -> ::String`) + inferred return type; falls back to default body when receiver shape isn't a supported nominal or method doesn't resolve. (A2) `ConstantReadNode` / `ConstantPathNode` shows FQN + singleton type + defined-in path (read from `RbsLoader#class_decl_paths` when attribution is available). (A3) Local + instance variable nodes show name + narrowed type, with ivar additionally showing enclosing class via `scope.self_type.class_name`. (A4) Literal-bearing nodes (Integer / Float / Rational / Imaginary / String / Symbol / Regex / true / false / nil / Array / Hash) drop the debug `node:` row in favour of `# Type` / `# Erased` framing, and surface canonical refinement names (`non-empty-string`, …) for `Type::Refined` / `Type::Difference` carriers. **Completion (4 slices)**: new `CompletionProvider` collaborator advertised via `completionProvider.triggerCharacters: [".", ":"]`. (B1) Method completion for `obj.|`: parses + locates + enumerates `Reflection.instance_definition(class_name).methods` (or `singleton_definition` for `Type::Singleton` receivers); filters out non-public methods on explicit receivers; renders each as LSP `CompletionItem` with kind=2 (Method), detail (RBS signature with `#`/`.` separator), insertText, filterText, sortText. (B2) Constant-path completion for `Foo::|`: enumerates `RbsLoader#known_class_names_set` filtered to immediate children of the parent FQN; CompletionItem kind=7 (Class). (B3) Composite-receiver handling: `Refined` / `Difference` → underlying nominal's methods; `Tuple` → `Array`'s methods; `HashShape` → `Hash`'s methods; `Union` → intersection of member methods (conservative default per design § "Union receiver completion"); `Intersection` → union of member methods. (B4) Parse-recovery sentinel for mid-edit buffers: when the original buffer fails Prism's parse AND the cursor sits right after a `.` (or `::` modulo trailing whitespace), the provider splices a sentinel identifier (`__rigor_lsp_sentinel__` for method, `RigorLspSentinelXyz` for constant-path — uppercase-leading because Ruby constants reject `_`-leading names) into the source before reparsing, so editor users get completion in the `obj.|` / `Foo::|` shapes editors actually trigger on. Spec coverage: +25 cases across `hover_renderer_spec` (10) / `completion_provider_spec` (16 — minus 1 because two spec files share one existing case). End-to-end smoke confirms `"hi".upcase` hover surfaces `String#upcase: () -> ::String`, completion against `"hi".` returns `upcase`/`downcase`/`reverse`, completion against `Process::` returns `Status`/`GID`/`Sys`/etc. **Out of scope** (per design § "Out of scope for v2", queued): `textDocument/signatureHelp`, snippet expansion, hash-key completion, bare-name (implicit-self) completion, symbol completion, multi-overload signature display, telemetry-based ranking, `completionItem/resolve` deferred-payload round-trip, plugin-side completion contributions.
- **Language Server v1 — `rigor lsp` subcommand + in-process Ruby LSP.** Closes [ADR-0](docs/adr/0-concept.md)'s deferred LSP commitment by building on editor mode v1 (`docs/design/20260516-editor-mode.md`) as the substrate. Single in-process Ruby LSP hosts `Rigor::Analysis::Runner`, plugins, `Environment`, and a `Cache::Store(read_only: true)`; per-request work is per-buffer inference only, with cold-start tax paid once per session. **Architecture decision**: option B (in-process Ruby) over option A (CLI shell-out, 500ms-1.5s/req) and option C (polyglot + Ruby daemon, IPC overhead). Library: `language_server-protocol` gem (thin) with Rigor owning its own dispatcher / lifecycle / pool integration rather than living inside `ruby-lsp`'s addon framework. Full design + open questions in [`docs/design/20260517-language-server.md`](docs/design/20260517-language-server.md). **Eight-slice cut**: (1) `rigor lsp` CLI subcommand + `Rigor::LanguageServer::Server` lifecycle state machine (initialize → initialized → shutdown → exit, with JSON-RPC error envelopes for state violations); (2) stdio JSON-RPC dispatcher Loop (gem's `Io::Reader/Writer` + Rigor's `Loop` thin wrapper, request/notification distinguished by `id` presence, JSON parse errors surface as `ParseError`(-32700) with `id: null`); (3) `BufferTable` virtual file table + `textDocument/didOpen` / `didChange` / `didClose` handlers (FULL sync semantics; LSP `TextDocumentSyncKind = 1`); (4) `textDocument/publishDiagnostics` end-to-end pipeline via new `DiagnosticPublisher` collaborator + `Uri` `file://` ↔ filesystem helper with byte-level percent-decode for multi-byte UTF-8; (5) `textDocument/hover` via new `HoverProvider` (same `NodeLocator + ScopeIndexer + Scope#type_of` chain `rigor type-of` already drives, 0-based ↔ 1-based coordinate translation at the boundary); (6) `textDocument/documentSymbol` via Prism AST walk emitting LSP `DocumentSymbol[]` with nested Class/Module/Method outlines; (7) `Rigor::LanguageServer::ProjectContext` per-session cache (lazy `Environment.for_project` + read-only `Cache::Store`) + `workspace/didChangeWatchedFiles` + `workspace/didChangeConfiguration` invalidation; (8) per-key `Debouncer` (200ms quiet-time, cooperative cancellation) + `SynchronizedWriter` (Mutex-wrapped STDOUT for concurrent debouncer-thread + main-loop writes). Editor-mode v1 regression fix landed in slice 4: `Runner#expand_paths` now treats the buffer's logical path as a valid file even when absent on disk (LSP common case: editing a brand-new file). New runtime dependency: `language_server-protocol ~> 3.17`. Out-of-scope for v1 (per design doc § "Out of scope for v1"): completion / codeAction / formatting / semanticTokens / inlayHint / rename / multi-root workspaces / TCP transport / incremental sync (queued as slice 10) / Ractor pool dispatch for parallel multi-buffer publishes (the design doc's slice-8 second half, queued behind concrete demand). Spec coverage: +77 cases across `server` / `loop` / `buffer_table` / `uri` / `diagnostic_publisher` / `hover_provider` / `document_symbol_provider` / `project_context` / `debouncer` / `synchronized_writer` / `cli` (lsp subcommand).
- **Editor mode v1 — `--tmp-file` / `--instead-of` paired flags on `rigor check` and `rigor type-of`.** Mirrors PHPStan's "Editor Mode" semantics (see `references/phpstan/website/src/user-guide/editor-mode.md`): an external tool writes the user's in-flight buffer to a temp file, and Rigor analyses as if that file replaced one logical path inside the project. Diagnostics are reported under the logical path (`lib/foo.rb`), so editors highlight the buffer the user is editing rather than the on-disk file. **Seven-slice cut**: (1) new `Rigor::Analysis::BufferBinding` Ractor-shareable value object + `Runner.new(buffer:)` plumbing; (2) `Runner#analyze_file` / `WorkerSession#analyze` route reads through a new `parse_source` helper that reads from the buffer's physical path but stamps `Prism.parse(..., filepath: logical)` so location data stays on the logical path; (3) `Cache::Store#initialize(read_only: true)` suppresses every disk-side side-effect (entry writes, schema-version marker, destructive schema-mismatch repair) — multiple editor invocations against the same `.rigor/cache` root are safe because there's no writer; Runner auto-flips the supplied store to read-only when `buffer:` is bound; (4) `rigor check --tmp-file=PATH --instead-of=PATH` CLI flags with a paired-or-error envelope (either alone returns `EXIT_USAGE`; missing `--tmp-file` is a usage error too); (5) **single-file scope** — under editor mode only the buffer's logical path produces per-file diagnostics, but the project-wide pre-passes (`SyntheticMethodScanner`, `ProjectPatchedScanner`) see the buffer's bytes through the binding's `resolve` helper. If `--instead-of=Y` names a path not under `paths:`, the buffer is still analyzed (per design § "Failure envelope"); (6) `rigor type-of --tmp-file / --instead-of` extends the same surface to the hover-to-type probe — bytes come from the buffer when the position file matches the logical path, and the on-disk version is allowed to be absent (user editing a brand-new file); (7) Ractor pool degrades to sequential when `buffer:` is bound (`pool_mode?` returns false even if `workers > 0`) because the pool's warm-up cost dominates single-file wall time. Design: `docs/design/20260516-editor-mode.md`. **Out of scope for v1** (queued in design § "Out of scope"): per-file diagnostic cache (the lever for "option B" project-scope-with-substitution), project-context snapshot cache, multi-buffer, `--also` dependent files, LSP daemon. Spec coverage: +18 cases across `buffer_binding_spec` (3) / `cache/store_spec` (6) / `analysis/runner_spec` (6) / `analysis/worker_session_spec` (1) / `cli_spec` (4 check + 3 type-of) + a BufferBinding case in `ractor_readiness_spec`.
- **ADR-17 slice 4 — `pre_eval:` glob support.** Each entry in `pre_eval:` is now accepted as either a literal path (slice 1 contract) OR a `File.fnmatch?`-shaped glob pattern (`lib/core_ext/**/*.rb`). Glob meta characters (`*` / `?` / `[`) trigger `Dir.glob` expansion at config-load time; the resulting file list is folded into the `pre_eval:` set with `uniq`. Literal entries that don't exist on disk continue to surface as `pre-eval.file-not-found` `:error` (slice 1 behaviour); glob entries that match nothing degrade silently to "no contribution from this entry" so a templated `**` pattern in a fresh project doesn't generate spurious errors. Configuration JSON schema description updated. Spec coverage: +4 cases (glob expansion / literal preservation / match-less glob silence / dedup across multi-glob).
- **ADR-18 slice 5 — `rigor-dry-struct` consumes `:dry_type_aliases` for end-to-end precision uplift.** First worked consumer of ADR-18's `returns_from_arg:` DSL. The `rigor-dry-struct` plugin's manifest now declares `consumes: [{plugin_id: "dry-types", name: :dry_type_aliases, optional: true}]` and points each `attribute` / `attribute?` emit row at the cross-plugin fact via `returns_from_arg: { position: 1, lookup_via: { plugin_id: "dry-types", fact: :dry_type_aliases } }`. Together with `rigor-dry-types` (loaded alongside), `attribute :city, Types::String` now synthesises `Address#city` with `return_type: "String"` (resolved through the alias table); the slice-6b `environment.nominal_for_name` promotion path then yields `Nominal[String]` at the dispatcher. When `rigor-dry-types` isn't loaded OR the call site uses an unresolvable shape (`Types::String.constrained(...)`, inline method-chain whose chain-head isn't extracted), the row falls back silently to `Dynamic[Top]`. rigor-dry-struct gemspec version bumps `0.1.0` → `0.2.0`. Spec coverage: +1 integration case asserting end-to-end precision uplift through the captured `SyntheticMethodIndex`.
- **ADR-18 slices 1+2+3 — substrate per-call-site return-type DSL (`returns_from_arg:` + scanner extraction + fact-store lookup).** Lands the substrate amendment proposed in [ADR-18](docs/adr/18-substrate-per-call-site-return-type.md): `Plugin::Macro::HeredocTemplate::Emit` gains a `returns_from_arg:` field (new `ReturnsFromArg` value class carrying `position:` + `lookup_via: {plugin_id:, fact:}`). When present, the substrate's pre-pass scans the call site at the declared `position:`, extracts the argument's qualified-constant source representation (`ConstantReadNode` / `ConstantPathNode` shapes; chained-call shapes deferred), consults the named cross-plugin fact, and uses the resolved underlying class name as the synthetic method's `return_type`. Three-tier fallback unchanged: `returns_from_arg:` → static `returns:` → `"untyped"` (Dynamic[Top]). `Emit#returns` is now optional (was required) — manifest authoring with neither `returns:` nor `returns_from_arg:` lands at the Dynamic[Top] floor with no error. The `SyntheticMethodScanner.scan` API gains a `fact_store:` keyword; `Analysis::Runner` reorders plugin `prepare(services)` to run BEFORE the synthetic-method scanner (with diagnostics captured in an ivar and re-surfaced in `pre_file_diagnostics`) so cross-plugin facts (`:dry_type_aliases` etc.) are visible to the scanner's per-call-site lookups. Pool-mode workers continue to re-run prepare per worker (Phase 4b contract unchanged). Public-API drift snapshot updated for `Emit#returns_from_arg`. Slice 4 (`TraitRegistry` parity) and slice 5 (rigor-dry-struct manifest update + worked end-to-end uplift) land in follow-up commits.
- **ADR-12 slice 3 — `rigor-dry-types` user-authored compositions.** Extends slices 1+2 with recognition of `Email = String.constrained(format: …)` / `Status = Strict::Symbol` / `PositiveInt = Integer.constrained(gt: 0).optional` declarations inside the alias-module body. The new LHS constant is registered under the canonical head's underlying Ruby class, so `Types::Email` resolves to `String`, `Types::Status` to `Symbol`, `Types::PositiveInt` to `Integer`. Recognised RHS shapes (recursive on `node.receiver`): bare canonical name, nested-category constant-path (`Strict::String` / `Coercible::Integer` / …), method chain (`.constrained(...)` / `.optional` / `.default(...)` / arbitrary single-arg method) — anything whose innermost receiver is a canonical name. Unions (`String | Integer`) and intersections (`String & Foo`) are skipped because there's no single underlying class for multi-class compositions; transitive references to other compositions (`ManagerEmail = Email`) stay deferred. Spec coverage: +2 integration cases (composition publication / union-skip).
- **ADR-17 slice 3a — return-type heuristic + duplicate-declaration `:info`.** Builds on slice 2's `ProjectPatchedMethods` registry. `ProjectPatchedMethods::Entry` grows a `return_type:` field (default `nil`); `ProjectPatchedScanner` populates it via the same `Analysis::DependencySourceInference::ReturnTypeHeuristic` the ADR-10 walker uses — a `def to_url; "hello"; end` patched onto `String` now resolves `s.to_url` to `Dynamic[Nominal[String]]` instead of slice-2's `Dynamic[Top]`. The dispatcher's `try_project_patched_method` wraps a non-nil `return_type` in `Type::Combinator.dynamic(facet)`; the heuristic's `nil` answer (non-literal tail expression) falls back to `Dynamic[Top]`. **Duplicate-declaration diagnostic**: when two `pre_eval:` files declare the same `(class_name, method_name, kind)`, the scanner emits one `:info` `pre-eval.duplicate-declaration` per shadowed entry pointing at both source locations. The registry's first-write-wins behaviour is unchanged; the diagnostic just makes the shadowing visible. Cache descriptor (per-file invalidation) stays deferred to slice 3b. Spec coverage: +5 cases (heuristic return_type extraction / nil for non-literal / duplicate-declaration emission / dispatcher Dynamic[T] wrapping / kind-aware lookup).
- **ADR-12 slice 2 — `rigor-dry-types` nested coercion-category aliases.** Extends slice 1's canonical-shortcut table with the four parallel dry-types coercion categories: `Coercible::*` (everything-to-target coercion), `Strict::*` (no coercion; raise on mismatch), `Params::*` (HTTP / query-string coercion used by Hanami / Roda / dry-web request handling), `JSON::*` (JSON-shape coercion). Each category exposes the same fifteen canonical names; for Rigor's purposes the underlying class is the same regardless of category (coercion semantics are a runtime concern). A single `module Types; include Dry.Types(); end` now publishes **75 entries** (15 canonical + 15 × 4 nested) into the `:dry_type_aliases` cross-plugin fact — `Types::Coercible::Integer` / `Types::Strict::Symbol` / `Types::Params::Bool` / `Types::JSON::Date` all resolve. User-authored compositions (`Email = Types::String.constrained(format: …)`) and `.constrained` / `.optional` / `.default` chaining stay deferred to slice 3+. Spec coverage: +1 integration case asserting all four categories publish the canonical 15 names.
- **ADR-17 slice 2 — `ProjectPatchedMethods` registry + pre-eval pre-pass + dispatcher tier.** Builds on slice 1's `pre_eval:` config plumbing: new `Rigor::Inference::ProjectPatchedScanner` walks every `pre_eval:` file once at run start, harvests `class C; def m; end; end` shapes (including `def self.m` / `class << self; def m; end; end`) into a frozen `Rigor::Inference::ProjectPatchedMethods` registry keyed by `(class_name, method_name, kind)`. The dispatcher gains a new `try_project_patched_method` tier between the substrate / plugin tiers and the dependency-source inference tier per ADR-17 § "Inference contract"; matches resolve to `Type::Combinator.untyped` (Dynamic[Top]) at the slice-2 floor. Parse errors in a pre-eval file degrade fail-soft to a `:warning` `pre-eval.parse-error` accumulated alongside slice-1's `:error` `pre-eval.file-not-found` stream — analysis proceeds. Slice 2 closes the "patched cross-file call resolves" surface; precise return-type extraction for patched methods, cache-descriptor invalidation per pre-eval file, and the `pre-eval.duplicate-declaration` `:info` diagnostic stay deferred to slice 3+. Environment public surface grows by one accessor (`project_patched_methods`) and one `Environment.for_project` keyword (`project_patched_methods:`); `spec/rigor/public_api_drift_spec.rb` + `sig/rigor/environment.rbs` pinned in lockstep. Spec coverage: 8 unit cases (Registry + Scanner) + 2 Runner integration cases (cross-file `String#to_url` resolves cleanly / parse-error file → fail-soft `:warning`).
- **ADR-12 slice 1 — `plugins/rigor-dry-types/` (dry-rb Tier A foundation plugin).** First concrete dry-rb adapter following the per-gem + meta umbrella decision in [ADR-12](docs/adr/12-dry-rb-packaging.md). The plugin recognises the canonical dry-types alias-module declaration (`module Types; include Dry.Types(); end`) anywhere in the project's `paths:`, walks the AST during `prepare(services)`, and publishes the `{aliased_name => underlying_class_name}` table as the `:dry_type_aliases` cross-plugin fact ([ADR-9](docs/adr/9-cross-plugin-api.md)). Covers fifteen canonical-shortcut names (`String` / `Integer` / `Float` / `Decimal` / `Symbol` / `Bool` / `True` / `False` / `Nil` / `Date` / `DateTime` / `Time` / `Hash` / `Array` / `Any`) — nested namespaces (`Coercible::Integer` / `Strict::Symbol` / `Params::Bool` / `JSON::Date`) and user-authored compositions (`.constrained(…)` chains) stay deferred to slice 2.
