Slice 1 ships **fact-publication only**: no user-facing diagnostics yet — the visible payoff lands when downstream plugins consume the fact (e.g., `rigor-dry-struct`'s slice-6 precision promotion that would turn `address.city` from `Dynamic[T]` into `Nominal[String]`). Examples count: twenty-four → twenty-five. Spec coverage: 4 integration cases (manifest shape / canonical Types module → 15-entry table / nested `App::Types` → namespaced entries / no-`include Dry.Types()` shape stays absent from the fact).
- **ADR-17 slice 1 — `pre_eval:` config plumbing + file-existence diagnostic.** New `Rigor::Configuration#pre_eval` accessor + matching `pre_eval:` JSON-schema entry let users declare project files Rigor will pre-evaluate before per-file inference once slice 2 lands the registry. Slice 1 ships **plumbing only**: the configuration loader reads the list, resolves each entry relative to the config file's directory (`PATH_KEYS`), and `Analysis::Runner#pre_file_diagnostics` emits one `pre-eval.file-not-found` `:error` diagnostic per resolved path that doesn't exist on disk. Loud-on-config-mistake by design — a missing pre_eval path is a user-fixable error, not a fail-soft warning. No registry, no dispatcher tier, no walker integration yet; those land in slice 2 per [ADR-17](docs/adr/17-monkey-patch-pre-evaluation.md) § "Implementation slicing". Spec coverage: 3 `Runner` integration cases (missing path → one diagnostic, present path → silent, two missing paths → two diagnostics).
- **ADR-10 walker — heuristic return-type extraction (Phase B floor).** Opt-in gem-source inference's walker (`Rigor::Analysis::DependencySourceInference::Walker`) now extracts a heuristic return type from each method body's tail expression and records it on the per-method `CatalogEntry`. The dispatcher consumes the heuristic via `Type::Combinator.dynamic(static_facet)`: a `def foo; "hello"; end` in an opt-in gem now resolves `obj.foo` to `Dynamic[Nominal[String]]` instead of the pre-enhancement `Dynamic[Top]`, letting downstream chains (`obj.foo.bytesize`) make progress. The heuristic is **strict-floor**: literal scalar tails (`Integer` / `Float` / `Symbol` / `true` / `false` / `nil`) fold to `Constant<value>`; literal `String` tails fold to `Nominal[String]` (NOT `Constant<"x">` — string literals are mutable under `# frozen_string_literal: false`); literal `Array` / `Hash` tails fold to `Nominal[Array]` / `Nominal[Hash]` (element-type inference deferred); anything else (method calls, explicit `return`, `self`, ivar reads, …) returns `nil` and the dispatcher falls back to `Dynamic[Top]`. Walker `Outcome.catalog` value shape moved from bare `Symbol` (`:instance` / `:singleton`) to `Walker::CatalogEntry(kind:, return_type:)`; `DependencySourceInference::Index` normalizes legacy bare-Symbol callers at construction so existing in-tree specs continue to work unchanged. ADR-10 § "Open questions — `mode: :full` should be allowed at all" stays answered (`:full` still supported), and the per-gem budget contract is unchanged. Lazy / on-demand return-type inference (option C from the design discussion) stays demand-driven for a later slice. Spec coverage: 13 `ReturnTypeHeuristic` unit cases (literal scalars / String → Nominal / Array→Nominal / Hash→Nominal / multi-statement tail / non-literal tails / empty body / explicit return / self / ivar); 3 `MethodDispatcher` integration cases (Constant<Integer> wrap / Nominal[String] wrap / nil-heuristic fallback).

### Performance

- **Spec suite — opt-in shared cache in `plugin_helpers#run_plugin` (`sorbet_plugin_spec` first consumer).** `spec/integration/plugins/sorbet_plugin_spec.rb` isolated wall time **13.1s → 4.7s (-64%)**; full integration sequential **90.2s → 78.6s (-13%)**. `PluginHelpers` gains a process-wide `shared_cache_store` (lazy `Dir.mktmpdir`-rooted `Cache::Store`) plus a `default_run_plugin_cache_store` instance method spec files override to opt in (`let(:default_run_plugin_cache_store) { :shared }`). The pattern is opt-in rather than default because cache I/O overhead exceeds the per-call env build savings up to and including ≈17 examples per spec file (an empirical measurement on `units_plugin_spec`'s 17-example file showed a ~12 % slowdown under shared cache); the crossover sits closer to the high end of plugin-spec example counts, so only the heaviest files opt in today (sorbet at 48). Three integration specs (actioncable / actionmailer / rails-i18n) cannot opt in until their plugin's `cache_for(...)` producer descriptor includes the project files the producer reads from (`app/channels/**/*.rb`, `app/mailers/**/*.rb` + `app/views/**/*.{erb,html,text}`, `config/locales/**/*.{yml,yaml}` respectively); the same cache-incompleteness affects users of `rigor check` with a persistent cache — captured as a follow-up in `docs/CURRENT_WORK.md` § Open Engineering Items. No production code touched; the change is entirely under `spec/integration/examples/support/plugin_helpers.rb` + the one opt-in line in `sorbet_plugin_spec.rb`.
- **Spec suite — content-keyed sig dir + shared workspace in `RunnerHelpers#analyze`.** `runner_spec.rb` isolated wall time **39.6s → 25.4s (-36%)**; `make verify` parallel wall time **65.6s → 52.6s (-20%)** on 12-core. Root cause: the persistent `Cache::Store` keys the RBS environment on `(path, sha256)` per `RbsDescriptor::FileEntry`, so the prior helper's per-call `Dir.mktmpdir` forced every `analyze(sig: ...)` to rebuild the env from scratch (~1.8 s per call with default libraries). The new helper hashes `sig:` content and materialises it at a stable `<sig_cache_root>/<sha-prefix>/` path so equivalent sigs share one warm cache entry — `runner_spec.rb`'s 12 sig-using examples were sharing only 3 distinct sig hashes, so 12 × 1.8 s collapses to 3 × 1.8 s + 9 × ~30 ms. The source-only fast path (the dominant ~80 % of `analyze` calls) reuses a single per-process empty workspace directory so the runner's `Dir.pwd`-sensitive surfaces (Gemfile.lock / bundler / rbs collection discovery) observe a stable empty root rather than a fresh tmpdir per call. Callers that pass `files:` or that override `paths:` / `signature_paths:` in `config:` continue on the existing per-call `Dir.mktmpdir` path. No production code touched; the change is entirely under `spec/support/runner_helpers.rb`. Closes the queued "Spec-suite runtime breakdown (a)" follow-up in [docs/ROADMAP.md](docs/ROADMAP.md) § "Performance / scalability" through a different mechanism than the original sketch (cache-key normalisation in the helper rather than `before(:context)` Environment sharing in the spec — the helper-side fix needed zero changes to runner_spec.rb's group structure).

## [0.1.5] - 2026-05-16

### Added

- **O2 closed — ADR-16 macro / DSL expansion substrate (floor delivered).** Four-tier substrate landed through twelve slices (commits 584ae85…0359152) that closes [ROADMAP](docs/ROADMAP.md) open item O2 at the WD13 floor. Plugin authors target metaprogramming-shaped DSLs by declaring **manifest entries** instead of writing AST walkers — the substrate handles literal-symbol extraction, name interpolation, registry lookup, per-method synthesis, and cross-file dispatch resolution.
  - **Tier A (block-as-method).** New `Rigor::Plugin::Macro::BlockAsMethod` value class declares `receiver_constraint:` + `verbs:` for class-level DSL calls whose blocks run as instance methods on the receiver class. The engine hook (`Rigor::Inference::MacroBlockSelfType.narrow_self_type_for`) consults registered entries in `ExpressionTyper`'s block-return path; on a match the block's `Scope#self_type` is narrowed to `Nominal[receiver_class]` for body typing. Worked consumer: `plugins/rigor-sinatra/` — declarative recognition of the nine Sinatra HTTP verb methods (`get`, `post`, `put`, `delete`, `head`, `options`, `patch`, `link`, `unlink`) against `Sinatra::Base` subclasses so bare `params` / `redirect` / `halt` calls inside the block resolve through Sinatra::Base's RBS.
  - **Tier C (heredoc-template expansion).** New `Rigor::Plugin::Macro::HeredocTemplate` value class declares `receiver_constraint:` + `method_name:` + `symbol_arg_position:` + `emit:` / `class_level_emit:` rows. New `Rigor::Inference::SyntheticMethod` value class records synthesised methods; new `Rigor::Inference::SyntheticMethodIndex` stores them per `(class_name, method_name, kind)`; new `Rigor::Inference::SyntheticMethodScanner` pre-pass walks project source twice (hierarchy collection + class-body match) to populate the index before per-file inference. `Environment` gains `synthetic_method_index:` kwarg (default `EMPTY`); `Analysis::Runner` builds the index after `load_plugins` + `expand_paths`. `MethodDispatcher` gains a new `try_synthetic_method` tier between `RbsDispatch.try_dispatch` and `try_dependency_source` (per WD13: user-authored RBS overrides substrate synthesis). A match returns `Type::Combinator.untyped` (Dynamic[T]) per the slice-2b floor — precise return-type promotion via ADR-13's `Plugin::TypeNodeResolver` is reserved for a later slice (slice 6, demand-driven). Worked consumer: `plugins/rigor-dry-struct/` — declarative `attribute :name, T` / `attribute? :name, T` recognition on `Dry::Struct` subclasses.
  - **Tier B (trait-inlining registry).** New `Rigor::Plugin::Macro::TraitRegistry` value class declares `receiver_constraint:` + `method_name:` + `symbol_arg_position:` + `modules_by_symbol:` + `always_included:`. The slice-3b scanner extends the Tier C pre-pass with a Tier B walk that, for each `<X>.<method_name>(:trait_a, :trait_b)` call, resolves each symbol through the bundled `modules_by_symbol:` table and per-method-explodes each included module's RBS instance methods into the existing `SyntheticMethodIndex` (with `origin_module:` recorded in provenance for future precision promotion). Unknown trait symbols silently skipped; `always_included:` modules apply regardless of selection. Worked consumer: `plugins/rigor-devise/` — bundled registry mirroring Devise's `lib/devise/modules.rb` strategy table (11 strategies + the always-included `Devise::Models::Authenticatable`).
  - **Tier D (external-Ruby-file inclusion, CONTRACT ONLY).** New `Rigor::Plugin::Macro::ExternalFile` value class declares `glob:` + `receiver_type:` + `bound_ivars:` and lands as a forward-compatible manifest hook. Engine integration (file discovery + top-level `self_type` narrow + ivar pre-binding) is **deferred to slice 5b**, gated on demonstrated demand from concrete plugin targets (Redmine webhook payloads, tDiary plugin loader). Declarations round-trip through `Manifest#to_h` (cache-key stable) today but the substrate does not yet act on them.
  - **Concern re-targeting walker.** `SyntheticMethodScanner` recognises the `ActiveSupport::Concern.included do ... end` shape — when a class body contains `include M` and M `extend ActiveSupport::Concern`s a module with deferred DSL calls in `included do`, each deferred call replays against the including class as if it had been written directly. Tier B and Tier C synthetic emits compose through the same path. Slice-4 floor: constant-path `include M` only; one-hop only (nested concerns NOT transitively replayed); `class_methods do ... end` deferred.
  - **`Plugin::Manifest`** gains four new public attr_readers — `block_as_methods` / `heredoc_templates` / `trait_registries` / `external_files` (all default `[]`) — and matching constructor kwargs / validators / `to_h` serialisation. Cache-key stability preserved through the existing manifest-digest path; the `RBS sig drift` snapshot at `spec/rigor/public_api_drift_spec.rb` adds rows for every new value-class instance method.
  - **Documentation:** new "Macro / DSL expansion substrate (ADR-16)" section in `docs/handbook/09-plugins.md` introducing the four tiers + Concern re-targeting + floor/ceiling framing + decision matrix between the substrate and a hand-rolled walker. The `skills/rigor-plugin-author/SKILL.md` Phase 2 splits into "Step 2A — Try the macro substrate first" + "Step 2B — Hand-rolled walker (when the substrate does not fit)" so SKILL-driven authoring picks the right path automatically. `examples/README.md` comparison table grows three rows (rigor-sinatra / rigor-dry-struct / rigor-devise) with the substrate tier labelled.
  - **Plugin examples count:** twenty-two → twenty-four entries (twenty-three worked plugins + one RBS bundle). Three new substrate consumers exercise the substrate end-to-end through the example plugin gem path (entry point + manifest + demo + integration spec).
  - **Slice 6 precision promotion (LANDED).** Two commits add per-tier promotion in the dispatcher's `try_synthetic_method`. **Slice 6a-TierB** (`d174fff`) — Tier B emissions promote through their `origin_module:` provenance: when a SyntheticMethod records its included-module name, the dispatcher redispatches the call on `Nominal[origin_module]` via the existing `RbsDispatch.try_dispatch`. Devise's `user.valid_password?("pw")` now returns `bool` (the module's authored RBS return type) instead of `Dynamic[T]`. **Slice 6b-TierC** (`d7b1943`) — Tier C emissions promote through `environment.nominal_for_name(return_type)`: the manifest's `returns:` String is looked up as a fully-qualified class name and the synthesised reader returns `Nominal[<class>]` when the name resolves in the RBS env. The placeholder `"untyped"` (Tier B's record-but-do-not-resolve marker from slice 3b) and the RBS-style `"void"` are excluded from the Tier C path so they self-route to the Tier B branch or to the WD13 floor. New private helpers `promote_synthetic_match` / `promote_via_origin_module` / `promote_via_return_type` consolidate the per-match walk in registration-order first-wins (WD11). Parameterised forms (`Array[String]`, `Hash[K, V]`) and plugin-supplied utility-type names (`Pick<T, K>`) intentionally stay at `Dynamic[T]` — routing them through ADR-13's full `Plugin::TypeNodeResolver` chain remains a future iteration, gated on demand from utility-type-shaped substrate consumers. Spec coverage at `spec/rigor/inference/method_dispatcher/synthetic_method_promotion_spec.rb` (7 cases: Tier B happy path / unresolvable module / first-wins / Tier C plain class promotion / `untyped` placeholder / unknown class fallback / parameterised-form decline).
  - **Remaining work (demand-driven, NOT in scope of this landing):** slice 5b (Tier D engine integration — top-level `self_type` narrow + `bound_ivars` pre-binding for matched external files), full ADR-13 `Plugin::TypeNodeResolver` chain wiring for the synthetic-method tier (unlocks utility-type-shaped substrate returns like `Array[String]` / `Pick<T, K>`). Both are pinned as roadmap items in `docs/ROADMAP.md`'s "v0.1.5 — accumulating on master" § Open items table and as ADR-16 § Implementation slicing footnotes.

- **`is_a?(C)` narrowing — lexical-nesting constant resolution.** `Inference::Narrowing#analyse_class_predicate` now resolves the predicate's bare class-name argument through the lexical-nesting chain derived from `scope.self_type`, mirroring Ruby's `Module.nesting`-driven constant lookup. For a method body inside `Foo::Bar::Baz`, the chain `["Foo::Bar::Baz", "Foo::Bar", "Foo"]` is tried before the top-level lookup; the first prefix where `<prefix>::<bare_name>` is known to the environment (RBS-known set) OR the scope's `discovered_classes` (in-source declarations) wins. The lookup is **conservative** — when nothing in the chain resolves, the bare name is passed through unchanged so existing single-segment class references keep working. Unlocks adding `singleton` to `DEFAULT_LIBRARIES`: before this fix, `is_a?(Singleton)` inside `Rigor::Type::Singleton#==` resolved to the unrelated top-level stdlib `Singleton` mixin (which has no `class_name` method), surfacing a spurious `call.undefined-method` on `other.class_name`. After the fix, the lexical chain `["Rigor::Type::Singleton", "Rigor::Type", "Rigor"]` finds `Rigor::Type::Singleton` itself on the second prefix and the narrowing routes correctly. The fix is surgical to the class-predicate narrowing surface; broader lexical-resolution (method-dispatch receivers, attribute access, etc.) stays at the existing bare-name semantics — gated on further demand. New integration fixture `spec/integration/fixtures/is_a_lexical_resolution.rb` exercises a nested-class lookup end-to-end.
- **DEFAULT_LIBRARIES — Stdlib coverage expansion (Slice A v2).** Thirty-one additional stdlib libraries now auto-load through `Environment::DEFAULT_LIBRARIES` so any Ruby program targeting them sees precise RBS types without the user having to enumerate `libraries:` in `.rigor.yml`: `pp`, `delegate`, `observable`, `abbrev`, `find`, `tsort`, `shellwords`, `benchmark`, `base64`, `did_you_mean`, `monitor`, `mutex_m`, `timeout`, `open3`, `erb`, `etc`, `ipaddr`, `bigdecimal`, `bigdecimal-math`, `prettyprint`, `random-formatter`, `time`, `open-uri`, `resolv`, `csv`, `pstore`, `objspace`, `io-console`, `cgi`, `cgi-escape`, `strscan`, `singleton`. Out-of-the-box RBS classes available: 1,273 → 1,427 (+154). `singleton` was initially held back due to a name conflict with `Rigor::Type::Singleton`, but the lexical-nesting fix in `Inference::Narrowing#analyse_class_predicate` (separate `[Unreleased]` entry above) unblocked it. `strscan` was initially excluded because the bundled RBS only declares `StringScanner#[](Integer)` while rigor uses `#[](Symbol)` for named captures; it was re-introduced after applying two `# rigor:disable argument-type-mismatch` directives at the affected call sites in `imported_refinements.rb`. `BundleSigDiscovery::SKIPPED_GEMS_BY_DEFAULT` was updated in lockstep so the same gem names don't double-load through the Layer-2 bundle path. The "csv is not in defaults" example in `spec/rigor/environment_spec.rb` § "DEFAULT_LIBRARIES merge ordering" now uses `singleton` instead so the test continues to exercise the opt-in merge path.
- **O4 Layer 3 slice 3 — graceful-degradation coverage report.** New `Rigor::Environment::RbsCoverageReport` module classifies each `Gemfile.lock`-locked gem against the four RBS resolution paths (`DEFAULT_LIBRARIES`, `data/vendored_gem_sigs/`, slice-1 bundle-shipped `sig/`, slice-2 `rbs_collection.lock.yaml`) and surfaces the `:missing` set. `Analysis::Runner#pre_file_diagnostics` emits a single `:info` `rbs.coverage.missing-gem` diagnostic at run start when one or more locked gems have no RBS coverage, listing the first five names and suggesting `rbs collection install`, ship `sig/` in the gem, or opt into `dependencies.source_inference:`. The diagnostic is suppressed when there's no `Gemfile.lock` (default behaviour for projects without Bundler) or when every locked gem is covered. `BundleSigDiscovery.gem_name_from_sig_path` was promoted from `private_class_method` to public so the report can classify discovered bundle paths without re-running discovery. Together with slices 1+2, slice 3 completes the user-facing surface of O4 Layer 3: the `Gemfile.lock` filter (slice 1) + `rbs collection install` awareness (slice 2) + missing-gem visibility (slice 3) close the "what RBS does my project actually have" question end-to-end. The per-gem-version cache descriptor (so `bundle update` invalidates only the affected gem's slice) stays queued as a larger architectural change. Spec coverage: 9 `RbsCoverageReport` unit cases (default_library / vendored_gem_sig / bundle_sig / rbs_collection / missing / sort / precedence / .missing filter / VENDORED_GEM_NAMES in-sync check); 2 `Runner` integration cases (missing-gem diagnostic fires once with rare gems / suppressed when every gem is covered).
- **O4 Layer 3 slice 2 — `rbs collection install` awareness.** New `Rigor::Environment::RbsCollectionDiscovery` module parses a target project's `rbs_collection.lock.yaml` (the RBS-ecosystem-standard output of `rbs collection install`) and surfaces every per-gem `<collection_path>/<name>/<version>/` directory as an `Array<Pathname>` so `Environment.for_project` can append them to `RbsLoader`'s `signature_paths:`. Source-type filter: `stdlib`-typed entries are skipped because rigor's bundled `DEFAULT_LIBRARIES` already covers that surface (avoiding the prism-style `RBS::DuplicatedDeclarationError` hazard O7 originally surfaced); `git`, `rubygems`, and `local` entries are admitted. The lockfile's `path:` field is resolved relative to the lockfile's directory (matching `RBS::Collection::Config::Lockfile#fullpath` semantics) so custom collection roots work without manual reconfiguration. Config schema: new `rbs_collection:` block with `lockfile:` (String, optional — resolved relative to the project root) and `auto_detect:` (Boolean, default true; auto-detects `<project_root>/rbs_collection.lock.yaml`). The lockfile path threads through `Configuration#rbs_collection_lockfile` / `#rbs_collection_auto_detect` → `Environment.for_project(rbs_collection_lockfile:, rbs_collection_auto_detect:)` → `Runner` + `WorkerSession` matching the existing Layer 1/2/Slice-1 fan-out. Failure modes are deliberately quiet: a missing lockfile, malformed YAML, missing collection root, or non-existent per-gem directory all degrade silently to "no extra sigs" while preserving slice-1 / Layer-1+2 behaviour. Together with slice 1, the two slices close most of the O4 envelope: end users running `rbs collection install` per the standard RBS workflow now get those signatures auto-loaded by rigor without any `signature_paths:` configuration; users without `rbs collection install` continue to benefit from the slice-1 `Gemfile.lock` filter against the bundle install root. Spec coverage: 10 `RbsCollectionDiscovery` unit cases (git source / stdlib filter / missing dir / multi-source-type / non-existent lockfile / missing collection root / auto-detect / malformed YAML / non-default `path:` / resolve_lockfile_path).
- **O4 Layer 3 slice 1 — `Gemfile.lock` parse + bundle filter.** New `Rigor::Environment::LockfileResolver` parses a target project's `Gemfile.lock` via `Bundler::LockfileParser` and exposes the locked gem set as a frozen `Hash[String, LockedGem]` keyed by gem name, where each `LockedGem` carries `(name, version, platform)`. `BundleSigDiscovery.discover` gains a `locked_gems:` keyword argument: when non-nil and non-empty, the discovery now FILTERS the per-gem `sig/` directories so only gems whose `(name, version, platform)` matches a lockfile entry are admitted to `signature_paths:`. Stale gems in the bundle install tree (out-of-band installs, leftover versions after `bundle update`) are silently dropped — the lockfile is treated as the source of truth for "what the project actually declares". Pre-Layer-3 behaviour is preserved when `locked_gems:` is `nil` (the default) or empty. Config schema: new `bundler.lockfile:` key (String, optional — resolved relative to the project root); when nil and `auto_detect: true`, the resolver looks for `<project_root>/Gemfile.lock`. The lockfile path threads through `Configuration#bundler_lockfile` → `Environment.for_project(bundler_lockfile:)` → `Runner` + `WorkerSession` matching the existing Layer 1/2 fan-out. Failure modes are deliberately quiet: a missing or malformed lockfile returns the empty map and analysis proceeds with pre-Layer-3 discovery. The substrate lays the foundation for Slice 2 (`rbs_collection.lock.yaml` recognition) and Slice 3 (per-gem-version cache descriptor + graceful-degradation messaging) without committing to a particular `gem_rbs_collection` sourcing strategy. Spec coverage: 11 `LockfileResolver` unit cases (explicit path / relative path / auto-detect / not-found / malformed-fail-soft / platform-tagged spec / resolve_lockfile_path), 6 `BundleSigDiscovery` integration cases (lockfile-matching filter / version-drift drop / platform-tagged match / nil-lockfile fallback / empty-lockfile fallback / skip_gems wins over lockfile presence).
- **O4 MVP — target-project Bundler awareness via `bundler.bundle_path` / `bundler.auto_detect`.** New `Rigor::Environment::BundleSigDiscovery` module walks a Bundler-installed gem tree (`<bundle>/ruby/X.Y.Z/gems/<name>-<ver>/sig/`) and feeds each per-gem `sig/` directory into `RbsLoader`'s `signature_paths:` automatically. Wired into `Environment.for_project` via two new keyword args (`bundler_bundle_path:`, `bundler_auto_detect:`); the Runner and the per-worker `WorkerSession` thread them through from `Configuration#bundler_bundle_path` / `#bundler_auto_detect`. Config schema: new `bundler:` block with `bundle_path:` (String, optional — resolved relative to the project root) and `auto_detect:` (Boolean, default true). Auto-detection order: `.bundle/config`'s `BUNDLE_PATH:` first, then `vendor/bundle/` under the project root, then no extra sigs. Auto-skip list (`SKIPPED_GEMS_BY_DEFAULT`) excludes gems already covered by rigor's `DEFAULT_LIBRARIES` (`pathname`, `optparse`, `json`, `yaml`, …, `prism`, `rbs`) and the `data/vendored_gem_sigs/` bundle (`pg`, `mysql2`, `nokogiri`, `bcrypt`, `redis`, `idn-ruby`) so the gem-shipped versions don't collide with rigor's pre-loaded ones. Quantitative impact on Mastodon (a Docker `bundle install` of its 343 gems against `ruby:4.0.3-slim-trixie`, with `bundler.bundle_path: /tmp/mastodon-bundle` set): **RBS classes available 1,178 → 2,136 (+958)** from the 7 non-skipped gem-shipped sigs (`aws-sdk-core`, `aws-sdk-s3`, `aws-sdk-kms`, `playwright-ruby-client`, `mutex_m`, `webrick`, `base64`, `stoplight`, `ffi`); wall time grew from ~3 s → 5.5 s (cold) due to the extra env build, peak RSS 125 MB → 427 MB. Diagnostic count stayed at 124 with a mild rule-mix shift (`call.undefined-method` 28 → 22 — the new AWS / playwright surfaces resolve cleanly). Spec coverage: 11 cases under `spec/rigor/environment/bundle_sig_discovery_spec.rb` (explicit path / relative path / non-existent path / skip-list / skip-list override / `.bundle/config` parse / `vendor/bundle/` fallback / no-detect / malformed-config fail-soft / platform-suffixed gem dirs e.g. `ffi-1.17.4-aarch64-linux-gnu/`). With O7's failure-memo in place, sig conflicts now degrade gracefully — `bundler.bundle_path` is safe to opt into experimentally. **Layer 3 (`Gemfile.lock` parse + `gem_rbs_collection` integration) remains queued.**
- **O7 closed — `RbsLoader#env` now memoises failure, killing the per-file slowdown when a `signature_paths:` entry duplicates a stdlib RBS declaration.** Root cause traced through five rounds of bisection on a Mastodon controller analysis: the gem-shipped `prism/sig/prism.rbs` redeclares `Prism::VERSION: String`, which clashes with Rigor's bundled stdlib RBS (Ruby 4.0+ ships prism in core). `RBS::Environment.from_loader(...).resolve_type_names` raised `RBS::DuplicatedDeclarationError`. Pre-fix, the `@state[:env] ||= ...` memo in `RbsLoader#env` did not capture the failure, so every `env` access — one per AST node touched during analysis — re-parsed the entire signature set and re-resolved type names from scratch. Measured impact: 1 controller file + a 3-line conflicting sig built env **390 times** in 35 s (vs ~0.3 s with no conflict). Fix: `#env` now sets a `@state[:env_loaded]` flag on first attempt regardless of success, captures `RBS::BaseError` to `nil`, and emits a single user-facing `warn(...)` identifying the conflicting `.rbs` path so users can remove it. The `nil` env propagates fail-soft through `each_known_class_name`, `class_decl_paths`, `constant_names`, `compute_class_known`, `build_instance_definition`, and `build_singleton_definition` (each gained an explicit `return … if env.nil?` short-circuit). Post-fix: same 5-controller workload runs in **0.15 s** (~550× speedup). Spec coverage under `spec/rigor/environment/rbs_loader_spec.rb` § "env build failure short-circuit (O7)" (3 cases: memoisation, single-warning emission, nil-env fail-soft consumers). Unblocks `gem_rbs_collection` integration and a meaningful path toward O4 (target-project Bundler awareness): with the cliff gone, end users can now experimentally wire gem-shipped `sig/` directories into `signature_paths:` and see actual analysis results — though conflicts with rigor's bundled stdlib RBS still degrade gracefully to "no RBS" rather than hanging.
- **Docker-based Mastodon `bundle install` demonstration + reinforcement of why vendored RBS is the right shape.** Ran `bundle install` on Mastodon inside `ruby:4.0.3-slim-trixie` (Docker) — worked end to end after one Trixie package rename (`libpcre3-dev` → `libpcre2-dev`) and adding `git` to the apt list (Mastodon's Gemfile sources `webpush` from GitHub). 343 gems resolved in 271 MB. **Critical finding: only 10 of 343 gems ship a `sig/` directory in their gem package (~3%)** — `prism` / `aws-sdk-s3` / `aws-sdk-kms` / `aws-sdk-core` / `playwright-ruby-client` / `mutex_m` / `webrick` / `base64` / `stoplight` / `ffi`. None of `pg` / `mysql2` / `nokogiri` / `bcrypt` / `redis` / `idn-ruby` / `actionpack` / `activerecord` / `activesupport` / `sidekiq` / `devise` ship `sig/`. This is direct evidence that `gem_rbs_collection` is the de facto source of typed contracts for the popular gem family AND that rigor's vendored RBS direction is the realistic out-of-the-box path. **Open item O7 is more severe than initially characterised**: adding even ONE gem-shipped sig (specifically prism's, ~19 `.rbs` files) on top of rigor's analysis hangs `RBS::Environment.from_loader` for >5 minutes. Likely overlaps with rigor's pre-loaded prism RBS (rigor uses prism internally) trigger a duplicate-class resolver explosion. Until O7 is fixed, the gem-shipped sig path is not usable for new bundles. Survey notes updated under `docs/notes/20260515-real-world-rails-survey.md` § "Docker-based bundle install demonstration (Mastodon, 2026-05-15)".
- **Vendored gem RBS stubs for common Rails native extensions (built-in to rigor itself).** New `data/vendored_gem_sigs/<gem>/` tree ships RBS signatures for six native-extension / "everywhere in Rails" gems: `pg` (PostgreSQL), `mysql2` (MySQL), `nokogiri` (XML/HTML), `bcrypt` (Devise password hashing), `redis` (cache / Sidekiq), `idn-ruby` (IDN / Mastodon's `bundle install` blocker). The four with collection-vendored sigs (`mysql2`, `nokogiri`, `bcrypt`, `redis`) carry `LICENSE.upstream` provenance from [`ruby/gem_rbs_collection`](https://github.com/ruby/gem_rbs_collection) (MIT). The two without collection coverage (`pg`, `idn-ruby`) are minimal hand-written stubs covering the most-used API surface (MPL-2.0). `Rigor::Environment::RbsLoader.build_env_for` appends `vendored_gem_sig_paths` AFTER user-supplied `signature_paths:`, so user paths win on name conflicts. `Rigor::Cache::RbsDescriptor.build` includes every vendored `.rbs` so a stub edit invalidates the RBS env cache. Per-gem `*_extras.rbs` files (`nokogiri_html5.rbs`, `redis_extras.rbs`) carry hand-written additions to fill gaps in the upstream RBS snapshots (Nokogiri 1.12+ HTML5 module, Redis 4.5+ `#scan` / `#ttl` / `#type` / `#exists` family etc.). Out-of-the-box RBS classes available: 1,134 → 1,273 (+139). Across 14 surveyed real-world projects (Redmine, Discourse, Mastodon, GitLab FOSS, Forem, Solidus, Chatwoot, Canvas LMS, OpenProject, Loomio, Publify, Diaspora, Dependabot Core, tDiary Core): the net diagnostic change is +24 — the precision/coverage trade-off where added RBS catches both real new issues AND incomplete-stub false positives. Closing the residual gaps is incremental via `<gem>_extras.rbs`. See `data/vendored_gem_sigs/README.md` for the full design rationale (why default-on rather than opt-in, contrast with the ActiveSupport core-ext bundle).
- **Bundler-aware analysis exploration (open item O4 partial).** Survey notes now document the two paths that work today for analysing a project with its gems' RBS in scope: (a) running rigor inside the target's Bundler context (`BUNDLE_GEMFILE=...` + `bundle exec rigor check`) so `RBS::EnvironmentLoader.add(library: gem_name)` finds gem-bundled `sig/`, and (b) wiring [`gem_rbs_collection`](https://github.com/ruby/gem_rbs_collection) per-gem-version paths into `.rigor.yml`'s `signature_paths:`. Practical hurdles surfaced: (1) native-gem builds (libidn / libpq / libxml2) routinely fail under a stock Nix shell; (2) Ruby version mismatches — most of the 14 surveyed projects pin Ruby 3.3 / 3.4 in `.ruby-version`, while rigor's Flake provides 4.0.4. Quantitative experiment on Diaspora (Rails 6.1) with O1 v2 + a 5-gem subset (`activerecord/6.1` + `activesupport/7.0` + `activemodel/7.1` + `actionpack/7.2` + `activejob/6.0`): RBS classes 1,039 → 2,478, cold wall 1.35 s → 9.47 s, warm wall 1.05 s, diagnostics 5 → 3. On Mastodon (Rails 8) with the same subset: diagnostics 124 → 128 — the textbook precision/coverage trade-off, where added RBS surfaces both new real bugs and new false positives. **New open item O7: RBS env-build performance falls off a cliff when >10 gem RBS sigs are loaded together** — Diaspora's "full" experiment with 16 `signature_paths:` entries cold-loaded for 11+ minutes before being killed; the same workload with 5 paths completes in 7-9 s. Likely a non-linear interaction in `RBS::Environment.from_loader` / `resolve_type_names`. Investigate before O4 lands — a real-world Rails `Gemfile.lock` typically lists 50-150 gems, not 5. Survey notes updated under `docs/notes/20260515-real-world-rails-survey.md` § "Bundler-aware analysis — exploring O4 today".
- **Real-world Rails survey — round 3.** Five additional projects swept: Loomio (Rails, 563 files), Publify (Rails app shell, 15 files), Diaspora (Rails, 371 files), Dependabot Core (NOT Rails, 1,089 files across 19 ecosystem dirs), tDiary Core (NOT Rails, 244 files). Pool ≡ sequential proven on all five (zero IsolationErrors). Quantitative impact of the O1 v2 RBS bundle: Loomio 207 → 63 (−70%), Diaspora 65 → 5 (−92%), Dependabot Core 205 → 58 (−72% — the ActiveSupport-shaped bundle helps non-Rails Ruby too, because `Object#blank?`/`#present?`/`#try` / `Enumerable#index_by` / `String#exclude?` are widespread idioms outside Rails). tDiary Core 111 → 106 (−5% — pre-Rails-era idiom with no ActiveSupport in scope; residual is dominated by `instance_eval`'d plugin files where the receiver class isn't visible from the `def` site — exactly the open item O2 case). Across the now fourteen swept projects: pool ≡ sequential everywhere, zero IsolationErrors over 31,840 files. Survey notes updated under `docs/notes/20260515-real-world-rails-survey.md` § "Round-3 projects".
- **`rigor-activesupport-core-ext` v2 — extend coverage based on a five-project second-round survey (Forem / Solidus / Chatwoot / Canvas LMS / OpenProject).** Adds `Array#compact_blank` / `Hash#compact_blank` (Rails 6.1+); `Array#exclude?` / `String#exclude?` / `Enumerable#exclude?`; `Enumerable#index_with` / `#index_by` / `#pluck` / `#pick` / `#sole` / `#including` / `#excluding` / `#without`; `Hash.from_xml` / `#reverse_merge` / `#reverse_merge!` / `#slice!`; `DateTime` calculations (`#utc`, `#in_time_zone`, `#yesterday`, `#tomorrow`, `#beginning_of_*` / `#end_of_*`, `#ago`, `#since`). Combined v1 + v2 quantitative impact across all nine survey projects (Redmine + Discourse + Mastodon + GitLab FOSS + Forem + Solidus + Chatwoot + Canvas LMS + OpenProject): **total diagnostics 12,502 → 3,071 (−75%)**, `call.undefined-method` 10,589 → 1,426 (−87%). Pool ≡ sequential proven on all nine projects (zero IsolationErrors across 28,114 files swept). The pool wall-clock crossover sits between Mastodon / Forem (~1.3 K files, pool slower) and Discourse / Solidus (~1.8 K files, pool 1.3-1.5× faster); on the largest target (GitLab FOSS, 11.1 K files), pool=8 is 1.6× faster than sequential. Survey notes updated under `docs/notes/20260515-real-world-rails-survey.md` § "Round-2 projects".
- **`rigor-activesupport-core-ext` — community RBS bundle for the ActiveSupport core extensions that dominate Rails-project `call.undefined-method` noise.** New `plugins/rigor-activesupport-core-ext/` ships an opt-in RBS bundle covering roughly the top 40 selectors from the four-project Rails survey (Redmine + Discourse + Mastodon + GitLab FOSS — see `docs/notes/20260515-real-world-rails-survey.md`): `Integer` / `Float` duration multipliers (`#days`, `#hours`, `#minutes`, …) and bytes multipliers; `Time` / `Date` calculations (`.current`, `#yesterday`, `#beginning_of_*`, `#end_of_*`, `#ago`, `#since`, `#in`, `#change`); `String` inflections / filters (`#underscore`, `#camelize`, `#classify`, `#constantize`, `#demodulize`, `#pluralize`, `#singularize`, `#humanize`, `#tableize`, `#parameterize`, `#squish`, `#truncate`, `#truncate_words`, `#html_safe`, `#starts_with?` / `#ends_with?`, `#indent`, `#mb_chars`, `#to_time` / `#to_date` / `#to_datetime` / `#to_hours`); `Array.wrap`, `Array#to_sentence`, `Array#in_groups_of`, `Array#second` / `#third` / `#fourth`; `Hash#deep_dup`, `#deep_merge`, `#symbolize_keys` / `#stringify_keys` (+ deep / bang variants), `#assert_valid_keys`, `#to_query`; `Object#blank?` / `#present?` / `#presence` / `#try` and the `NilClass` / `TrueClass` / `FalseClass` specialisations. Packaged as RBS files only (no analyzer-side plugin code) and opt-in via `.rigor.yml`'s `signature_paths:`. Quantitative impact across the four survey projects: **total diagnostics 5,281 → 1,333 (−75%)**, `call.undefined-method` 4,411 → 536 (−88%). Per-project breakdown: Redmine 343 → 160 (−53%), Discourse 1,435 → 452 (−68%), Mastodon 521 → 142 (−73%), GitLab FOSS 2,982 → 579 (−81%). Scope is deliberately not exhaustive — the bundle covers the head of the distribution; project-private monkey-patches and the deep tail of ActiveSupport extensions remain. The plugin manifest does NOT yet expose a hook for "extend signature_paths on load"; the bundle's `lib/rigor-activesupport-core-ext.rb` is a pure documentation stub. Adding the manifest hook for auto-wiring is a queued separate slice.
- **Ractor migration — Phase 4b.x follow-up #3: `MethodDispatcher::CONSTANT_CONSTRUCTORS` shareable.** The Hash-of-Proc registry (`{ "Pathname" => ->(arg) { Pathname.new(arg) } }`) was shallow-frozen; the Proc value remained non-shareable. Worker Ractors reading `CONSTANT_CONSTRUCTORS[class_name]` tripped `Ractor::IsolationError`, which `constant_constructor_lift`'s `rescue StandardError` silently swallowed — `meta_new` then fell back to `Nominal[Pathname]` in pool mode while sequential built the `Constant<Pathname>` lift. The precision divergence surfaced downstream as a spurious `call.argument-type-mismatch` on GitLab FOSS's `lib/gitlab/mail_room.rb:17` (`Pathname.new("../..").expand_path(__dir__)`): sequential's `argument_type_diagnostic` short-circuits on Constant<Pathname> (Pathname isn't in its CONSTANT_CLASSES table); pool's Nominal[Pathname] doesn't short-circuit and the RBS arg check rejects `String | nil` against the `String` parameter. Now `Ractor.make_shareable` on both the outer Hash and each lambda; new audit assertion pins the invariant. After the fix, pool ≡ sequential on GitLab FOSS (2,982 == 2,982; previously 2,983 vs 2,982). (Survey notes: open item O6.)
- **Parametrized-ancestor projection — `Hash[K, V] <:= Enumerable[[K, V]]` now accepted.** `Inference::Acceptance#accepts_nominal_from_nominal` gained a `project_to_target_arity` step that runs before the element-wise covariance check. When the class hierarchy proves `actual <:= target` but the type-arg arities differ, the projection rewrites the actual to the target's parametrization view. Today only the canonical `Hash[K, V]` → `Enumerable[Tuple[K, V]]` mapping is hand-rolled (RBS encodes this as `include Enumerable[[K, V]]` in Hash's class body); a general implementation that consults `definition.ancestors[i].args` for arbitrary `include` / inheritance relations remains the principled follow-up. Surfaced on Discourse via the four `URI.encode_www_form(some_hash)` call sites flagged as `call.argument-type-mismatch` (Discourse: 8 → 4 instances of the rule; the remaining 4 are unrelated nullable-receiver cases). Spec coverage in `spec/rigor/inference/acceptance_spec.rb` § "generics (Slice 4 phase 2d)" adds two cases: the accepting Hash → Enumerable projection and a still-rejecting projection where the projected Tuple's element types differ from the target's. (Survey notes: `docs/notes/20260515-real-world-rails-survey.md` § "Open items", item O5.)
- **Ractor migration — Phase 4b.x follow-up #2: one more deep-shareability gap surfaced on Discourse.** A multi-project Rails survey (Redmine + Discourse + Mastodon + GitLab FOSS) found `MethodDispatcher::ShapeDispatch::REFINED_STRING_PROJECTIONS` (Hash keyed by two-element Symbol arrays — same shape as the Phase 4b.x follow-up #1 sites) still shallow-frozen, tripping 8 `Ractor::IsolationError` on Discourse's `lib/inline_uploads.rb` / `app/controllers/search_controller.rb` / `app/helpers/application_helper.rb` / etc. Now `Ractor.make_shareable`; new audit assertion pins the invariant. After the fix, pool `workers=4` on Discourse is byte-identical to sequential (1,439 == 1,439) and 22% wall-clock faster than sequential (6.29s vs 7.46s on a 1,804-file project). Pool also wins on GitLab FOSS at 11,130 files: 15.43s pool=8 vs 25.27s sequential — 1.6× wall-clock speedup with peak RSS at 1.3 GB. Survey notes under `docs/notes/20260515-real-world-rails-survey.md`.
- **Narrowing — assignment-in-condition (`if cond && (var = expr)`) now narrows the bound local.** The narrowing analyser (`Inference::Narrowing#analyse`) gained four new write-node cases — `LocalVariableWriteNode` plus the symmetric `InstanceVariableWriteNode` / `ClassVariableWriteNode` / `GlobalVariableWriteNode` — that mirror `analyse_local_read` on the just-bound name (the StatementEvaluator's per-kind `eval_*_write` has already bound the assigned value in `post_pred` by the time narrowing runs, so the write's truthiness IS the bound value's truthiness). The bare-assignment form `if x = expr` already happened to work because `x`'s assigned type was typically `Dynamic[top]` for plugin-less call sites (no `nil` member to drop); the `if cond && (x = expr)` form did NOT because `eval_and_or`'s nil-injection union'd `nil` back into `x`'s post-`&&` binding and no narrowing rule consumed it. On Redmine (`app/` + `lib/`, 347 files) this alone reduces `call.possible-nil-receiver` from 69 → 23 (−46 False Positives, zero new diagnostics, zero changes to other rule families). The pattern is dominant in `application_helper.rb` (`if cond && (changeset = Changeset.visible.find_by_…)` style guards) and `app/models/issue.rb` (`if issue_id && p = Issue.find_by_id(issue_id)`). Spec coverage in `spec/rigor/inference/narrowing_spec.rb` § `predicate_scopes` adds three regression cases (bare assignment / `cond && (var = expr)` / `cond && var = expr` without parens).
- **Ractor migration — Phase 4b.x follow-up: three deep-shareability gaps surfaced on real-world target projects.** Pool mode applied to a non-trivial Rails codebase (Redmine `app/` + `lib/`, 347 files) revealed three more module-level constants whose shallow `.freeze` left inner nodes mutable, tripping `Ractor::IsolationError` in worker dispatch: (a) `Rigor::Inference::Builtins::NumericCatalog`'s singleton-class `@catalog` ivar (the YAML-parsed nested Hash/Array graph — eager-loaded in the prior Phase 4b.x change but not deep-shared); (b) `Rigor::Type::Refined::CANONICAL_NAMES` (Hash keyed by two-element `[String, Symbol]` arrays whose inner arrays were unfrozen under shallow `.freeze`); (c) `Rigor::Builtins::RegexRefinement::RULES` (Array of `[Regexp, Symbol]` rows, same shape). All three now use `Ractor.make_shareable(...)` instead of `.freeze`. After the fix, pool `workers=4` on Redmine produces a diagnostic stream byte-identical to sequential (389 == 389, zero IsolationErrors). New audit assertions in `spec/rigor/ractor_readiness_spec.rb` § "Phase 4b.x — module catalog shareability" pin the invariant for all three.
- **Ractor migration — Phase 4b.x: worker-side env-build + module-shareability fixes.** Pool mode (`Runner.new(workers: N > 0)`) now reliably analyzes non-trivial source files. Previously the worker's first RBS class lookup fell through to `RBS::EnvironmentLoader.new`, which read a chain of non-`Ractor.shareable?` RubyGems / RBS module constants (`DEFAULT_CORE_ROOT`, `DEFAULT_STDLIB_ROOT`, `Gem::Requirement::DefaultRequirement`, …) and tripped `Ractor::IsolationError`. The fix is two-pronged: (1) the coordinator pre-warms every cached RBS producer on the main Ractor before spawning workers via the new `RbsLoader#prewarm` (drives `Cache::RbsEnvironment` / `RbsKnownClassNames` / `RbsConstantTable` / `RbsClassTypeParamNames` / `RbsClassAncestorTable` / `RbsInstanceDefinitions` / `RbsSingletonDefinitions` in order); afterwards worker queries serve from the Marshal blob on disk and never touch `EnvironmentLoader.new`. (2) Every Rigor-side module-level dispatch table / lazy singleton the worker dispatch loop touches is now `Ractor.make_shareable` (or eager-init): `MethodDispatcher::ConstantFolding::CATALOG_BY_CLASS` / `MODULE_CATALOGS`; `KernelDispatch::NUMERIC_CONSTRUCTORS`; `MethodParameterBinder::RBS_TYPE_PROVIDERS` / `KEYWORD_PROVIDER`; `ShapeDispatch::SIZE_RETURNING_NOMINALS`; `Narrowing::ZERO_CLASS_PREDICATE_RULES`; `Type::IntegerRange::ALIAS_NAMES`. Lazy class-level `@ivar ||= …` writes converted to eager init: `Trinary.yes/.no/.maybe`; `Type::Bot.instance` / `Top.instance`; `Type::Combinator.untyped`; `Analysis::FactStore.empty`; `Builtins::MethodCatalog` (eager YAML load — was lazy and the make_shareable freeze would otherwise trip `FrozenError`); `Builtins::NumericCatalog`. Pool mode without a `cache_store` degrades gracefully to sequential with a `pool-degraded` `:warning` diagnostic (the `--no-cache` route). `make test-ractor-pool` runs the pool spec in isolation (8/8 green including a new "non-trivial source that exercises the RBS dispatch chain" test). Pool spec stays excluded from default `make verify` due to a separate latent Bus Error in `fact_store.rb:27` under Ractor cleanup + RBS C-ext interaction; the cache pre-warm work is independent of that. **Self-check benchmark (lib/ — 161 files, MacBook M3 Max 12 cores; 3 runs each)**: sequential cold 2.35s / warm 1.66s; pool `workers=1` 2.92s/2.34s; `workers=2` 2.87s/2.24s; `workers=4` 4.03s/3.26s; `workers=8` 8.93s/8.49s — pool mode is currently SLOWER than sequential on this project size because the per-Ractor env build + Marshal restore (~500ms each) plus Ractor message overhead dominates the parallel speedup for small per-file work (~10ms/file). Pool mode is positioned as an experimental opt-in for large projects (5000+ files); the wall-clock crossover with sequential is the open question Phase 4c's deferred OQ1 (per-Ractor `Cache::Store`-shared facade) targets. Documentation: ADR-15 § "Phase 4 — Ractor worker pool" 4b.x flipped LANDED with the benchmark caveat; design doc § "Phase 4b.x" mirrors.
- **`rigor-activestorage` (Tier 3E) — `has_*_attached` macro recognition + attachment-accessor return-type narrowing.** New `plugins/rigor-activestorage/` plugin walks the configured AR model paths (`app/models/` by default) for `has_one_attached :avatar` / `has_many_attached :photos` macros, records them on a per-run `AttachmentIndex`, and contributes `Nominal[ActiveStorage::Attached::One]` / `Nominal[ActiveStorage::Attached::Many]` for chained `user.avatar` / `user.photos` calls. The chained `.attached?` / `.purge` / `.url` etc. then routes through ActiveStorage's RBS surface instead of falling to the untyped envelope. `flow_contribution_for` declines on (a) non-Nominal receivers, (b) unknown class names, (c) attachment-name calls with arguments (setters etc.), and (d) method names that don't match a discovered attachment. An `:info` `attachment-call` diagnostic surfaces per recognised call site so users can verify the model → attachment mapping the plugin sees. Manifest declares `consumes: [{ plugin_id: "activerecord", name: :model_index, optional: true }]` so the plugin coexists with `rigor-activerecord` (no hard dependency — the discoverer reads model files independently). Integration spec at `spec/integration/plugins/activestorage_plugin_spec.rb` covers discovery (3 — singular / collection / no-attachment models), diagnostic emission (3 — recognised / unknown attribute / unknown class), and flow contribution shape (5 — singular narrowing / collection narrowing / non-Nominal decline / argumented call decline / unknown class decline). `examples/README.md` plugin count bumps to twenty.
- **`rigor-activerecord` extensions — associations / enums / scopes / validations / callbacks.** Closes the AR extension queue carried over from v0.1.3 / v0.1.4. The plugin's `ModelDiscoverer` now walks the model class body for five additional DSL families: `belongs_to` / `has_one` / `has_many` (associations), `enum` (both Rails ≤6 hash form and Rails 7+ positional form), `scope`, `validates` (+ the `validates_*_of` shorthand family), and the eleven callback hooks (`before_save` / `after_create` / `around_update` / …). Each is recorded on the `ModelIndex::Entry` via dedicated slots (`associations` / `enums` / `scopes` / `validations` / `callbacks`) plus matching readers (`association(name)` / `enum?(name)` / `enum_values(name)` / `scope?(name)` / `validation?(name)` / `callback_targets`). Analyzer-side, `Model.where(enum_col: :unknown)` now surfaces a new `unknown-enum-value` `:error` diagnostic naming the declared values and a did-you-mean hint when one of the existing enum values is close. `Activerecord#flow_contribution_for` extends to instance-side navigation: when the receiver type resolves to `Nominal[Model]` and the method name matches a discovered `belongs_to` / `has_one` association, the return type narrows to `Nominal[Target] | nil`. `has_many` is intentionally NOT contributed because Rigor does not yet carry an ActiveRecord::Relation type that would be more precise than the RBS envelope. `Inflector.classify` / `.singularize` / `.camelize` ship as the inverse of the existing `tableize` / `pluralize` so the association target class can be inferred from the symbol name when `class_name:` is omitted. ADR-9 publication grows: the `:model_index` fact now carries the new per-class rows (`associations`, `enums`, `scopes`, `validations`, `callbacks`) alongside the existing `table` / `columns`, so downstream Tier-2 / Tier-3 plugins can consume the full model surface without depending on `Activerecord` carrier classes. Specs (+14): associations (6 — belongs_to/has_one/has_many records on the index, explicit `class_name:`, singular flow contribution, has_many no-contribution), enums (5 — hash form / positional form / unknown-value diagnostic / known-value silence / non-Symbol decline), scopes (1 — recorded names), validations + callbacks (2 — attribute list + callback target list).
- **`Method#curry` precision through `Type::BoundMethod` (Open Engineering Item #5, Option A).** `Inference::MethodDispatcher::MethodFolding.try_backward` recognises `:curry` alongside `:call` / `:[]` and treats it as IDENTITY on the carrier — `<bound>.curry` keeps the same `(receiver_type, method_name)` pair so a subsequent `<curried>.call` still routes through the recursive dispatch the backward fold already wires. Closes the regression where `[:to_i, :to_f, :to_sym].map { |m| "1".method(m).curry.call }` inferred to `Tuple[Dynamic[Top]×3]` (because `Method#curry` collapsed to `Nominal[Proc]` and `Proc#call` fell through to `Dynamic[Top]`); the new behaviour returns `Tuple[Constant<1>, Constant<1.0>, Constant<:"1">]`. Partially-applied forms (`.curry(n).call(a)`) lose precision and fall through to `Type::Combinator.untyped` — a faithful `Type::CurriedBoundMethod(receiver_type, method_name, accumulated_args)` carrier is reserved for a future slice when concrete user demand surfaces. Specs (+3): `.curry` keeps the BoundMethod carrier, `.curry.call` routes through the bound dispatch (zero-arg form), Tuple per-element precision survives `.curry.call` over a Symbol Array.
- **`rigor check --stats` (default ON) — end-of-run summary on stderr.** New `Rigor::Analysis::RunStats` value carrier captures four cheap-to-measure groups: **check targets** (`expand_paths` file count), **type universe** broken down into `project sig/` (declarations whose source path is under the configured `signature_paths`) vs `bundled` (RBS core + stdlib libraries + gem-bundled RBS), **gem source-walk** (ADR-10 `dependencies.source_inference` class + gem counts), and **process** (wall seconds + peak RSS). The split between "check targets" and "type universe" makes explicit that the analyser's diagnostic surface is bounded by the user-controlled `paths:`; the much larger RBS class universe is symbol-discovery, not a diagnostic surface (mirroring PHPStan's `analyse` vs `scan` distinction). Stats are written to STDERR (so `--format=json` consumers can pipe stdout cleanly without trailing text) and are ON by default; pass `--no-stats` to suppress. Collection forces the RBS env build at end-of-run so `class_decl_paths` (the new `RbsLoader#class_decl_paths` reader) can attribute each class to a real source file; the cost is one cache-warm env load (~50-200ms on warm cache, several seconds on cold cache). Sequential mode runs only — pool mode reports 0 RBS classes because the workers' envs don't cross the Ractor boundary (a Phase 4b.x follow-up). When the env is loaded from cache, `RBS::Environment` reports `<cached>` for every location's buffer name; the formatter detects this and prints `(source attribution unavailable on cache-hit runs; --no-cache surfaces it)` instead of a misleading 0-vs-total breakdown. `Analysis::Result#stats` slot exposes the carrier programmatically; `Result#to_h` embeds it under `"stats"` so `--format=json` consumers can read it too. RSS source ordering: `/proc/self/status` `VmHWM:` (Linux peak) first; `ps -o rss= -p <pid>` (macOS / BSD current RSS) fallback; `nil` when neither route works. Wall time via `Process.clock_gettime(:CLOCK_MONOTONIC)`. New `Runner#initialize` keyword `collect_stats: true` (default); the CLI's `--no-stats` flag threads `false` through so trivial-fixture runs don't warm the cache. Spec coverage in `spec/rigor/analysis/run_stats_spec.rb` (partition_classes / attribution_available? / peak_rss_bytes / format / to_h) and `spec/rigor/cli_spec.rb` § "check --stats / --no-stats" (default-on, suppression, explicit form).
- **Parallel test runner via `parallel_tests`.** New
  `bundle exec rake spec_parallel` task and the matching
  `make test-parallel` / `make verify-parallel` Makefile
  targets split the spec suite across worker processes for
  developer iteration. `PARALLEL_TEST_PROCESSORS=N` pins the
  worker count; defaults to the CPU count. Plugin registry
  state stays isolated per worker because `parallel_tests`
  uses separate processes. Sequential `make test` /
  `make verify` is unchanged so CI reproducibility is
  preserved.

### Changed

- **Ractor migration — Phase 4c: `--workers=N` / `RIGOR_RACTOR_WORKERS` / `.rigor.yml` `parallel.workers:` user-facing opt-in.** Phase 4b's `Runner.new(workers: N)` programmatic surface now reaches users via three precedence-ordered overrides: CLI `--workers=N` (most explicit) > env `RIGOR_RACTOR_WORKERS` > `.rigor.yml` `parallel.workers:` > `0` (sequential default). `Rigor::Configuration#parallel_workers` reads the YAML key (validated as a non-negative Integer; negative or non-numeric values raise `ArgumentError` so typos fail loudly). The CLI's new private `resolve_workers` method walks the precedence chain; negative env-var values clamp to `0` so a stray `-1` from a shell pipeline doesn't crash the pool spawn loop. Sequential remains the documented default — pool mode stays opt-in until the worker-side env-build stability work (Phase 4b.x, see design doc) lands. Spec coverage in `spec/rigor/configuration_spec.rb` § "parallel.workers" (default / YAML read / to_h round-trip / non-numeric error / negative error) and `spec/rigor/cli_spec.rb` § "check --workers / RIGOR_RACTOR_WORKERS / parallel.workers:" (CLI override, env fallback, config fallback, precedence order, default 0, negative env clamp). ADR-15 § "Phase 4 — Ractor worker pool" 4c flipped LANDED; design doc § "Phase 4c" expands the three-surface contract; Open Engineering Items #8 refreshed. No CLI behaviour change for users who don't opt in.
- **Ractor migration — Phase 4b: `Runner#analyze_files_in_pool` Ractor pool around `WorkerSession`.** Builds on Phase 4a — adds the actual Ractor wiring while keeping the sequential coordinator path bit-for-bit unchanged. New `Rigor::Analysis::Runner#initialize` keyword `workers: N` (default `0` = sequential); when `N > 0`, per-file analysis dispatches across a pool of `N` Ractor workers. Each worker consumes the shareable payload (`Configuration`, `cache_root` String, `Array<Plugin::Blueprint>`, `explain` Boolean), builds its OWN `WorkerSession` inside the Ractor body, and writes per-file diagnostics back to the main Ractor's mailbox via `Ractor.main.send` (Ruby 4.0+ Ractor model — `Ractor.yield` was removed; bidirectional comms now use the per-Ractor mailbox). Three message kinds: `[:prepare, diagnostics]` once at startup (coordinator keeps the FIRST snapshot only — `#prepare` is deterministic per plugin, so N workers' identical snapshots dedupe to one), `[:file, path, diagnostics]` one per analysed file, `[:done, drained_reporters]` once at exit. Diagnostic order is preserved by re-flowing the per-path result Hash through the original file order before flattening. Per-worker reporter snapshots (`RbsExtended::Reporter` + `BoundaryCrossReporter`) are merged into the runner-side accumulators via the existing `record_*` APIs, which dedupe naturally on the same keys as a single-session run would. `Environment::ClassRegistry.default` made `Ractor.make_shareable` so worker Ractors can lazy-read it without tripping `Ractor::IsolationError`; `Runner#analyze_files_in_pool` pre-warms it on the main Ractor before spawning to force the lazy-init write into the main Ractor's view. `WorkerSession` constructor no longer writes `MethodDispatcher::FileFolding.fold_platform_specific_paths` (process-global; non-main Ractor writes raise) — the Runner sets it once on the main Ractor before dispatching to workers. Audit-spec coverage in `spec/rigor/ractor_readiness_spec.rb` § "Phase 4b — Ractor pool readiness" plus an end-to-end equivalence + plugin-replay + prepare-dedup spec at `spec/rigor/analysis/runner_pool_spec.rb`. **Known limitation:** the pool spec triggers Ractor cleanup that occasionally surfaces a Bus Error in the RBS C-extension state of LATER sequential specs under Ruby 4.0 + rbs 4.0.2. The pool spec is excluded from the default `make verify` via `spec_helper.rb`'s `exclude_pattern`; opt in via `RIGOR_INCLUDE_RACTOR_POOL=1 bundle exec rspec spec/rigor/analysis/runner_pool_spec.rb` or the new `make test-ractor-pool` target. Phase 4c will address the underlying worker-side env-build stability. The legacy sequential path (`workers: 0`) remains the documented default until Phase 4c wires `RIGOR_RACTOR_WORKERS` / `.rigor.yml` `parallel.workers:`. No CLI behaviour change yet.
- **Ractor migration — Phase 4a: `Analysis::WorkerSession` per-worker substrate.** Closes the next sub-phase of ADR-15 Phase 4 ahead of any actual Ractor wiring (4b/4c). New `Rigor::Analysis::WorkerSession` value carrier takes only `Ractor.shareable?` constructor inputs (`configuration`, `cache_store`, `Array<Plugin::Blueprint>`, `explain`) and builds its OWN `Plugin::Services` + materialised `Plugin::Registry` (via `Registry.materialize`) + `DependencySourceInference::Index` + `Environment` + per-session `RbsExtended::Reporter` + `BoundaryCrossReporter` internally. Plugin `#prepare` runs once at construction; raises are captured into `#prepare_diagnostics` so the caller can drain them alongside the per-file stream. `#analyze(path)` is the per-file substrate equivalent of `Runner#analyze_file` + `plugin_emitted_diagnostics` + `explain_diagnostics`; `#drain_reporters` returns frozen reporter snapshots for end-of-pool merge. Severity-profile re-stamping is intentionally left to the caller as a per-run aggregate concern. The session itself is NOT `Ractor.shareable?` — it owns mutable plugin instances and reporters by design; the contract is that its INPUTS are shareable so a Phase 4b worker Ractor can receive them across the boundary and build its own session inside the Ractor body. `Analysis::Runner` is unchanged for v0.1.4 / earlier releases — Phase 4b will swap the per-file iteration to a Ractor pool around `WorkerSession`. Equivalence with `Runner#analyze_file` is proven by `spec/rigor/analysis/worker_session_spec.rb` (clean file, parse error, `--explain`, plugin emission, plugin prepare-error capture, plugin runtime-error isolation, reporter ownership). Audit spec `spec/rigor/ractor_readiness_spec.rb` gains a Phase 4a describe documenting the substrate contract (inputs shareable, session itself not shareable, per-session reporter ownership). Documentation: ADR-15 § "Phase 4 — Ractor worker pool" decomposes into 4a/4b/4c with 4a marked landed; design doc § "Phase 4 sub-phase decomposition" mirrors. No runtime behaviour change — this is purely an additive substrate.
- **Ractor migration — Phase 3a: `Plugin::Blueprint` + `Registry.materialize` factory.** Frozen, `Ractor.shareable?` `Rigor::Plugin::Blueprint` value carrier records `klass_name` + a deep-shareable `config` Hash; `Plugin::Registry#blueprints` exposes the aligned `Array<Blueprint>` the loader builds during `Loader.load`, and `Plugin::Registry.materialize(blueprints:, services:)` replays each blueprint through `Object.const_get + klass.new + #init`. Plugin INSTANCES intentionally stay non-shareable (per-run mutable accumulators — `rigor-sorbet`'s `@reachable_absurd_nodes` / `@reveal_type_calls` / `@assert_type_mismatches`; every Rails-plugin `*_index` Hash). The cross-Ractor pattern is ship blueprints across the boundary, materialise once per worker, own instances locally. Audit-spec coverage in `spec/rigor/ractor_readiness_spec.rb` § "Phase 3 — Plugin contract".
- **Ractor migration — Phase 2b: `Environment::Reflection` extracted.** New `Rigor::Environment::Reflection` value object captures the loader's read-only RBS query surface (`class_known?`, `instance_definition`, `singleton_definition`, `class_type_param_names`, `constant_type`, `class_ordering`, `each_known_class_name`) with frozen backing tables. The Reflection is `frozen?` but NOT `Ractor.shareable?` — the cached `RBS::Definition` objects transitively reference `RBS::Location` (C-extension state that `Ractor.make_shareable` rejects). Phase 4 worker pool sidesteps this by having each worker build its OWN Reflection from the shared `Cache::Store`. The cross-Ractor sharing point is the Store, NOT the Reflection. `Environment#reflection` lazily builds the facade on first access and caches it.
- **Ractor migration — Phase 2a: `Configuration` deep-freeze.**
  `Configuration#initialize` now `.freeze`s its `@paths` Array
  + calls `freeze` on `self` at the end, so
  `Rigor::Configuration.new(...)` is `Ractor.shareable?` at
  construction time. The audit-spec's
  `Rigor::Configuration` example flips from `skip` to a
  passing assertion. Backward-compatible — no production
  code mutates a Configuration post-construction.
- **Ractor migration — Phase 1: value-object shareability.** Every
  `Rigor::Type::*` carrier (16 classes), every `Rigor::TypeNode::*`
  parser AST node, `Cache::Descriptor`, `Analysis::FactStore`, and
  `FlowContribution` is now `Ractor.shareable?` at construction
  time. The TypeNode constructors freeze their internal `String` /
  `Array` fields so the result stays shareable even when callers
  pass non-frozen strings (e.g. parser output, dynamic key
  construction). Regression guard at
  `spec/rigor/ractor_readiness_spec.rb` enumerates the assertion
  per carrier so a future addition without matching audit coverage
  is flagged. Phase 2 (`Configuration` deep-freeze;
  `Environment` / `RbsLoader` split into frozen reflection + per-
  Ractor cache layer), Phase 3 (plugin contract), and Phase 4
  (Ractor worker pool in `Analysis::Runner`) are documented in the
  new `docs/design/20260514-ractor-migration.md` design doc;
  each phase is independently useful + independently
  revert-able. No runtime behaviour change for any consumer.
- **`Cache::Store` is now thread-safe.** A re-entrant `Monitor`
  guards `@memo` and the hit / miss / write counters, so a
  future fork / Ractor / file-walk-parallel runner can share
  one `Store` across workers without races. Backwards-compatible
  — the lock is uncontended in the single-threaded default
  path.
- **`Cache::Store` now memoises in-process** so repeated
  `fetch_or_compute` calls in the same process skip the
  disk read AND the `Marshal.load` deserialise step. The
  memo is keyed by `(producer_id, cache_key)` — content-
  derived, so cross-fixture contamination is impossible.
  Stackprof on the spec workload identified `Marshal.load`
  as 82% of wall time for repeated `Analysis::Runner`
  invocations; the in-process layer eliminates that on the
  second-and-later hit. The disk-corruption-tolerance specs
  in `spec/rigor/cache/store_spec.rb` now construct a fresh
  `Store` after corruption (mirroring the "process restart"
  scenario the tolerance contract applies to) so the memo
  does not mask the disk-read path under test.

### Performance

- **Spec suite wall-clock 162s → 27s** (about 6× on a
  12-core dev machine; about 1.6× sequential) through three
  compounding changes: per-process shared `Cache::Store` in
  `RunnerHelpers#analyze` (so RBS core/stdlib resolution
  stays warm across examples), the in-process memo layer
  above, and the `parallel_tests` runner. The `Rigor::Analysis::Runner`
  spec — the dominant 132-example group at 82s/run — drops
  to 20s.

## [0.1.4] - 2026-05-14

The fourteenth preview. Theme: **finish the ADR-10 / ADR-11 / ADR-13 deferred queues, land ADR-14 `rigor sig-gen` end-to-end, and add the `Type::BoundMethod` carrier so `Object#method(:sym).call` round-trips with precision**. v0.1.3 cut the ADR-10 / ADR-11 / ADR-13 primary surfaces; v0.1.4 closes every remaining "Open questions" follow-up (ADR-10 slice 5c `dynamic.dependency-source.boundary-cross` under `mode: :full`; ADR-11 per-call-site assertion gating; ADR-13 parser admits Symbol / String literals + `|`-unions at type-arg position). ADR-14 `rigor sig-gen` ships all five slices plus four rounds of self-dogfood follow-ups, ending in clean `make verify` against Rigor's own `sig/`. The new `Type::BoundMethod` precision tier covers `Object#method(:sym).call` / `.()` / `[]`. The eighteen worked plugin examples land in `examples/` (Rails Tier 1 + Tier 2 + Tier 3 + `rigor-sorbet` + `rigor-typescript-utility-types`). Lint-side, `.rubocop.yml` Maxes were calibrated against the working code so ~310 in-file `# rubocop:disable` markers dropped to ~157.

### Added

- **`Method` carrier (`Type::BoundMethod`) — `Object#method(:sym).call` round-trips with precision.** New `Rigor::Type::BoundMethod` carrier stores the bound `(receiver_type, method_name)` pair. Two folding tiers in `Rigor::Inference::MethodDispatcher::MethodFolding` consume / produce it: the **forward** fold lifts `<receiver>.method(:sym)` (or `.method("sym")`) into the carrier when the argument is a `Constant<Symbol>` / `Constant<String>`; the **backward** fold rewrites `<bound>.call(...)` / `<bound>.()` / `<bound>[]` into a recursive `MethodDispatcher.dispatch` on the bound `(receiver_type, method_name)` so every downstream tier (constant folding, shape dispatch, RBS, plugin contributions) sees the original call site's context. The forward tier sits in `dispatch_precise_tiers`; the backward tier runs at the top of `dispatch` so RBS never sees a `BoundMethod` receiver (RBS would otherwise collapse to `Method#call: (*untyped) -> untyped`). `RbsDispatch.receiver_descriptor` falls a `BoundMethod` receiver through to `["Method", :instance, []]` so reflective Method members (`#owner` / `#name` / `#arity` / …) still resolve via RBS — the carrier is a precision-bearing alias for `Nominal[Method]`, not a replacement. The carrier erases to `"Method"` at the RBS boundary so values passed into typed parameters continue to interop. Example: `[:to_i, :to_f, :to_sym].map { |m| "1".method(m).call }` now infers to `Tuple[Constant<1>, Constant<1.0>, Constant<:"1">]` instead of `Tuple[Dynamic[Top]×3]`. New `Combinator.bound_method_of(receiver_type, method_name)` factory. Specs (+13): carrier value-object coverage (5 — fields, equality, validation, describe, erase), dispatcher forward fold (3 — bare Symbol, String coercion, decline on non-literal arg), backward fold (4 — `.call`, `.()`, `[]`, per-element Tuple fold), drift-snapshot pin of the new factory. `sig/rigor/type.rbs` grows a `class BoundMethod` block + the `Combinator.bound_method_of` factory. `spec/integration/fixtures/proc_catalog.rb` updates the `5.method(:+)` assertion from `"Method"` to `"Method<5#+>"` — the precise carrier IS the new behavior; existing reflective-method assertions (`m.owner`, `m.name`, `m.arity`) keep their pre-existing answers via the RBS fallback path.
- **ADR-10 slice 5c — boundary-cross diagnostic under `mode: :full`.** Closes the last "Open questions" follow-up on ADR-10. The `Index` value object now exposes per-class mode awareness through `#mode_for(class_name)` / `#full_mode?(class_name)` by chaining the existing `class_to_gem` reverse-lookup with the new `gem_modes` table (populated by `Builder#absorb_resolved` from each `Resolved#mode`). After `RbsDispatch.try_dispatch` resolves a call, `MethodDispatcher.dispatch` consults `Environment#boundary_cross_reporter` and records a `dynamic.dependency-source.boundary-cross` event when (a) the receiver class belongs to a `mode: :full` opt-in gem AND (b) the gem's source catalog has the same `(class_name, method_name)` AND (c) the RBS-side result is not the trivial `Dynamic[Top]` envelope. RBS still wins on the dispatch result — the `:info` diagnostic is purely advisory so the user can audit whether the RBS contract and the gem's source have drifted. Reporter dedupes per `(class_name, method_name, gem_name)` so a method called from many call sites yields one diagnostic. `Analysis::Runner` drains the reporter at end-of-run; severity profile re-stamps the rule per project taste. Implementation: new `Rigor::Analysis::DependencySourceInference::BoundaryCrossReporter` class (mutex-guarded, `Entry` Data with `class_name` / `method_name` / `gem_name` / `rbs_display`), `Environment#boundary_cross_reporter` attr_reader threaded through `Environment.new` + `Environment.for_project`, dispatcher helper `record_boundary_cross_if_applicable` + `boundary_cross_class_name` preflight. The Builder gains a `BuildState` accumulator to keep the per-build mutation under the ABC budget. Specs (+8): index `mode_for` / `full_mode?` coverage (+3), reporter coverage (+5 — empty, record, dedup, distinct-method, frozen-snapshot), dispatcher records on `mode: :full` + does NOT record on `mode: :when_missing` or catalog-miss (+3). Public-API drift snapshots grow `boundary_cross_reporter()` on `ENVIRONMENT_INSTANCE`, `key:boundary_cross_reporter` on `ENVIRONMENT_SINGLETON`'s `for_project` signature, and the two new `UNSIGNED_NAMESPACES` entries (`BoundaryCrossReporter` + its `Entry` Data); `sig/rigor/environment.rbs` adds the matching `attr_reader` + keyword. `docs/internal-spec/dependency-source-inference.md` swaps the "Pending" row + Open Questions item for the landed state.
- **ADR-13 follow-up — RBS::Extended Symbol / String literal tokens + literal unions.** Closes the `docs/CURRENT_WORK.md` engineering item that ADR-13 slice 6's integration spec previously worked around with synthetic AST. Three new `TypeNode` AST carriers — `SymbolLiteral(value: Symbol)`, `StringLiteral(value: String)`, and `Union(nodes: Array)` — flow through the resolver pass as `Type::Constant<value>` / `Type::Union` carriers. `ImportedRefinements::Parser` grows two regexes (`SYMBOL_LITERAL` matches `:identifier_with_optional_predicate_suffix`; `STRING_LITERAL` matches `"text"` without escape sequences — sufficient for TS-style key unions) and refactors `parse_type_arg_ast` into a `parse_single_type_arg_ast` + literal-union folding loop. The resolver translates each leaf literal via the shared `LITERAL_AST_NODES`-driven `case` arm and folds unions via `Type::Combinator.union`; any failing member collapses the whole union to nil so callers fall back to the underlying RBS-declared type. `Pick[T, :a | :b]` / `pick_of[Shape, "a" | "b"]` now round-trip end-to-end through `ImportedRefinements.parse`. Public-API drift snapshot grows `TYPE_NODE_SYMBOL_LITERAL_INSTANCE` / `TYPE_NODE_STRING_LITERAL_INSTANCE` / `TYPE_NODE_UNION_INSTANCE`; `UNSIGNED_NAMESPACES` gains the three new entries. Specs (+10): three TypeNode value-object specs (symbol-literal, string-literal, union — type/equality/validation coverage), three parser specs (symbol-only / string-only / mixed-Symbol-String union tokenisation), one end-to-end resolution check, plus three drift-snapshot describe blocks.
- **rigor-sorbet — per-call-site assertion gating (last ADR-11 deferred item).** Sorbet itself only enforces type errors at `# typed: true`+; the harvest-time `enforce_sigil` gate (default `true`) already mirrored that for cataloged sigs, but assertion recognisers (`T.let` / `T.cast` / `T.must` / `T.bind` / `T.assert_type!` / `T.reveal_type` / `T.unsafe`) used to fire regardless of the caller's sigil. They now go through `assertion_enforced_here?(scope)` which consults the per-file sigil cache (`@sigil_by_path`, populated during harvest before the `# typed: ignore` short-circuit) and returns false (gate closed) when the caller's sigil is `:false` / `:ignore` / unknown. Three failing-open fallbacks keep the spec ergonomics honest: `scope.source_path == nil` (synthetic call sites), path not in the harvest map (out-of-tree call site), AND `enforce_sigil: false` config all open the gate. New `Rigor::Scope#source_path` slot + `with_source_path` builder land first; `Analysis::Runner#analyze_file` stamps it once per file, every `with_*` rebuild + `build_joined_scope` propagates it. Public-API drift snapshot grows `source_path()` / `with_source_path(req:path)` on `SCOPE_INSTANCE` and adds `key:source_path` to `SCOPE_SINGLETON`'s `empty` signature; `sig/rigor/scope.rbs` adds the matching `attr_reader source_path: String?` + `with_source_path` declaration. Specs (+4): assertion recogniser fires at `# typed: true`, suppressed at `# typed: false`, suppressed at sigil-less (Sorbet treats as `:false`), AND fires regardless of sigil when `enforce_sigil: false`. The pre-existing `reveal-type` / `assert-type-mismatch` diagnostic specs gain explicit `# typed: true` prefixes so they continue exercising the active assertion path.
- **ADR-14 follow-up — sig-gen self-dogfood gaps (c) / (d) / (e) closed.** Closes the three remaining gaps from `docs/CURRENT_WORK.md` Open Engineering Items #3 (gaps (a) and (b) landed earlier in `b6d3286`). **Gap (c) — nested-class-inside-class emission.** `Writer#render_new_file` now builds a namespace tree keyed by qualified-name segments (with intermediate ancestors inserted automatically) instead of `group_by(&:class_name)`. When one candidate's `class_name` is a strict prefix of another (`GemResolver` vs `GemResolver::Resolved`), the child class is nested inside the parent's block rather than emitted as a sibling top-level wrap. The tree node renderer (`render_tree_node`) defaults each segment's keyword from `namespace_kinds[qualified_name]` falling back to `:class` for method-bearing leaves / `:module` for intermediate (children-only) segments; the previous `wrap_in_modules` flat-pass helper is retired. **Gap (e) — `Const = Data.define(...)` / `Struct.new(...)` recognition.** `Generator#walk_defs` recognises `Prism::ConstantWriteNode` whose RHS is `Data.define(...)` or `Struct.new(...)`, records the fully-qualified constant name in `@class_shells` (carried through every candidate via the new `MethodCandidate#class_shells` slot) AND `@namespace_kinds`. The walker continues descending into the RHS so a trailing `do ... end` block carrying method defs is still walked. The writer's new `collect_class_shells` / `merge_class_shells` consume the shell set: `render_new_file` emits an empty `class Foo\nend` tree node at the correct nested position; `update_existing` injects a missing shell into the nearest existing ancestor via `insert_class_shell` (walks up from leaf segment, anchors at the deepest existing decl, splices the missing-segment subtree in just before the anchor's closing `end`). The hand-stub `class Unresolvable; end` in `sig/rigor/analysis/dependency_source_inference/gem_resolver.rbs` is no longer required — sig-gen now emits it automatically. **Gap (d) — `&:symbol` block-pass dispatcher recognition.** `Inference::ExpressionTyper#block_return_for` now handles `Prism::BlockArgumentNode` carrying a `SymbolNode` by dispatching the symbol on the expected block param type (computed via `MethodDispatcher.expected_block_param_types`) — `Symbol#to_proc`'s `{ |x| x.method_name }` desugaring made literal at the analyzer surface. Falls back to `Dynamic[Top]` when the inner dispatch declines, when the param type is unknown, or when the block argument is `&proc_local` / `&method(:foo)` / similar non-SymbolNode shapes; the fall-back is non-nil so the outer `MethodDispatcher.dispatch` STILL signals block-presence to `OverloadSelector` via `block_required: !block_type.nil?`. `Hash#transform_values(&:freeze)` now resolves through the block-bearing overload (returns `Hash[K, V]`) instead of the no-block one (had returned `Enumerator[V, Hash[K, untyped]]`). Verified end-to-end on `Analysis::CheckRules::IvarWriteCollector#collect`: sig-gen now classifies the method as `:new_method` returning `Hash[untyped, untyped]` instead of the previous `Enumerator` artefact. Specs (+9): writer (+2, nested-class-inside-class emission and update_existing class-shell injection), generator (+3, Data.define + Struct.new shell recognition + non-Data assignments not registered), expression typer (+2, `&:freeze` on Hash#transform_values + `&:to_s` on Array#map both route through the block-bearing overload). `MethodCandidate` gains a new optional `class_shells:` keyword (frozen Array of qualified-name Strings).
- **ADR-14 follow-up — namespace-kind tracking + `module_function` recognition (gap #3 a/b).** Closes gaps (a) and (b) from the `lib/rigor/analysis/` dogfood backlog in `docs/CURRENT_WORK.md` Open Engineering Items #3. `Generator#walk_defs` now records every encountered `ClassNode` / `ModuleNode` segment into `@namespace_kinds[qualified_name] => :class | :module`, AND tracks `module_function` (no-arg) directives in module bodies so subsequent `def`s land in `@module_function_methods`. The per-file maps thread through every `MethodCandidate` (new `namespace_kinds:` keyword), letting the Writer's `wrap_in_modules` emit `class Foo` vs `module Foo` per segment kind instead of defaulting every intermediate to `module`. The renderer's new `method_def_prefix` helper picks `def self?.name` (RBS spelling for `module_function`-style dual instance + singleton dispatch) when the candidate is module_function-marked. `walk_defs` now special-cases `Prism::StatementsNode` (`walk_statements`) so `module_function` followed by sibling `def`s correctly affects later defs but not earlier ones — state carries forward within the body. The `module_function_active` flag is reset to `false` when entering nested `class` / `module` bodies (`walk_namespace_body`) per Ruby's module-only semantics.
