Dogfood verification: reverting `sig/rigor/analysis/dependency_source_inference/gem_resolver.rbs` and re-running `rigor sig-gen --write` against `lib/rigor/analysis/dependency_source_inference/gem_resolver.rb` now produces the correct `module GemResolver` (kind detection) + `def self?.resolve` / `def self?.locate_gem_spec` (module_function recognition) automatically. Only the small `class Unresolvable; end` stub (a `Const = Data.define(...)`-assigned class — separate ADR-14 gap-c/Data-define-as-class) still needs a hand-add. Specs (+6): namespace_kinds map records `:module` / `:class` per segment, `def self?.name` rendered for module-function methods, defs BEFORE `module_function` stay plain, `module_function` doesn't propagate into nested classes, Writer uses candidate's `namespace_kinds` for segment keywords, Writer defaults to `module` when a segment is unmapped.
- **ADR-14 dogfood — `lib/rigor/analysis/` coverage + nested-syntax emission.** Fourth-round dogfood expansion. Sig-gen now uses nested `module Rigor; module Analysis; … class Leaf; end; end; end`-shaped output when creating a new sig file (`Writer#render_nested_class` + `wrap_in_modules`) instead of the flat `class Rigor::Analysis::DependencySourceInference::GemResolver` spelling that earlier emissions used. The flat form failed Steep's constant resolver whenever an intermediate path segment (`Rigor::Analysis::DependencySourceInference`) wasn't declared as `module …` anywhere else in `sig/` (RBS's `RBS::Resolver::ConstantResolver` raises `not found by ::Rigor::A::B::C` for the missing parent). Nested syntax declares every intermediate `module` block by construction, matching how `sig/rigor.rbs` and `sig/rigor/inference/builtins.rbs` are hand-authored. Four new sig files land in `sig/rigor/analysis/`: `check_rules/always_truthy_condition_collector.rbs` (2 methods), `check_rules/dead_assignment_collector.rbs` (2 methods), `dependency_source_inference/gem_resolver.rbs` (3 methods — hand-fixed: leaf is `module GemResolver` with `def self?.resolve` for `module_function` semantics + inner `class Resolved` + stub `class Unresolvable` for the Data.define unresolvable case), `dependency_source_inference/index.rbs` (1 method). Four files deliberately deferred: `check_rules/ivar_write_collector.rb` (sig-gen inferred `Enumerator` from a `Hash#transform_values(&:freeze)` whose block isn't recognised by the dispatcher; `rigor check` correctly flagged the round-trip mismatch), `check_rules.rb` (26 candidates with several `BlockNode`-barrier artefacts like `constant_class_name: () -> nil` missing iterator-block returns), `dependency_source_inference/walker.rb` (`walk_root: () -> false` missing the `return true if accumulator.size >= budget` inside the `each` block), and `runner.rb` / `result.rb` / `diagnostic.rb` / `rule_catalog.rb` (consolidated coverage already in `sig/rigor.rbs` is more specific than the stub would be). The new gaps queue as ADR-14 follow-ups in `docs/CURRENT_WORK.md`: kind tracking (module vs class on each segment), `module_function` recognition, nested-class emission inside a class (vs as a sibling module wrap), and `&:freeze`-style block-pass recognition in the dispatcher. `make verify` clean: 2826 examples, no rubocop offenses, `rigor check lib` reports zero diagnostics. `make steep-check` clean.
- **ADR-14 follow-up — 1:N consolidated sig file layout for the writer.** Closes the long-running path-mapper limitation surfaced repeatedly through the dogfood passes (most visibly when `sig-gen --write` on `lib/rigor/type/bot.rb` created `sig/rigor/type/bot.rbs` alongside the existing consolidated `sig/rigor/type.rbs`, producing duplicate class declarations that `RBS::DefinitionBuilder` silently failed on). New `Rigor::SigGen::LayoutIndex` pre-scans every `.rbs` file under the configured `signature_paths` (auto-detecting `<project_root>/sig` when nil) and builds a `qualified_class_name → sig_file_path` index. It walks nested module / class declarations recursively, so a deeply-nested `Rigor::Type::Nominal` declared inside `module Rigor; module Type; class Nominal; …; end; end; end` registers at its fully-qualified name. First-found wins on cross-file collisions. `PathMapper#target_for` gains a `class_name:` keyword that, when supplied, consults the LayoutIndex first and routes the candidate to the consolidated file when a match exists; otherwise it falls back to the 1:1 lib-to-sig mirror. The writer gains `write_all(candidates)` that groups candidates by target file (after per-candidate resolution) and processes each target as a unit — multiple candidates from different source files now correctly land in the same consolidated target rather than fighting over separate mirror files. `Writer#find_class_decl` walks the parsed decl tree recursively (tracking the enclosing module/class prefix) so it can match a fully-qualified candidate `class_name` against the nested-syntax declaration inside the file. Legacy `Writer#write(source_path, candidates)` stays for backward compatibility with existing specs. The CLI command (`SigGenCommand#dispatch_write`) now constructs the `LayoutIndex` from `configuration.signature_paths` and threads it through. Specs (+8): `spec/rigor/sig_gen/layout_index_spec.rb` (6 examples — top-level, nested, unknown class, auto-detect default, parse-error tolerance, first-found-wins) + `spec/rigor/sig_gen/writer_spec.rb` (2 examples — single-class routing to consolidated, multi-source grouping into one target). Dogfood verification: reverting `sig/rigor/inference/builtins/method_catalog.rbs` to the all-`untyped` form and running `rigor sig-gen --params=observed --observe=lib --write --overwrite lib/rigor/inference/builtins/method_catalog.rb` produces the precise stub automatically (consolidated routing → existing-decl detection → `tightens_untyped?` matches → replaced via the broadened `--overwrite`). The `Rigor::Type::Nominal` case (existing has more specific declaration than the `untyped` stub) correctly skips as user-authored — no spurious top-level `class Rigor::Type::Nominal` appended to `sig/rigor/type.rbs`.
- **ADR-14 follow-up — broaden `--overwrite` to cover NEW_METHOD candidates that tighten existing `untyped` positions.** The previous commit (observation-aware initialize stubs) demonstrated that `MethodCatalog#initialize` could be improved from `(path: untyped, ?mutating_selectors: untyped) -> void` to `(path: String, ?mutating_selectors: Hash[String, Set[untyped]]) -> void` — but `--write --overwrite` declined to apply the change automatically. Reason: the initialize-stub classifier produces `NEW_METHOD` (not `TIGHTER_RETURN`) and the writer's replacement logic only handled `TIGHTER_RETURN` conflicts. Hand-application was the workaround. This commit fixes that. The writer's `replace_tighter_returns` is renamed to `replace_eligible_conflicts` and gains a second classification branch via `eligible_for_replacement?`: `NEW_METHOD` candidates are also replaceable when `tightens_untyped?` proves the new RBS has strictly fewer bare `untyped` tokens than the existing source-side declaration. The check uses word-boundary `\buntyped\b` matching so identifier substrings don't count. Same-count and untyped-introducing cases stay user-authored (correctly skipped). Without `--overwrite` the behaviour is unchanged regardless of classification — the gate stays explicit. Specs (+4): NEW_METHOD with fewer untypeds is replaced, same-count cases are skipped, untyped-introducing cases are skipped, no-`--overwrite` defaults remain user-authored. Dogfood verification: reverting `sig/rigor/inference/builtins/method_catalog.rbs` to the all-`untyped` form and re-running `rigor sig-gen --params=observed --observe=lib --write --overwrite lib/rigor/inference/builtins/method_catalog.rb` now produces the precise stub automatically — the hand-edit step from commit `393621d` is no longer needed for this style of tightening.
- **ADR-14 follow-up — observation-aware `initialize` stubs (kwargs + `.new` → `:initialize` routing).** Closes the three orthogonal gaps queued in `docs/CURRENT_WORK.md` after the user pointed out that `sig/rigor/inference/builtins/method_catalog.rbs` was emitted with `def initialize: (path: untyped, ?mutating_selectors: untyped) -> void` — every keyword position fell back to `untyped` even though every call site (`MethodCatalog.new(path: ..., mutating_selectors: ...)`) used precise keyword args. **(A)** `ObservationCollector#observation_key` routes `Class.new(args)` under `[Class, :initialize]` instead of `[Class, :new]` — `.new` → `#initialize` implicit binding is now visible at the observation layer. The check is gated on `Type::Singleton` receivers + `:new` method name; ordinary instance-method calls keep the existing `[Class, method_name]` routing. **(B)** New `Rigor::SigGen::ObservedCall` value object (`lib/rigor/sig_gen/observed_call.rb`) carries `positional: Array[Type]` AND `keyword: Hash[Symbol, Type]` separately. `ObservationCollector#collect_args` partitions arguments by AST node type — `KeywordHashNode` elements get unpacked into the `keyword` slot via `read_keyword_pairs`, other args go to `positional`, splat / block / forwarding still abort the whole observation. `Generator#initialize` normalises legacy plain-Array observations through `ObservedCall.from` so earlier spec fixtures keep working without modification. **(C)** `Generator#render_initialize_param_list` now consults `@observations[[class_name, :initialize]]` filtered by positional-arity-in-range, then renders each positional slot as the per-position union of observed types (fall back to `untyped`) and each keyword slot as the per-keyword union (fall back to `untyped`). The shape mirroring (required / optional / rest / keyword / keyword-rest / block) and `void` return are unchanged. Dogfood verification: `rigor sig-gen --params=observed --observe=lib lib/rigor/inference/builtins/method_catalog.rb` now produces `def initialize: (path: String, ?mutating_selectors: Hash[String, Set[untyped]]) -> void` — the precise stub the user requested. Specs (+5): keyword-only observation captured, `.new` positional-only routed to `:initialize`, `.new` kwarg-only routed, stub fills observed kwargs (`name: "Alice", ?count: 42`), stub fills observed positionals. The slice-3 `def`-method observation path also migrates from the legacy `Array[Array[Type]]` shape to `Array[ObservedCall]`; existing specs unchanged.
- **RBS coverage sweep for `lib/rigor/plugin/` and `lib/rigor/inference/builtins/`.** Hand-wrote [`sig/rigor/plugin/loader.rbs`](sig/rigor/plugin/loader.rbs) to declare `Plugin::Loader.self.load(configuration:, services:, requirer:)` — without it Steep had matched against `Kernel#load(::String, ?(::Module | bool)) -> bool` and emitted `MethodParameterMismatch` on the keyword-only shape. Ran `rigor sig-gen --write` against `lib/rigor/plugin/` and accepted the 11 generated sigs after three hand fix-ups: `fact_store.rbs` got the missing outer `Rigor::Plugin::FactStore` namespace (the inner `Conflict` was emitted alone), `manifest.rbs` had `validate_config` widened from `([String] | [])` to `Array[String]` (sig-gen inferred too narrowly from finite observations), and `base.rbs` / `load_error.rbs` got the missing `self.manifest` declaration + `< StandardError` inheritance + `plugin_ref` / `cause` / `reason` reader exposure. Added [`sig/rigor/plugin.rbs`](sig/rigor/plugin.rbs) for the top-level `Rigor::Plugin` module's `register` / `registered_for` / `registered` / `unregister!` singleton surface. For `lib/rigor/inference/` ran the same sweep but kept only the two `builtins/*.rbs` sigs: every other auto-generated file conflicted with the canonical [`sig/rigor/inference.rbs`](sig/rigor/inference.rbs) (either kind mismatch `class` vs `module`, or `Non-overloading method definition` duplicates), and the `method_dispatcher/*.rbs` sigs caused 14 new `call.undefined-method` false positives because sig-gen emitted incomplete public-surface coverage for the `module_function`-style sub-dispatchers (missing `try_dispatch` / `try_fold` singleton entries). Skipped `lib/rigor/type/` entirely — [`sig/rigor/type.rbs`](sig/rigor/type.rbs) is already canonical and complete (279 lines covering 14 Type classes); the autogen output was pure duplication. `Inference::MethodDispatcher#try_discovered_method` lost its `rbs_class_known?` early-return guard: with the autogen sigs naming many classes that don't enumerate every method, the gate was preventing the discovered-method tier from firing on calls whose receiver class IS in RBS but whose specific method isn't. The dispatcher's tier ordering (RBS → dependency-source → discovered → user-class) is unchanged; RBS-known methods still win because RBS dispatch returns non-nil upstream of the discovered tier. Net result: Steep clean (`No type error detected. 🍵`), `rigor check lib` clean (no diagnostics), `make verify` clean (2811 specs, 0 failures, RuboCop clean). RBS coverage: `sig/` 16 → 31 files; 12 namespaces removed from `PublicApiDriftSnapshots::UNSIGNED_NAMESPACES` (the per-namespace RBS-coverage backlog gauge).
- **ADR-14 — RBS signature generation from inference (proposed).** New ADR proposes `rigor sig-gen`, a CLI subcommand that emits RBS skeletons from Rigor's inference results. The command classifies every candidate method as `new-file` / `new-method` / `tighter-return` / `equivalent` / `skipped`; the `--params` policy (`untyped` default, `observed` opt-in via `--observe=PATH...`, `observed-strict` reserved) enforces the ADR-5 robustness asymmetry at the CLI surface (returns may tighten by default per clause 1, parameters require explicit opt-in per clause 2). The `tighter-return` path reuses the `def.return-type-mismatch` strict-acceptance predicate so the generator never emits a tightening the analyzer itself would flag as unsound. Positioned as the project's standard tool for closing RBS coverage gaps; replaces ad-hoc / freehand RBS authoring. Implementation slicing covers five steps (MVP `--print` / `--diff` for `def` methods → `--write` with `RBS::Parser` merge → caller-side `--params=observed` collector → `attr_*` / `define_method` / singleton-side defs → `rigor-rspec` integration + handbook). The `sig.*` diagnostic family is reserved for slice-by-slice telemetry. CLAUDE.md ADR index updated.
- **ADR-14 follow-up — Steep cross-check + initialize-stub emission + paren-wrap return unions.** Ran `make steep-check` against the dogfood-expanded `sig/`. Surfaced 12 warnings: 2 pre-existing (`Plugin::Loader.self.load(configuration:, services:, requirer:)` vs the inherited `Kernel#load(String, ?(Module | bool)) -> bool` — `.load` class method conflicting with `Kernel#load`; needs a `class Loader` declaration to override, queued as a separate follow-up) and 10 caused by ADR-14's sig-gen for CLI command classes. Three orthogonal generator-quality issues identified and resolved: **(1) initialize-stub emission**: when a class has a non-trivial `initialize(argv:, ...)` constructor, the previous `initialize_excludes?` guard skipped emission entirely. The partial-class sig then triggered `Ruby::MethodParameterMismatch` because Steep falls back to `Object#initialize: () -> void` for the inherited declaration, which mismatches the runtime parameter shape. `Generator#initialize_excludes?` now only skips DEFAULT (zero-parameter) constructors — non-default constructors route through a new `initialize_stub_candidate` that emits `def initialize: (<shape>) -> void` with `untyped` types at every position. The shape mirrors the runtime parameter list (required / optional / rest / keyword / keyword-rest / block) via `render_initialize_param_list`. **(2) Paren-wrap return unions**: bare top-level unions in return position (`def m: () -> 0 | 1`) fail the RBS parser — the trailing `| 1` isn't a valid method-type start. `render_rbs_line` now consults `paren_wrap_union` which detects top-level ` | ` (ignoring brackets / parens / braces depth) and wraps in `(...)` only when needed. `render_attr_rbs_line` gets the same treatment for the return side. **(3) Path-mapper consolidation limitation**: the writer's 1:1 lib-to-sig mapping (`lib/rigor/cli/type_of_command.rb` → `sig/rigor/cli/type_of_command.rbs`) duplicates declarations when `sig/rigor.rbs` already consolidates the per-class signatures. The first-pass regeneration created duplicate `TypeOfCommand` / `TypeScanCommand` class declarations triggering `RBS::DuplicatedMethodDefinition`. Worked around by deleting the redundant files and trimming `type_scan_command.rbs` to just the `ScanAccumulator` helper. The renderer classes (`TypeOfRenderer` / `TypeScanRenderer`) also had to be dropped — their public `render` body types as `untyped` (untyped-return skip), leaving the new sig with only an `initialize` stub and no `render` method, which broke `rigor check` lookups. Final state: 5 CLI sig files (diff/explain/sig_gen command + type_scan_command/ScanAccumulator), all clean under both `rigor check` AND Steep. Steep down from 12 → 2 (both pre-existing). Specs (+2): keyword-arg constructor stub, positional-arg constructor stub.
- **ADR-14 dogfood — tighten 6 literal-return RBS declarations in `sig/rigor/type.rbs` + guard against computed-Constant artefacts.** Applied 6 of 7 literal-return tightenings surfaced by the Fix-A dogfood pass: `Type::Top#erase_to_rbs: () -> "top"` / `#inspect: () -> "#<Rigor::Type::Top>"`, `Type::Bot#erase_to_rbs: () -> "bot"` / `#inspect: () -> "#<Rigor::Type::Bot>"`, `Type::Dynamic#erase_to_rbs: () -> "untyped"`, `Type::IntegerRange#erase_to_rbs: () -> "Integer"`. Each method body is literally `"<that-string>"` and only that string — these are ADR-5 clause 1 precision wins that downstream RBS consumers (Steep, IDE tooltips, gem-published `sig/` trees) directly benefit from. The 7th candidate (`Inference::FallbackTracer#size: Integer -> 0`) was rejected: its body is `@events.size`, where `@events` is initialised to `[]` and never explicitly assigned elsewhere at the class-ivar pre-pass level. The empty-tuple cardinality folds `.size` to `Constant<0>` — the carrier knows the answer exactly for the initial state — but the runtime contract is `Integer` because callers add events through `#record(...)`. The `Generator#tighter?` predicate gains a fourth lenience-loss guard: `computed_literal_tightening?` refuses to tighten when the inferred type is a `Type::Constant` AND the body's last expression is NOT a directly-authored literal node (`Prism::IntegerNode` / `FloatNode` / `StringNode` / `SymbolNode` / `TrueNode` / `FalseNode` / `NilNode`). Direct authored literals (`def status; 200; end`, `def kind; :singleton; end`) keep the tighter-return classification; computed Constants from ivar reads, method calls, arithmetic, and similar chains route to equivalent. Path-mapper limitation surfaced during application: `lib/rigor/type/bot.rb` maps to `sig/rigor/type/bot.rbs` but the existing rigor `sig/` consolidates per-directory (`sig/rigor/type.rbs` holds all `Type::*` declarations). The writer created new sig files alongside the existing consolidated one, producing duplicate class declarations that RBS::DefinitionBuilder couldn't reconcile (silent — `Reflection.instance_method_definition` returned nil). Worked around by reverting the writer-created files and hand-applying the tightenings to the existing consolidated `sig/rigor/type.rbs` (sig-gen's proposed text dropped in verbatim, user-authorised per the rigor hand-edit policy). A separate ADR-14 follow-up tracks "1:N consolidated sig file layout" as a writer enhancement. Specs (+2): direct-literal body still tightens, computed-Constant body stays equivalent.
- **ADR-14 follow-up — `Constant#erase_to_rbs` preserves RBS Literal precision.** Third-round dogfood pass exposed that `determine_exit: () -> 0 | 1` (and `CLI::EXIT_USAGE = 64` style numeric / symbolic / string constants) collapsed to `Integer` / `Symbol` / `String` at the RBS boundary, losing the precision the carrier had already proven. `Type::Constant#erase_to_rbs` now emits the matching RBS `Literal` form for Integer (positive AND negative — RBS accepts both), Symbol, and String values: `Constant<64>` erases to `64`, `Constant<:foo>` to `:foo`, `Constant<"hi">` to `"hi"`. `Constant<Float>` / `Constant<Range>` / `Constant<Rational>` / `Constant<Complex>` / `Constant<Regexp>` / `Constant<Pathname>` keep their pre-existing widen-to-class-name behaviour because RBS rejects their literal spellings as syntax errors. `Rigor::Inference::RbsTypeTranslator#translate_literal` already maps the parsed RBS Literal back to `Constant<value>`, so the round-trip is lossless: a sig file declaring `def m: () -> 64` re-imports as `Constant<64>` exactly. `dump.type` diagnostic display, `rigor type-of` output, and sig-gen emission all immediately benefit — the dogfood case `determine_exit` now emits as `() -> 0 | 1` instead of `() -> Integer`. The Bug-1 Union dedupe still applies to Constants whose erased forms collide on a non-literal envelope (`Constant<1.5>` / `Constant<2.5>` both erase to `Float` → deduped to `Float`); distinct literals stay distinct (`"a" | "b"` is the precise union spelling). Specs updated across `spec/rigor/inference/expression_typer_spec.rb` (3 cases), `spec/rigor/type/combinator_spec.rb` (2 cases + 1 new dedup case), `spec/rigor/sig_gen/generator_spec.rb` (~8 fixtures), `spec/rigor/sig_gen/observation_collector_spec.rb` (5 fixtures), `spec/rigor/cli_spec.rb` (~10 fixtures). Project-wide tighter-return suggestions against Rigor's own `lib/` now show 7 legitimate literal-return tightenings (e.g. `Type::Bot#erase_to_rbs: String -> "bot"`, `Type::Top#erase_to_rbs: String -> "top"`) — these are precision wins under ADR-5 clause 1 but gated by `--overwrite` for opt-in adoption; the dogfood policy on whether to lock implementations to their current literal return values is a separate decision.
- **ADR-14 follow-ups — visibility filter, initialize skip, explicit-return union, dogfood expansion to `lib/rigor/cli/`.** Four generator-quality improvements surfaced by the second-round dogfood pass on Rigor's own `lib/rigor/cli/` directory, plus the resulting sig-file additions. **(1) Visibility-aware emission**: `Generator` now skips private / protected instance methods by default — they're implementation details, not part of the type contract downstream consumers (Steep, IDE, gem users) read. The new `--include-private` CLI flag restores the earlier "emit everything" behaviour. The check consults `Scope#discovered_method_visibility`; singleton-side defs are always emitted because they have no Ruby visibility surface today. **(2) `initialize` exclusion**: instance-side `def initialize` is always skipped — Ruby's `initialize` return value is conventionally `void`, and the body-typing path types the last expression (often an ivar assignment whose rvalue happens to be `[]` / `{}`), which produces nonsense return types for `sig/`. Singleton-side `def self.initialize` (rare; class-method, not the constructor) is still emitted. Users who want a specific `initialize` sig hand-author it; the inherited `Object#initialize` RBS fallback covers the default lookup. **(3) Explicit-return union**: the body-typer now walks the def body for `Prism::ReturnNode`s and unions their value types with the implicit-return expression's type. Earlier output produced single-branch artefacts like `parse_options: () -> nil` (the actual runtime return is `options | nil`) and `find: () -> V` (actually `V | nil` via `return nil unless ...`); the walk catches both. `Prism::DefNode` / `LambdaNode` / `BlockNode` are barrier nodes so returns from nested blocks / inner defs do not leak into the enclosing method's return type. Bare `return` is treated as `Constant<nil>`. Multi-arg `return a, b` (tuple-packing) contributes no type to keep the implementation focused. **(4) Erasure-aware `dynamic_top?` skip**: post-explicit-return-union, the carrier ends up as a `Union[Dynamic, ...]` whose Bug-1 erasure rule renders it as `untyped`. The `:untyped_return` skip check now treats any carrier whose `erase_to_rbs` is `"untyped"` as the same case — emitting `def m: () -> untyped` obscures rather than helps. Together the four follow-ups dropped `lib/rigor/cli/` candidate noise from 33 to 4 emittable across the 8 CLI files; the 4 clean outputs ride into [`sig/rigor/cli/diff_command.rbs`](sig/rigor/cli/diff_command.rbs), [`sig/rigor/cli/sig_gen_command.rbs`](sig/rigor/cli/sig_gen_command.rbs), and [`sig/rigor/cli/type_scan_command.rbs`](sig/rigor/cli/type_scan_command.rbs) — three new sig files written via `rigor sig-gen --write`, all under the no-AI-generated-RBS policy. Specs (+7): visibility filter (+2), initialize exclusion (+2), explicit-return union (+3) — covering the implicit-return + explicit-return case, bare `return` as `nil`, and nested-block returns not leaking.
- **ADR-14 follow-up — lenience-preserving guard against incomplete-inference tightenings.** The first project-wide dogfood pass of `rigor sig-gen` against Rigor's own `lib/` produced seven `tighter-return` candidates that all turned out to be inference incompletenesses — `return nil unless ...` early-exit paths missed (so `T | nil` looked like `T`), two-valued booleans literal-folded to one branch (`false | true` → `true`), `Float | Integer` numeric alternates collapsed to one term, `Array[T]` collapsed to a Tuple shape from a single literal-list body, and `Hash[String, untyped]` re-typed with a concrete value union that contradicted the author's intentional `untyped` lenience. None were precision wins; all would have replaced more-accurate hand-authored RBS with worse signatures if `--overwrite` had been used uncritically. The `Generator#tighter?` predicate gains three composite guards via `loses_declared_lenience?`: (a) `loses_declared_union_member?` — refuses when the declared form is a top-level Union and one or more declared members are not covered by the inferred form; (b) `narrows_collection_to_shape?` — refuses when the declared form is a generic collection (Array / Hash / Set / Range / Enumerable / Enumerator / Enumerator::Lazy) and the inferred form is a fixed-shape Tuple or HashShape; (c) `replaces_untyped_type_arg?` — refuses when the declared form has `Dynamic` at a `type_args` position the inferred form replaces with a concrete carrier. Together the guards catch all seven self-dogfood cases — the tighter-return count against Rigor's own `lib/` drops from 7 to 0. Rejected candidates reclassify as `equivalent` (silently skipped) rather than `tighter-return` (which would have surfaced under `--diff` / `--write --overwrite`). Specs (+3) under `spec/rigor/sig_gen/generator_spec.rb` cover each of the three guard branches with focused fixtures. The handbook chapter ([`docs/handbook/11-sig-gen.md`](docs/handbook/11-sig-gen.md)) gains a "When tightening is *probably* incomplete inference" subsection documenting the principle and the user-visible workflow. Memory rule promoted: in the rigor project, any tightening that contradicts an existing RBS declaration is suspected incomplete inference until proven otherwise; the dogfood policy is "don't auto-overwrite on contradiction" — already enforced by the `--overwrite` flag, now additionally hardened at the classification layer.
- **ADR-14 slice 5 — RSpec-aware observations + handbook chapter.** Final ADR-14 slice. The `ObservationCollector` gains a built-in RSpec-binding recogniser that walks `RSpec.describe Foo do … end` / bare `describe Foo` blocks, records `subject { … }` / `subject(:name) { … }` / `let(:name) { … }` / `let!(:name)` block-body types into a per-file `{ binding_name => Type }` map, and stores the surrounding describe target as the synthetic `:described_class` slot. `record_call` then routes its receiver through `resolve_receiver_type`, which: (a) recognises `described_class.new(...)` and returns `Type::Nominal[<class_name>]` from the recorded singleton; (b) recognises bare no-arg CallNodes whose name matches a `subject` / `let` binding and returns the bound type; (c) falls through to `scope.type_of` for everything else, preserving slice-3 behaviour. The recogniser is built-in to sig-gen rather than wired through `rigor-rspec`'s plugin contract — the rigor-rspec plugin's full scope analyser (its scope_walker) remains a separate machine; sharing the recognisers across both would need an ADR-9-style cross-plugin fact channel that ADR-14 does not commit to. Limitations: same-name `let` bindings across nested `describe` / `context` blocks are last-wins (no scope tree reconstruction); RSpec hook bodies (`before`, `around`) are not consulted for binding mutations. New handbook chapter [`docs/handbook/11-sig-gen.md`](docs/handbook/11-sig-gen.md) walks the command's UX, the five-classification model (`new-file` / `new-method` / `tighter-return` / `equivalent` / `skipped`), the three output modes (`--print` / `--diff` / `--write`), the three `--params` policies and the ADR-5 trade-off they surface, the RSpec recogniser, and a recommended five-step iteration workflow. Handbook README TOC + non-goals updated. Specs: 5 new examples under [`spec/rigor/sig_gen/observation_collector_spec.rb`](spec/rigor/sig_gen/observation_collector_spec.rb) covering `subject`, `let`, `described_class.new`, bare `describe Foo`, and named `subject(:foo)`. Closes ADR-14 slices 1–5; the deferred follow-ups (`define_method`, `Data.define`-specific emission, `:strict` acceptance mode, capability-role-aware `--params=observed-strict`, complex parameter shapes) ship as 0.2.0+ minor bumps.
- **ADR-14 slice 4 — singleton methods + `attr_*` declarations.** Fourth ADR-14 slice. Extends the generator's method discovery beyond plain instance `def` to cover three additional shapes: (a) `def self.foo` singleton-side methods, (b) `class << self; def foo; end` singleton-class-nested methods, and (c) `attr_reader` / `attr_writer` / `attr_accessor` declarations against accumulated instance-variable types. `MethodCandidate#kind` now carries `:instance` or `:singleton` end-to-end; the renderer emits `def self.foo: ...` for singleton kinds. Existing-RBS lookup routes through `Reflection.singleton_method_definition` for singleton candidates, matching how the `def.return-type-mismatch` rule resolves call-site dispatch. The `attr_*` walker is wired separately ([`SigGen::Generator#collect_attr_candidates`](lib/rigor/sig_gen/generator.rb)) and reads ivar types from `Scope#class_ivars_for(class_name)` — `ScopeIndexer#build_class_ivar_index` populates this before any statement evaluation runs, so attr declarations work even when they appear lexically before the corresponding ivar write. `attr_reader :name` emits one reader candidate (`def name: () -> T`); `attr_writer :name` emits one writer candidate (`def name=: (T) -> T`); `attr_accessor :name` emits both. Slice 4 uses the long-form `def` spelling rather than the idiomatic short form so the writer-side merge path stays unchanged; users who prefer the `attr_reader name: T` short form can normalise post-emit. Writer-side member detection now recognises `RBS::AST::Members::AttrReader` / `AttrWriter` / `AttrAccessor` as user-authored alongside `MethodDefinition`, so a source-side `attr_reader :name` paired with an RBS-side `attr_reader name: T` correctly classifies as `equivalent` and never produces a duplicate `def` insertion. The `MethodDefinition` member-match now also keys on the singleton-vs-instance kind so a source-side `def self.n` and an existing `def n: ...` (instance) coexist as separate members. `define_method` recognition and `Data.define`-specific emission are deferred follow-ups (the existing core inference already exposes `Data.define`-derived readers via `discovered_methods`; the generator picks them up through the instance-def walker once a method body exists). Specs at [`spec/rigor/sig_gen/generator_spec.rb`](spec/rigor/sig_gen/generator_spec.rb) (+5 examples for singleton + attr_reader + attr_accessor + attr_writer-no-ivar + dual coverage) and [`spec/rigor/sig_gen/writer_spec.rb`](spec/rigor/sig_gen/writer_spec.rb) (+5 examples for singleton-instance separation + singleton tighter-return skip + attr_reader / attr_accessor / attr_writer member detection).
- **ADR-14 slice 3 — `--params=observed` caller-side argument observation.** Third ADR-14 slice. The generator now accepts an optional `observations:` map at construction time; when populated, `render_rbs_line` consults `{[class_name, method_name] => Array<Array<Type>>}` and emits a per-position union per observed tuple (only tuples whose arity matches the def's required-positional count contribute — observations for a different arity describe an overload the MVP does not emit). New CLI flag `--params=observed` opts in to caller-side harvesting: the new [`Rigor::SigGen::ObservationCollector`](lib/rigor/sig_gen/observation_collector.rb) walks `--observe=PATH...` (defaults to `spec/` when no flag is given AND a `spec/` directory exists), parses each `.rb` file with Prism, scope-indexes it, and records every `Prism::CallNode` whose receiver types as a `Type::Nominal`. To make project-source classes resolvable in spec files where only the constant name is referenced, the collector pre-walks `configuration.paths` for `class` / `module` declarations and seeds the result via `Scope#with_discovered_classes` before `ScopeIndexer.index` runs; [`Inference::ScopeIndexer.index`](lib/rigor/inference/scope_indexer.rb) now MERGES indexer-found classes on top of the base scope's existing table rather than replacing it (indexer-found entries still win on collision — same-file declarations are the most specific authority). MVP scope: explicit-receiver calls only (`foo.bar(...)`), positional arguments only (splat / keyword / block / forwarding shapes route through `sig.skipped.complex-shape`-style silent skip), zero-argument calls yield no observation. ADR-5 clause 2 compliance is preserved: observed union is BY CONSTRUCTION the most permissive parameter contract that satisfies every existing caller; default stays `--params=untyped`. `--params=observed-strict` remains reserved-but-inert (rejected with a usage error until the capability-role catalog ships). Post-erasure dedupe collapses trailing `String | String` noise when distinct `Constant<String>` carriers (e.g. `"Alice"` / `"Bob"`) share an envelope. Specs at [`spec/rigor/sig_gen/observation_collector_spec.rb`](spec/rigor/sig_gen/observation_collector_spec.rb) (7) + Generator observation tests (3 new) + 4 new CLI integration tests under [`spec/rigor/cli_spec.rb`](spec/rigor/cli_spec.rb) cover explicit-receiver / mixed-types union / no-arg / implicit-self / non-Nominal-receiver / splat-keyword-block / arity-mismatch fallback paths.
- **ADR-14 slice 2 — `rigor sig-gen --write` with RBS::Parser-based merge + `--overwrite`.** Lands the second slice of the inference-driven generator. The new `--write` mode walks the slice-1 candidate stream, groups by source file, and applies each group to the target `.rbs` file under the configured signature tree. Path mapping ([`lib/rigor/sig_gen/path_mapper.rb`](lib/rigor/sig_gen/path_mapper.rb)) follows the Ruby community convention: `lib/foo/bar.rb` → `sig/foo/bar.rbs` (strip the source root prefix from `configuration.paths.first`, place under `configuration.signature_paths.first`). Merge logic ([`lib/rigor/sig_gen/writer.rb`](lib/rigor/sig_gen/writer.rb)) parses the existing target with `RBS::Parser.parse_signature` to locate the matching `class` / `module` declaration, inserts new method declarations just before the declaration's closing `end` keyword (preserving every other byte of the source — comments, blank lines, surrounding declarations all stay verbatim), and appends a new `class Foo … end` block to the file end when the class is not yet declared. Non-existent target files are created with a fresh class skeleton; the writer always operates inside the configured signature tree and routes anything outside through `WriteResult(action: :skipped_outside_sig_root)`. User-authored declarations are NEVER replaced unless `--overwrite` is set AND the candidate's classification is `tighter-return`; `--overwrite` then slices the old declaration's byte range and substitutes the new RBS one-liner, preserving the original column indent. CLI output ([`lib/rigor/sig_gen/renderer.rb`](lib/rigor/sig_gen/renderer.rb) `#render_write`) reports per-source-file action (`created` / `updated` / `noop` / `skipped_outside_sig_root`) with `(+N, skipped M user-authored)` suffix; `--format=json` emits a machine-readable results array. Specs at [`spec/rigor/sig_gen/path_mapper_spec.rb`](spec/rigor/sig_gen/path_mapper_spec.rb) (4) + [`spec/rigor/sig_gen/writer_spec.rb`](spec/rigor/sig_gen/writer_spec.rb) (8) + 5 new CLI integration tests under [`spec/rigor/cli_spec.rb`](spec/rigor/cli_spec.rb) cover new-file / merge-into-existing-class / append-new-class / `--overwrite` / user-authored-skip / outside-sig-root / noop paths. Comment-preservation has the known upstream limit: replacements inside `RBS::Parser` location ranges discard whitespace inside that range but preserve everything outside; the `--diff` review surface from slice 1 stays the user's escape hatch when this is a problem.
- **ADR-14 slice 1 — `rigor sig-gen` MVP (`--print` / `--diff`, return-only).** First implementation slice lands the new top-level CLI subcommand `rigor sig-gen` plus its supporting generator core under [`lib/rigor/sig_gen/`](lib/rigor/sig_gen/). The MVP walks every `.rb` file under the given paths (defaults to `configuration.paths` when none are passed) with `Inference::ScopeIndexer`, finds every plain instance `def` inside a nameable `class` / `module` body, types the body's implicit-return expression under the indexed scope (mirroring the `def.return-type-mismatch` rule's body-typing path), and classifies each candidate. New-method and tighter-return candidates render as RBS one-liners (`def NAME: (untyped, ...) -> TYPE`) grouped per source file + class; the `--diff` mode shows the existing-declared vs. inferred spelling side-by-side; the `--format=json` mode emits a machine-readable payload with the per-method classification table. Parameter rendering is hard-coded `untyped` per ADR-5 clause 2 — `--params=observed` is parsed but rejected with a "reserved for slice 3" usage error so the CLI surface stays stable. `def self.foo` singleton-side methods, top-level / DSL-block defs, methods with optional / rest / keyword / block parameters, and methods whose inferred return collapses to `Dynamic[top]` are skipped (`sig.skipped.complex-shape` / `sig.skipped.untyped-return`). Tighter-return detection uses gradual-mode acceptance (`declared.accepts(inferred).yes?` AND NOT `inferred.accepts(declared).yes?`) — `:strict` mode remains reserved on `Inference::Acceptance` and is a follow-up. Specs cover the new-method / equivalent / tighter-return / complex-shape / untyped-return / singleton-side / top-level paths under [`spec/rigor/sig_gen/generator_spec.rb`](spec/rigor/sig_gen/generator_spec.rb), plus the CLI shell (`--print` default, `--format=json`, `--diff`, `--params=observed` rejection, help-text listing) under [`spec/rigor/cli_spec.rb`](spec/rigor/cli_spec.rb). The `sig.*` diagnostic family is reserved in `docs/type-specification/diagnostic-policy.md` for slice 2+'s per-method telemetry — slice 1 surfaces the same identifiers through JSON output fields instead.
- **ADR-13 slice 7 — handbook TypeScript appendix update.** [`docs/handbook/appendix-typescript.md`](docs/handbook/appendix-typescript.md) replaces the five "(no analogue)" / "(none)" rows for `Readonly` / `Partial` / `Required` / `Pick` / `Omit` (the "everyday types" table at lines 53-56, the generics-comparison row near line 163, and the "Be honest about what you give up" bullet near line 216) with links to the opt-in `rigor-typescript-utility-types` plugin and the matching Rigor-canonical shape-projection type functions (`pick_of` / `omit_of` / `partial_of` / `required_of` / `readonly_of`). The migration vignette gains a sibling RBS snippet showing the plugin-supplied `%a{rigor:v1:return: Pick[T, K]}` annotation. Pure docs (~30 LoC). Closes the last ADR-13 polish item; ADR-13 slices 1–7 all land in v0.1.3.
- **ADR-13 slice 3b — caller-side `name_scope:` / reporter threading + two new `dynamic.*` diagnostics.** Closes the caller-side gap slice 3 left open. New `Rigor::RbsExtended::Reporter` per-run accumulator (`unresolved_payloads` / `lossy_projections` event streams, mutex-guarded, deduped per `(payload, source_location)` and `(head, source_location)`). Every `RbsExtended.read_*` reader (`read_predicate_effects` / `read_assert_effects` / `read_return_type_override` / `read_param_type_overrides` / `param_type_override_map` / `read_flow_contribution`) gains an optional `environment:` keyword that decomposes into `name_scope:` and `reporter:` for the underlying `Builtins::ImportedRefinements.parse(payload, name_scope:, reporter:, source_location:)` / inner `parse_*_annotation` / `resolve_directive_rhs` calls; defaults preserve the pre-slice-3b silent fail-soft on every call site that does not opt in. `Environment` grows `name_scope` (built once per run from `plugin_registry.type_node_resolvers` via `TypeNode::ResolverChain` / `TypeNode::NameScope`; `nil` when no plugin contributes resolvers) and `rbs_extended_reporter` accessors (and a matching keyword on `Environment.new` / `Environment.for_project`). The Resolver in `Builtins::ImportedRefinements` records a `lossy_projection` event when one of the five shape-projection heads (`pick_of` / `omit_of` / `partial_of` / `required_of` / `readonly_of`) is applied to a carrier the slice-5 `Type::Combinator.shape_projection_lossy?` predicate flags. The seven existing call sites in the analyzer surface (`Analysis::CheckRules` × 2, `Inference::StatementEvaluator`, `Inference::Narrowing`, `Inference::MethodParameterBinder`, `Inference::MethodDispatcher::OverloadSelector`, `Inference::MethodDispatcher::RbsDispatch`) now pass `environment:` so the diagnostics fire from every consumer of the RBS::Extended directive surface. `Analysis::Runner` drains the reporter at end-of-run into two `:info` diagnostic families: `dynamic.rbs-extended.unresolved` (whole-payload resolution failure, attributed to the source annotation's RBS::Location when available) and `dynamic.shape.lossy-projection` (projection authored against a non-shape carrier; suggests authoring a HashShape / Tuple). Severity profile re-stamps both rules per project taste. Public-API drift snapshot pins the new Environment surface and the three new namespaces (`Rigor::RbsExtended::Reporter` + its two `Data.define` entries); `sig/rigor/environment.rbs` adds the two `attr_reader` rows + the keyword on `for_project` / `initialize`. Closes ADR-13 slice 3b; leaves slice 7 (handbook TypeScript appendix update) as the last v0.1.4 polish item.
- **ADR-13 — plugin `TypeNode` resolver hook + TypeScript-utility-type adapter (proposed).** New ADR proposes a PHPStan-`TypeNodeResolverExtension`-shaped plugin hook for resolving custom named / generic type vocabulary in RBS::Extended payloads (`%a{rigor:v1:return: …}` / `%a{rigor:v1:param: …}` / `%a{rigor:v1:assert: …}`), paired with five Rigor-canonical shape-projection type functions (`pick_of[T, K]` / `omit_of[T, K]` / `partial_of[T]` / `required_of[T]` / `readonly_of[T]`) and an opt-in `rigor-typescript-utility-types` example plugin that maps TS-canonical names (`Pick`, `Omit`, `Partial`, `Required`, `Readonly`) onto the new core type functions and the existing operator surface (`T - U`, `T & U`, `T - nil`, `T[K]`). The three-piece split keeps shape semantics in core while leaving TS-canonical names plugin-supplied per ADR-0 / ADR-1's RBS-canonical stance. Spec contract added in the same cycle: `type-operators.md` operator catalog + new "Shape projection" section, `imported-built-in-types.md` "Initial type functions and operators" table + updated "Deferred or rejected imports" rows that direct TypeScript-utility-name authors at the opt-in plugin path. ADR-12 stays reserved for the dry-rb packaging strategy. Implementation queued with no committed milestone — seven-slice envelope documented in the ADR.
- **ADR-13 slice 1 — `Rigor::TypeNode` value objects.** Lands the two leaf carriers ADR-13 commits to as the durable shape every later slice consumes: `Rigor::TypeNode::Identifier(name:)` (bare named-type reference; kebab-case for built-in refinement names, PascalCase for class-like names) and `Rigor::TypeNode::Generic(head:, args:)` (parameterised reference; `args` is a frozen Array of `Identifier` / `Generic` for recursive shapes). Both Data classes validate field types at construction (non-empty `String` heads / names; `Array` of node types for `args`). Public-API drift snapshots pin the carrier surfaces; `lib/rigor.rb` requires the new namespace early enough for plugin manifests in slice 2 to consume it. No parser-side wiring yet — that is slice 3.
- **ADR-13 slice 2 — `Plugin::TypeNodeResolver` base class + manifest hook + registry aggregation.** New `Rigor::Plugin::TypeNodeResolver` abstract base whose `#resolve(node, scope)` default returns `nil` (safe fall-through). `Plugin::Manifest` accepts a new optional `type_node_resolvers:` keyword that takes an Array of `TypeNodeResolver` instances; validation rejects non-Array inputs, non-instance entries, and bare resolver classes (instances required). The frozen `#type_node_resolvers` reader and a class-name list under `#to_h["type_node_resolvers"]` round-trip through the existing manifest equality / hash contract. `Plugin::Registry#type_node_resolvers` aggregates the per-plugin lists into a flat read-side view in plugin registration order (preserves intra-plugin order). Public-API drift snapshots pin the new method on `Plugin::Manifest`, the new method on `Plugin::Registry`, and the resolver base's `resolve(node, scope)` surface. No parser-side wiring yet — slice 3 wires `Rigor::Builtins::ImportedRefinements::Parser` into the resolver chain via `Rigor::TypeNode::NameScope`.
- **ADR-13 slice 6 — `rigor-typescript-utility-types` example plugin + Resolver scope-handle fix.** New plugin under [`plugins/rigor-typescript-utility-types/`](plugins/rigor-typescript-utility-types/) registers five `Plugin::TypeNodeResolver` subclasses (`Resolvers::Pick` / `Omit` / `Partial` / `Required` / `Readonly`) that translate the TypeScript-canonical utility-type spellings onto the Rigor-canonical shape-projection core functions added in slice 4. The plugin is opt-in via `.rigor.yml`; off by default. Resolvers recursively resolve nested arguments through `scope.resolver.resolve(arg, scope)`, which now goes through the FULL resolution pass (built-in registry → plugin chain → RBS Nominal fallback) — slice 6 fixes a slice-3 design gap where `NameScope#resolver` exposed only the bare plugin chain. The Resolver now constructs a fresh `NameScope` for chain consultation whose `resolver:` is the Resolver itself (responding to the same `resolve(node, scope)` interface as `Plugin::TypeNodeResolver`), so plugin authors get the full vocabulary (`non-empty-string`, RBS classes, etc.) when recursively resolving sub-args. Unmapped TypeScript names (`Parameters`, `ReturnType`, `InstanceType`, `Awaited`, casing helpers) fall through the chain to the parser's existing Nominal fallback — they remain deferred until core ships the corresponding projection operators (`params_of`, `return_of`, `instance_type`). Integration spec at [`spec/integration/plugins/typescript_utility_types_plugin_spec.rb`](spec/integration/plugins/typescript_utility_types_plugin_spec.rb) covers end-to-end parsing through the plugin (lossy degrade on Nominal inputs, fallback to Nominal on resolver decline / arity mismatch) AND resolver-level semantics over synthetic AST + scope (full HashShape pick / partial / required / readonly behaviour). `examples/README.md` plugin-contract table grows a row for the new plugin.
- **ADR-13 slice 5 — Shape-projection phase B (Tuple) + lossy-projection predicate.** Extends `pick_of` / `omit_of` to `Type::Tuple` carriers — `pick_of[Tuple[A, B, C], 0 | 2]` evaluates to `Tuple[A, C]`; integer-index `K` extraction accepts `Constant<Integer>` and `Union[Constant<Integer>, …]`, deduplicates indices, and drops out-of-range / negative entries silently (the diagnostic surface lands when slice 5b wires caller-side `dynamic.shape.lossy-projection` emission). `partial_of` / `required_of` / `readonly_of` keep their phase-A scope (HashShape-only); Tuple inputs return unchanged because Tuples have positional required-by-structure entries with no read-only marker carrier in Rigor's type model. New `Rigor::Type::Combinator.shape_projection_lossy?(type)` predicate returns `true` for every carrier that the projections degrade on (everything other than `HashShape` and `Tuple`); slice 5b's diagnostic emission consults this predicate to decide whether to fire `dynamic.shape.lossy-projection`. Drift snapshot grows the predicate; `sig/rigor/type.rbs` adds the signature. Object-shape support (named-interface carriers with method-set projections) stays deferred until a concrete consumer materialises.
- **ADR-13 slice 4 — Core shape-projection type functions (phase A: HashShape).** Five new module-level builders on `Rigor::Type::Combinator` deliver the canonical shape-projection vocabulary committed to in `docs/type-specification/type-operators.md` § "Shape projection": `pick_of(type, keys)` restricts a `HashShape` to entries whose key is in the literal-key set extracted from `keys`; `omit_of(type, keys)` is its dual (set difference); `partial_of(type)` flips every required entry to optional; `required_of(type)` is its inverse; `readonly_of(type)` marks every entry read-only in the current view. Each preserves the source shape's required / optional / read-only classification AND extra-keys policy on the kept entries. `partial_of` does NOT widen value types to nil — Rigor's HashShape distinguishes "key absent" from "key present with nil value", so flipping required-ness is sufficient (TypeScript's implicit `T | undefined` is not imported per ADR-13 § "Required-ness flips"). Non-`HashShape` inputs return unchanged ("lossy degradation" — the `dynamic.shape.lossy-projection` diagnostic lands in slice 5). Literal-key extraction supports `Constant<Symbol|String>` and `Union[Constant…]` shapes; mixed / non-literal `K` inputs leave the shape unchanged. `Rigor::Builtins::ImportedRefinements`'s `PARAMETERISED_TYPE_BUILDERS` grows five rows (`pick_of` / `omit_of` / `partial_of` / `required_of` / `readonly_of`) so RBS::Extended payloads like `pick_of[Foo, :a | :b]` resolve through the existing AST → Type pass. Drift snapshots pin the five new methods on `Type::Combinator`'s singleton surface.
- **ADR-13 slice 3 — Parser integration via `NameScope` + `ResolverChain` and a separate AST → Type resolver pass.** Refactors `Rigor::Builtins::ImportedRefinements::Parser` from inline "scan + resolve" into "scan to AST" + a sibling `ImportedRefinements::Resolver` that walks the AST and produces a `Rigor::Type` carrier. The resolver applies ADR-13's resolution order at every named-type production: built-in `lookup` → `PARAMETERISED_TYPE_BUILDERS` / `PARAMETERISED_INT_BUILDERS` → plugin `TypeNodeResolver` chain (when a `name_scope:` is supplied) → RBS Nominal fallback for class-shaped names. Three new AST value objects ride alongside slice 1's `Identifier` / `Generic`: `Rigor::TypeNode::IntegerLiteral(value:)` for `int<5, 10>` / `int_mask[1, 2, 4]` integer args, `Rigor::TypeNode::IndexedAccess(receiver:, key:)` for `T[K]` trailing chains, and two resolver-context primitives under the same namespace — `Rigor::TypeNode::NameScope(resolver:, class_context:, type_alias_table:)` and `Rigor::TypeNode::ResolverChain` (walks an ordered resolver list, first non-nil wins per ADR-13 WD3 / WD5). `ImportedRefinements.parse(payload, name_scope: nil)` accepts the new keyword; passing `nil` (the default) preserves the slice-2 behaviour bit-for-bit. `Generic#args` validation was extended additively to also accept `IntegerLiteral` so the parser can emit a uniform AST for both bracket flavours. Drift snapshots updated; `UNSIGNED_NAMESPACES` grows the four new `TypeNode::*` namespaces. No diagnostic emission yet (`dynamic.rbs-extended.unresolved` is deferred to slice 3b together with caller-site threading from `RbsExtended`).

The thirteenth preview. Theme: **deliver ADR-10 (opt-in dependency-source inference) end-to-end and absorb ADR-11 (rigor-sorbet plugin) plus Rails Tier 2 (rigor-actionpack 4 phases + rigor-factorybot)**. Closes ADR-10's five-slice envelope plus four "Open questions" follow-ups (per-receiver plugin veto / β budget semantics / config-conflict diagnostic; boundary-cross is documented as deferred behind a `mode: full` distinct-dispatch prerequisite). Closes ADR-11's primary surface — slices 1–6 + 8 of the original plan plus light follow-ups for `T.must_because` / `T.reveal_type` / `T.assert_type!` / `T.bind` / per-file `enforce_sigil` gating — leaving only per-call-site sigil enforcement as a deferred ADR-11 item. Lands rigor-actionpack's full 4-phase surface (route helpers / filter chains / render targets / strong parameters → AR column validation) and rigor-factorybot Phase 1 (a)+(c), with rigor-activerecord publishing `:model_index` as the first ADR-9 publish-and-consume cycle exercised end to end. Plugin-side `truthy_facts` / `falsey_facts` / `post_return_facts` now flow through the narrowing engine, closing ADR-7 § "Slice 4-A"'s plugin half — the same substrate PHPStan-style Type-Specifying Extensions need to be authorable from any Rigor plugin.

### Added

#### ADR-10 — opt-in dependency-source inference (five-slice envelope)

- **Slice 1 (configuration plumbing).** `.rigor.yml` gains a top-level `dependencies.source_inference[]` section, each entry carrying `gem:` (required, non-empty String), `mode:` (`disabled` / `when_missing` (default) / `full`), and an optional `roots:` array (defaults to `["lib"]`). Parsed into a frozen `Rigor::Configuration::Dependencies` value object exposed via `Configuration#dependencies`; round-trips through `#to_h`. JSON schema (`schemas/rigor-config.schema.json`) gains a `$defs/sourceInferenceEntry` definition matching the loader. Validation rejects malformed entries (non-Hash entries, empty / missing `gem:`, unknown `mode:`, explicitly empty `roots:`) with `ArgumentError` at load time.
- **Slice 2a (gem resolver + Runner wiring).** `Analysis::DependencySourceInference::GemResolver.resolve(entry)` maps a `Configuration::Dependencies::Entry` to either a frozen `Resolved(gem_name, version, gem_dir, mode, roots)` value or an `Unresolvable(gem_name, reason: :not_in_bundle)`. `Builder.build(dependencies)` partitions entries and returns a frozen `Index`; `mode: :disabled` entries are skipped without resolution. `Analysis::Runner` builds the index once per run, exposes it via `#dependency_source_index`, and surfaces every unresolvable entry as a `dynamic.dependency-source.gem-not-found` `:warning` diagnostic anchored at `.rigor.yml`.
- **Slice 2b-i (walker + method catalog).** `Walker.walk(gem_dir:, roots:)` parses every `*.rb` file under each accepted root with Prism, traverses `class` / `module` / `singleton class << self` / `def` shapes the same way `Inference::ScopeIndexer#walk_methods` does for project source, and returns a flat `Hash{[class_name, method_name] => :instance | :singleton}`. Hard exclusions per ADR-10 § "Hard exclusions" are applied at root granularity: `spec/` / `test/` / `bin/` are dropped before any filesystem walk; the user CANNOT override the list. C extensions fall out automatically. Per-file parse / read failures degrade silently to "no contribution from this file" — the project-source diagnostic stream MUST NOT carry parse errors the user cannot fix.
- **Slice 2b-ii (dispatcher tier).** `Inference::MethodDispatcher.dispatch` gains a new tier between `RbsDispatch.try_dispatch` and `try_user_class_fallback`: when the receiver is a `Type::Nominal` / `Type::Singleton` whose class name matches an `Index` entry, the tier returns `Type::Combinator.untyped` (a `Dynamic[top]` carrier per ADR-10 § "Inference contract") so the call site stops emitting `call.undefined-method`. RBS / RBS::Inline / generated stubs / plugin contracts always win — the new tier sits strictly below them. `Environment.for_project` / `Environment.new` accept `dependency_source_index:`; `Environment#dependency_source_index` accessor pinned via drift snapshot + `sig/rigor/environment.rbs`.
- **Slice 3 (cache descriptor + per-gem-version invalidation).** `Cache::Descriptor::DependencyEntry(gem_name:, gem_version:, mode:)` value object + new `dependencies:` slot on `Cache::Descriptor`; `compose_by_key` over `gem_name` raises `Conflict` on disagreeing version / mode; canonical-hash slot order is `configs / dependencies / files / gems / plugins`; `SCHEMA_VERSION` bumped to 2 so stale-shape entries get wiped at first run. New `DependencySourceInference::Index#cache_descriptor` lifts every `Resolved` row into a `DependencyEntry` and returns a frozen descriptor — the primitive future cache producers compose with their own descriptors so a `bundle update` on a listed gem invalidates exactly that gem's slice.
- **Slice 4 (per-gem budget pool).** New `dependencies.budget_per_gem` config entry (default `5000`, range `1250..20000` per ADR-10 § "Budget interaction"). `Walker.walk` accepts a `budget:` keyword and returns `Walker::Outcome(catalog:, truncated:)`; harvesting stops at the cap and `truncated?` propagates to `Index#budget_exceeded`. `Analysis::Runner` emits one `dynamic.dependency-source.budget-exceeded` `:warning` per listed gem (per-gem dedupe, not per-call-site).
- **Slice 5 (normative documentation).** New `docs/internal-spec/dependency-source-inference.md` covers the analyzer contract end to end. Cross-links in `docs/type-specification/special-types.md` / `inference-budgets.md` / `diagnostic-policy.md` upgrade from "ADR-10 only" to "ADR-10 (analyzer contract: dependency-source-inference.md)" so readers find the implementation contract one click away.

#### ADR-10 — open-questions follow-ups

- **Per-receiver plugin veto (5a).** `Rigor::Plugin::Manifest#owns_receivers` new attr (frozen Array of class-name Strings). Plugins declare `manifest(owns_receivers: ["ActiveRecord::Base"])` to claim sole ownership of a receiver class. The dispatcher's `receiver_matches_owner?` consults `Environment#class_ordering(class_name, owner)` and accepts `:equal` / `:subclass`, so a plugin declaring `["ActiveRecord::Base"]` automatically owns every `ActiveRecord::Base` subclass without enumerating each one.
- **β budget semantics (5b).** New `dependencies.budget_overrun_strategy` config knob (`:walker_cap` (default — α semantics) / `:dependency_silence` (β)). Under β, the dispatcher consults a per-class reverse-lookup table on catalog miss: if the receiver class belongs to a budget-exceeded gem, the call resolves to `Dynamic[top]` rather than falling through to the user-class fallback. `Index#class_to_gem` (frozen `Hash<class_name, gem_name>`, first-write-wins) and `Index#gem_for(class_name)` are the new lookup surface.
- **Config-conflict diagnostic (5d).** `dynamic.dependency-source.config-conflict` `:warning` surfaces `dependencies.source_inference[]` entries for the same gem that disagree on `mode:` across an `.rigor.yml` `includes:` chain. The later (downstream-include) entry's mode wins; `roots:` are unioned silently. One diagnostic per conflicting `(gem, prior-mode, new-mode)` triple. `Configuration.merge_dependencies_hash` carves out `dependencies.source_inference[]` from the standard right-wins merge so `Dependencies.from_h` sees every contributor's entries; `Dependencies.dedupe_entries` performs the per-gem merge with conflict detection.

#### ADR-11 — rigor-sorbet plugin

- **Slice 1 (sig parser + base type vocabulary).** `plugins/rigor-sorbet/` reads inline Sorbet `sig { params(...).returns(T) }` / `sig { void }` blocks above first-party method definitions and contributes the parsed return type at every call site through the v0.1.1 Track 2 slice 7 `flow_contribution_for` substrate. Slice 1 vocabulary covers class constants → `Nominal`, `T.untyped` → `Dynamic[top]`, `T.anything` → `top`, `T.noreturn` → `bot`, `T.nilable(X)` → `Union[X, nil]`, `T.any(...)` → `Union`, `T.all(...)` → `Intersection`, `T::Boolean` → `Union[Constant[true], Constant[false]]`. Modifiers (`abstract` / `override` / `overridable` / `final`) are recognised and recorded.
- **Slice 2 (type assertions).** `T.let` / `T.cast` / `T.must` / `T.unsafe` recognised at call sites. New `AssertionRecognizer` hooks into `flow_contribution_for` BEFORE the catalog lookup. `T.let(expr, T)` / `T.cast(expr, T)` translate the second argument and return that as the call's contribution; `T.must(expr)` returns `inferred(expr) - nil` via the `Difference` carrier; `T.unsafe(expr)` returns `Dynamic[top]`.
- **Slice 3 (widened type vocabulary).** `T::Array[E]` / `T::Hash[K, V]` / `T::Set[E]` / `T::Range[E]` / `T::Enumerable[E]` / `T::Enumerator[E]` translate to `Nominal[<class>, [<type-args>]]`. `T.class_of(C)` → `Singleton[C]`; `T::Class[T]` → `Singleton[T-class-name]` (lossy). Bare tuple literals `[A, B]` and shape literals `{a: A, b: B}` in sig position translate to `Tuple` / `HashShape`.
- **Slice 4 (RBI tree walking).** New `rbi_paths:` config (default `["sorbet/rbi"]`) tells the plugin where to find Sorbet's RBI tree. The walker treats `.rbi` files as Ruby source — sig+def pairing applies the same way it does to project source — so signatures from autogenerated and hand-written gem stubs feed the same catalog as project sigs. The Tapioca subdirectories (`gems/`, `annotations/`, `dsl/`, `shims/`) participate via parent-root recursion. Set `rbi_paths: []` to opt out.
- **Slice 5 (sigil detection at catalog harvest).** `SigilDetector` reads Sorbet's `# typed:` magic comment from the head of each file. `# typed: ignore` files are skipped during catalog harvest. Levels `:false` / `:true` / `:strict` / `:strong` are recognised; the per-file gating extends in the `enforce_sigil` light follow-up below.
- **Slice 6 (T.absurd exhaustiveness).** `AbsurdRecognizer` hooks into `flow_contribution_for` ahead of every other recogniser. Every `T.absurd(x)` call gets a `FlowContribution` with `return_type: bot` and `exceptional: :raises`, faithful to Sorbet's runtime behaviour. When the discriminant `x`'s narrowed type at the absurd call hasn't collapsed to `bot`, the recogniser records the call node and `diagnostics_for_file` surfaces a `plugin.sorbet.absurd-reachable` `:warning`.
- **Slice 7 (handbook chapter 10).** New end-user handbook chapter at `docs/handbook/10-sorbet.md` covers the basic translation contract, the type-vocabulary table, `T.let` / `T.cast` / `T.must` / `T.unsafe` assertions, RBI walking, `# typed:` sigils, `T.absurd` exhaustiveness, the Tapioca DSL mixin pattern, the dispatcher tier ordering, and three migration patterns (run side-by-side, Sorbet-for-sigs / Rigor-for-narrowing, gradual Sorbet → RBS). Cross-link from `docs/handbook/01-getting-started.md` "When inference is not enough" gains a fifth escape hatch entry.
- **Slice 8 (Tapioca DSL mixin chain resolution).** `Catalog` gains `record_mixin(class_name:, kind:, module_name:)` and `mixins_for(class_name)`; `CatalogWalker.walk_statements` recognises `include` / `extend` calls alongside the existing sig+def pairing; `Sorbet#chain_lookup` walks the recorded chain on every call site. Closes the Tapioca DSL RBI gap — `Post#body` resolves through `Post::GeneratedAttributeMethods#body` automatically.
- **Light follow-up — `T.must_because` + `T.reveal_type`.** `T.must_because(expr, "explanation")` is recognised as an alias of `T.must`. `T.reveal_type(expr)` returns `expr` unchanged + surfaces the inferred type as a `plugin.sorbet.reveal-type` `:info` diagnostic at the call site.
- **Light follow-up — `T.assert_type!`.** Returns the asserted type as the call's contribution (T.cast-compatible). The plugin runs the static subtype check at recogniser time via `Rigor::Inference::Acceptance.accepts(asserted, inferred, mode: :gradual)`: a `:no` result records the call for `plugin.sorbet.assert-type-mismatch` `:error` emission; `:yes` and `:maybe` are silenced (gradual consistency — Dynamic[top] and uncertain shapes trust the user, matching the runtime check's role).
- **Light follow-up — `T.bind(self, T)`.** `AssertionRecognizer.recognize_bind` builds a `FlowContribution` with `return_type: Constant[nil]` AND `post_return_facts: [Fact(target_kind: :self, type: translated T)]`. The engine's plugin-fact wiring picks up the post-return fact and narrows `scope.self_type` for the surrounding scope via `apply_self_post_return_fact`. In a block body, the surrounding scope is the block's own scope, so the narrowing applies to the rest of the block — exactly Sorbet's documented contract. The recogniser requires the first argument to be a literal `Prism::SelfNode`.
- **Per-file sigil enforcement (`enforce_sigil` knob).** New `enforce_sigil` config knob (default `true`). Mirrors Sorbet's contract: types are not enforced at `# typed: false`, so under the default Rigor doesn't record sigs from `# typed: false` (or sigil-less, treated identically) files. `# typed: true` / `:strict` / `:strong` files contribute sigs as before; `# typed: ignore` continues to skip the file entirely. Set `enforce_sigil: false` to record every parseable file's sigs regardless of sigil. Parse-error diagnostics still surface for sigil-gated files (sigs flow into a discardable per-file catalog rather than the per-run one). Assertion recognisers (`T.let` / `T.cast` / `T.must` / `T.must_because` / `T.unsafe` / `T.reveal_type` / `T.assert_type!` / `T.bind`) are NOT gated — the user wrote those calls deliberately.

#### Plugin contract / cross-plugin substrate

- **Plugin-side `post_return_facts` wiring through the narrowing engine.** `Inference::StatementEvaluator#apply_plugin_assertions` is the sibling of the existing `apply_rbs_extended_assertions`: it collects every loaded plugin's `flow_contribution_for(call_node:, scope:)` result, merges through `FlowContribution::Merger.merge`, and applies the resulting `post_return_facts` via the existing `apply_post_return_fact` / `apply_self_post_return_fact` path. Closes ADR-7 § "Slice 4-A"'s plugin half: ADR-7 committed to "every call-site merge composes built-in, RBS::Extended, and (future) plugin contributions together"; the (future) plugin half lands here. The same wiring is the **substrate PHPStan-style Type-Specifying Extensions need** to be authorable from a Rigor plugin without engine changes — `T.bind` is the first concrete consumer.
- **rigor-activerecord ADR-9 `:model_index` publication.** `manifest(produces: [:model_index])` + new `prepare(services)` hook publishes a Marshal-clean `Hash{class_name => { table:, columns: }}` to the cross-plugin fact store. Downstream Tier-2 consumers (rigor-actionpack Phase 1, rigor-factorybot Phase 1 (c)) read it via `services.fact_store.read(plugin_id: "activerecord", name: :model_index)` without coupling to this plugin's `ModelIndex` / `SchemaTable::Column` carrier classes. Discovery failures leave the fact unpublished — consumers see `nil` and degrade to their own no-op path.

#### Rails ecosystem — Tier 1

- **`rigor-rails-routes`.** Statically interprets `config/routes.rb` via Prism — no Rails runtime dependency — and validates every `*_path` / `*_url` call site against the generated helper table. Recognises the v0.1.0 DSL surface from the Rails plugins roadmap: `Rails.application.routes.draw`, `resources` / `resource` (with `only:` / `except:`), `get`/`post`/`patch`/`put`/`delete` with `as:`, `root to:`, one level of `namespace`, one level of nested `resources`. `manifest(produces: [:helper_table])` publishes the parsed helper table as an ADR-9 fact for rigor-actionpack Phase 4 to consume.
- **`rigor-rails-i18n`.** Walks `locale_search_paths` (default `config/locales/`), reads every `.yml` / `.yaml` file through the trusted `IoBoundary`, and folds the per-locale tree into a flat `dotted_key => Entry` index. Validates every `t(literal_key, ...)` / `I18n.t(...)` call site — `unknown-key` (with did-you-mean), `missing-locale` (per-locale presence + `default:` suppression), `wrong-interpolation` / `extra-interpolation` (placeholder mismatch). `YAML.safe_load(aliases: true)` parser; reserved I18n option keys (`default:` / `scope:` / `locale:` / `count:` / `raise:` / etc.) are excluded from the extra-interpolation check.
- **`rigor-actionmailer`.** Discovers ActionMailer subclasses by walking `mailer_search_paths` (default `app/mailers/`), indexes each class's `def`s as action methods, and validates `Mailer.action(args).deliver_*` call sites — direct calls, `.with(...)` parametrization, and trailing delivery. `mailer-call` (info), `wrong-arity` (error), `unknown-action` (error), `missing-view` (warning, anchored at the action's `def` line in the mailer file).
- **`rigor-activejob`.** Discovers ActiveJob subclasses by walking `job_search_paths` (default `app/jobs/`), reads each `#perform`'s syntactic parameter list, and validates `Job.perform_later(...)` / `.perform_now(...)` / `.perform(...)` argument arity at every call site.

#### Rails ecosystem — Tier 2

- **`rigor-actionpack` Phase 4 (route-helper consumption).** Reads the `:helper_table` fact published by `rigor-rails-routes` (`manifest(consumes: [{ plugin_id: "rails-routes", name: :helper_table, optional: true }])`) and validates every implicit-self `*_path` / `*_url` call inside files under `controller_search_paths` (default `app/controllers`). Three diagnostics: `helper-call` (info), `unknown-helper` (error with did-you-mean), `wrong-helper-arity` (error). The `optional: true` flag means rigor-actionpack still loads when `rigor-rails-routes` isn't configured; Phase 4 silently degrades to a no-op.
- **`rigor-actionpack` Phase 2 (filter chain validation).** `before_action :name` / `after_action` / `around_action` / `skip_*` / `prepend_*` family validated against the controller's effective method set (own methods plus methods defined on the immediate parent class — one level of inheritance). `controller_index` cached producer walks `controller_search_paths` (default `app/controllers`), parses each file with Prism, and records `(class_name, defined_methods, parent_class_name)` for every top-level class node. Two diagnostics: `filter-call` (info), `unknown-filter-method` (error with did-you-mean drawn from the controller's effective method set).
- **`rigor-actionpack` Phase 3 (render-target validation).** `render :symbol` / `render "string/path"` / `render partial: "..."` validated against `view_search_paths` (default `["app/views"]`). The recogniser walks every `def` body for explicit `render` calls and derives a candidate view path from the controller's class name + the render argument shape. Pass-through shapes (`render plain:`, `render json:`, `render layout:`, `render text:`, `render inline:`, `render :nothing => true`) are silently skipped. Two diagnostics: `render-target` (info), `missing-template` (error). The `.html.erb` / `.text.erb` extension family is hardcoded in this slice; configurable template families ship behind a follow-up.
- **`rigor-actionpack` Phase 1 (strong-parameter validation).** `params.require(:user).permit(:name, :email)` chains validated against AR columns. The recogniser walks every `:permit` call site whose receiver is a `:require` call with a literal `Prism::SymbolNode` first argument, takes the symbol's `.to_s.split("_").map(&:capitalize).join` as the candidate model class name (`:user` → `User`, `:order_item` → `OrderItem`), and validates each permit `:key` against the column list published by `rigor-activerecord` (via the cross-plugin `:model_index` fact). Two diagnostics: `permit-call` (info), `unknown-permit-key` (error with did-you-mean drawn from the model's column list). Namespaced models (`:admin_user` → `Admin::User`), non-literal `permit` arguments, and nested permit (`permit(profile_attrs: [:name])`) are deferred.
- **`rigor-factorybot` (Phase 1 (a) self-contained validation).** Validates every `FactoryBot.create(:name, key: ...)` / `.build` / `.build_stubbed` / `.attributes_for` / `.create_list` / `.build_list` / `.build_stubbed_list` call (and the legacy `FactoryGirl.*` aliases) against a per-run `FactoryIndex` built from `factory_search_paths` (default `["spec/factories", "spec/factories.rb"]`). Three diagnostics: `factory-call` (info), `unknown-factory` (error with did-you-mean), `unknown-attribute` (error with did-you-mean). What's recognised inside `factory :name do ... end`: `name { "Alice" }` (modern method_missing block form), `name "Alice"` (legacy positional form), `add_attribute(:name) { ... }` (explicit form). Sequences, associations, traits, and parent / child relationships are deferred.
- **`rigor-factorybot` (Phase 1 (c) AR column cross-check).** Cross-plugin consumer of rigor-activerecord's `:model_index`. When both plugins are loaded, kwargs in `FactoryBot.create(...)` calls are validated against the UNION of (1) the factory's declared attributes and (2) the AR model's columns. FactoryBot's runtime accepts any AR attribute regardless of whether the factory declared it; the static check now mirrors that. A kwarg that's NEITHER a factory attribute NOR a model column still fires `unknown-attribute`.

#### Rails ecosystem — Tier 3

- **`rigor-pundit`.** Discovers Pundit policy classes by walking `policy_search_paths` (default `app/policies/`) — direct-superclass match against `policy_base_classes` (default `ApplicationPolicy`) plus a `*Policy` suffix gate — indexes each class's instance-side predicate methods (`def name?`), and validates `authorize(record, :action)` / `policy(record)` / `policy_scope(scope)` call sites. Constant arguments hit the policy class directly; expression arguments query `Scope#type_of` and only fire on `Nominal[T]`. Predicate normalisation: both `:update` and `:update?` resolve to `update?`. Three diagnostics: `policy-call` (info), `unknown-policy-class` (error with did-you-mean), `unknown-policy-method` (error with did-you-mean).
- **`rigor-sidekiq`.** Discovers Sidekiq workers by walking `worker_search_paths` (default `app/workers`, `app/sidekiq`) — direct `include` match against `worker_marker_modules` (default `Sidekiq::Job` / legacy `Sidekiq::Worker`) — and validates `Worker.perform_async(...)` / `.perform_inline(...)` / `.perform_in(...)` / `.perform_at(...)` argument shape. Schedule-aware arity: direct entries forward every argument to `#perform`; scheduled entries consume the FIRST argument as the schedule and forward the rest. Three diagnostics: `worker-call` (info), `wrong-arity` (error), `missing-schedule` (error).
- **`rigor-rspec` (deliberately scoped).** Duplicate `let` / `subject` declarations within the same scope (warning) and self-referencing `let` / `subject` blocks (error). Pure syntactic walk; no RSpec runtime dependency. Scope-aware: `ScopeWalker` builds a tree of `describe` / `context` scopes anchored on each `RSpec.describe` block. Inner shadows are intentional — only same-scope duplicates are flagged. Two diagnostics: `duplicate-let` (warning), `self-reference` (error).
- **`rigor-actioncable`.** Discovers ActionCable channel classes by walking `channel_search_paths` (default `app/channels/`), indexes each channel's instance-side action methods, and recursively walks each channel body for `stream_from` / `stream_for` registrations. Two-axis validation: `<X>Channel.broadcast_to(record, data)` validates the channel class exists; `ActionCable.server.broadcast("stream_name", data)` validates the literal stream name is registered. Dynamic-stream suppression: when ANY discovered channel uses a dynamic registration, the `unknown-stream` warning is suppressed across the whole project. Four diagnostics: `broadcast-target` (info), `broadcast-stream` (info), `unknown-channel` (error with did-you-mean), `unknown-stream` (warning with did-you-mean).

#### Documentation

- **Handbook chapter 7 — `@phpstan-assert` correspondence table.** New "Coming from PHPStan?" section in `docs/handbook/07-rbs-and-extended.md` maps every `@phpstan-assert*` PHPDoc annotation to its `RBS::Extended` directive equivalent: `@phpstan-assert T $x` ⇄ `%a{rigor:v1:assert: x is T}`, `@phpstan-assert-if-true` / `-if-false` ⇄ `%a{rigor:v1:predicate-if-*}`, negation form `!T` ⇄ `~T`. Includes a worked "assertNotNull" example plus the self-targeted shape for receivers narrowing themselves. Cross-links to chapter 9 noting that PHPStan-style "Type-Specifying Extensions" (call-shape recognition) are now expressible from Rigor plugins.
- **ADR-10 5c boundary-cross diagnostic deferral.** Investigated the boundary-cross slice and confirmed it has no condition under which it would fire in v0.1.3's dispatcher: plugin and RBS contributions preempt gem-source entirely (plugins → RBS → gem-source in the tier order), so there's no overlap to surface as a "boundary cross." The diagnostic only becomes meaningful once `mode: full` dispatches distinctively. `docs/internal-spec/dependency-source-inference.md` updates the `boundary-cross` row in the diagnostic-family table and the "Open questions" entry to spell out the dependency.

### Fixed

- **`FlowContribution::Merger` no longer raises `NameError` when invoked through `exe/rigor`.** The merger references `MergeResult` / `Conflict` / `Fact` but only `lib/rigor.rb` (the gem entry) eagerly loaded them; `rigor/cli` → `analysis/runner` → `inference/method_dispatcher` → `flow_contribution/merger` did not. Plugin contributions through `flow_contribution_for` therefore crashed at the first call site whenever the demos / CLI exercised them. Adds `require_relative` for the three siblings at the top of `merger.rb` so the load order matches the eager gem-entry path.
- **`make cache-clean` Makefile target + AGENTS.md cache-maintenance doc.** [ADR-6](docs/adr/6-cache-persistence-backend.md) commits to a sharded, "no eviction" cache: each unique `(configs, dependencies, files, gems, plugins)` descriptor produces its own ~25 MiB cache slice, and config / dependency churn over a long-lived clone accumulates stale slices indefinitely (100+ MiB on this repo's development clone). `bundle exec exe/rigor check --cache-stats lib` already inventoried the per-slot footprint, but the only documented purge path was `rigor check --clear-cache` (which conflates "clear" with "run the analyzer"). New `make cache-clean` `rm -rf .rigor/cache` is the lightweight standalone button; AGENTS.md "Common Commands" names both surfaces and links the design choice.
- **`Inference::MethodDispatcher` gains a `try_discovered_method` tier for sibling-private calls.** Self-analysis on `lib/` showed `Prism::CallNode` falling back at a 20.4 % rate — the dominant pattern was implicit-self calls to sibling private methods (`sibling_helper(arg)`) inside Rigor's own internals, where the helper is defined via `def name` but has no RBS entry (analyzer-internal private helpers are deliberately not in `sig/`). The new tier sits between `try_dependency_source` and `try_user_class_fallback`, consults `Scope#discovered_method?` for `(receiver_class_name, method_name, kind)`, and returns `Type::Combinator.untyped` on a hit (the bare `def` shape doesn't carry a return-type contract, so the dispatcher cannot infer better). RBS-known classes are skipped (RBS dispatch already had its turn upstream).
