Instance-side methods with a re-typable body (`scope.user_def_for(...)` non-nil) ALSO decline so `ExpressionTyper#try_user_method_inference` retains its precise body-re-typing path — `Constant[true]` answers from `is_odd(3)`-style fixtures stay intact.
- **`StatementEvaluator#build_fresh_body_scope` now carries `discovered_methods` and `discovered_method_visibilities` through into method-body / class-body scopes.** Pre-fix, both tables were populated on the root scope by `ScopeIndexer` but dropped the moment the evaluator entered any def or class body — `Scope.empty(environment:)` was rebuilt without them. Side effect: `Analysis::CheckRules.undefined_method_diagnostic`'s in-source method suppression `return nil if scope.discovered_method?(...)` silently failed inside every def body, emitting false-positive `call.undefined-method` errors for user-defined helpers when called on an RBS-known receiver from inside a sibling def. The propagation also unlocks the new `MethodDispatcher.try_discovered_method` tier. `discovered_def_nodes` deliberately stays root-only — propagating it into nested bodies triggers cascading body re-typing that surfaces unrelated inference imprecisions; the existing `ExpressionTyper#try_user_method_inference` path already reads `discovered_def_nodes` directly from the seeded scope so the precision-relevant lookups still work.
- **Self-analysis numbers (`bundle exec exe/rigor type-scan lib`).** Total unrecognized nodes: 2737 → 2097 (3.9 % → 3.0 %); `Prism::CallNode` unrecognized: 2409/11829 (20.4 %) → 1785/11845 (15.1 %); 624 fewer fail-soft fallback events. The remaining `CallNode` slack is dominated by calls whose receiver type Rigor couldn't tighten upstream (so the dispatcher never sees a `Nominal`/`Singleton`) — separate inference engine work.
- **`Data.define` carriers refactored to `class X < Data.define(...)` form for Steep compatibility.** The block form `X = Data.define(...) do ... end` trips two known Steep limitations: a hard `RuntimeError: Unexpected self_type: untyped` crash when the block contains a `def self.*` (e.g., `Provenance.builtin` in [`lib/rigor/flow_contribution.rb`](lib/rigor/flow_contribution.rb)), and `Ruby::MethodParameterMismatch` warnings on every `def initialize` inside the block (Steep binds the inner `def` to the OUTER class's signature; the `FactStore::Target` and `FactStore::Fact` carriers in [`lib/rigor/analysis/fact_store.rb`](lib/rigor/analysis/fact_store.rb) generated 5 such warnings). Rewrites all 21 sites where the block form contained a `def` so Steep can resolve the body's `self` correctly: [`lib/rigor/flow_contribution.rb`](lib/rigor/flow_contribution.rb), [`lib/rigor/rbs_extended.rb`](lib/rigor/rbs_extended.rb) × 2 (PredicateEffect / AssertEffect), [`lib/rigor/configuration/dependencies.rb`](lib/rigor/configuration/dependencies.rb), [`lib/rigor/analysis/rule_catalog.rb`](lib/rigor/analysis/rule_catalog.rb), [`lib/rigor/analysis/fact_store.rb`](lib/rigor/analysis/fact_store.rb) × 2 (Target / Fact), [`lib/rigor/analysis/dependency_source_inference/gem_resolver.rb`](lib/rigor/analysis/dependency_source_inference/gem_resolver.rb), [`lib/rigor/analysis/dependency_source_inference/walker.rb`](lib/rigor/analysis/dependency_source_inference/walker.rb), [`lib/rigor/plugin/manifest.rb`](lib/rigor/plugin/manifest.rb), [`lib/rigor/cli/type_scan_report.rb`](lib/rigor/cli/type_scan_report.rb), [`lib/rigor/flow_contribution/conflict.rb`](lib/rigor/flow_contribution/conflict.rb), [`lib/rigor/flow_contribution/element.rb`](lib/rigor/flow_contribution/element.rb), [`lib/rigor/flow_contribution/fact.rb`](lib/rigor/flow_contribution/fact.rb), [`lib/rigor/inference/fallback.rb`](lib/rigor/inference/fallback.rb), [`lib/rigor/inference/coverage_scanner.rb`](lib/rigor/inference/coverage_scanner.rb), [`lib/rigor/type_node/identifier.rb`](lib/rigor/type_node/identifier.rb), [`lib/rigor/type_node/generic.rb`](lib/rigor/type_node/generic.rb), [`lib/rigor/type_node/name_scope.rb`](lib/rigor/type_node/name_scope.rb), [`lib/rigor/type_node/indexed_access.rb`](lib/rigor/type_node/indexed_access.rb), [`lib/rigor/type_node/integer_literal.rb`](lib/rigor/type_node/integer_literal.rb). The 15 block-less `Data.define(...)` sites (no inner `def`) stay as-is — they don't tickle the Steep bug. Steep findings on a fresh `steep check` drop from `Detected 8 problems from 2 files` + 1 internal crash to `Detected 2 problems from 1 file` (the remaining two are Kernel-shadow `Plugin::Loader.load` warnings blocked on adding RBS for `Rigor::Plugin::*`). `Style/DataInheritance` cop is excluded under `lib/**/*` in `.rubocop.yml` because the cop's stylistic preference loses to Steep correctness here. `spec/rigor/public_api_drift_spec.rb`'s `instance_signatures` helper now walks one level up through anonymous Data-generated parents so the surface snapshot is invariant to the carrier form. No runtime-behaviour change for any consumer; `Data#==` / `hash` / `with` / `to_h` / `deconstruct[_keys]` / member readers all preserve their semantics.
- **`FactStore#fact_targets` return-type narrowing now matches the `Array[Target]` declaration AND survives Steep's narrowing.** The old `Array(fact.target)` formulation widened to `Array[Target | Array[Target]]` under Steep's view of `Kernel#Array`; the new explicit `is_a?(Array)` early-return form narrows `fact.target` to bare `Target` in the non-array arm so the wrapped tuple `[target]` is exactly `Array[Target]`. Runtime semantics unchanged: `Target` inputs wrap; `Array[Target]` inputs pass through.
- **LHS-only `*TargetNode` Prism kinds now recognised as non-value positions.** `ExpressionTyper::PRISM_DISPATCH` only registered `Prism::LocalVariableTargetNode`; the seven sibling target-node kinds fell through to `fallback_for(node, family: :prism)` even though they have no value to extract (they appear only on the left of destructuring assignment, inside pattern bodies, as block-parameter destructuring slots, or as the inner shape of `for x in xs` indices). Self-analysis on `lib/` showed `Prism::MultiTargetNode` and `Prism::InstanceVariableTargetNode` both at 100 % unrecognised (14 + 2 sites). Adds `Prism::MultiTargetNode` / `InstanceVariableTargetNode` / `ClassVariableTargetNode` / `GlobalVariableTargetNode` / `ConstantTargetNode` / `ConstantPathTargetNode` / `CallTargetNode` / `IndexTargetNode` to the dispatch table, all mapped to `:type_of_non_value` (returns `Dynamic[Top]` AND records no fallback event, matching the existing `LocalVariableTargetNode` treatment). Binding the inner names back into scope remains `StatementEvaluator` / `MultiTargetBinder` / `BlockParameterBinder`'s concern. Self-analysis on `lib/`: total unrecognised drops from 2737 → 2721 (the exact 14 + 2 sites that were 100 % blind); the `--explain` stream stops emitting `info: fail-soft fallback at Prism::MultiTargetNode` / `Prism::InstanceVariableTargetNode` entries. No user-facing diagnostic shift — info-level fallback events are only surfaced via `--explain`.

## [0.1.3] - 2026-05-11

The thirteenth preview. Theme: **deliver ADR-10 (opt-in dependency-source inference) end-to-end and absorb ADR-11 (rigor-sorbet plugin) plus Rails Tier 2 (rigor-actionpack 4 phases + rigor-factorybot)**. Closes ADR-10's five-slice envelope plus four "Open questions" follow-ups (per-receiver plugin veto / β budget semantics / config-conflict diagnostic; boundary-cross is documented as deferred behind a `mode: full` distinct-dispatch prerequisite). Closes ADR-11's primary surface — slices 1–6 + 8 of the original plan plus light follow-ups for `T.must_because` / `T.reveal_type` / `T.assert_type!` / `T.bind` / per-file `enforce_sigil` gating — leaving only per-call-site sigil enforcement as a deferred ADR-11 item. Lands rigor-actionpack's full 4-phase surface (route helpers / filter chains / render targets / strong parameters → AR column validation) and rigor-factorybot Phase 1 (a)+(c), with rigor-activerecord publishing `:model_index` as the first ADR-9 publish-and-consume cycle exercised end to end. Plugin-side `truthy_facts` / `falsey_facts` / `post_return_facts` now flow through the narrowing engine, closing ADR-7 § "Slice 4-A"'s plugin half — the same substrate PHPStan-style Type-Specifying Extensions need to be authorable from any Rigor plugin.

### Added

#### ADR-10 — opt-in dependency-source inference (five-slice envelope)

- **Slice 1 (configuration plumbing).** `.rigor.yml` gains a top-level `dependencies.source_inference[]` section, each entry carrying `gem:` (required, non-empty String), `mode:` (`disabled` / `when_missing` (default) / `full`), and an optional `roots:` array (defaults to `["lib"]`). Parsed into a frozen `Rigor::Configuration::Dependencies` value object exposed via `Configuration#dependencies`; round-trips through `#to_h`. JSON schema (`schemas/rigor-config.schema.json`) gains a `$defs/sourceInferenceEntry` definition matching the loader. Validation rejects malformed entries (non-Hash entries, empty / missing `gem:`, unknown `mode:`, explicitly empty `roots:`) with `ArgumentError` at load time.
- **Slice 2a (gem resolver + Runner wiring).** `Analysis::DependencySourceInference::GemResolver.resolve(entry)` maps a `Configuration::Dependencies::Entry` to either a frozen `Resolved(gem_name, version, gem_dir, mode, roots)` value or an `Unresolvable(gem_name, reason: :not_in_bundle)`. `Builder.build(dependencies)` partitions entries and returns a frozen `Index`; `mode: :disabled` entries are skipped without resolution. `Analysis::Runner` builds the index once per run, exposes it via `#dependency_source_index`, and surfaces every unresolvable entry as a `dynamic.dependency-source.gem-not-found` `:warning` diagnostic anchored at `.rigor.yml`.
- **Slice 2b-i (walker + method catalog).** `Walker.walk(gem_dir:, roots:)` parses every `*.rb` file under each accepted root with Prism, traverses `class` / `module` / `singleton class << self` / `def` shapes the same way `Inference::ScopeIndexer#walk_methods` does for project source, and returns a flat `Hash{[class_name, method_name] => :instance | :singleton}`. Hard exclusions per ADR-10 § "Hard exclusions" are applied at root granularity: `spec/` / `test/` / `bin/` are dropped before any filesystem walk; the user CANNOT override the list. C extensions fall out automatically. Per-file parse / read failures degrade silently to "no contribution from this file" — the project-source diagnostic stream MUST NOT carry parse errors the user cannot fix.
- **Slice 2b-ii (dispatcher tier).** `Inference::MethodDispatcher.dispatch` gains a new tier between `RbsDispatch.try_dispatch` and `try_user_class_fallback`: when the receiver is a `Type::Nominal` / `Type::Singleton` whose class name matches an `Index` entry, the tier returns `Type::Combinator.untyped` (a `Dynamic[top]` carrier per ADR-10 § "Inference contract") so the call site stops emitting `call.undefined-method`. RBS / RBS::Inline / generated stubs / plugin contracts always win — the new tier sits strictly below them. `Environment.for_project` / `Environment.new` accept `dependency_source_index:`; `Environment#dependency_source_index` accessor pinned via drift snapshot + `sig/rigor/environment.rbs`.
- **Slice 3 (cache descriptor + per-gem-version invalidation).** `Cache::Descriptor::DependencyEntry(gem_name:, gem_version:, mode:)` value object + new `dependencies:` slot on `Cache::Descriptor`; `compose_by_key` over `gem_name` raises `Conflict` on disagreeing version / mode; canonical-hash slot order is `configs / dependencies / files / gems / plugins`; `SCHEMA_VERSION` bumped to 2 so stale-shape entries get wiped at first run. New `DependencySourceInference::Index#cache_descriptor` lifts every `Resolved` row into a `DependencyEntry` and returns a frozen descriptor — the primitive future cache producers compose with their own descriptors so a `bundle update` on a listed gem invalidates exactly that gem's slice.
- **Slice 4 (per-gem budget pool).** New `dependencies.budget_per_gem` config entry (default `5000`, range `1250..20000` per ADR-10 § "Budget interaction"). `Walker.walk` accepts a `budget:` keyword and returns `Walker::Outcome(catalog:, truncated:)`; harvesting stops at the cap and `truncated?` propagates to `Index#budget_exceeded`. `Analysis::Runner` emits one `dynamic.dependency-source.budget-exceeded` `:warning` per listed gem (per-gem dedupe, not per-call-site).
- **Slice 5 (normative documentation).** New `docs/internal-spec/dependency-source-inference.md` covers the analyzer contract end to end. Cross-links in `docs/type-specification/special-types.md` / `inference-budgets.md` / `diagnostic-policy.md` upgrade from "ADR-10 only" to "ADR-10 (analyzer contract: dependency-source-inference.md)" so readers find the implementation contract one click away.

#### ADR-10 — open-questions follow-ups

- **Per-receiver plugin veto (5a).** `Rigor::Plugin::Manifest#owns_receivers` new attr (frozen Array of class-name Strings). Plugins declare `manifest(owns_receivers: ["ActiveRecord::Base"])` to claim sole ownership of a receiver class. The dispatcher's `receiver_matches_owner?` consults `Environment#class_ordering(class_name, owner)` and accepts `:equal` / `:subclass`, so a plugin declaring `["ActiveRecord::Base"]` automatically owns every `ActiveRecord::Base` subclass without enumerating each one.
- **β budget semantics (5b).** New `dependencies.budget_overrun_strategy` config knob (`:walker_cap` (default — α semantics) / `:dependency_silence` (β)). Under β, the dispatcher consults a per-class reverse-lookup table on catalog miss: if the receiver class belongs to a budget-exceeded gem, the call resolves to `Dynamic[top]` rather than falling through to the user-class fallback. `Index#class_to_gem` (frozen `Hash<class_name, gem_name>`, first-write-wins) and `Index#gem_for(class_name)` are the new lookup surface.
- **Config-conflict diagnostic (5d).** `dynamic.dependency-source.config-conflict` `:warning` surfaces `dependencies.source_inference[]` entries for the same gem that disagree on `mode:` across an `.rigor.yml` `includes:` chain. The later (downstream-include) entry's mode wins; `roots:` are unioned silently. One diagnostic per conflicting `(gem, prior-mode, new-mode)` triple. `Configuration.merge_dependencies_hash` carves out `dependencies.source_inference[]` from the standard right-wins merge so `Dependencies.from_h` sees every contributor's entries; `Dependencies.dedupe_entries` performs the per-gem merge with conflict detection.

#### ADR-11 — rigor-sorbet plugin

- **Slice 1 (sig parser + base type vocabulary).** `plugins/rigor-sorbet/` reads inline Sorbet `sig { params(...).returns(T) }` / `sig { void }` blocks above first-party method definitions and contributes the parsed return type at every call site through the v0.1.1 Track 2 slice 7 `flow_contribution_for` substrate. Slice 1 vocabulary covers class constants → `Nominal`, `T.untyped` → `Dynamic[top]`, `T.anything` → `top`, `T.noreturn` → `bot`, `T.nilable(X)` → `Union[X, nil]`, `T.any(...)` → `Union`, `T.all(...)` → `Intersection`, `T::Boolean` → `Union[Constant[true], Constant[false]]`. Modifiers (`abstract` / `override` / `overridable` / `final`) are recognised and recorded.
- **Slice 2 (type assertions).** `T.let` / `T.cast` / `T.must` / `T.unsafe` recognised at call sites. New `AssertionRecognizer` hooks into `flow_contribution_for` BEFORE the catalog lookup. `T.let(expr, T)` / `T.cast(expr, T)` translate the second argument and return that as the call's contribution; `T.must(expr)` returns `inferred(expr) - nil` via the `Difference` carrier; `T.unsafe(expr)` returns `Dynamic[top]`.
- **Slice 3 (widened type vocabulary).** `T::Array[E]` / `T::Hash[K, V]` / `T::Set[E]` / `T::Range[E]` / `T::Enumerable[E]` / `T::Enumerator[E]` translate to `Nominal[<class>, [<type-args>]]`. `T.class_of(C)` → `Singleton[C]`; `T::Class[T]` → `Singleton[T-class-name]` (lossy). Bare tuple literals `[A, B]` and shape literals `{a: A, b: B}` in sig position translate to `Tuple` / `HashShape`.
- **Slice 4 (RBI tree walking).** New `rbi_paths:` config (default `["sorbet/rbi"]`) tells the plugin where to find Sorbet's RBI tree. The walker treats `.rbi` files as Ruby source — sig+def pairing applies the same way it does to project source — so signatures from autogenerated and hand-written gem stubs feed the same catalog as project sigs. The Tapioca subdirectories (`gems/`, `annotations/`, `dsl/`, `shims/`) participate via parent-root recursion. Set `rbi_paths: []` to opt out.
- **Slice 5 (sigil detection at catalog harvest).** `SigilDetector` reads Sorbet's `# typed:` magic comment from the head of each file. `# typed: ignore` files are skipped during catalog harvest. Levels `:false` / `:true` / `:strict` / `:strong` are recognised; the per-file gating extends in the `enforce_sigil` light follow-up below.
- **Slice 6 (T.absurd exhaustiveness).** `AbsurdRecognizer` hooks into `flow_contribution_for` ahead of every other recogniser. Every `T.absurd(x)` call gets a `FlowContribution` with `return_type: bot` and `exceptional: :raises`, faithful to Sorbet's runtime behaviour. When the discriminant `x`'s narrowed type at the absurd call hasn't collapsed to `bot`, the recogniser records the call node and `diagnostics_for_file` surfaces a `plugin.sorbet.absurd-reachable` `:warning`.
- **Slice 7 (handbook chapter 10).** New end-user handbook chapter at `docs/handbook/10-sorbet.md` covers the basic translation contract, the type-vocabulary table, `T.let` / `T.cast` / `T.must` / `T.unsafe` assertions, RBI walking, `# typed:` sigils, `T.absurd` exhaustiveness, the Tapioca DSL mixin pattern, the dispatcher tier ordering, and three migration patterns (run side-by-side, Sorbet-for-sigs / Rigor-for-narrowing, gradual Sorbet → RBS). Cross-link from `docs/handbook/01-getting-started.md` "When inference is not enough" gains a fifth escape hatch entry.
- **Slice 8 (Tapioca DSL mixin chain resolution).** `Catalog` gains `record_mixin(class_name:, kind:, module_name:)` and `mixins_for(class_name)`; `CatalogWalker.walk_statements` recognises `include` / `extend` calls alongside the existing sig+def pairing; `Sorbet#chain_lookup` walks the recorded chain on every call site. Closes the Tapioca DSL RBI gap — `Post#body` resolves through `Post::GeneratedAttributeMethods#body` automatically.
- **Light follow-up — `T.must_because` + `T.reveal_type`.** `T.must_because(expr, "explanation")` is recognised as an alias of `T.must`. `T.reveal_type(expr)` returns `expr` unchanged + surfaces the inferred type as a `plugin.sorbet.reveal-type` `:info` diagnostic at the call site.
- **Light follow-up — `T.assert_type!`.** Returns the asserted type as the call's contribution (T.cast-compatible). The plugin runs the static subtype check at recogniser time via `Rigor::Inference::Acceptance.accepts(asserted, inferred, mode: :gradual)`: a `:no` result records the call for `plugin.sorbet.assert-type-mismatch` `:error` emission; `:yes` and `:maybe` are silenced (gradual consistency — Dynamic[top] and uncertain shapes trust the user, matching the runtime check's role).
- **Light follow-up — `T.bind(self, T)`.** `AssertionRecognizer.recognize_bind` builds a `FlowContribution` with `return_type: Constant[nil]` AND `post_return_facts: [Fact(target_kind: :self, type: translated T)]`. The engine's plugin-fact wiring picks up the post-return fact and narrows `scope.self_type` for the surrounding scope via `apply_self_post_return_fact`. In a block body, the surrounding scope is the block's own scope, so the narrowing applies to the rest of the block — exactly Sorbet's documented contract. The recogniser requires the first argument to be a literal `Prism::SelfNode`.
- **Per-file sigil enforcement (`enforce_sigil` knob).** New `enforce_sigil` config knob (default `true`). Mirrors Sorbet's contract: types are not enforced at `# typed: false`, so under the default Rigor doesn't record sigs from `# typed: false` (or sigil-less, treated identically) files. `# typed: true` / `:strict` / `:strong` files contribute sigs as before; `# typed: ignore` continues to skip the file entirely. Set `enforce_sigil: false` to record every parseable file's sigs regardless of sigil. Parse-error diagnostics still surface for sigil-gated files (sigs flow into a discardable per-file catalog rather than the per-run one). Assertion recognisers (`T.let` / `T.cast` / `T.must` / `T.must_because` / `T.unsafe` / `T.reveal_type` / `T.assert_type!` / `T.bind`) are NOT gated — the user wrote those calls deliberately.

#### Plugin contract / cross-plugin substrate

- **Plugin-side `post_return_facts` wiring through the narrowing engine.** `Inference::StatementEvaluator#apply_plugin_assertions` is the sibling of the existing `apply_rbs_extended_assertions`: it collects every loaded plugin's `flow_contribution_for(call_node:, scope:)` result, merges through `FlowContribution::Merger.merge`, and applies the resulting `post_return_facts` via the existing `apply_post_return_fact` / `apply_self_post_return_fact` path. Closes ADR-7 § "Slice 4-A"'s plugin half: ADR-7 committed to "every call-site merge composes built-in, RBS::Extended, and (future) plugin contributions together"; the (future) plugin half lands here. The same wiring is the **substrate PHPStan-style Type-Specifying Extensions need** to be authorable from a Rigor plugin without engine changes — `T.bind` is the first concrete consumer.
- **rigor-activerecord ADR-9 `:model_index` publication.** `manifest(produces: [:model_index])` + new `prepare(services)` hook publishes a Marshal-clean `Hash{class_name => { table:, columns: }}` to the cross-plugin fact store. Downstream Tier-2 consumers (rigor-actionpack Phase 1, rigor-factorybot Phase 1 (c)) read it via `services.fact_store.read(plugin_id: "activerecord", name: :model_index)` without coupling to this plugin's `ModelIndex` / `SchemaTable::Column` carrier classes. Discovery failures leave the fact unpublished — consumers see `nil` and degrade to their own no-op path.

#### Rails ecosystem — Tier 1

- **`rigor-rails-routes`.** Statically interprets `config/routes.rb` via Prism — no Rails runtime dependency — and validates every `*_path` / `*_url` call site against the generated helper table. Recognises the v0.1.0 DSL surface from the Rails plugins roadmap: `Rails.application.routes.draw`, `resources` / `resource` (with `only:` / `except:`), `get`/`post`/`patch`/`put`/`delete` with `as:`, `root to:`, one level of `namespace`, one level of nested `resources`. `manifest(produces: [:helper_table])` publishes the parsed helper table as an ADR-9 fact for rigor-actionpack Phase 4 to consume.
- **`rigor-rails-i18n`.** Walks `locale_search_paths` (default `config/locales/`), reads every `.yml` / `.yaml` file through the trusted `IoBoundary`, and folds the per-locale tree into a flat `dotted_key => Entry` index. Validates every `t(literal_key, ...)` / `I18n.t(...)` call site — `unknown-key` (with did-you-mean), `missing-locale` (per-locale presence + `default:` suppression), `wrong-interpolation` / `extra-interpolation` (placeholder mismatch). `YAML.safe_load(aliases: true)` parser; reserved I18n option keys (`default:` / `scope:` / `locale:` / `count:` / `raise:` / etc.) are excluded from the extra-interpolation check.
- **`rigor-actionmailer`.** Discovers ActionMailer subclasses by walking `mailer_search_paths` (default `app/mailers/`), indexes each class's `def`s as action methods, and validates `Mailer.action(args).deliver_*` call sites — direct calls, `.with(...)` parametrization, and trailing delivery. `mailer-call` (info), `wrong-arity` (error), `unknown-action` (error), `missing-view` (warning, anchored at the action's `def` line in the mailer file).
- **`rigor-activejob`.** Discovers ActiveJob subclasses by walking `job_search_paths` (default `app/jobs/`), reads each `#perform`'s syntactic parameter list, and validates `Job.perform_later(...)` / `.perform_now(...)` / `.perform(...)` argument arity at every call site.

#### Rails ecosystem — Tier 2

- **`rigor-actionpack` Phase 4 (route-helper consumption).** Reads the `:helper_table` fact published by `rigor-rails-routes` (`manifest(consumes: [{ plugin_id: "rails-routes", name: :helper_table, optional: true }])`) and validates every implicit-self `*_path` / `*_url` call inside files under `controller_search_paths` (default `app/controllers`). Three diagnostics: `helper-call` (info), `unknown-helper` (error with did-you-mean), `wrong-helper-arity` (error). The `optional: true` flag means rigor-actionpack still loads when `rigor-rails-routes` isn't configured; Phase 4 silently degrades to a no-op.
- **`rigor-actionpack` Phase 2 (filter chain validation).** `before_action :name` / `after_action` / `around_action` / `skip_*` / `prepend_*` family validated against the controller's effective method set (own methods plus methods defined on the immediate parent class — one level of inheritance). `controller_index` cached producer walks `controller_search_paths` (default `app/controllers`), parses each file with Prism, and records `(class_name, defined_methods, parent_class_name)` for every top-level class node. Two diagnostics: `filter-call` (info), `unknown-filter-method` (error with did-you-mean drawn from the controller's effective method set).
- **`rigor-actionpack` Phase 3 (render-target validation).** `render :symbol` / `render "string/path"` / `render partial: "..."` validated against `view_search_paths` (default `["app/views"]`). The recogniser walks every `def` body for explicit `render` calls and derives a candidate view path from the controller's class name + the render argument shape. Pass-through shapes (`render plain:`, `render json:`, `render layout:`, `render text:`, `render inline:`, `render :nothing => true`) are silently skipped. Two diagnostics: `render-target` (info), `missing-template` (error). The `.html.erb` / `.text.erb` extension family is hardcoded in this slice; configurable template families ship behind a follow-up.
- **`rigor-actionpack` Phase 1 (strong-parameter validation).** `params.require(:user).permit(:name, :email)` chains validated against AR columns. The recogniser walks every `:permit` call site whose receiver is a `:require` call with a literal `Prism::SymbolNode` first argument, takes the symbol's `.to_s.split("_").map(&:capitalize).join` as the candidate model class name (`:user` → `User`, `:order_item` → `OrderItem`), and validates each permit `:key` against the column list published by `rigor-activerecord` (via the cross-plugin `:model_index` fact). Two diagnostics: `permit-call` (info), `unknown-permit-key` (error with did-you-mean drawn from the model's column list). Namespaced models (`:admin_user` → `Admin::User`), non-literal `permit` arguments, and nested permit (`permit(profile_attrs: [:name])`) are deferred.
- **`rigor-factorybot` (Phase 1 (a) self-contained validation).** Validates every `FactoryBot.create(:name, key: ...)` / `.build` / `.build_stubbed` / `.attributes_for` / `.create_list` / `.build_list` / `.build_stubbed_list` call (and the legacy `FactoryGirl.*` aliases) against a per-run `FactoryIndex` built from `factory_search_paths` (default `["spec/factories", "spec/factories.rb"]`). Three diagnostics: `factory-call` (info), `unknown-factory` (error with did-you-mean), `unknown-attribute` (error with did-you-mean). What's recognised inside `factory :name do ... end`: `name { "Alice" }` (modern method_missing block form), `name "Alice"` (legacy positional form), `add_attribute(:name) { ... }` (explicit form). Sequences, associations, traits, and parent / child relationships are deferred.
- **`rigor-factorybot` (Phase 1 (c) AR column cross-check).** Cross-plugin consumer of rigor-activerecord's `:model_index`. When both plugins are loaded, kwargs in `FactoryBot.create(...)` calls are validated against the UNION of (1) the factory's declared attributes and (2) the AR model's columns. FactoryBot's runtime accepts any AR attribute regardless of whether the factory declared it; the static check now mirrors that. A kwarg that's NEITHER a factory attribute NOR a model column still fires `unknown-attribute`.

#### Rails ecosystem — Tier 3

- **`rigor-pundit`.** Discovers Pundit policy classes by walking `policy_search_paths` (default `app/policies/`) — direct-superclass match against `policy_base_classes` (default `ApplicationPolicy`) plus a `*Policy` suffix gate — indexes each class's instance-side predicate methods (`def name?`), and validates `authorize(record, :action)` / `policy(record)` / `policy_scope(scope)` call sites. Constant arguments hit the policy class directly; expression arguments query `Scope#type_of` and only fire on `Nominal[T]`. Predicate normalisation: both `:update` and `:update?` resolve to `update?`. Three diagnostics: `policy-call` (info), `unknown-policy-class` (error with did-you-mean), `unknown-policy-method` (error with did-you-mean).
- **`rigor-sidekiq`.** Discovers Sidekiq workers by walking `worker_search_paths` (default `app/workers`, `app/sidekiq`) — direct `include` match against `worker_marker_modules` (default `Sidekiq::Job` / legacy `Sidekiq::Worker`) — and validates `Worker.perform_async(...)` / `.perform_inline(...)` / `.perform_in(...)` / `.perform_at(...)` argument shape. Schedule-aware arity: direct entries forward every argument to `#perform`; scheduled entries consume the FIRST argument as the schedule and forward the rest. Three diagnostics: `worker-call` (info), `wrong-arity` (error), `missing-schedule` (error).
- **`rigor-rspec` (deliberately scoped).** Duplicate `let` / `subject` declarations within the same scope (warning) and self-referencing `let` / `subject` blocks (error). Pure syntactic walk; no RSpec runtime dependency. Scope-aware: `ScopeWalker` builds a tree of `describe` / `context` scopes anchored on each `RSpec.describe` block. Inner shadows are intentional — only same-scope duplicates are flagged. Two diagnostics: `duplicate-let` (warning), `self-reference` (error).
- **`rigor-actioncable`.** Discovers ActionCable channel classes by walking `channel_search_paths` (default `app/channels/`), indexes each channel's instance-side action methods, and recursively walks each channel body for `stream_from` / `stream_for` registrations. Two-axis validation: `<X>Channel.broadcast_to(record, data)` validates the channel class exists; `ActionCable.server.broadcast("stream_name", data)` validates the literal stream name is registered. Dynamic-stream suppression: when ANY discovered channel uses a dynamic registration, the `unknown-stream` warning is suppressed across the whole project. Four diagnostics: `broadcast-target` (info), `broadcast-stream` (info), `unknown-channel` (error with did-you-mean), `unknown-stream` (warning with did-you-mean).

#### Documentation

- **Handbook chapter 7 — `@phpstan-assert` correspondence table.** New "Coming from PHPStan?" section in `docs/handbook/07-rbs-and-extended.md` maps every `@phpstan-assert*` PHPDoc annotation to its `RBS::Extended` directive equivalent: `@phpstan-assert T $x` ⇄ `%a{rigor:v1:assert: x is T}`, `@phpstan-assert-if-true` / `-if-false` ⇄ `%a{rigor:v1:predicate-if-*}`, negation form `!T` ⇄ `~T`. Includes a worked "assertNotNull" example plus the self-targeted shape for receivers narrowing themselves. Cross-links to chapter 9 noting that PHPStan-style "Type-Specifying Extensions" (call-shape recognition) are now expressible from Rigor plugins.
- **ADR-10 5c boundary-cross diagnostic deferral.** Investigated the boundary-cross slice and confirmed it has no condition under which it would fire in v0.1.3's dispatcher: plugin and RBS contributions preempt gem-source entirely (plugins → RBS → gem-source in the tier order), so there's no overlap to surface as a "boundary cross." The diagnostic only becomes meaningful once `mode: full` dispatches distinctively. `docs/internal-spec/dependency-source-inference.md` updates the `boundary-cross` row in the diagnostic-family table and the "Open questions" entry to spell out the dependency.

### Fixed

- **`FlowContribution::Merger` no longer raises `NameError` when invoked through `exe/rigor`.** The merger references `MergeResult` / `Conflict` / `Fact` but only `lib/rigor.rb` (the gem entry) eagerly loaded them; `rigor/cli` → `analysis/runner` → `inference/method_dispatcher` → `flow_contribution/merger` did not. Plugin contributions through `flow_contribution_for` therefore crashed at the first call site whenever the demos / CLI exercised them. Adds `require_relative` for the three siblings at the top of `merger.rb` so the load order matches the eager gem-entry path.


## [0.1.2] - 2026-05-09

The twelfth preview. Theme: **put the v0.1.1 plugin substrate to work, broaden CheckRules coverage, and round out the DX surface**. Track 1 migrates four of the seven worked example plugins (`rigor-lisp-eval`, `rigor-pattern`, `rigor-units`, `rigor-activerecord`) from "info diagnostic only" to `flow_contribution_for` return-type narrowing — chained calls now resolve through the analyzer's normal dispatch instead of the RBS-level `untyped` envelope. Track 2 ships six new CheckRules (`flow.unreachable-branch`, `flow.dead-assignment`, `flow.always-truthy-condition`, `def.method-visibility-mismatch`, `def.ivar-write-mismatch`, plus a refinement-aware extension of `def.return-type-mismatch`), each with a deliberately conservative envelope shaped by self-analysis on `lib/`. Track 3 lands the DX-tooling slate the v0.1.1 "Out of scope" list named: `rigor explain <rule>` for terminal rule docs, `rigor diff <baseline.json>` for CI baseline-aware checking, `# rigor:disable-file <rule>` for file-scope suppression, `schemas/rigor-config.schema.json` for editor autocomplete via the yaml-language-server convention. Engine-depth follow-ups close two long-standing items: `Plugin::IoBoundary#open_url` lifts behind a host allowlist (`network_policy: :allowlist` + `allowed_url_hosts:`), and the C-body classifier now recognises `_modify` / `_modifiable`-named mutation gates so `String#replace` and friends classify natively without per-class blocklists. Two `OverloadSelector` precision fixes (interface-strictness + `Data.define` block-body method discovery) close self-analysis gaps the prior release flagged.

### Added

- **Example plugins migrated from "info diagnostic only" to `flow_contribution_for` return-type narrowing.** Four of the seven worked plugin examples now contribute call-site return types via the v0.1.1 Track 2 slice 7 substrate, so chained calls resolve through the analyzer's normal dispatch instead of the RBS-level `untyped` fall-back. The diagnostic trace stays — both channels run from the same interpretation.
  - **`rigor-lisp-eval`** — `Lisp.eval(literal)` narrows to the carrier the literal interpreter produces (`Nominal[Integer]`, `Nominal[Float]`, `Union[Constant[true], Constant[false]]`, or unions across `:if` branches). Type-error and unknown-expression cases stay at the RBS untyped envelope so the existing `:error` / silent fall-through behaviour is unchanged.
  - **`rigor-pattern`** — `validate(:name, value)` narrows to the value argument's type on a successful match (typically `Constant<String>` after Rigor's literal-string folding). Mismatches keep the `literal-mismatch` diagnostic and stay untyped — propagating `bot` would silence the diagnostic-driven feedback the README centres on.
  - **`rigor-units`** — Dimensional arithmetic / chained constructors / queries narrow through the existing `MethodTable` dispatch (`Distance / Time -> Speed`, `Distance + Distance -> Distance`, `Speed * Time -> Distance`, `.in_<unit>` queries return `Float`, etc.). The plugin's `dimension_for_type` helper folds Rigor's nominal carriers back into the table's dimension Symbols and translates the result back through `DIMENSION_NOMINALS`.
  - **`rigor-activerecord`** — `Model.find(id)` narrows to `Nominal[Model]`, so chained `User.find(1).name` resolves through the analyzer's normal dispatch. `Model.find_by(...)` narrows to `Nominal[Model] | nil` because Rails returns nil when no row matches. `where` / `find_or_*` are intentionally deferred (relations need a richer carrier than the current Nominal/Tuple shapes carry).
- **`spec/integration/examples/support/plugin_helpers.rb` accepts a `signature_paths:` keyword.** Lets a plugin integration spec materialise an RBS sig file under the per-test tmpdir and thread its directory through `Configuration#signature_paths`. The new narrowing tests use this to provide minimal sigs for user-defined classes (`User` for `rigor-activerecord`, the `Distance / Time / Speed` family for `rigor-units`) so `call.undefined-method` can fire on them — the rule's `rbs_class_known?` gate would otherwise silence the diagnostic for unknown classes.
- **`schemas/rigor-config.schema.json` — JSON Schema for `.rigor.yml`.** A JSON Schema 2020-12 description of every key the loader recognises (`target_ruby` / `paths` / `exclude` / `plugins` / `disable` / `libraries` / `signature_paths` / `fold_platform_specific_paths` / `cache.path` / `plugins_io.network` / `plugins_io.allowed_paths` / `severity_profile` / `severity_overrides` / `includes`) with type / pattern / enum constraints derived from `Configuration::DEFAULTS`, `SeverityProfile::VALID_PROFILES`, `SeverityProfile::VALID_SEVERITIES`, and the plugin entry coercion rules. Editors that recognise the [`# yaml-language-server: $schema=...`](https://github.com/redhat-developer/yaml-language-server) magic comment (VS Code's YAML extension, IntelliJ-family IDEs, Helix, Neovim with `yaml-ls`) get autocomplete, hover docs, and structural validation while editing. The committed `.rigor.dist.yml` ships the comment pointing at `schemas/rigor-config.schema.json` (relative path); `rigor init` writes the same comment with an absolute GitHub URL so freshly-initialised projects pick up the schema without a working tree of Rigor's source. A structural spec (`spec/rigor/config_schema_spec.rb`) pins that every `Configuration::DEFAULTS` key has a schema entry and that the `severity_profile` / `severity_overrides` / `plugins_io.network` enums match the runtime `VALID_*` constants — so the schema and the loader cannot drift apart silently. Closes the DX-tooling track item "`.rigor.yml` JSON schema for editor autocomplete".
- **`rigor diff <baseline.json>` CLI command.** Compares the current `rigor check` diagnostics against a saved baseline JSON (the output of a previous `rigor check --format=json` run) and prints the new / fixed delta. Identity for matching is the tuple `(path, line, column, rule, source_family, message)` — strict enough that an edit moving a diagnostic to a new line surfaces as one fixed + one new pair. CI workflow: commit `rigor.baseline.json` once, run `rigor diff rigor.baseline.json` per-PR; exit code is `1` only when new diagnostics appear, so adding regressions fails CI but legacy errors recorded in the baseline don't. Two operating modes — by default the command runs `rigor check` for the current side, or `--current=<path>` compares two saved files without invoking the analyzer. `--format=json` emits the diff payload (`{baseline, baseline_count, current_count, new, fixed}`) for editor / dashboard consumption. Closes the DX-tooling track item "rigor diff <baseline>".
- **File-scope suppression — `# rigor:disable-file <rule>`.** Rules listed after `disable-file` apply to every line in the file, not just the line carrying the comment. Convention is to put the comment near the top, but Rigor scans every comment in the file so any placement works. `# rigor:disable-file all` suppresses every rule across the file. Family wildcards (`call`, `flow`, `assert`, `dump`, `def`) and legacy unprefixed names compose the same way as on the line-scope `# rigor:disable` comment. The line-scope `# rigor:disable` form is unchanged — file-scope is purely additive. Closes the DX-tooling track item "`# rigor:disable-file <rule>`".
- **`rigor explain <rule>` CLI command.** Prints the catalog entry for a canonical rule id, a legacy alias (`undefined-method`), or a family wildcard (`call`, `flow`, `assert`, `dump`, `def`). Without arguments lists every rule's id and one-line summary. Each entry carries: summary, "fires when" / "does not fire when" bullet lists, suppression note, authored severity, severity-by-profile table, and the version the rule shipped in. `--format=json` is supported for editor / IDE integrations. The new `Rigor::Analysis::RuleCatalog` module is the single-source-of-truth: `Rigor::Analysis::RuleCatalog::ENTRIES` is keyed by canonical rule id, `RuleCatalog.resolve(token)` handles canonical / legacy / family-wildcard lookup. The command is read-only — no parser, no analyzer, no I/O — useful when a user sees a diagnostic in the editor and wants to know what the rule means without leaving the terminal.
- **C-body classifier recognises `_modify` / `_modifiable` naming-convention helpers.** The v0.0.5 mutator-helper recogniser only matched bodies whose source was exactly `{ rb_check_frozen(arg); }`. v0.1.2 extends the seed to also catch helpers whose **name** matches the `_modify` / `_modifiable` Ruby C convention (regex `/(?:_(?:modify|modifiable))(?:\b|_)/`) AND whose body issues a `rb_check_frozen` / `rb_check_lockedtmp` call. The body-content guard rules out functions that just happen to have `_modify` in the name without being a mutation gate. The naming-convention seed catches gates the strict regex misses — `str_modifiable` (body has `RUBY_ASSERT` + `if` + `rb_check_frozen`), `rb_struct_modify`, `range_modify`, `rb_class_modify_check`, etc. Catalog regen flips the `c_effects: [mutate]` / `purity: mutates_self` markers for `String#replace`, `String#initialize_copy`, `String#chomp!`, `String#delete_suffix!`, `String#force_encoding`, `Range#initialize`, `Range#initialize_copy` (all were previously absorbed by per-class blocklists). The transitive-closure-on-first-arg-formal-param approach was also explored during this slice but reverted — it caught a few real chains (`time_localtime_m -> time_localtime -> time_modify`) but false-positived on functions where the first arg is a formal yet the helper doesn't actually mutate it (`rb_ary_reject` passes `ary` to `ary_reject` which iterates `ary` and pushes to a separately-passed array). Static C-level analysis can't distinguish "uses formal as receiver" from "uses formal as input"; the seed-only recogniser is the principled middle ground. Per-class blocklists in `lib/rigor/inference/builtins/*_catalog.rb` continue to absorb anything still missing.
- **`def.return-type-mismatch` honours `%a{rigor:v1:return: <refinement>}`.** When the RBS sig carries a return-type override annotation, the refinement carrier (`non-empty-string`, `positive-int`, `non-empty-array[Integer]`, etc.) replaces the bare RBS class for the rule's acceptance check. So a body the underlying class would accept can still fire the rule when it fails the refinement — `def name; ""; end` returns `Constant[""]`, accepted by `String` but rejected by `non-empty-string`. The annotation surface already powered call-site narrowing through `RbsExtended.read_return_type_override`; this hooks the same source into the static return-type check, closing the v0.1.1 "Out of scope" `def.return-type-mismatch` for type-carrier predicates item.
- **`flow.always-truthy-condition` rule.** The inferred-constant counterpart to v0.1.2's `flow.unreachable-branch` (which only fires on syntactic literals). Fires when an `if` / `unless` / ternary's predicate folds to a `Type::Constant` and the surrounding code does NOT match one of the false-positive shapes Rigor's incomplete loop / mutation / RBS-strictness modelling produces. Two surgical skips: (1) predicate inside any `WhileNode` / `UntilNode` / `ForNode` / `BlockNode` ancestor — Rigor's mutation tracking through loop bodies is incomplete, so an inferred `Constant<bool>` there is suspect; (2) predicate is a defensive `.nil?` / `.empty?` / `.zero?` / `.any?` / `.none?` / `.all?` / `.respond_to?` call — these typically fire when the user is being more cautious than the RBS strict-on-returns sig admits (e.g. `Module#name -> String` per RBS but anonymous classes really do return nil). Predicates whose direct AST shape is a literal are skipped so `flow.unreachable-branch` doesn't double-fire alongside this rule. The diagnostic points at the predicate (the user's claim), not the dead branch (which `flow.unreachable-branch` already covers). New `Analysis::CheckRules::AlwaysTruthyConditionCollector` walks the tree top-down with `in_loop_or_block:` depth tracking. Authored severity `:warning`; severity profile entries: `:info` (lenient), `:warning` (balanced), `:error` (strict). Suppressible via `# rigor:disable always-truthy-condition` on the predicate line. Closes the v0.1.1 "Out of scope" `flow.always-truthy-condition` item.
- **`Plugin::IoBoundary#open_url` allowlist surface.** v0.1.0 shipped `#open_url` as an always-deny stub. v0.1.2 lifts the policy gate behind a host allowlist: `Plugin::TrustPolicy` accepts `network_policy: :allowlist` and a new `allowed_url_hosts:` array. `Plugin::IoBoundary#open_url` now performs a GET-only HTTPS fetch when `policy.allow_url?(url)` returns true (HTTPS scheme + exact-match hostname on the allowlist) and raises `AccessDeniedError` otherwise. Caps: 10s timeout, 10 MB body. The fetcher is dependency-injected via the `http_client:` keyword on `IoBoundary.new`, defaulting to a thin `DefaultHttpClient` over `Net::HTTP`; specs inject a fake client without hitting the network. Successful fetches record a `Cache::Descriptor::ConfigEntry` keyed `"url:<url>"` with the response body's SHA-256, so plugin contributions invalidate when the remote document changes. Configuration adds `plugins_io.allowed_url_hosts:` (default `[]`); the JSON schema documents the new key and extends the `plugins_io.network` enum to `["disabled", "allowlist"]`. `Configuration::VALID_NETWORK_POLICIES` and `TrustPolicy::VALID_NETWORK_POLICIES` stay in lockstep via the `config_schema_spec.rb` drift pin. The `:protected` arm and wildcard hostnames are intentionally deferred. Closes the v0.1.1 "Out of scope" `Plugin::IoBoundary#open_url` allowlist item.
- **`flow.dead-assignment` rule.** Walks every `DefNode` body and emits a diagnostic for each plain `LocalVariableWriteNode` whose target name is never read in the same body. The new `Analysis::CheckRules::DeadAssignmentCollector` does the work; the conservative envelope keeps the rule signal-rich without surfacing false positives. Skipped on purpose: top-level / class-body assignments (their reachability spans the file's introspection / require surface), names starting with `_` (Ruby's "intentionally unused" convention), operator-writes (`x += 1`) and and-/or-writes (`x ||= 1`) (their write semantics are intertwined with reads), `MultiWriteNode` destructures (`a, b = foo`), and the trailing assignment of a method body (Ruby's implicit return treats `def foo; x = 1; end` as returning `1`). Reads are gathered from the entire def subtree — closure captures, `return x`, block-call arguments, and interpolated strings all count as a read. Authored severity `:warning`; `severity_profile` re-stamps to `:info` (lenient), `:warning` (balanced), `:error` (strict). Suppressible via `# rigor:disable dead-assignment` or by renaming the local to `_<name>`.
- **`def.ivar-write-mismatch` rule.** Walks every class / module body, gathers each instance method's `@var = ...` writes per-class, and emits a diagnostic when a later write's concrete class disagrees with the first write's. Catches the typo / refactoring-leftover case of `@name = "Alice"` followed later by `@name = 42` without surfacing the legitimate widening idioms (`@cache = nil` to clear, multiple writes that share a class, separate classes that happen to share an ivar name). Concrete classes are taken from `concrete_class_name` (Nominal / Singleton / Constant / Tuple → "Array" / HashShape → "Hash"); Union / Dynamic / IntegerRange / shape-varied carriers fall through. `NilClass` is allowlisted for the "intentional clear" idiom. Singleton-method bodies (`def self.foo`) and class-body ivar writes outside any def are skipped — the latter is a separate `Module#@var` surface the engine doesn't yet model. Authored severity `:error`; severity profile entries: `:warning` (lenient and balanced), `:error` (strict). Suppressible via `# rigor:disable ivar-write-mismatch`. The dedicated `Analysis::CheckRules::IvarWriteCollector` performs the per-class walk and is the read-side companion to `Inference::ScopeIndexer#build_class_ivar_index` (the indexer unions the rvalue types into a single per-ivar carrier; this collector preserves the per-write list so the rule can compare).
- **`def.method-visibility-mismatch` rule.** Fires when an explicit-receiver `Prism::CallNode` targets a user-class method whose source-side visibility is `:private`. The new `Scope#discovered_method_visibilities` table is populated by `Inference::ScopeIndexer` from the surrounding class body — modifier blocks (`private` / `protected` / `public` with no args switch the running default for subsequent `def`s) and the named-argument form (`private :foo, :bar` back-patches specific names). Implicit-self calls and `self.foo` are skipped (Ruby 2.7+ permits `self.private_method`). Receiver MUST resolve to a `Type::Nominal` so the rule has a single class identity to query; unions / Dynamic / shape carriers are skipped. Authored severity `:error`; `severity_profile` re-stamps to `:warning` (lenient), `:error` (balanced and strict). Suppressible via `# rigor:disable method-visibility-mismatch` and `disable: [method-visibility-mismatch]`. The `:protected` arm is intentionally deferred — the access check needs subclass tracking the engine does not yet model. RBS-known classes are also intentionally NOT consulted yet — RBS visibility is reliable but the broader surface needs separate per-call-site triage. Closes the v0.1.1 "Out of scope" `def.method-visibility-mismatch` item.

- **`flow.unreachable-branch` rule.** Fires when an `if` / `unless` / ternary's predicate is a syntactic literal (`true` / `false` / `nil` / Integer / Float / String / Symbol / Regexp) AND the corresponding dead branch carries a non-empty body. The diagnostic points at the dead branch, not the predicate, so the squiggle lands on the code that never runs. Authored severity `:warning`; `severity_profile` re-stamps to `:info` (lenient), `:warning` (balanced), `:error` (strict). Suppressible via `# rigor:disable unreachable-branch` on the dead-branch line and `disable: [unreachable-branch]` in `.rigor.yml`. The literal-only envelope is intentional — inferred-constant predicates (e.g. `module.name.nil?` folded to `Constant<false>` because `Module#name -> String` per RBS but anonymous classes really do return nil at runtime, or accumulator `truthy_members.empty?` whose `<<` mutation Rigor's loop modelling doesn't widen) can be false positives, so v0.1.2 ships only the unambiguous user-written shape. Broadening to inferred-constant predicates is queued for a later v0.1.x release once those gaps close.

### Changed

- **`Data.define` / `Struct.new` block-body method discovery.** `ScopeIndexer.walk_methods` and `walk_def_nodes` now recurse into the block body of a `Const = Data.define(*sym) do ... end` / `Const = Struct.new(*sym) do ... end` write with `Const`'s qualified name pushed onto the prefix. Block-body `def initialize(...)` and any other method overrides are registered under the constant's name in both `discovered_methods` and `discovered_def_nodes`, so a `Point.new` whose `Point` constant is RBS-known no longer surfaces a false-positive `call.undefined-method` for block-defined accessors. Closes the v0.0.5 follow-up "override-aware initializer-signature dispatch (using the block's `def initialize(...)` as the canonical sig)".
- **Interface-strictness preference in `OverloadSelector`.** The selector now runs in two passes. Pass 1 considers only overloads whose param types stay strictly typed — `RBS::Types::Alias` (`::int`, `::interned`, …), `RBS::Types::Interface` (`_ToInt`, `_Each[T]`, …), `RBS::Types::Intersection`, and the explicit `RBS::Types::Bases::Any` (`untyped` keyword) all degrade to `Dynamic[Top]` through the translator and gradually accept anything, so they're skipped in pass 1. Pass 2 falls back to the existing gradual matcher when no fully strict overload applies. The strict pass is also skipped when any arg is itself `Dynamic[Top]` (literal `untyped`), because gradual acceptance against an untyped arg accepts every param indiscriminately and would lock in an arbitrary strict overload (e.g. `Regexp#=~(nil) -> nil` would beat `(::interned?) -> Integer?`). The change closes a long-standing self-analysis miss: `Array[String]#[](Range)` now returns `Array[String]?` (via the `(::Range[::Integer?]) -> ::Array[Elem]?` overload) instead of `String` (via the `(::int) -> Elem` alias-typed overload that previously won by coming first). The matching `# rigor:disable call.undefined-method` workaround at `tool/extract_builtin_catalog.rb:750` is removed.
- The `rigor-deprecations`, `rigor-statesman`, and `rigor-routes` example plugins remain diagnostic-only. Deprecation reports and state-machine declarations have no return-type fit; route helpers (`users_path`) are already RBS-expressible.

## [0.1.1] - 2026-05-08

The eleventh preview, and a **multi-track follow-up to the v0.1.0 plugin-contract release**. Four parallel tracks landed: Track 1 (literal-string / refinement narrowing depth — regex pattern → refinement-name recogniser, digit-only refinement propagation through `to_i` / `Integer(s)`, full self-narrowing in predicate / assert paths, additional `literal-string` propagation rules); Track 2 (cross-plugin API per [ADR-9](docs/adr/9-cross-plugin-api.md) — `Plugin::FactStore`, `Plugin::Base#prepare(services)`, `manifest(produces:/consumes:)`, topo-sorted plugin loading, `#flow_contribution_for` return-type contribution tier); Track 3 (plugin authoring DX — extracted spec helpers, per-demo cache isolation under `tmp/`, examples re-included in RuboCop); Track 4 (maintenance — prelude `composed` reclassification, three `lib/` sig drifts closed).

This release also introduces a **two-file configuration convention**: `.rigor.yml` is the developer-local override (auto-discovered first, `.gitignore`d by default), `.rigor.dist.yml` is the project default (committed to the repo). The two files are NEVER merged automatically — when a developer keeps a `.rigor.yml`, that file is the sole source of config for that developer's runs. PHPStan-style `includes:` lets an override extend the project default explicitly; path-bearing keys (`paths:`, `signature_paths:`, `plugins_io.allowed_paths:`, `includes:`) resolve relative to the directory of the config file that declares them. The committed config in this repo and every `examples/rigor-*/demo/.rigor.yml` moved to `.rigor.dist.yml` accordingly; `rigor init` now writes `.rigor.dist.yml` by default. The full rationale lives in [`docs/handbook/01-getting-started.md`](docs/handbook/01-getting-started.md) § "A first walk through Rigor's config file".

A seventh worked plugin example also lands in this cycle: **`rigor-activerecord`** validates `Model.find` / `Model.find_by` / `Model.where` calls against `db/schema.rb` and the discovered AR model classes — combining DSL interpretation, multi-file `IoBoundary` reads, chained cache producers, and two-pass discover-then-validate.

### Added

#### Steep cross-check baseline closes the only `[error]` ahead of v0.1.1

- **`sig/rigor.rbs:67`** referenced `Rigor::Cache::Store` but no `sig/rigor/cache/store.rbs` exists (the Cache namespace is in `UNSIGNED_NAMESPACES` per `spec/rigor/public_api_drift_spec.rb`). `make steep-check` raised the only `[error]`-level diagnostic — `RBS::UnknownTypeName: Rigor::Cache::Store`. Replaced with `untyped` until the full Cache::Store sig lands.
- **`Rigor::Analysis::Runner`'s sig** also gained `attr_reader plugin_registry: untyped` and `?plugin_requirer: untyped` to match the v0.1.1 Track 2 slice 7 surface (the `plugin_registry` / `plugin_requirer` Runner additions added without sig updates would have shown up as drift on the next sig audit).
- **Remaining 8 `[warning]`s** are all Steep's coarse handling of Ruby idioms (`Data.define do def initialize ... end end` override blocks, `Kernel#Array` narrowing on `Target | Array[Target]`, lambda defaults inside `def`). They sit in `D::Ruby.lenient` warning territory and will dissolve naturally as `Plugin::*` / `Cache::Store` sigs land. Triage at [`docs/notes/20260503-steep-cross-check-triage.md`](docs/notes/20260503-steep-cross-check-triage.md) updated with a v0.1.1 follow-up section.
- `make steep-check` is intentionally NOT part of `make verify` — it's an advisory cross-checker, not a release blocker.

#### `.rigor.yml` `exclude:` setting + built-in defaults

- **New `Configuration#exclude_patterns`** — a list of `File.fnmatch?` glob patterns layered over the project's directory globs. The runner's `expand_paths` consults the list when expanding a directory argument and skips any file whose path matches an exclusion pattern. Explicit file arguments to the CLI bypass the filter — only directory expansion is filtered.
- **Built-in defaults** that users can't disable (and rarely want to): `**/vendor/bundle/**`, `**/.bundle/**`, `**/node_modules/**`. These cover the cases where Rigor would otherwise walk into vendored gems / build artefacts and emit floods of diagnostics for code the user doesn't own.
  - Earlier self-analysis surfaced 1677 errors over 132 files when running `rigor check tool` against this repo, almost all of them inside `tool/steep/vendor/bundle/`. After this slice, `rigor check tool` reports `No diagnostics`.
- **User additions** layer on top via `.rigor.yml`:
  ```yaml
  exclude:
    - "spec/integration/fixtures/**"
    - "examples/*/demo/**"
  ```
  The `to_h` / round-trip surface returns only the user-supplied entries — built-in defaults are implicit and not echoed back.
- **`tool/extract_builtin_catalog.rb:750`** — point fix via `# rigor:disable call.undefined-method` for the `lines[j..k].join` site. Self-analysis revealed Rigor's overload selector currently picks `Array#[](int) -> Elem` over `Array#[](Range) -> Array[Elem]?` because the RBS `int` alias expands to `Integer | _ToInt` and Rigor translates the `_ToInt` interface to `Dynamic[top]`, which gradually accepts any argument including a Range. Tracked as a deferred v0.1.x item under "interface-strictness on overload selection" in `MILESTONES.md`.

#### `Const = Struct.new(*Symbol)` discovery — symmetric with `Data.define`

- **`Inference::ScopeIndexer.record_meta_new_constant?`** (renamed from `record_data_define_constant?`) now recognises `Const = Struct.new(:a, :b)` and the `keyword_init:` variant `Const = Struct.new(:a, :b, keyword_init: true)` alongside the existing `Const = Data.define(:a, :b)` recogniser. The discovered constant gets registered as `Singleton[<qualified-Const>]`, so `Const.new(...)` resolves to a fresh `Nominal[<qualified-Const>]` via `meta_new` instead of the un-narrowed `Dynamic[top]` returned by the default `Class#new` envelope.
- **Cleared 30 false positives across the seven worked plugin examples.** Before this slice, `examples/rigor-deprecations/lib`, `examples/rigor-routes/lib`, `examples/rigor-units/lib`, `examples/rigor-lisp-eval/lib`, `plugins/rigor-activerecord/lib` all fired `error: undefined method 'new' for Struct` at every site that used the canonical `Foo = Struct.new(:a, :b)` pattern. `bundle exec exe/rigor check examples/rigor-*/lib` now reports `No diagnostics` for each.
- **Trailing `KeywordHashNode`** (the `keyword_init: <expr>` form Ruby 3+ requires for keyword-arg struct types) is recognised and ignored at member-discovery time. Members must still all be `Prism::SymbolNode`; non-symbol positional args (e.g. `Struct.new(:x, "not_a_symbol")`) decline. `Struct.new()` with no positional args is a degenerate form Ruby allows but Rigor declines, since it has no members to discover.
- Spec coverage: 5 new examples in `spec/rigor/inference/scope_indexer_spec.rb` (basic discovery, `keyword_init:` variant, qualified-class-path discovery, non-symbol arg rejection, empty-arg rejection).

#### v0.1.1 Track 1 — narrowing depth

##### Slice 1 — regex pattern → refinement-name recogniser

- **New `Rigor::Builtins::RegexRefinement` module** at [`lib/rigor/builtins/regex_refinement.rb`](lib/rigor/builtins/regex_refinement.rb). Pure recogniser that maps a curated table of canonical regex sub-patterns (`\d+`, `\d{N}`, `\d{N,M}`, `\h+`, `[0-9a-fA-F]+`, `[0-9a-f]+`, `[0-9A-F]+`, `\h{N}`, `[0-7]+` and bounded forms, `[a-z]+`, `[A-Z]+`, `[[:digit:]]+`, all with `+` or `{n}` / `{n,m}` quantifiers where `n >= 1`) onto the imported refinement carriers Rigor already ships (`decimal-int-string`, `hex-int-string`, `octal-int-string`, `lowercase-string`, `uppercase-string`, `numeric-string`). Bodies that admit the empty string (`*`, `?`, `{0,N}`) or sit outside the audited table return `nil` so callers fall back to the v0.1.0 baseline (plain `String`). Spec at [`spec/rigor/builtins/regex_refinement_spec.rb`](spec/rigor/builtins/regex_refinement_spec.rb).
- **`Inference::Narrowing.analyse_match_write` consults the recogniser.** When the `MatchWriteNode`'s wrapped `=~` call has a statically known `RegularExpressionNode` receiver, every `(?<name>body)` whose body matches a recogniser row narrows the truthy edge of `if regex =~ str` to the matching refinement instead of plain `String`. Bodies with nested groups, alternation, anchors, or anything else outside the table fall back unchanged. The falsey edge is still `nil` per the v0.1.0 contract.
- A pattern like `if /(?<year>\d{4})-(?<month>\d{1,2})/ =~ str; year; month; end` now binds `year` and `month` to `decimal-int-string` in the truthy branch.

##### Slice 2 — digit-only refinements propagate through `to_i` / `Integer(s)`

- **2a — `String#to_i` / `#to_int`.** `MethodDispatcher::ShapeDispatch.dispatch_refined` gains four rows in `REFINED_STRING_PROJECTIONS` covering `to_i` / `to_int` on `decimal-int-string` and `numeric-string` receivers, returning `non-negative-int`. End-to-end: `if /(?<year>\d+)/ =~ str; year.to_i; end` resolves to `non-negative-int` instead of `Integer`. No-arg form only.
- **2b — `Kernel#Integer`.** `MethodDispatcher::KernelDispatch.try_integer_from_refinement` mirrors the rule for the single-arg `Kernel#Integer(s)` form.

##### Slice 3 — full `self`-narrowing in `predicate-if-*` / `assert-if-*` / `assert`

- **Three additional receiver shapes now narrow `self`-targeted facts** (in addition to the v0.1.0 LocalVariableReadNode case):
  - `Prism::InstanceVariableReadNode` — `@buddy.logged_in?` narrows `@buddy` itself on each edge via `Scope#with_ivar`.
  - `Prism::SelfNode` — `self.logged_in?` narrows `scope.self_type` via `Scope#with_self_type`.
  - Implicit self (nil receiver) — `logged_in?` with no receiver inside an instance method body narrows `scope.self_type`. `Inference::Narrowing#analyse_call` no longer rejects nil-receiver call shapes outright; the RBS::Extended path is allowed to run when the receiver is implicit.
- **`Inference::Narrowing#apply_self_fact`** is the new dispatch helper covering all four receiver shapes. Mirrored as `StatementEvaluator#apply_self_post_return_fact` for the `assert self is T` post-return path. `resolve_rbs_extended_method` consults `scope.self_type` when the receiver is nil so the implicit-self call's method definition is reachable.
- Inference engine spec at [`docs/internal-spec/inference-engine.md`](docs/internal-spec/inference-engine.md) updated. Integration fixture `spec/integration/fixtures/self_predicate/` extended with `User#greet_buddy` (ivar receiver) and `User#greet` (implicit self) cases.

##### Slice 4 — `String#start_with?` / `#end_with?` / `#include?` flow facts

- **`Inference::Narrowing#analyse_string_predicate`** recognises `s.start_with?("foo")`, `s.end_with?(...)`, and `s.include?(...)` against a `Constant<String>` needle and a `Prism::LocalVariableReadNode` receiver. The truthy edge attaches an `Analysis::FactStore::Fact` (`bucket: :relational`, `predicate:` the method name, `payload:` the needle string, `polarity: :positive`); the falsey edge attaches the `:negative` mirror.
- **No type narrowing.** Rigor has no "starts-with-X" carrier today, so the receiver's type stays unchanged on both edges. The slice intentionally lands the lightweight FactStore-based form first; a heavier carrier-based form may follow if downstream consumers need it. Receiver type gating is left to consumers (`String#start_with?` is String-only, but `Array#include?` exists with different semantics — facts are advisory).

##### Slice 5 — `literal-string` / `digit-int-string` propagation through additional methods

- **5a — `#strip` / `#lstrip` / `#rstrip` / `#chomp` (no-arg) / `#chop` / `#scrub` (no-arg).** `MethodDispatcher::LiteralStringFolding`'s `LITERAL_PRESERVING_METHODS` rule. Each strips a known character subset from the ends of the string (or replaces invalid bytes for `#scrub` — a no-op on always-valid literal source code), so a literal-bearing receiver stays literal-bearing. Result is plain `literal-string` (`non-empty-literal-string` collapses because `"   ".strip == ""`).
- **5b — `Integer#to_s` precision on non-negative `IntegerRange`.** `MethodDispatcher::ShapeDispatch` gains an `IntegerRange` receiver handler. When the range's lower bound is `>= 0`, `to_s(base)` returns a digit-string with no leading sign and lifts to the matching imported refinement: base 10 → `decimal-int-string`, base 8 → `octal-int-string`, base 16 → `hex-int-string`. Other bases (2, 36, …) and signed ranges keep the v0.1.0 baseline.
- **5c — `String#center` / `#ljust` / `#rjust`.** `LiteralStringFolding.fold_width_pad` lifts these on a literal-bearing receiver to `literal-string` when the width arg is Integer-typed and the (optional) padding arg is literal-bearing.
- **`Numeric#to_s` (no args)** intentionally not implemented: `Float#to_s` produces `.`-bearing strings, signed `Integer#to_s` produces `-`-prefixed strings, and no current Rigor refinement captures either. The non-negative-Integer case is covered by 5b.

#### v0.1.1 Track 2 — cross-plugin API + return-type contributions (ADR-9)

##### Slice 1 — `Rigor::Plugin::FactStore` value object

- **New `Rigor::Plugin::FactStore`** at [`lib/rigor/plugin/fact_store.rb`](lib/rigor/plugin/fact_store.rb). Per-run cross-plugin fact storage per [ADR-9](docs/adr/9-cross-plugin-api.md). A producer plugin (e.g. `rigor-activerecord`) publishes a typed `(plugin_id, name) -> value` triple in its `#prepare` hook (slice 3); a consumer plugin reads it via `services.fact_store.read(plugin_id:, name:)`. Constructed fresh at the start of every `Analysis::Runner.run` and discarded at the end — caching the underlying expensive computation is the producer's job (`Plugin::Base.producer`); the FactStore just publishes the *reference* to that already-cached result.
- **API surface** (frozen by `spec/rigor/public_api_drift_spec.rb`): `#publish(plugin_id:, name:, value:)`, `#read(plugin_id:, name:)`, `#published?(plugin_id:, name:)`, `#each_fact(&)`, plus the `Fact = Data.define(:plugin_id, :name, :value)` shape and `Conflict` exception class. `plugin_id` canonicalises to String, `name` to Symbol. Thread-safe via internal Mutex.
- **Conflict semantics.** A duplicate `publish` with the same value (`==`) is a no-op; differing values raise `Conflict`. Real conflict only happens when a single plugin publishes twice with differing values — the conflict signals a plugin-author bug, never a load-time interaction between unrelated plugins.

##### Slice 2 — `Plugin::Services#fact_store` accessor

- **`Rigor::Plugin::Services`** gains a `fact_store` attribute and a matching keyword arg. When no `fact_store:` is supplied, a fresh `Plugin::FactStore` instance is constructed per Services. Drift snapshot updated.

##### Slice 3 — `Plugin::Base#prepare(services)` hook + Runner invocation

- **New `Plugin::Base#prepare(services)`** default-no-op hook. Producer plugins override to compute and publish facts other plugins consume:
  ```ruby
  def prepare(services)
    services.fact_store.publish(plugin_id: manifest.id, name: :model_index, value: model_index)
  end
  ```
- **`Analysis::Runner` calls `#prepare`** on every loaded plugin once per `run`, after `#init` and before per-file iteration. Failure isolation: a `#prepare` raise becomes a `:plugin_loader runtime-error` diagnostic, mirroring the `#diagnostics_for_file` raise envelope. The plugin's facts are considered un-published; downstream consumers see `nil` from `fact_store.read` and degrade gracefully.

##### Slice 4 — `manifest(produces:)` / `manifest(consumes:)` declarations

- **`Rigor::Plugin::Manifest`** gains two new declarative fields. `produces:` is an `Array<Symbol>` listing the names this plugin publishes; `consumes:` is an `Array<{ plugin_id:, name:, optional: }>` listing the `(plugin_id, name)` pairs this plugin reads. The new `Rigor::Plugin::Manifest::Consumption` frozen `Data.define(:plugin_id, :name, :optional)` shape coerces hash entries (string- or symbol-keyed) into Consumption instances; malformed entries raise `ArgumentError` at class-definition time.

##### Slice 5 — topological sort + missing-producer detection in `Plugin::Loader`

- **`Plugin::Loader.load` topologically sorts** loaded plugins by their `manifest(consumes:)` declarations so producer plugins run before consumers in the runner's `#prepare` pass. Tie-break preserves `Configuration#plugins` order, keeping the v0.1.0 contract intact for plugins that don't opt into the cross-plugin API. The topo sort is skipped entirely when no loaded plugin declares a `consumes:` entry — same observable behaviour as before slice 5.
- **Missing producer detection.** A non-optional `consumes:` entry that names a `(plugin_id, name)` no loaded plugin produces emits a `Plugin::LoadError` with `reason: :"missing-producer"`, dropping the offending consumer. `optional: true` consumes skip the check.
- **Cycle detection.** A consumes graph that forms a cycle emits a `Plugin::LoadError` with `reason: :"dependency-cycle"` naming the offending plugins. Non-cycle plugins still load. `Plugin::LoadError` gains an optional `reason:` field carrying the new symbol codes; older callers omit `reason:` and the field defaults to nil.

##### Slice 7 — `Plugin::Base#flow_contribution_for` return-type contribution tier

- **New `Plugin::Base#flow_contribution_for(call_node:, scope:)`** default-no-op hook. Plugins override to return a `Rigor::FlowContribution` whose `return_type` slot pins the call site's result type. Hooks that raise have their contribution silently dropped per-call so the dispatch chain keeps going.
- **`MethodDispatcher.dispatch`** gains optional `call_node:` and `scope:` keywords. When both are provided and the receiver scope's environment carries a non-empty `plugin_registry`, a new tier between the precision tiers and `RbsDispatch` walks every loaded plugin's hook, merges via `FlowContribution::Merger`, and returns the merged `return_type` if present. Internal dispatcher callers (per-element block fold, etc.) skip the tier by passing nil for `call_node` / `scope`.
- **`Environment#plugin_registry`** new optional reader (default nil; `Environment.for_project(plugin_registry:)` accepts it). `Analysis::Runner` threads its per-run `Plugin::Registry` through here so the dispatcher tier can consult the loaded plugins from any call site.
- Migrating the seven example plugins from "info diagnostic only" to "narrowed return type" stays deferred — slice 7 lands the substrate; per-plugin migration is incremental follow-up.

#### Rails ecosystem plugin roadmap and ADR-9 design

- **[`docs/design/20260508-rails-plugins-roadmap.md`](docs/design/20260508-rails-plugins-roadmap.md)** captures the full plan for the `rigor-*` Rails ecosystem plugin family, building on the existing `rigor-activerecord` example. Three tiers: Tier 1 plugins (`rigor-rails-routes`, `rigor-rails-i18n`, `rigor-actionmailer`, `rigor-activejob`) need only the v0.1.0 plugin contract; Tier 2 (`rigor-actionpack` Phase 1, `rigor-factorybot`) blocks on ADR-9; Tier 3 plugins (`rigor-rspec`, `rigor-pundit`, `rigor-sidekiq`, `rigor-graphql`, `rigor-activestorage`, `rigor-actioncable`) author when there is concrete user demand. A future `rigor-rails` meta-gem aggregates Tier 1+2 dependencies.
- **[ADR-9 — Cross-plugin API](docs/adr/9-cross-plugin-api.md)** proposes the missing piece for Tier 2 plugins. Six independently shippable implementation slices documented; slices 1 → 5 + slice 7 landed in v0.1.1.
- **`docs/MILESTONES.md`** gains a "Rails ecosystem plugins" section listing the running parallel track. **CLAUDE.md** "Read these first" table and ADR table updated with ADR-9. **`skills/rigor-plugin-author/SKILL.md`** gains "Real-Rails alignment" and "Cross-plugin facts (post-ADR-9)" sections.

#### Example plugin: `rigor-activerecord`

- **Seventh worked example of the v0.1.0 plugin authoring surface** under [`plugins/rigor-activerecord/`](plugins/rigor-activerecord/) — and the most architecturally complete. Validates `Model.find` / `Model.find_by` / `Model.where` calls against the project's `db/schema.rb` and discovered AR model classes. Combines DSL interpretation (Prism walk over the `create_table "users" do |t| ... end` schema), multi-file `IoBoundary` reads (schema + every `app/models/*.rb`), chained cache producers (`:schema_table` + `:model_index`, both auto-invalidating on the digests of every file the boundary touched), and two-pass discover-then-validate.
- **Diagnostics.** `:info plugin.activerecord.model-call`; `:error plugin.activerecord.unknown-column` with Levenshtein-distance ≤ 3 did-you-mean suggestions; `:error plugin.activerecord.wrong-arity`; `:warning plugin.activerecord.load-error`.
- **Configurable.** `schema_file:`, `model_search_paths:`, `model_base_classes:`. **Schema parser** is a small Prism interpreter (no `eval`). **Inflector** is bundled, no `activesupport` runtime dependency.
- **Limitations** (intentional for v0.1.0 of the plugin): direct-superclass match only; `db/schema.rb` only (no `db/structure.sql`); no instance-method typing; no associations / scopes / strong parameters / controllers.
- **Demo project**, integration spec (14 examples). Eventual extraction via `git subtree split`.

#### Runtime audit guards for every `.rigor.yml` setting

- **New `Rigor::Analysis::Runner` "configuration wiring at runtime (audit guard)" spec block** verifies that each documented `.rigor.yml` setting actually flows from `Configuration` into the runtime, beyond the existing per-attribute load tests. The block was prompted by two phantom-setting bugs caught earlier in this batch (`cache.path` and `target_ruby`); the guard prevents the same regression class from re-opening silently.
  - `libraries:` reaches `Environment.for_project(libraries:)`.
  - `signature_paths:` reaches `Environment.for_project(signature_paths:)`, and a custom `.rbs` declared on a non-default path makes the class known via `Reflection`.
  - `plugins_io.allowed_paths:` extends `Plugin::TrustPolicy#allowed_read_roots`.
  - `cache.path:` is honoured by `Rigor::CLI#run_check` when constructing the `Cache::Store` (covered in `cli_spec.rb`).
  - `target_ruby:` is honoured by `Prism.parse_file(version:)` and a Prism-rejected version surfaces a single configuration-error diagnostic.

### Changed

#### Two-file config convention — `.rigor.yml` (dev-local) + `.rigor.dist.yml` (project default)

- **Auto-discovery order**: `Configuration.load(nil)` (the default when `--config=PATH` is not passed) now reads the **first** of `.rigor.yml` then `.rigor.dist.yml` it finds in the project root. Both files are **never merged automatically** — when a developer keeps a `.rigor.yml`, that file is the sole source of config for that developer's runs. The repo's own committed config moved from `.rigor.yml` to `.rigor.dist.yml`; the seven worked examples under `examples/rigor-*/demo/.rigor.yml` moved to `.rigor.dist.yml` for the same reason. `.gitignore` (top-level + each demo's `.gitignore`) now ignores `/.rigor.yml` so a developer's local override does not get accidentally committed.
- **`includes:` directive** (PHPStan-style). To extend the project default, an override file lists the dist file (and any others) under `includes:`:
  ```yaml
  # .rigor.yml
  includes:
    - .rigor.dist.yml
  disable:
    - call.undefined-method
  ```
  Processed in declaration order; later content overrides earlier; the current file's keys override every included file. Circular includes raise `ArgumentError`.
- **Path resolution** mirrors [PHPStan's rule](https://phpstan.org/config-reference#paths). Every path-bearing key (`paths:`, `signature_paths:`, `plugins_io.allowed_paths:`, `includes:`) is resolved relative to the **directory of the config file that declares it**. `paths: [lib]` in `<root>/.rigor.dist.yml` means `<root>/lib`; the same line in `<root>/sub/extra.yml` means `<root>/sub/lib`. `cache.path:` is the one exception — it stays as the literal user-supplied string so `--cache-stats` / `--clear-cache` messages read project-relative.
- **`rigor init`** now writes `.rigor.dist.yml` by default (the committed project default). Pass `--path=.rigor.yml` for the developer-local override.
- **`docs/handbook/01-getting-started.md`** § "A first walk through Rigor's config file" expanded with the new "Two file names, no implicit merge" + "Path resolution rules" subsections.

#### v0.1.1 Track 3 — plugin authoring DX

##### Slice 8 — plugin spec helper module extracted

- **New `Rigor::IntegrationSupport::PluginHelpers` module** at [`spec/integration/examples/support/plugin_helpers.rb`](spec/integration/examples/support/plugin_helpers.rb), auto-included for every `*_plugin_spec.rb` file under `spec/integration/examples/`. Replaces per-spec boilerplate (`requirer` lambda, hand-rolled `run_plugin`, hand-rolled `plugin_diagnostics` filter) with five helpers. All seven example plugin specs migrated; spec total dropped ~15%. `spec/spec_helper.rb` now also loads `spec/integration/**/support/**/*.rb`. SKILL Phase 6 updated with the slimmed boilerplate.

##### Slice 9 — strict per-demo cache isolation under `tmp/`

- **Each `examples/rigor-*/demo/.rigor.yml` now sets `cache.path: tmp/.rigor/cache`** so demo runs write under `tmp/` instead of `.rigor/cache/`. Each demo gains a `/tmp/`-only `.gitignore` (anchored to the demo root). The layout survives the future `git subtree split` per the `rigor-plugin-author` SKILL without depending on the parent repo's `.gitignore`.
- **CLI fix.** `Rigor::CLI#run_check` previously hardcoded `cache_root = ".rigor/cache"` and ignored `.rigor.yml`'s `cache.path:` setting. The CLI now consults `configuration.cache_path` so the demo `.rigor.yml` setting actually takes effect. The slice intentionally avoids "demo mode" auto-detection — demos opt in explicitly. `rigor-plugin-author` SKILL Phase 5 updated.

##### Slice 10 — examples re-included in RuboCop with documented relaxations

- **`.rubocop.yml`** removed the blanket `examples/**/*` exclusion. The new layout disables `Metrics/*` and `Naming/FileName` for examples, relaxes `Style/TopLevelMethodDefinition` and `Style/OneClassPerFile` for `examples/*/demo/**/*`, and excludes `Lint/StructNewOverride` / `Layout/LineLength` / `Lint/DuplicateBranch` / `Style/EmptyElse` for `examples/**/*`. Each carve-out is annotated inline. RuboCop now inspects 262 files / 0 offenses (was 210).
